# OpenZCAD master roadmap

**Single source of truth for priorities, dependencies and delivery status.**
Consolidated 2026-09-12 against OpenZCAD `main` at `28d1551f` (PR #315) and
reconciled 2026-09-17 against `main` at `3d7fdc33` (#316–#356).
Facts refreshed 2026-11-22 against `main` at `b36eebaf` (#1918): canonical
documents use schema v148 (`PROJECT_DOCUMENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` in
[packages/shared/src/index.ts](packages/shared/src/index.ts); per-version
history lives in that file and the evidence of the row that added it), and
the paired `remus-wasm` and `remus-wasm-io` packages are version
`2026.1.158`, pinned to immutable Remus commit
`a732c84d43efdffdb051eadfa003d3ca39f1af9a` in
[the adapter manifest](packages/kernel-adapter/package.json) and
[pnpm-lock.yaml](pnpm-lock.yaml). This source snapshot is not a fresh
qualification of upstream Remus `main` or a production-site acceptance report.

Revised 2026-09-26 against a feature inventory of a mainstream direct-modeling
CAD ([comparison record](docs/reviews/reference-cad-comparison-2026-09-26.md)); that pass added
U05–U07, S07, S08, F05, M10–M13, I04, I05, L08 and L09 and named slices inside
existing rows. It changed no delivery status.

Expanded 2026-09-29 with a file-interoperability scope: I06–I20 add format
qualification and native-CAD translation; D01/D04, I02 and I04 retain their
existing PDF, DXF, STEP-fidelity and export-option ownership. The
[format specification](docs/plans/file-format-interoperability.md) defines
directions, priorities and acceptance; these are planned capabilities.

Reviewed 2026-09-30 through the installed FreeCAD 1.1.3 interface
([hands-on comparison](docs/reviews/freecad-comparison-2026-09-30.md)).
This pass sharpens existing sketch, expression, reference, drawing and assembly
acceptance; it adds no delivery claim or new specialist-workbench lane.

Reviewed 2026-10-14 in a full project review
([record](docs/reviews/project-review-2026-10-14.md)): 39 confirmed bugs and 16
improvements across persistence, sharing, import, the kernel boundary, the
viewport and tooling. Its fix PRs record their evidence in the rows they touch;
the remaining findings are assigned to the master rows below; this evidence update changes no delivery status.

Reviewed 2026-11-15 against a desk survey of cloud, desktop, direct-modeling,
open-source and code-first CAD tools and their 2025–2026 releases (products
are unnamed here, following the repository convention). That pass added S09,
F06, M17, M18, D07, AS10 and L14–L18, moved L05's surfacing, configuration
and simulation discovery into those rows (sheet metal is M16), and added maker
hand-off slices to L09. It corrects M11 and AS08 from Open to Partial
against merged code, and with the owner it re-prioritized the
[Delivery order](#delivery-order) and moved L03, L08, L09 and M12 from
Deferred to Open. Its main findings: users name reliable fillets and booleans, an unbroken history and
fast edits before any new category, so kernel correctness ([K10](#k10)) and
edit latency ([H02](#h02), [W01](#w01)) bound the product more than breadth;
sketch inference ([S08](#s08)), feature patterns and standard holes
([M01](#m01), [M02](#m02)) and interference ([AS04](#as04)) are the
remaining table-stakes gaps; an agent API ([L14](#l14)) and scripting
([L15](#l15)) are the cheapest high-signal platform work; and a private free
tier with end-to-end encryption ([L11](#l11)) is a position no surveyed tool
offers.

Re-baselined 2026-11-22 against `main` at `b36eebaf`, after about 340 merges
in the week since the 2026-11-15 pass. Of that pass's active lanes, sketch
inference ([S08](#s08)), the assembly MVP ([AS03](#as03), [AS04](#as04)) and
maker hand-off ([L09](#l09), [D06](#d06)) are Complete. Every item on its
Next list except [M04](#m04) has also merged: L14, L15, F06, D02–D04, D07,
M09, R01, M06, M12, I02, I04, I06, M17, L08 and L03. [K10](#k10)'s ledgers
show no wrong cells and no untyped refusals. The
[Delivery order](#delivery-order) keeps the owner's 2026-11-15 priorities
(kernel correctness and edit speed, then encryption) and gives the finished
lanes' slots to the remainders of rows already in milestones 1–2 and to
assembly depth. A row-by-row status audit corrects F01 from Open to Partial,
K06, K07, W05 and L07 from Revalidate to Partial, and AS06 and AS07 from
Partial to Complete. Each correction is recorded as an evidence fragment
that quotes its row.

Start here to choose work. The documents in [Supporting plans](#supporting-plans)
retain specifications, acceptance details and historical evidence; their old
phase numbers, priorities and status tables no longer define independent queues.
ADRs retain architecture decisions. Record delivery status here through
[evidence fragments](docs/roadmap-evidence/README.md), and update a
supporting specification only when its contract changes.

## Analytics integration

| Master ID | Status | Scope and evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A01 | Partial | Glimpse: backend counters; fresh opt-in diagnostic/feature/size/part aggregates and user-shared reports; separate expiring cloud-account/storage census with no exported identifiers. [Setup and privacy boundaries](docs/glimpse.md). Regression coverage in `analytics.test.ts` and `worker/glimpse.test.ts`; production collector and consent migrations verified; activation enables both deployment switches while preserving fresh browser and separate account consent. Live delivery is checked during rollout. Local-only reopen probes now skip absent account copies while genuine failures remain observable (`accountProjectLoad.test.ts`, `local-project-cloud-load.spec.ts`); live rollout remains separate. 2026-11-05 WS3 refusal harvest: opt-in aggregate `kernel_refused` event whose bucket carries only the refusing operation, the kernel refusal family/operation/category/code, the warning kind and the target/neighbour surface types, each from a fixed vocabulary validated by the mirrored `glimpseContract.ts` in client and relay ([Kernel refusal harvest](docs/glimpse.md#kernel-refusal-harvest-ws3)). `analytics.test.ts` plants names, ids, hashes, dimensions and file names in every field and proves none reach the wire; `worker/glimpse.test.ts` proves the relay rejects non-canonical buckets and extra keys. The Glimpse service still has to mirror the vocabulary before it records the event. PR #1255 review fixes retain the real `direct-edit` family and operation in the client/relay vocabulary. Only user edit checks enter the harvester; background copies, demos, baseline/lineage builds and assistant analysis cannot count refusals or interfere with captures. `useRefusalHarvest.test.tsx` covers project/version changes and out-of-order replies. Approved 13 Nov 2026 Glimpse review: opt-in `error_unresponsive`, `error_gpu_lost`, `error_out_of_memory` and `error_worker` codes (`runtimeDiagnostics.test.ts`), and census `active7d`/`active30d`/`idle90d` counts computed in SQL with the k = 5 rule applied before sending, over account-statistics permission version 3 only (migration 0047, `glimpse-census.test.ts`). PR #1381. Needs esaueng/glimpse#32 deployed and Glimpse migration 0005 applied. Hosted OpenZCAD publishes aggregate cloud account and web login totals, and counts visible browser workspace starts independently of optional analytics. Beta and stable use distinct Glimpse entrypoints. See [metrics and rollout](docs/glimpse.md). |

## Where we are

OpenZCAD has a working browser CAD foundation: exact solid modeling, editable
feature history, expressions, direct manipulation, local persistence, optional
cloud sync and collaboration. As of 2026-11-22, reference geometry
([R01](#r01)), configurations ([F06](#f06)), a dimensioned and toleranced
drawing hand-off ([D02](#d02)–[D04](#d04), [D06](#d06), [D07](#d07)), the
assembly MVP ([AS03](#as03), [AS04](#as04)), the agent API and scripting
([L14](#l14), [L15](#l15)) and standard parts ([M17](#m17)) are Complete.
The remaining product gaps are sketch tool and constraint depth
([S05](#s05)–[S07](#s07)), part-modeling remainders ([M02](#m02),
[M03](#m03), [F01](#f01), [M10](#m10), [M11](#m11)), assembly couplings and
closed-loop solving ([AS08](#as08), [AS09](#as09)), native-CAD exchange
([I10](#i10)–[I14](#i14)), kernel capability limits that refuse by type, and
edit latency measured on target hardware ([W01](#w01)). The rest of this
section records how the product got here (written before 2026-11-22): the
largest gaps then were complete sketch constraint workflows, reference
geometry, drawings and assemblies.
Recent work improved existing modeling and the bounded imported-holder workflow,
and the 14 September kernel-adoption wave (#330–#342) moved booleans, patterns,
blends, mesh import, sketch modify tools and sections onto typed kernel entry
points; it did not complete those larger milestones.
The 26 September comparison with a mainstream direct-modeling CAD confirmed
those four gaps and added a fifth that needs no kernel work: the workspace
conventions every mainstream tool shares (a full hotkey set, saved views, item
groups, history step actions, notable-point measurement picks, X-ray display). It also
recorded that the reference CAD ships drawings, visualization and share links
with no assemblies at all, which supports the milestone order below.
The FreeCAD walkthrough reinforces the same sequence: actionable sketch feedback,
coherent expression/feature editing and a dimensioned drawing handoff, then
component instances and grounding before joint solving. Keep the quiet-stage
workspace; breadth of a reference CAD is not a reason to copy its toolbar density.

| Delivered baseline | Evidence and boundary |
| --- | --- |
| All schema-backed sketch constraint tools; editable persistent distance/angle/radius annotations | #142, #144, #231, #232; `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/dimensionAnnotations.test.ts`. Saved driving-label placement is complete under S01; reference dimensions remain S02. |
| Editable hole, mirror, split, shell, offset, draft, thicken, loft/sweep/helical-sweep history; fillet retargeting | #307, #309, #311, #314; `test/e2e/profile-feature-edit.spec.ts`. Advanced feature options remain M01–M06. |
| Validate-before-commit improvements, extrude orientation/text undo, diagnostics and availability fixes | #302–#306. Remaining command coherence is U01/U02; kernel trust is K01–K04. |
| Bounded growing-holder recognition, verified assistant proposals, width/height/bore controls and previews | #283, #284, #286, #288, #293, #296, #300; `test/e2e/growing-holder.spec.ts`. Supported-family delivery; H01–H03 retain acceptance, latency and geometry limits. |
| Hole-preserving caps on canonical section views | #313; `packages/viewport/src/scene/sectionCaps.test.ts`. Display meshes only; M08/D03 cover exact and arbitrary sections. |
| Positive overlapping-pattern, cross-drill and fillet-volume regressions | #228/#260 and `test/{overlapping-pattern,cross-drilled-render,filleted-body-volume}.test.ts`. Bounded examples, not complete boolean/measurement qualification. |
| Bounded boolean carrier and generated fillet-face lineage | #211; `test/boolean-carrier-lineage.test.ts`, `test/fillet-result-lineage.test.ts`. Shared/split carriers and unsupported classes remain K05. |
| Cloud sync, role/lease enforcement, save-state restore/branch, project backups and thumbnails | ADR-016/022; `test/e2e/save-state-history.spec.ts`, `test/e2e/project-thumbnails.spec.ts`. Restore/branch browser coverage and capture-on-leave are already implemented; live rollout evidence remains L01. |

Remaining string seams are the recorded allowances in W04. |

| Kernel-built patterns with per-instance face lineage; boolean entity evolution read as verified lineage evidence | #331, #340; `test/pattern-instance-lineage.test.ts`, `test/boolean-evolution-{lineage,geometry-invariant}.test.ts`. Probe doubles boolean rebuild cost (measured 2.03×); K05 owns the remainder. |

**Status vocabulary:** `Open` means a defined remainder; `Partial` means a
bounded subset is merged; `Complete` means the row's contract is merged and
only live acceptance, if any, remains; `Revalidate` means the older finding
lacks current proof and must be reproduced before implementation; `Deferred`
means deliberately outside the next delivery sequence. No other status is
used: a row whose work has merged is `Partial` or `Complete`, never "In
review", "Planned" or "Done". No feature row below is claimed actively
owned without a linked open implementation PR. “Delivered” means merged source
with referenced evidence; production acceptance is recorded separately.

## Delivery order

Finish useful workflows before adding a second way to do the same action.
Re-baselined 2026-11-22 against `main` at `b36eebaf`. The owner's 2026-11-15
priority stands: users name reliable fillets and booleans, an unbroken
history and fast edits before any new category, so kernel correctness and
edit speed stay the first active lane and end-to-end encryption the second.
The 2026-11-15 lanes for table-stakes inference, the assembly MVP and maker
hand-off have closed (S08, AS03, AS04, L09 and D06 are Complete), and all of
its Next list except M04 has merged. Their slots go to the remainders of
rows already in milestones 1–2 (part modeling and sketching, where users
meet the product every day) and to assembly depth. New categories
(native-CAD exchange, simulation hand-off, surfacing) wait in Next and Later
for the owner to select. Kernel work that does not serve the first lane
(other K-, W- and O-rows) runs alongside only where dependencies and touched
files are disjoint. This order keeps the 2026-11-15 Active 1, 2 and 6 and
replaces its finished lanes; that table had replaced the 2026-11-11
re-baseline. Each row is an outcome, not necessarily one PR. Split a large
row into named, bounded slices under the same ID before coding; never treat
a partial slice as the whole row's completion.

| Order | Lane or milestone | Exit signal |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Active 1 | Kernel correctness and edit speed ([K10](#k10) with [K01](#k01), [K07](#k07), [K08](#k08), [M03](#m03); [H02](#h02), [W01](#w01)) | K10 keeps no wrong cells and no untyped refusals on every pin bump. On 2026.1.157 its 1,508 cells were 1,289 qualified and 219 typed refusals, and #1925 re-recorded them unchanged on 2026.1.158. Each 2026-11-14 [production QA](docs/qa/2026-11-14/complex-cad-kernel-limits.md) P1 builds or refuses by type (K01/K08 evidence on 2026.1.138). What remains is adopting the upstream fixes as pins carry them: Remus #1772 (draft after rounds), #1778 (filleted drafted walls and pocket loops) and #1781 (face offset in a fillet loop) for K08, and #1742 for K01. Each regression then flips from typed refusal to build. M03 variable blends extend through per-vertex radii. W01's 100-feature isolated edit has to meet 100 ms on the RX 6600; it was 121 ms median on 2026-11-15. H02's first edit after reload and W01's budgets also have to be measured on integrated-GPU hardware, which is owner-pending with the commands ready. [K11](#k11) streaming drag stays paused. W03 threading stays Deferred unless those budgets cannot be met without it. |
| Active 2 | End-to-end encryption ([L11](#l11), with [L01](#l01) and [L10](#l10)) | Written outside-review clearance with no open P0/P1, then default-on, the plaintext freeze and the deletion PR, each under [ADR-024](docs/adrs/ADR-024-end-to-end-encrypted-projects.md) and its own authorization. Also L01 live cross-device acceptance and published free-tier terms. Until then: the remaining acceptance, migration/legacy-reader qualification, and the owner's copy and default decisions (I75, I76, I80, I84). |
| Active 3 | Part-modeling close-out ([M02](#m02), [M01](#m01), [F01](#f01), [M10](#m10), [M11](#m11)) | M02 repeats the remaining feature kinds and adds uniform or rotated circular orientation and a third linear axis. M01's named slices are on main (sketch-point positions, ISO/ANSI tables, tapped holes on M15 data, cosmetic threads, hole tables), so it closes with #1808 and a contract audit of hole handles under U01. F01 extends the inferred add/cut rule, with U01's override badge, to revolve, sweep and loft, adds start-from-plane, and takes to-face past cylinders. M10 adds rotate about a model edge or datum axis, Align, and non-uniform scale once Remus B74 affine lineage qualifies. M11 adds re-pick, target/tool badges, a switchable boolean type and multi-tool split. U11 brings its pick-and-drag interaction to each new tool. |
| Active 4 | Sketch completeness ([S05](#s05), [S07](#s07), [S06](#s06), [I01](#i01)) | S05 gets multi-selection drag (the driving offset dimension merged in #1919). S07 gets relations created by dragging onto geometry, arc tangency and the diameter dimension. S06 gets the entry/re-entry, Finish/Enter and body-pick behaviors and the prototypes it names. I01 gets silhouette and spline/ellipse edge projection and offsetting a body edge chain into a sketch, then trim, offset, mirror and pattern of splines and ellipses; spline constraints wait on Remus B75. |
| Active 5 | Assembly depth ([AS08](#as08), [AS09](#as09); [AS01](#as01) and [AS02](#as02) close-out) | AS08 adds screw couplings and a user-set ratio between any two joints, then video/GIF export of a motion study. AS09 starts with a design note, then closed-loop solving; until it lands, a loop joint is flagged "not solved". AS01 adds explicit part switching and two instances of one source with independent placement and stale behavior. AS02 closes with a contract audit. AS05 and AS10 stay Deferred. |
| Active 6 | iPad, touch and Apple Pencil ([L02](#l02)) | Evidence only: all eight slices have merged source; each closes with real-iPad evidence for its device-only behavior and measured rendering budgets. Do not add slices. Mouse and trackpad input stays unchanged. |
| Alongside | Workspace, drawings and gears ([U05](#u05)–[U07](#u07), [U10](#u10), [U11](#u11), [D01](#d01), [M16](#m16), [M14](#m14), [I07](#i07)) | U05: the remaining hotkeys and the hotkey-vs-search setting. U06: uniform card actions. U07: navigation presets and hidden edges. U10: a real-iPad frame-timing check (every planned lookdev slice has merged). U11: one tool per PR, coordinated with Active 3. D01: all sheets in one PDF, per-view scale and custom view angles. M16: the drawing-sheet bend table. M14: split end-cap and shoulder edge naming. I07: SVG import. |
| Next | Platform and exchange | [I10](#i10) native-CAD translator qualification, which gates I11–I14; licence and hosting are owner decisions. [L16](#l16) simulation hand-off. [M04](#m04) sweep and loft remainders as Remus qualifies their exact constructions. [M08](#m08) arbitrary, datum and face-aligned sections. [M05](#m05)/[M07](#m07) imported and direct-edit depth. [F03](#f03) AI vocabulary (Hole is behind `AI_PATCH_HOLE_ENABLED`). [F05](#f05) expression unit threading. [F02](#f02) persisted density. [I05](#i05) reference-image gizmo and tracing. [L13](#l13) SpaceMouse remapping. The L14 Phase 2 live bridge, an owner decision. Take these when an active lane is blocked or done. |
| 0 | Close the shipped workflow gaps | H01 has an explicit acceptance matrix; its live walkthrough needs the owner's source. H02 is now Active 1. U01/U02 resolve reproduced command/value/feedback disagreements, such as region extrude opening at Distance 0 and a resized bore's status. L01 records outstanding live checks. |
| 1 | Complete the modeling foundation | S01–S04, S08, R01 and F06 are Complete. S05–S07, F01–F05 and U05–U07 remain (Active 3–4 and Alongside): durable dimensions, understandable constraints, editable sketch geometry, useful reference/parameter surfaces, and the keyboard, history/items and viewing conventions of a mainstream tool. |
| 2 | Deepen part modeling and inspection | M06, M09, M12, M15, M17, I02, I04 and I06 are Complete. Selected M01–M16, I01, I05, I07 and I08 remainders work through creation, revision and export with their kernel prerequisites proved (Active 3 and Next). |
| 3 | Drawings MVP | Delivered: D02–D04, D06 and D07 are Complete, a dimensioned and toleranced shop hand-off with PDF and DXF. The D01 remainder runs alongside; D05 is Deferred. |
| Later | Reach and new body types | Select L04–L07, L12, L17, L18, D05, K09, S09, M18, AS05, AS10, I09 or I15–I20 from a measured use case; do not expand every lane at once. Surfacing (M18) waited for a clean K10, and K10's ledgers are now clean; it still needs K09 sheet and wire bodies first. |

Assemblies were milestone 4; they moved to the active lanes when AS01, AS02,
AS06 and AS07 merged (#1182). L02 moved from Later when it was selected on
2026-11-05. Drawings (milestone 3) closed as a milestone when D02–D04, D06
and D07 were Complete.

**Next bounded picks (reviewed 2026-11-22):**

1. [K10](#k10)/[K08](#k08)/[K01](#k01): on the current pin, check which of
   Remus #1772, #1778, #1781 and #1742 have landed. Where one has, flip its
   P1 regression in `test/qa-2026-11-14-p1.test.ts` from typed refusal to
   build, and make that test assert the pin's actual outcome instead of
   accepting either. Retire `fuseAllMergingFaceContacts` once #1742
   qualifies.
2. [W01](#w01): take the 100-feature isolated edit under 100 ms on the
   RX 6600. The worker answers in 46 ms, and about 73 ms goes to main-thread
   install plus the next frame, so about 21 ms has to come off the main
   thread.
3. [M03](#m03): land the open variable-radius PRs (#1914 per-vertex radii,
   #1927 radius stations; #1927 conflicts), then the remaining laws within
   what Remus qualifies.
4. [M02](#m02): the remaining feature kinds, circular orientation and a
   third linear axis. Then the [M01](#m01) close-out with #1808.
5. [F01](#f01): the inferred add/cut rule with an override badge for revolve,
   sweep and loft, then start-from-plane.
6. [S07](#s07): relations created by dragging onto geometry, arc tangency
   and the diameter dimension, then [S05](#s05) multi-selection drag.
7. [AS08](#as08): screw and ratio couplings, then study video/GIF export.
   Write the [AS09](#as09) design note.
8. [L11](#l11): the remaining acceptance and migration/legacy-reader
   qualification. Outside-review clearance, default-on, deletion and the
   owner's copy decisions wait on the owner and the reviewer, not on an
   agent.
9. [I10](#i10): the translator qualification note (design only; licence
   purchase and hosting are owner decisions).

**Waiting on the owner (not agent work):** H02/W01 integrated-GPU runs;
L02 real-iPad acceptance; L11 outside review; L13's SpaceMouse Pro device
run; the H01 live walkthrough; L01, L10 and A01 deployed migrations and live
checks (including esaueng/glimpse#32 and Glimpse migration 0005); and the L14
Phase 2 bridge decision. Live acceptance that needs credentials, devices or
source data stays pending without blocking independent work.

When every active pick is blocked on owner or outside input, take Next items,
then the milestone 0 remainders: [H01](#h01) from its matrix and the
[U01](#u01)/[U02](#u02) findings. Refresh `main`, the kernel pin and open PRs
before choosing. This list replaces the 2026-11-15 picks and the conflicting
next lists in older plans.

These milestones are not a global gate: independent foundation work can run
while live acceptance is pending, and drawing design can start before every
part-modeling row is complete. No calendar estimates are carried forward from
audits of earlier interfaces.

## 0. Close the shipped workflow gaps

| ID | Status | Outcome and completion evidence | Depends on / supporting detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="h01"></a>H01 | Partial | Parameter edits now preflight dependent geometry, reject measured-limit violations and preserve the last valid model; Tweak shows limits and refusals. Local regression coverage: `parameter-validation.spec.ts`, `growing-holder.spec.ts`. Close holder acceptance: audit existing browser/Vitest coverage, add missing equivalent-unit and worker-interruption cases, specify/test ambiguous drawing handling, then record the original-source import → proposal → combined edits → undo/reopen → STEP/STL flow on the deployed revision. Record synthetic, local-private and live evidence separately. Delivered 2026-09-17: the [acceptance matrix](docs/qa/2026-09-17/holder-acceptance-matrix.md) names every Phase 7 case with its evidence and tier; recognition margins now scale with document units (`test/growing-holder-units.test.ts`), the recipe build in an inch document is a pinned kernel refusal at the bridge union (K07), ambiguity is pinned end to end (`test/growing-holder-ambiguous.test.ts`), and reloads mid-Apply and mid-edit leave the document whole (`test/e2e/growing-holder-interruption.spec.ts`). Remaining: the live walkthrough on the deployed revision with the private source (checklist in the matrix), and the guided pick after ambiguity (H03). Since consolidation: assistant text toggles (#319), moved/rotated holders parameterize in place with lettering riding rigidly (#338, #351; `test/growing-holder-lettering.test.ts`, `test/e2e/growing-holder.spec.ts`). Rotated input is still refused unless the opening stays world-axis aligned. 2026-10-02 candidate: assistant follow-ups retain the originating selection intent, and explicit selected-edge subsets stay scoped during grounding; ambiguous multi-body selections refuse. Contract and panel regressions cover preview/apply targeting. 2026-10-14 review fix (this PR): on-demand imported-face recognition no longer scales document units a second time, so an inch document reads a 5 mm bore as 0.197 in, matching the measure pass (`test/imported-kernel-recognized-features.test.ts`). Full-suite follow-up qualifies the inch recipe through 2-inch growth and millimetre STEP replay, with strict solids, closed display meshes, measured bounds and independent material witnesses. Mesh closure searches adjacent quantization cells within the existing weld tolerance instead of rejecting coincident f32 boundaries at a grid split. Linear recognition rounds at the same physical precision in every unit, avoiding inch-dependent recipe shifts. Countersunk bore shrinking retains broken-out cone trims and certifies split circular rim coverage; native and STEP tests check closed-form added volume, unchanged retained carriers and source geometry, and watertight STEP replay. Four supported wedge end-cap fillets now have independent inner/outer closed-form volume oracles. The synthetic STEP fixture is regenerated from the pinned exporter, thread refusals use typed codes, and portable synthetic recovery tests preserve production tmpfs/swap and resolved-path guards. The six formerly failing archive suites also join the CI selection; no assertion or gate is removed. Large mesh sewing now stays within the pinned native arena's tolerance ceiling, so inch-sized imports remain readable through cached replay; millimetre, inch and enlarged fixtures verify strict solids, bounds and volume after serialization into a fresh kernel. Browser regressions cover invitation dismissal during document loading, last-valid exact drag previews, fillet rearming, font retry recovery and repeated unitless mesh imports. Library action menus stay inside the visible shelf body rather than hiding their first action behind its toolbar; desktop and phone browser coverage checks menu containment, opening properties and unchanged saved documents. Validated commits register timing intent before publishing their geometry, retaining installation and frame evidence when a preflight result is reused. The complete browser sweep also exposed reentrant assembly animation scheduling: synchronous pose notifications started a second frame loop. Playback now permits one pending frame across joint sweeps, studies and tool previews; regression coverage checks sustained motion, pause, completion and unmount without modifying the saved document. Drag continuity checks measure the rendered face boundary for both valid preview routes. A holed-body regression forces the exact worker stream and holds final validation while pressing the retained handle, preserving the selection, validation-lock and no-flash assertions. Successive drags also run against a manually held analysis window; the test requires all four edits and their cumulative 5 mm rendered boundary. Section controls retain their viewport clamp while opening and while compact rails reflow; light and dark browser checks require the complete DXF action to remain directly clickable at four viewport sizes, including short landscape. Running commands consume context clicks on empty space as well as geometry, including where a rim stood before its fillet preview moved it. Multi-edge fillet coverage preserves both input picks across all three cases. Project-shelf navigation follows the stable Back to projects action while the responsive header hides its optional Beta badge; save-failure and local reopening assertions retain their original storage and request checks. Legacy demo fixtures release their live workspace save owner before rewriting IndexedDB. They prove the old record exists and await the parameter, repaired placement and repair revision together in one committed snapshot. Sweep path clicks reframe after the staged sketch arrives and after each exact preview, checking projected points stay on the canvas and each path segment becomes valid before continuing. Panel drag picks wait for camera rest and the current hover frame instead of accepting a stale cursor. Section placement excludes CSS transitions of its translation, including under reduced-motion's global duration override. Browser coverage requires programmatic placement to move the visible panel within the same frame and keeps controls reachable through the Computing → Exact size change. A failed feature now passes its input body through like a manual suppression (ADR-017 November 2026 amendment): only the failed step reports failure, its dependents build on its unchanged input and are flagged "after failed step" in History, reference repairs are withheld downstream, and commit gates still refuse the breaking edit. Synthetic coverage: `test/failed-feature-pass-through.test.ts`, `packages/kernel-adapter/src/exact-suppression.test.ts`. Local-private evidence: replaying the reported fillet-radius history at r 3 mm, failures fell from 20 to 14 and "target is unavailable" from 9 to 0. Hash-only picks no longer stay fragile once their entity is named: a clean committed rebuild now writes the body's v5 reference beside the stored hash of every hole, thicken, thread, on-body gear, shell-opening, draft and face-distance-opposite face pick, as it already did for fillet/chamfer edges and a direct edit's face, all or nothing and never past the first failed, refused or suppressed step; an upstream resize then resolves the pick by lineage (ADR-013 H01 hash-only upgrade amendment; `test/hash-only-reference-upgrade.test.ts`, `packages/kernel-adapter/src/exact-reference-repair.test.ts`, `topologyReferenceRepairs.test.ts`). A commit that stores a new unnamed pick says so once, and Diagnostics counts the steps that hold one (`fragileSelections.test.ts`, `DiagnosticsPanel.test.tsx`). Forked histories (two active features replacing the same body, so two overlapping solids of one part are built) are now reported on the later branch as an attributed `history-fork` warning that no commit gate reads, and `CommandManager` refuses an edit that creates or retargets a feature onto a replaced body, except at the rollback marker; stored logs replay unchanged and Duplicate no longer offers Pattern (ADR-017 November 2026 "forked histories" amendment; stable body lineage identity proposed in docs/adrs/ADR-025-stable-body-lineage-identity.md). Synthetic coverage: `packages/document-core/src/body-consumption.test.ts`, `test/history-fork.test.ts`, `test/duplicate-step.test.ts`. Resuming a rolled-back History (Resume full history, or moving the marker later) rebuilds the candidate first and, when resumed features would fail, asks Keep rolled back / Resume anyway with each failure and its reason, instead of committing the breakage silently (ADR-017 November 2026 "preflight resume" amendment; `apps/web/src/lib/historyResume.test.ts`, `ResumeHistoryDialog.test.tsx`, `test/e2e/feature-history.spec.ts`). Resuming one History row from its own Resume action now takes the same preflight: the candidate is rebuilt first and, when the row or a later feature that was building would fail, asks Keep suppressed / Resume anyway with each failure and its reason; a clean resume commits the same single command with the checked rebuild (ADR-017 November 2026 "preflight resume" amendment; `apps/web/src/lib/historyResume.test.ts`, `ResumeHistoryDialog.test.tsx`, `test/e2e/feature-history.spec.ts`). | [Holder contract](docs/plans/step-parameter-hammer-holder-plan.md). No private fixture publication; unsupported input must refuse honestly. |
| <a id="h02"></a>H02 | Partial | Characterize the reported 18–31 s first edit after reload: measure cold and warm runs on named hardware, identify the bottleneck and pin a justified regression budget, keeping preview timing separate from exact completion ([plan](docs/plans/edit-pipeline-performance.md)). **Delivered:** M5 Pro characterization finds edited-body measurement, not replay, is the bottleneck, with within-run budgets in `test/e2e/perf-holder-reload.spec.ts`; fixed Text no longer replays; publication determinism fixes; a lineage-runtime candidate cuts M5 p95 27.8% (≥20% gate) and passes the private 80-edit session; geometry-only and prepared face-drag previews, drag checkpoints and faster viewer installation; viewer CPU and first-hover fixes; checked Offset Face rim joining via Remus #1489 and paired 2026.1.104 (esaueng/remus#1496); 2.30x faster complex-fillet dragging (PR #1155); typed refusal past a fillet flat. **Remaining:** quiet-host and target-device timing, cold-join latency, whole-worker heap retention, the detached Text cache (unimplemented), sustained-session acceptance, unmet face-drag targets, orbit tails and the complex-fillet 2x target; the lineage-runtime candidate's adoption on `main` is not recorded. [Full history](docs/roadmap-history/h02.md). Face-offset and cylinder-diameter releases are accepted with a draft display: the commit keeps every gating check, refusal, exact measurement and volume, and the moved body is drawn with the drag tessellation until idle worker work swaps in the full-quality mesh; only the display-gap advisory waits for that refinement (`test/draft-display-release.test.ts`, `geometryWorker.test.ts`, `useGeometryWorker.test.tsx`). Local release → accepted on a private 32-feature filleted-union reproduction (top face, two runs of three): 291/282/292 and 317/286/530 → 117/85/92 and 119/92/98 ms ([edit sessions](docs/interactive-edit-sessions.md), PR #1364). Face-offset and cylinder-diameter releases: the commit adopts its own validation sync as the live result instead of a duplicate broadcast rebuild (`useGeometryWorker.test.tsx`, `test/e2e/filleted-union-face-offset.spec.ts`), and a release still validating can be taken again — set aside, never committed unvalidated, reused when the hand lands on the same value (`useDirectEditCommit.test.tsx`, `machine.test.ts`, retake e2e). Local release → live result on a private 32-feature filleted-union reproduction: top 488 → 293 ms, side −32 mm 602 → 381 ms; cylinder remains bound by the exact resize ([edit sessions](docs/interactive-edit-sessions.md), PR #1356). Multi-body edits no longer pay for unchanged bodies: the display-mesh closure verdict is remembered with the retained measurement, mesh buffers compare through `Uint32Array` views, and the worker publishes unchanged bodies by identity (`shareRetainedMeasurements`). Node replay of the worker path, edit to first geometry: 30 curved bodies 272 → 5.5 ms, 100 boxes 57 → 17 ms; the headless-SwiftShader browser edit was unchanged, bounded by main-thread frames (`test/exact-shared-measurements.test.ts`, `test/display-mesh-closure.test.ts`, [QA note](docs/qa/2026-11-12/multi-body-sync-overhead.md), PR #1366). Offset-face drags on native filleted histories stop replaying unchanged upstream work: carry-proposing previews and commit checks share history checkpoints with plain syncs while every hash-only fillet/chamfer pick resolves as stored (`test/carry-neutral-checkpoints.test.ts` proves a carry and a plain refusal of the same document are never served for each other); a checked sync keeps a terminal face edit's result next to its source when the schedule allows, so the broadcast after a commit replays nothing (`test/drag-source-checkpoint.test.ts`); and face-move drag frames mesh at the disposable drag tessellation with full quality at rest and on commit (`test/display-deflection-hold.test.ts`, PR #1350). Reload without replay: the geometry worker persists the history kernel as a Remus whole-kernel session snapshot (Remus #1664) with the last feature's build state, digests and projection signature, in its own IndexedDB database (never in backups, downloads, account sync or shares; only for the user's own local projects, never a shared link's model or a collaborator's project; bounded, retired with its project; keyed by content and app/Remus/kernel build). A reopened project restores it instead of replaying, publishes only after its projection matches the captured one (exact identities and lineage, measurements within 1e-9 relative) and replays on any mismatch or refusal; an idle, cancellable warm-up then rebuilds the ordinary checkpoint table in a second kernel (`test/kernel-session-snapshot.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/kernelSessionStore.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/worker/kernelSessionPersistence.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/worker/geometryWorkerSessions.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/kernelSessionPolicy.test.ts`, [persisted sessions](docs/history-cache-retention.md)). Local measurement on a 32-feature filleted native part (Linux, Chromium, Vulkan RX 6600, local Remus 2026.1.134 build): reload to input-ready 1.00–1.10 s with the session versus 3.96–4.03 s replaying in the same build; Node restore plus measure pass 135–176 ms versus 1.5–4.0 s replay; 3.7 MB snapshot, 15–17 ms idle write, about 3 s idle warm-up. A boss diameter release commits the resize its drag prepared instead of rebuilding it: a drag preview builds only the dragged edit display-only, so a prefix its first frame replays is checkpointed under the digests the checked release restores (`test/prepared-resize-release.test.ts`). Local release → accepted on the private 32-feature filleted-union part, first diameter release: 773/780 → 609/609 ms, the kernel's checked commit being 530–535 ms of it ([edit sessions](docs/interactive-edit-sessions.md), PR #1370). Reopen: picking a face on the saved projection while a reopened project is still replaying its history no longer supersedes that rebuild. The pick adds lineage demand; when the adapter's boolean-probe decisions (and so the rebuild result key) are unchanged, the newer broadcast joins the running build instead of cancelling it at the next feature boundary and replaying again from the last history checkpoint. A pick that does need a probe, or a newer version, still supersedes it (`apps/web/src/worker/geometryWorkerDemand.test.ts`). Local measurement on a 32-feature native part (Linux, Chromium with hardware GL) finds reload to first editable state about 6.7–7.0 s either way, because checkpoints usually limit the restart to the in-flight feature; one software-GL run lost about 3 s to the restart. The remaining reload cost is the full exact history replay; persisting kernel state needs a whole-kernel snapshot (topology, journal, handle namespace) that the pinned Remus does not expose. Correction, as of 2026-11-13: the earlier closing note that persisting kernel state needs a whole-kernel snapshot "that the pinned Remus does not expose" is superseded. The pinned Remus exposes whole-kernel session snapshots (Remus #1664), and reopening a local project now restores one instead of replaying its history, as recorded under "Reload without replay" above (`packages/kernel-adapter/src/exact-session-snapshot.ts`, `test/kernel-session-snapshot.test.ts`). QA 2026-11-14 finding #16: an in-place pattern's Done commits the command its preview built (same ids and name) with that preview's exact result, instead of rebuilding the whole history again in a cold disposable worker. The preview is built with the live lineage demand, so the live rebuild after Done restores it from its checkpoint (`test/pattern-preview-commit-reuse.test.ts`). Closing the card cancels the preview's worker job (`useGeometryWorker` `syncOnce` signal), the card's Cancel stops a pattern Done still rebuilding, a slow preview counts its seconds, and Done applies a count still being typed in Options (`test/e2e/in-place-axis-picks.spec.ts`). 2026-11-15 Active 1 first-edit-after-reload baseline, three quiet-host runs on main `90c163c62` with Remus 2026.1.141, on a Ryzen 5 5600 with a discrete Radeon RX 6600 through Vulkan ANGLE. The machine has no integrated GPU, so that wording in Active 1 maps to this discrete GPU here; a real integrated-GPU device remains unmeasured ([record](docs/performance/2026-11-15-active1-baselines.md)). With the persisted session restored instead of replayed, reload → exact ready is 648 / 461 ms on the drilled (15 features) and lettered (31 features) holders. The first edit after a settled reload is 556 / 260 ms against a warm 435 / 146 ms, and an edit typed during the reload is 854 / 577 ms. Every run falls within 5% of its median except the lettered holder's first edit (−9% / +7%) and reload (−4% / +7%). Budgets (medians): reload 1,000 / 700 ms, settled first edit 850 / 400 ms, edit during reload 1,300 / 900 ms, plus the retained within-run ratio (first edit ≤ 1.5 × warm + 250 ms). `node scripts/measure-active1-baselines.mjs --check` reruns and enforces them (`scripts/active1-budgets.mjs`; probes record renderer, load and memory per sample). Contributors: after a session restore the lettered holder's first edit replays 27 of 31 features (138 ms of history against 60 ms warm), because the restored session lacks the independent-reuse state until one edit has run; the drilled holder replays all 15 features on every edit. The large-pattern preview baseline is also in this record: a 10 × 10 grid on a public reconstruction of QA's ×50 bar previews in 7.5 s, against a budget of 10 s that gates M02; `fuseAll` is 91% of the frame's kernel time. Not measured: the hammer-holder STEP behind the 18–31 s report. On this pin its verified "Parameterize holder and text" recipe fails exact-geometry preflight, so it has no parameter to edit. Whole-worker retention over a sustained session is bounded. A checkpoint restore retires arena slots as tombstones that are never reused, and the replay-work recycle counts features, not slots: 300 edits of the public filleted union left 343k retired slots, grew WASM linear memory 32 → 270 MB and the checkpoint estimate 1.2 → 157 MB (past the 128 MiB admission budget), and slowed one-feature edits about 47 → 58 ms. The adapter now classifies arena wear from the stats checkpoint admission already reads (`historyArenaWear`): a worn kernel (65,536 retired slots, four times the live ones) is compacted by the idle warm-up into a fresh kernel, which the worker now schedules for every live project (it stores nothing), and an exhausted one (262,144) is recycled before the next build (`recycleReason: 'arena-retention'`); a worn kernel is never persisted. Same session after: five idle compactions of about 220 ms, WASM memory plateau 102 MB, estimate ≤ 32 MB, edits flat at 43–47 ms, Node heap flat ([record](docs/performance/2026-11-20-sustained-session.md); `test/sustained-session-retention.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/worker/geometryWorkerSessions.test.ts`). Browser-worker heap on target hardware remains owner-pending. A reopened project's first edit no longer replays independent work its session already held. The persisted kernel session now carries the capturing kernel's independent-reuse tables (primitive and standalone standard-part results, prepared transforms and intersections), whose handles the snapshot keeps; a restore adopts every entry still at its recorded position and digest, so the first edit reuses what the capturing session would have instead of replaying every feature before the session's single checkpoint (the 2026-11-15 baseline's contributor 3: the lettered holder replayed 27 of 31 features after a restore against 12 warm). A stale entry is only that entry's miss, a malformed table refuses the record, and older records restore as before ([persisted sessions](docs/history-cache-retention.md); `test/kernel-session-snapshot.test.ts`). Browser first-edit timing on target hardware remains owner-pending (`node scripts/measure-active1-baselines.mjs --only h02 --check`). Dense display-mesh smoothing retains each edge's incident triangle corners. On the public 125,042-triangle STEP hammer holder, smoothing took 75.4 → 62.3 ms and full CPU viewport preparation took 104.2 → 90.5 ms (27 samples per revision). All prepared buffers, tangent edge IDs and silhouette data matched. In three interleaved Chromium runs per build on an RTX 3090, the median typed −6 mm face preview took 265 → 259 ms, Apply 613 → 622 ms and Undo 263 → 227 ms. The 2× end-to-end target remains open. The opt-in `test/e2e/perf-face-drag.spec.ts` accepts `OZ_PERF_ANGLE=gl` for hardware rendering. Detached Text cache, without a kernel branch operation: an `imported-step` result is now self-contained for independent reuse (its digest names source bytes, reader, selection, emboss and placement; it reads no parameter or body), so a lettered holder saved with its fixed Text import after the parameter-driven union keeps the lettering in the live arena across holder edits instead of re-importing it; nothing is restored from a checkpoint, and the parameter-driven placement still replays. Hits publish exactly what a cache-free build does; emboss, source-bytes, units, order, suppression and kernel-restart changes each re-import (`test/detached-text-cache.test.ts`; `reusedImports` on the cache event); a changed import is re-measured while independent bodies after it are reused (`test/bounded-history-cache.test.ts`). Acceptance conditions in the [design](docs/plans/h02-private-holder-cache-design.md#detached-text-reuse-in-the-live-arena-2026-11-20); the private-holder p95 comparison remains owner-pending. Face-drag, orbit and sustained-session probes join `scripts/measure-active1-baselines.mjs` (`face-drag`, `orbit`, `sustained`), with budgets in `scripts/active1-budgets.mjs` and summary coverage in `test/active1-summary.test.ts`; drag and orbit run on an `OZ_PERF` instrumented build of their own ([record](docs/performance/2026-11-20-drag-orbit-sustained-baselines.md)). On the RX 6600 (Vulkan), two runs: the pointer is followed at 14–15 ms p50 / 15–16 ms p95 (target p95 < 50 ms met), frames at 60 fps over the whole motion (119–122 frames in 2 s; intervals 16.6 ms p50, 17.2–18.2 ms p95, at most 26 ms, read from the drag trace), but first geometry within 100 ms is not met for exact geometry: it reaches the screen 2.3–2.7 s after the first move, because the exact preview is requested only once the hand rests (its kernel time is 61–404 ms) and release → accepted in 76–167 ms; every orbit scene draws 58.5–60 viewer frames per second with no viewer frame over 16.8 ms at ×1 and ×4 CPU, so the ordinary orbit tails do not reproduce here. The complex-fillet 2× target's code (OpenZCAD #1155, Remus #1492) is on `main`; its private-part requalification, integrated-GPU and quiet-host timing are listed owner-pending with commands in the record. Undo after a typed −6 mm face offset on the public 125,042-triangle Hammer Holder took 236.2 ms on the original build, 209.9 ms on the current control and 80.2 ms with dense mesh preparation reuse (2.95× against the original and 2.62× against the control). These are medians from six fresh Chromium runs per build on an RTX 3090 using the same input-to-rendered-frame probe (`test/e2e/perf-face-drag.spec.ts`). Restored shading, silhouettes and tangent edge IDs matched fresh preparation. Per viewport, two recent entries each have a 12 MiB admission budget for backing buffers and estimated metadata. Eligible parameter edits reuse their validated exact geometry and can save the committed kernel session. After a settled Chromium reload, median input-to-exact time fell from 588 to 240 ms on the opening width fixture and from 324 to 118 ms on the holder height fixture. The [paired browser measurements](docs/performance/2026-11-20-parameter-commit-reuse.md) include warm edits, reloads, immediate edits and cache outcomes. Typed face offsets start the exact worker before the local deformation and reuse moving-region cell classifications during the gesture. Rigid STEP imports can seed up to 256 raw face measurements for the first preview. Replaying the holder’s 318,095 recorded position queries returned identical classifications and reduced the median from 54.9 to 25.1 ms over 12 runs. In three fresh Chromium sessions per build, source reuse alone reduced first exact preview worker time from 103.5 to 83.8 ms on the same part. The opt-in `perf-face-drag.spec.ts` measures the full input-to-render interval. The imported fillet and through-hole regression compares the mesh and face topology of the first preview against a cold adapter. Face-drag targets settled (owner decision 2026-11-21): H02's 60 fps, first-geometry ≤ 100 ms and p95 ≤ 50 ms targets apply to what follows the pointer, and proxy-led dragging meets them on the RX 6600 (proxy frames 15–16 ms p95; 119–122 frames in a 2 s drag, 17.2–18.2 ms p95, #1834). First exact geometry, built once the hand rests, stays a measured and budgeted row (2.3–2.7 s from the first move; kernel time 61–404 ms), and an exact preview fast enough to stream while the pointer moves, including the complex-fillet case's 385–404 ms exact build, is split out as [K11](ROADMAP.md#k11) ([record](docs/performance/2026-11-20-drag-orbit-sustained-baselines.md); `scripts/active1-budgets.mjs`). A checked fillet can reuse the dense display mesh prepared for its preview when the mesh buffers, face ranges and hashes, edge IDs and adjacent faces match. On a 32 × 20 × 12 mm box with a 1 mm fillet on all twelve edges, median Apply to the committed frame fell from 186 to 158 ms for immediate Apply and from 99 to 83 ms after validation had settled. All six pairs improved for each timing on an RTX 3090 with OpenZCAD control 41f34fe3c and Remus c81a719a. Preview and commit had identical mesh buffers, and Undo restored the original box in all 36 browser sessions. Moving a sketch point in a 100-hole plate with 42,412 displayed triangles took 836.15 ms from Apply to the matching changed frame on the parent and 778.15 ms with the display worker started during sketch editing and the tangent calculation shared. The interval from the geometry reply to that frame fell from 144.30 to 91.30 ms. Six alternating pairs in hardware Chromium on an RTX 3090 used the same released kernel and matched the mesh buffers, topology, volume and warnings. History sketch dimension edits can commit their checked exact geometry. On a plate with a downstream Boolean through-cut and 30 independent boxes, the 22, 23 and 24 mm width edits fell from 155.8 to 34.6 ms (4.50×), 113.0 to 33.3 ms (3.39×), 101.4 to 26.8 ms (3.78×), respectively (three browser runs per revision). Each edit sent one worker sync instead of two and produced the expected 864, 904 or 944 mm³. The [paired Chromium benchmark](docs/performance/2026-11-20-history-sketch-reuse.md) times Apply to the committed exact-analysis event. Adaptive resolution during slow orbit and pan, including buttonless trackpad pan. Two consecutive slow moving frames can lower the backing ratio to 1.25× or 0.75×. A still frame restores full detail, and image export retains its normal dimensions and styling throughout. The browser regression exercises a slow trackpad pan and opens Export Image while the canvas is reduced. In two Chromium SwiftShader runs on the 88-bore tooling plate at 1366 × 1024 CSS pixels and 2× display density, median intervals between changed camera draws fell from 354–359 to 158–159 ms in orbit and from 338–340 to 155–158 ms in pan. Across whole gestures, including initial full-size draws, orbit rose from 2.9–3.0 to 3.9–4.5 changed draws/s and pan from 3.4–3.8 to 5.2–5.4. [Tooling plate fixture](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/blob/d4f588f3a68df33e1f9203c3dec50af0adfa2cb4/scripts/perf/tooling-plate/dense.openzcad). Exact gear generation, edge-modifier rebuilds and viewport interaction on edge-heavy models. A 60-tooth gear rebuilt in 941 ms versus 1,652–1,715 ms across two sessions per build. Fillet and chamfer edits on a 486-edge collared gear improved in both run orders. A steady torus contour update took 0.129 ms versus 0.416–0.420 ms, with an extra 1.1 ms on its first camera move. [Benchmarks, controls and frame timings](docs/performance/2026-11-22-edge-heavy-parts.md). Severely GPU-bound orbit and pan can enter the reduced framebuffer on the first moving draw. Two completed GPU measurements at rest or an observed backlog qualify the scene. Automatic full-size animation frames wait for the previous submission. At the 0.75× backing ratio, dense sharp-edge batches can use one physical pixel per stroke and retain every original segment and its depth bias. The normal strokes return at rest. Regression coverage includes a bent edge behind a face, a 33-body cache at capacity, a zoom crossing the severe threshold, and empty hovers during a slow fade. The Chromium comparison uses a 1366 × 1024 viewport at 2× density, a 12-second Shift-left-drag for orbit and a 12-second right-drag for pan. The reported rate counts scene submissions with a changed camera across the whole gesture, including its onset. [88-bore tooling plate](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/blob/d4f588f3a68df33e1f9203c3dec50af0adfa2cb4/scripts/perf/tooling-plate/dense.openzcad). | Existing holder instrumentation; [performance record](docs/performance-baseline.md). #296 improved warm rebuilds; it did not close cold latency. 21 October [existing-cache qualification](docs/qa/2026-10-21/h02-existing-cache-qualification.md): the current-pin private oracle matches every per-body serialized B-rep byte across 17 bodies/720 faces; real measurement-cache budget eviction, refusal/recovery and retired-checkpoint regressions pass. Full detail in [the history](docs/roadmap-history/h02.md#depends-on--supporting-detail). |

| <a id="u01"></a>U01 | Partial | One command/value owner across form, chip, keypad and Inspector, with consistent preview/commit/cancel and explicit direction/extrusion defaults; browser cases must prove displayed value = committed geometry and that cancellation preserves history. **Delivered:** one command name and diagnostic (9c282e42), with value/Enter/Escape proved in `command-contract.spec.ts`; one Escape clears to nothing selected; face offsets preview the whole body over a neutral ghost, keep the moved face selected and hold the camera; box, rounded-box and gear height/face-width drags; display-only fillet/chamfer drag frames (#1144, #1147) and all-edge selection; Delete on a picked face or edge removes its owning feature, including imported STEP details; Resize Body through later blends carries hash-only edge picks; multi-region extrude, type-first text and sketch drag/transform (#585, #587, #588, #592); hole Ø/R wording and bore selection through resize and undo/redo. **Remaining:** W11 (region extrude opens at Distance 0 with an error, not a default); resized bores read "Adjusted cylinder diameter" and lose through-hole status (U02 naming rule); UX-5 Edit Sketch pill follow-up has no recorded fix; held-pull caps unverified in browser/native. [Full history](docs/roadmap-history/u01.md). Instant push/pull preview for a planar face rounded into a taller wall: a proven blend that rises past the face (a box top filleted into a cylinder) now moves with it while the wall stretches, instead of declining the mesh preview and leaving the drag to exact rebuilds alone. Curved walls ruled along the drag are bounded by where their moving outline would cross their fixed one, so a sliver beside the seam no longer stops the preview at 0.0004 mm. On the filleted-union fixture the preview's volume change matches the exact offset within 0.1 mm³ for −6 to +3.5 mm; it reaches the cylinder rim (+4 mm) and the corner bands' floor (−21 mm). Rising planes and unrecognized surfaces still decline (`step-preview-profile.test.ts`). A local Chromium (ANGLE/Vulkan) drag on the 25-feature filleted union showed the proxy following every sampled pointer step to +8 mm, where it previously stayed on exact frames only. Correction (2026-11-12): the UX-5 Edit Sketch pill follow-up is fixed. #1001 (`5f4d3e9c`) clears the pill in a layout effect keyed on the project id, so it never paints in the next project (`apps/web/src/App.tsx`, "Nor does it outlive its project"); it no longer belongs in this row's remaining work. A rounded primitive cylinder's diameter drag is instant again and edits the cylinder's radius instead of appending a local "Resize Cylinder" step. Two regressions, both visible only in full browser runs, had stopped it. First, the curved-surface outline's preview cache, counted against the mesh projection's 2 MB budget since #1290/#1333, pushed a Ø140 R2-rimmed cylinder to about 2.3 MB, so every drag frame went to an exact rebuild. The budget is now 8 MB (`cylinderProfilePreview.test.ts` installs the outline at that size). Second, #1276 sent every filleted boss to the kernel's local blend-group resize. That route now owns only a wall that reaches its cylinder across a boolean, where seam blends belong to the body. A cylinder whose own rim blends are all that separate it from its primitive keeps the source edit, with exact rim replay and parametric history (`cylinderPrimitiveAncestry.test.ts`). `cylinder-preview.spec.ts` passes all eight cases, and its radius drag case is now `@ci-smoke`. Typed sizes on an armed in-place command reach its values instead of the view shortcuts (2026-11-12 hands-on review): a digit, minus or point opens the first value with a chip, Tab and Shift+Tab commit and step through the rest in the order Options list them with the field held in one place, Enter commits and returns focus to the command bar so the next Enter is Done, and a refused or escaped entry restores the value. `B 100 Tab 60 Tab 6 Enter Enter` builds a 100 × 60 × 6 mm box where it used to turn the camera and end in "Box canceled"; Cylinder, Cone, Sphere and Torus share the path (`in-place-primitives.spec.ts`, `NumericKeypad.test.tsx`, `exactEntryShortcut.test.ts`). A typed value that takes a creation preview out of the clear stage reframes the camera onto the created bodies, never for an edit or a drag (`fitFrame.test.ts`, `docs/interaction-design.md`), and the command card keeps 12 px clear of the view cube from 960 px to 1920 px wide. 13 November design review, Build command cards (I30, I28, I32, the fillet refusal in I10, and I34 polish): Enter on a command's bar while the preview of the value just typed is still being checked is held and applies once it is valid ("Applying…"), so "100 Tab 60 Tab 6 Enter Enter" makes the box; an Enter with nothing waiting to preview is not held. Fillet and Chamfer no longer send the user to an Options field that appears only after the first edge, and Gear says where module and teeth are set. Thread and Gear teeth say "Needs a cylindrical face" on a model whose live bodies have none. A blend refused at a size another builds at drops the repeated feature name, quotes both sizes in the document unit and offers the size that builds as one press ("Use 6.25 mm"). The state pill stays empty until there is something to preview, Position coordinates align with Size, a point's name wraps instead of ending "Base cen…", Scale says "Body center", and History names a fillet's radius "R" as its chip does. Coverage: `InPlaceCommandBar.test.tsx`, `sizeRefusal.test.ts`, `tools.test.ts`, `inPlace/tools.test.ts`, `featureSummary.test.ts`, and `build-command-cards.spec.ts` in the browser. 13 November design review, Move and body picks (I29, I31): the Move card is titled "Move" as the rail and drawer name it, carries the same state pill as every other command, words its Options, says its step once (the bar's hint, which now names the rings and Shift; the "Move/Rotate: …" toast is gone), and its Cancel or Escape says "Move canceled; nothing was added." as other commands do. With nothing selected, a model's only body is the body Scale, Mirror, Split, Rotate about axis, Solid offset and the patterns start on, as Move already did. A new primitive over a model that has bodies says that Done adds it as a separate body where it shows. Coverage: `DirectModelingOverlays.test.tsx`, `loneBody.test.ts`, `inPlace/tools.test.ts`, and `build-move-and-picks.spec.ts`, `move-hidden-sketch.spec.ts` and `production-qa-regressions.spec.ts` in the browser. No second search surface. First slice (research audit ZC-205, the keymap table of ZC-204): one keymap registry (`apps/web/src/lib/keymap.ts`) carries every remappable shortcut — the workspace map, tools (all of them, bound or not), views, sketch tools — and the workspace keydown handler, command bar rows, tooltips, accessible names, context menus and the `?` reference all read the live binding. Settings › Shortcuts remaps any command, refuses a taken key by naming the command that holds it (contexts that never hold at once, such as M for Move and Measure, do not clash), refuses the fixed keys (Escape, Enter, Tab, Delete, arrows) and browser chords, and resets one command or all. Remaps are an account/device preference (`AppSettings.keymap`), never project data; no document schema change. Explicit saved shortcuts in overlapping contexts keep their keys when a new default would conflict; that view appears unassigned, while disjoint sketch bindings still coexist. The [bounded acceptance contract](docs/plans/standard-view-shortcuts.md) defines keyboard/palette camera equivalence, remap/reload behavior and exclusions. | <a id="u10"></a>U10 | Partial | Viewport look development, planned 2026-11-08 ([plan](docs/plans/viewport-lookdev-plan-2026-11-08.md)): faces separate clearly by orientation, edges ink crisply, curved surfaces show an outline and the stage frames the model, judged against fixed lookdev captures in the production renderer. Slices: harness and material seam, lighting and material, edge ink, curved-surface outlines, stage, optional ambient occlusion. Follow-up, ambient occlusion: settled-frame contact shading in concave corners (`render/ambientOcclusion.ts`). It is computed once the view has rested for 150 ms, at half resolution (capped at 1.6 MP), with a tangent-plane estimator that leaves planar and convex faces untouched, and is applied inside the body shader so edge ink, overlays and the stage never darken. Moving frames cost nothing (measured: orbit CPU unchanged, a sub-microsecond camera check); the settle costs 7–19 ms of GPU once on an Intel UHD at an iPad Pro 12.9" canvas (2048 × 2732, 128 bodies). Off in X-ray, wireframe, E2E builds and off-canvas renders; `AMBIENT_OCCLUSION.enabled` and `window.__ozAmbientOcclusion = false` turn it off (`ambientOcclusion.test.ts`, `renderProfile.test.ts`). Slice 4, curved-surface outlines: cylinders, cones, spheres and fillets now outline where they turn away from the camera. Interior mesh edges of each curved face carry both neighbouring normals, and the fat-line vertex shader keeps only those straddling the view each frame, so the outline is GPU-side while orbiting and matches the B-rep edges' ink and width (`packages/viewport/src/render/silhouette.ts`, `silhouette.test.ts`). Slice 3, edge ink: idle edges are near-black (`#0b0d10`) at full opacity and 1.5 px, and tangent (fillet) rims rest at 0.42 instead of 0.18, which had all but vanished under the studio shading (`SELECTION_SEMANTICS.idle`). Project thumbnails take the same ink. Judged in the lookdev captures. iPad-class frame timing measured by proxy, not on a device (`perf-frame-timing.spec.ts`, `OZ_PERF=1`: desktop Chromium, 1366 × 1024 touch viewport at device pixel ratio 2, CDP CPU throttling ×1/×4/×6, Intel integrated GPU). The studio material and environment cost nothing measurable. The curved-surface outlines add 1–8 ms of GPU p95 per frame at ×1 and up to 9× the triangles drawn, and their candidate build stalls each curved body build for 22–320 ms (0.2–1.5 s at ×4–×6). The touch render budget absorbs most of the per-frame cost after the first gesture. Results and real-iPad instructions are in the plan's follow-up. Slice 1, no visual change: `OZ_LOOKDEV=1` builds the production renderer and `test/e2e/viewport-lookdev.spec.ts` captures five fixed scenes (`test/fixtures/lookdev-scenes.ts`) from three view-cube corners at device pixel ratio 2, canvas only, into `artifacts/lookdev/<label>`; `scripts/lookdev-compare.mjs` builds a click-to-flip before/after page (`test/lookdev-compare.test.ts`). `isBodyMaterial()` replaces the `MeshPhongMaterial` class checks that gated body buffer reuse and the selected-face fill, pinned in `bodyUpdate.test.ts`. Baseline captures taken locally at `3da524a4`. Face-offset push-in reads as one frosted slab over the part: while the ghost of the old part is shown, the moved face's selection fill is hidden, since seen through the ghost's differently lit top and sides it read as a misplaced blue panel standing off the part. The fill returns when the ghost clears (`ModelViewer.tsx`, held push and pull captured on the filleted union). Face-offset preview: the ghost of the part as it was reads as a frosted, outlined shape on the graphite stage (depth pre-pass hides its far side; stencil-masked, once-per-pixel edges never leak across the live solid). A pull outlines the old end on the extended part, and while the face is held the added material draws as ghost, solid again on release. Drag-time preview edges take the resting ink, so arcs no longer bead or dash before the commit (`bodyGhost.test.ts`, `bodyUpdate.test.ts`). Face-offset push-in no longer shows a black lid at the old face while the handle is held: the added-material veil now engages only when the displayed part reaches past the old face (a pull), and its cap carries the body's normals so it shades in the lit finish (`bodyGhost.test.ts`). Curved-outline candidates build 2–7× faster on the main thread (typed arrays and open-addressed hashing, bit-identical output; the rotational sleeve goes from 81 ms to 27 ms), and each build leaves an `oz:viewer.silhouettes` User Timing mark for the frame-timing probe (`silhouette.ts`, `silhouette.test.ts`). Curved-surface outlines are now picked on the CPU when the view changes and draw only the segments on the outline. Drawing every candidate each frame put 58,957 fat-line quads per frame on a filleted sleeve, which slowed `rotational-direct-edits` past its budget on CI's software rasterizer. That part now draws about 560 instances per frame instead of 59k, picking costs about 1 ms per view change, and the lookdev captures are pixel-identical (`updateSilhouette` and `installSilhouetteUpdates` in `packages/viewport/src/render/silhouette.ts`, `silhouette.test.ts`). Follow-up: faces of one smooth surface split by the kernel (a sphere's equator, cylinder halves, fillet rims) now share normals across tangent boundaries, removing the faint shading rings at those seams; genuine creases stay split (`tangentFacePairs`, `ViewportShading.test.ts`). The lookdev harness also captures hover, face and body selection and X-ray, which read clearly under the studio rig and keep their tuning. Slice 5, stage: the dark stage is a neutral graphite with a visible grid instead of blue-tinted near-black, so outlines and the ground shadow read; the default body colour desaturates to `#a2a6ae` at the same lightness. The light stage is unchanged. `stagePalette.test.ts` pins the fallback palette to the dark tokens; lookdev captures cover both themes (`OZ_LOOKDEV_SCHEME`). Slice 2, lighting and material: bodies are `MeshStandardMaterial` (roughness 0.52, dithered) lit by `STUDIO_RIG` (`packages/viewport/src/render/studio.ts`): a smooth Z-up gradient environment, a key light that turns with the camera, a dim world-fixed shadow key, a dark floor bounce and `NeutralToneMapping`. Tops, walls and undersides now separate by value from every lookdev corner with no tessellation crawl; thumbnails and the exact section fill share the rig. Contracts: `ViewportShading.test.ts`, `partThumbnailRenderer.test.ts`. Follow-up: Settings › Viewport › Tangent edges (Show / Faint / Hide; default Faint, the shipped 0.42 rim) restyles resting fillet and blend rims in Shaded + edges. Hidden rims still hover, select and highlight; wireframe, X-ray, silhouettes and thumbnails are unchanged. Evidence: `tangentEdges.test.ts` (each style, display modes, body selection, `suspendHighlights`, silhouettes), `PickService.test.ts` (a hidden rim still picks), `app-settings.test.ts`, `api-routes.test.ts` (carried across an older client's save), `SettingsPage.test.tsx`, `test/e2e/tangent-edge-setting.spec.ts`, and lookdev captures of the rounded-block and filleted-cylinder scenes in all three styles. Orbit mode preference: Settings → Controls → Orbit chooses Free (the default: no polar limit, a drag tumbles over the top and bottom views) or Turntable (world Z stays up, a vertical drag stops at the top and bottom views, and an upside-down view is turned back right way up as an orbit starts). It is read live by the camera, syncs with account settings, and an older client's save keeps the stored choice (`viewport.orbitMode`, `CameraController.test.ts`, `test/e2e/orbit-mode-setting.spec.ts`). Orbit mode shortcut and button: Shift+O (remappable, "Toggle orbit mode"), the command bar's "Orbit: switch to …" row and an Orbit mode button on the viewer bar (pressed in Turntable, "Now:" naming the mode) flip the orbit preference between Free and Turntable. All three change the same account setting as Settings → Controls → Orbit. The button leaves the bar only in the window heights where it would push the bar past the window's foot (≤540px, touch ≤611px, a one-column mouse rail 601–638px), where Shift+O and the command bar still switch it (`apps/web/src/lib/keymap.ts`, `ViewerToolbar.test.tsx`, `test/e2e/orbit-mode-setting.spec.ts`, `design-review-followups.spec.ts`). Orbit mode in the workspace status: the off-screen "Workspace status" summary that assistive tech reads (warnings, sync) now ends with the orbit mode, "orbit Free" or "orbit Turntable", and follows Shift+O, the orbit buttons and Settings; nothing changes on screen (`WorkspaceReadout.test.tsx`, `test/e2e/orbit-mode-setting.spec.ts`). An upside-down view survives reload: the camera's held up is recorded with the pose (`ViewportCameraState.up`, omitted when it is world +Z) and kept by the per-device view, saved views and the cross-device resume state; a saved view glides onto its roll instead of turning over at the end (`CameraController.test.ts`, `workspaceSession.test.ts`, `savedViews.test.ts`, `test/workspace-resume-field-of-view.test.ts`, `test/e2e/upside-down-view-reload.spec.ts`). Viewer navigation and axes: orbiting has no polar limit — a vertical drag carries over the top and bottom views and turns the view upside down, a horizontal drag stays a roll-free turntable about world Z that follows the hand when upside down (`packages/viewport/src/camera/freeOrbit.ts`, `CameraController.test.ts`). The world axes draw as full lines, positive half bold and negative half quieter, each clipped to the camera's view volume so it runs off screen in every perspective and orthographic view instead of stopping mid-frame (`scene.test.ts`, `updateAxesGizmo`). View cube help names the orbit mode: hovering the cube shows "Free orbit" or "Turntable orbit" with the key that switches it (Shift+O, or its remap) and what a drag does in that mode, and follows the mode as it changes. | <a id="u11"></a>U11 | Partial | Tool interaction overhaul, started 2026-11-08 with the owner: every Build tool works the same way, made simpler. Inputs are picked in order in the model (profile, then axis, path or faces) and every pickable input is drawn and named so it can be found; nothing the geometry already says is asked for (a helix radius is its profile's distance from the axis); at most one or two drag handles act on the result itself (drag the coil's end to wind turns); rare choices stay behind Options, which reads out the value the others decide. Helical sweep first, then thread, gear, booleans and patterns, one PR per tool with an e2e driven only by picks and drags. The exact (non-faceted) coil that starts at its profile needs the paired Remus `helicalSweepInPlace`; until it is pinned, a helical sweep body draws its facet joints in wireframe only. Helical sweep, first slice: profile, then an explicit axis (wide dashed candidates with clickable name tags, shared with Revolve, Rotate about axis and Circular pattern; a line through the profile is not offered; edges and cylinders count), no radius control, one stretch arrow and a coil-end ball that winds turns at the same spacing, Height & turns / Pitch & turns, Up/Down, an Options readout; edits round-trip unchanged; facet joints of a helical sweep body are wireframe-only (`tools.test.ts`, `edit.test.ts`, `useInPlaceCommand.test.tsx`, `coilRig.test.ts`, `axisPickerRig.test.ts`, `tangentEdges.test.ts`, `in-place-sketch-tools.spec.ts`, `profile-feature-edit.spec.ts`). Helical sweep, smooth coils: a new helix is wound about its axis where the profile sits, as exact curved surfaces (Remus `helicalSweepInPlace`, stored as the optional `construction: 'in-place'`, no schema bump), so the coil starts at the profile and the coil-end ball rides the built coil from there; the height arrow stands on the axis, clear of the ball, and the turns chip steps off the ball instead of covering it; one without the field replays the faceted `helicalSweep` unchanged, and an edit keeps how a helix was built; the faceting advisory no longer fires for an in-place coil (`tools.test.ts`, `edit.test.ts`, `modelingOperationsEdit.test.ts`, `advanced-modeling-features.test.ts`, `sweep-faceting-disclosure.test.ts`, `in-place-sketch-tools.spec.ts`). Thread, second tool: one pick, a cylindrical face or hole, and the thread starts at the end of the face nearer the click (an edit keeps its stored end); its diameter and length come from the face, and one arrow on the thread's free end, bounded by the face's far end, stops it short for a partial thread (Extent and From are no longer options); Options reads out what the face and the arrow decide ("Sized from the Ø6 mm face · Threads 8 of 12 mm, ends plain"); an untouched History edit writes the feature back unchanged, a stored partial thread included, with no schema change; a length arrow's chip now hangs clear of its head instead of covering it (`thread.test.ts`, `edit.test.ts`, `useInPlaceCommand.test.tsx`, `in-place-thread.spec.ts`, `extrude-in-place.spec.ts`). Approved thread behavior: the nearer picked end is retained, whole-face offsets read as To end, and the offset starting chamfer is omitted. Printing advisories remain visible as command guidance while Done is available. Preview gating uses the shared attributed warning rule: build failures, refusals, missing geometry and invalid inputs still block, including a failed edited feature with a pass-through body. Legacy warning deltas are counted by occurrence. Saved explicit lengths that reach the far end still read as partial with plain ends. Coverage: shared hook regressions across Box, Gear and Thread, plus the real M6 printing-advisory browser case in `in-place-thread.spec.ts`, PR #1361. No schema or dependency change. Correction, as of 2026-11-13: the earlier notes that the exact coil "needs the paired Remus `helicalSweepInPlace`; until it is pinned …" and, in this row's dependency cell, that it "needs Remus `helicalSweepInPlace` pinned through the paired-package update" are both retired. The pinned Remus exposes `helicalSweepInPlace` and the adapter builds in-place coils with it (`packages/kernel-adapter/src/exact-profile-builders.ts`), as the "smooth coils" entry above records. Faceted coils remain only as the replay of helixes saved without `construction: 'in-place'`. Union, Subtract and Intersect list their original bodies in pick order in Options, with Base and Tool labels and a remove action for each. The live guidance reads which originals the current Keep choice preserves. History opens the same ordered list and persisted choice. The browser exercises Union and Subtract on overlapping bodies, removes and restores the tool pick, and checks result volumes at creation and body counts after a History edit (`test/e2e/solid-result-options.spec.ts`). A picked cylinder wall's own edit is named by the quantity its drag and keypad enter: "Diameter" on the selection rail (Ø icon) and "Change diameter" in the viewport menu by default, switching to "Radius" (R icon) and "Change radius" when the value chip is toggled to R. Coverage: `selectionCallout.test.ts`, `viewportMenu.test.ts`, the Chromium smoke radius case in `test/e2e/cylinder-preview.spec.ts`, and the Ø/R toggle in `test/e2e/visual-selection-dimensions.spec.ts`. A picked cylinder wall (shaft or bore) offers Thread on its selection card, after its own Radius edit and before Axis and Shell. Pressing it starts Thread already sized from that wall, with no second pick, at the end nearer the click whether direct manipulation is on or off. Cones keep Axis and Shell only, and a curved wall is never offered Sketch or Hole. The command bar names the pick "1 cylindrical face", and the tool drawer's "fits" filter matches Thread and Gear teeth to it. Coverage: `selectionCallout.test.ts`, `commandContext.test.ts`, and the Chromium smoke cases in `test/e2e/in-place-thread.spec.ts` (both direct-manipulation settings). One command contract, stage 1 (Extrude) follow-ups. The model's warning chip now marks the value the bar names a refusal after: a refused 40° draft marks the draft angle's chip, and the distance arrow's chip only for a distance refusal; an option's refusal (Operation, Extent) or an unnamed one marks no chip.
