Before Done, the in-place Extrude bar already read out the boolean outcome and its body ("Preview: Cut · Plate"), with Options' Operation and Target body in step with drag and typed entry, and a History edit read the stored "Cut · Plate". A value the kernel refused, however, left the bar on "Auto · Drag the arrows" and the refusal only in the card, unattributed. The bar now says the refusal after the input that must change ("Draft angle: …"), named for the change that caused it, withdraws it while a newer value is checked, and returns the outcome readout once a value builds; the last built preview stays on screen throughout (`ExtrudeCommand.test.tsx`, `InPlaceCommandBar.test.tsx`, `test/e2e/extrude-refused-value.spec.ts`). Extrude can take a left-to-right window of fully enclosed sketch regions or a right-to-left crossing of regions touched by the box. The viewport tests the same filled triangles it draws and uses Extrude's whole-sketch rules to keep openings out of the bulk pick. Launching Extrude enables profile sweeps, and one selection drives the shared handles. A box spanning multiple sketches takes hits from the armed sketch when present, and asks for one sketch when the sweep is ambiguous. The browser test cuts a pair of pockets with one Extrude after sweeping the same box in both directions (`test/e2e/multi-region-extrude.spec.ts`), and the region overlay test checks a sweep wholly inside a hole. A blind, one-sided Extrude can explain when Section View clips its entire change. Auto reuses the bounds of its standalone sweep, and an explicit New body uses its result bounds. The advisory follows the current accepted preview and the active clipping plane. Show full model turns off the cut while retaining the Extrude draft and camera. The phone keypad offers the same action while a numeric entry is open. The existing Section View control brings back the same cut in the project session. The browser regression checks partially visible and hidden cuts, wireframe and X-ray, keyboard recovery, live numeric entry, Cancel, and the committed Cut's History through Undo, Redo, and reload (`test/e2e/extrude-section-recovery.spec.ts`). One command contract, stage 2 (Fillet, Chamfer, face Offset). Already in place: the in-place Fillet and Chamfer edit one draft from the edge arrow, its chip's keypad and Options, a History edit reopens the stored edges and values, and the face Offset keypad says whether it takes the Offset or the Total (the size before the edit and the move it makes). Two gaps are closed. A kernel refusal in the in-place Fillet or Chamfer bar took the last built preview down and did not say what to change. It now names the input or inputs changed since the last request that built (`R: …`, `Shape: …`, `Edges: …`), and that preview stays on screen until a value builds. A face offset refused while its keypad takes a Total described the limit, the remedy and Keep as an offset ("past 19 · Try a smaller offset", "Keep −18.99 mm"). These now read as Totals ("Total can’t go below 5 mm here", "Try a larger Total", "Keep Total 5.01 mm"). Coverage: `useInPlaceCommand.test.tsx`, `offsetChip.test.ts` and the Chromium smoke case `test/e2e/in-place-fillet-refused-radius.spec.ts`. The in-place edge command takes a double-clicked run as one selection. An unresolved run leaves the command's edge list intact and reports why. The Chromium smoke case in `test/e2e/chamfer-curved-chain.spec.ts` verifies the three-edge Chamfer on a straight–arc–straight rim from the viewport gesture through commit and reopening the saved picks and distance from History. A command waiting for a curved face (Thread and Gear teeth take a cylinder, Wrap a cylinder or cone) now reaches one hidden behind the body in front. Hover shows the nearest such face under the pointer through the host, using the hover's existing x-ray pass, and a click takes it. A bore's wall is picked through the stock's flat top, where the same click with no command selects the top. A sketch region or curve in front is still picked as itself, and Hole, which places itself at the click, never picks through to a hidden flat face. Coverage: `inPlace/tools.test.ts` and the Chromium smoke case "Thread takes a bore wall hidden behind the host" in `test/e2e/in-place-thread.spec.ts`. A refused in-place Fillet radius or Chamfer distance no longer sends its chip and keypad far off the model. The edge arrow had been drawn at the refused value (a 1000 mm radius stood 1000 mm along it, at the canvas edge). It now stands on the value the kept preview built from, at the edge being edited, while the chip and its keypad still read the refused value so it can be tapped and fixed. Face Offset and Extrude do not share this path: their arrows are the viewer's own offset and region rigs, and face Offset already draws its arrow at the displayed move while the chip reads the typed value. The selection-first Fillet keeps its last valid radius on its chip. Coverage: `useInPlaceCommand.test.tsx` and the Chromium smoke case `test/e2e/in-place-refused-value-chip.spec.ts`. Slot offers exact center spacing and radius after two center picks. Keyboard entry and the touch keypad size the same preview and use the existing slot transaction. The first center and picked direction anchor the spacing change. Extrude reports its automatic operation when the exact preview for the current draft arrives, and in-place commands distinguish checking from a ready preview. The browser workflow builds a rounded slotted plate, extrudes it to 10 mm and rounds two edges, then verifies the same Fillet through edit, Cancel, Undo, Redo, and reload (`test/e2e/u11-slotted-plate.spec.ts`). Slot keyboard and touch entry and an Extrude preview superseded by a new value have separate browser coverage (`test/e2e/sketch-point-slot.spec.ts`, `test/e2e/extrude-intent.spec.ts`). A picked flat face offers Text beside Sketch on its selection card and in its context menu ("Text on face"). One press opens a sketch on that face with the text card focused. The word is pinned and centred where the face was picked, so the pointer crossing the face on its way to Place does not move it, and Place puts the whole word there. A click on the face still places it where clicked. Text is refused for the same reason as Sketch. With direct manipulation off, the card's Sketch now sketches on the picked face instead of clearing the pick and asking for a plane. Coverage: `selectionCallout.test.ts`, `interaction/machine.test.ts`, and `test/e2e/text-on-face.spec.ts` (both direct-manipulation settings, as Chromium smoke cases, plus the Sketch case). After a cylindrical face pick, Thread shows internal or external, nominal size and pitch in the command bar during creation and History edits. The metric size comes from the same rule as the exact build, before clearance or crest truncation. Pipe threads show their standard designation and pitch. Chromium covers a shaft, a bore and an NPT choice in `test/e2e/in-place-thread.spec.ts`. Booleans, fourth tool (no schema change). Union, Subtract and Intersect are picked in the model alone, and each body they take is named where it stands by its role: Base from the first click, Tool after. These are the names Options lists, where the bodies used to wear pick numbers and one pick wore only its name. A Tool that the preview has consumed, such as a subtract's, is still named where it was picked, at the box it was picked with, so every input stays findable while the result shows. Picks whose labels would stand on the same spot (a core centred in its plate) share one label that reads both roles, Base first. Options' Remove takes a Tool back out. Picked again in the model, it is named Tool again. A boolean has no value to drag, so its picks are the whole interaction. Evidence: `booleanSwitch.test.ts` (roles by pick order, with each pick's name and box). `test/e2e/boolean-selection.spec.ts` (`@ci-smoke`) clicks two overlapping cubes in the model. It checks Base and Tool on the bodies and in Options, removes and re-picks the Tool, and checks that the Tool is still named once the preview consumed it. It then subtracts to 750 mm³. A second case subtracts a centred core and reads Base and Tool on one label. `solid-result-options.spec.ts` still passes. Gear, third tool (no schema change). A new gear's teeth drag on the gear itself. One arrow runs from the gear's centre, and its head stands on the pitch circle, half a module out per tooth. Dragging it out or in adds or removes teeth at the same module, so the gear grows or shrinks, as the coil-end ball winds turns at the same spacing. The drag lands on whole teeth. The face-width arrow is the second drag, and the pitch stays a chip. Spur, helical, ring, bevel and worm-wheel gears take the arrow. A worm's count is its starts and a rack has no circle, so those keep their chips. Gear teeth on a boss or bore keep their one pick and their length arrow, which is shared with Thread. Options now reads out what the face decides, as Thread's does ("Sized from the Ø22 mm face · Teeth run 4 of 6 mm"). With Set module, the module and pitch circle come first. Command handles take an optional drag step, so a count's arrow rests where its value does. Evidence: `tools.test.ts` (the arrow's scale for each sizing, the types that take it, the readout before and after a drag), `panel.test.ts`, and `test/e2e/gear.spec.ts`. Its `@ci-smoke` case picks the box's top, checks the arrow head on the Ø40 pitch circle, drags it to 24 teeth and the face width down to about 6, reads "Module 2 mm · Pitch Ø 48 mm" in Options, and writes an untouched History edit back unchanged. The Gear teeth case picks a boss and reads the face's readout. It then drags the arrow short and checks the stored length. 2026-11-22 fillet and chamfer arrows, after the owner's reference recording: the arrow on a selected edge or a picked fillet face is pulled away from the material to grow the blend and follows the hand there (it used to be pushed into an outside corner); a dashed leader runs from the middle of the blend to the arrow; the chip reads the value alone (`R 4 mm`, no `Edit Fillet ·` title) and, while the arrow is dragged, rides the leader tilted to read along it, just past the arrow or back on the leader where a panel or the command bar leaves no room (at rest it stands upright beside the arrow with the Fillet/Chamfer switch and the keypad where they were); the part keeps its own shading during the drag over a frosted ghost of its sharp edges instead of turning mint; and a drag snaps to round values as read at the current zoom (whole millimetres at an ordinary zoom or further out, finer as the part comes closer; Shift stays free). Typed entry, commit, refusal and History are unchanged (`edgeBlendFrame.test.ts`, `rigs.test.ts`, `blendDragStep.test.ts`, `visual-selection-fillet-edit.spec.ts`, `edge-chip-layout.spec.ts`, `command-contract.spec.ts`). | [U09](#u09)'s in-place command contract; the exact coil needs Remus `helicalSweepInPlace` pinned through the paired-package update. |
| <a id="u12"></a>U12 | Open | Compact mobile workspace, proposed 2026-11-26 ([plan](docs/plans/compact-mobile-workspace-plan-2026-11-26.md)): a phone-sized window (at most 600 px wide, or 500 px tall in landscape; Settings › Appearance › Layout overrides) gets a purpose-built shell instead of the desktop islands shrunk to fit: one full-bleed stage, a mode tab bar, a context tray and one bottom sheet with peek/half/full heights for every panel, card, menu and inspector, undo/redo on screen in every mode, and a softer compact visual language (rounder radii, softened colours, icon-first controls, a measured text budget). One typed action registry drives the keymap, command bar, menus and both shells, and a parity test fails while any action lacks a compact entry; nothing needs a keyboard, hover, right button or double-click. Fourteen slices; the desktop and tablet workspace is unchanged until slice 14 brings it the softer tokens, and the old phone layer is deleted only when compact becomes the default. Also makes the iOS shell universal (iPhone) with sign-in through a native passkey bridge (ADR first). Owner decisions recorded 2026-11-26. | [U04](#u04), [U08](#u08), [U11](#u11), [L02](#l02); device evidence under [W01](#w01). |
| <a id="l01"></a>L01 | Partial | Record live cross-device autosave, recovery-copy reload, revocation/lease expiry, required migrations and backup recovery against a named deployed build, and review export/artifact orphan cleanup and authorization evidence before retiring infrastructure. **Delivered:** 30-day share-link expiry and current-model-only anonymous links; account adoption with server-assigned IDs and legacy-history upgrade; durable autosave before departure (EDGE-03 fix #647 confirmed in production on `51c2a0d2`); collaboration checkpoint durability (#681) and lease-loss reconnect; SEC-3 sign-in throttling (#677); account data download (#974) and leaner portable backups; explicit conflict choices (#982); library folder sync (#1036–#1043, migration 0045) and owner-only share-link names (migration 0046); bounded beta save/reopen and lease evidence ([2026-09-20](docs/qa/2026-09-20/l01-live-acceptance.md)). **Remaining:** live cross-device sync, second-account revocation/expiry, deployment of migrations 0019–0021, 0023, 0026/0027 and later, live backup recovery, artifact-cleanup review, SEC-5/SEC-6 fixes, serving the previous asset generation after a deploy and encrypted link names; most slices have only local/browser-fixture evidence, and which migrations production has applied is not recorded here. [Full history](docs/roadmap-history/l01.md). Owner-authorized temporary beta QA capability permits the designated QA account to request email codes without Turnstile only on the beta hostname and only from a secret-provisioned browser. Each issuance expires within seven days, preserves email verification and rate limits, and supports immediate secret revocation. Renewal rotates the Worker secret and reprovisions the browser without a PR. Boundary and real SQLite code-flow coverage: `test/auth.test.ts`, `test/worker-public-request-limits.test.ts`, `SettingsPage.test.tsx`. Activation and live QA remain separate evidence; see [the runbook](docs/qa/temporary-turnstile-capability.md). Account library sync now unlocks an already trusted device after reload even when no encrypted project has loaded, so folders and sealed project pins can upload and appear on another device. Unlock and device-approval notifications retry the library immediately. Synthetic regression coverage uses the real sealed-folder codec for a cold upload/download and checks locked devices and account cancellation (`libraryFolderUnlock.test.ts`, `useLibraryFolderCloudSync.test.ts`); live cross-device acceptance remains L01. Beta allows 3,000 signed-in reads per account per minute in the Worker binding and its D1 fallback. Open workspaces poll an authorized version record and fetch the document only when it changes. Opening Settings for the same account reuses the shelf. Assistant Access checks names only for visible consent rows and updates a completed revocation from its response. Worker and browser regression coverage checks authorization, version-based sync and request counts. Project cards draw saved sketch curves and points when a model has no live body meshes. Reopening a sketch-only project replaces a cached empty preview, and Settings redraw can rebuild the same card. Regression coverage includes a cached empty card, an open curve on an offset plane, and a sketch-only card across browser reload (`projectThumbnailCapture.test.ts`, `partThumbnailRenderer.test.ts`, `projectThumbnailRegeneration.test.ts`, `project-thumbnails.spec.ts`). Stable allows 3,000 signed-in API reads per account per minute in its Cloudflare binding and D1 fallback. Release configuration tests check the quota in both public channels and their separate namespaces. Preview and self-hosted defaults allow 1,200. Encrypted link names: share links of end-to-end encrypted projects take owner-only names like plaintext links (a name field when creating one, and Name with Save on an open link card), sealed under the account key in one account record and synced between the owner's devices, so neither the server nor the project's members see them (ADR-024 amendment 2026-11-22; `SHARE_LINK_NAMES_RECORD`, `/api/share-link-names`, migration 0067). Each link's entry is last-writer-wins by stamp with tombstones, revoking a link drops its name, the Worker refuses a write that does not raise the sealed sequence, and a device keeps its own copy when the record cannot be opened. Evidence: `packages/crypto/src/sealed-records.test.ts` (fixed vector checked against Python `cryptography`, refusals), `test/share-link-names.test.ts` (migrated SQLite: compare-and-set, sequence, erasure fence, export, the route), `apps/web/src/lib/e2ee/sealedShareLinkNames.test.ts` (merge, rollback, account-key rotation, unreadable record kept), `EncryptedShareLinks.test.tsx` (naming, renaming and clearing). | [Cloud-sync record](docs/plans/project-cloud-sync-plan.md), E6/E7 and former TODO release gates. Deployment/resource changes require separate authorization. |

## 1. Modeling foundation

| ID | Status | Outcome and completion evidence | Depends on / legacy item |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="s01"></a>S01 | Complete | Driving-dimension labels drag in the sketch viewport and persist a plane-local offset keyed by constraint identity; the offset follows parameter-driven geometry without changing the driving value. The placement command is undoable/redoable and replayable through the saved command log (`apps/web/src/lib/sketch/dimensionAnnotations.test.ts`, `test/sketch-dimension-label-position.test.ts`). Browser coverage saves a large offset, reloads, reopens the sketch, keeps the label clear of the viewport rail, and opens its editor (`test/e2e/workspace-polish.spec.ts`). | Existing annotation engine; product S-2 remainder. Additive persistence design stored on the sketch node. |
| <a id="s02"></a>S02 | Complete | Driven/reference dimensions display measured values distinctly and cannot accidentally constrain geometry; round-trip, unit and expression behavior are tested. The design-only contract is recorded in the [S02 reference-dimensions plan](docs/plans/sketch-reference-dimensions-plan.md); slice S02-A is delivered: an additive v15 `SketchNode.referenceDimensions` field with stable `annotationId` identity and a pure distance/angle/radius resolver over primitive targets; reference records carry no value field and are excluded from GCS input by construction, unresolvable targets return a named `unresolved` status, and unit conversion is display-only (this PR; `packages/shared/src/sketch-reference-dimensions.test.ts`, `test/sketch-reference-dimensions.test.ts`). Primitive S02-B/C are delivered: the existing sketch palette authors absolute point distances, circle/arc radii and line angles as saved read-only references, with parenthesized Reference labels, tagged presentation commands, pointer/keyboard label placement, explicit deletion and measured-value updates. Add/delete/placement replay stable annotation identities and preserve them through undo/redo, native backup import and local reopen. References never enter GCS or exact-history digests; annotation-only changes preserve solve evidence, and referenced geometry deletion refuses until its annotations are explicitly removed. Invalid saved targets remain visibly unresolved and removable; normalization checks current and undo/redo-only annotation payloads. Regression evidence: `test/sketch-reference-commands.test.ts`, `test/sketch-reference-exact.test.ts`, `SketchReferenceDimensions.test.tsx`, `sketch/solveEvidence.test.ts`, dimension graphic/layout tests and `test/e2e/sketch-reference-dimensions.spec.ts`. The browser contract covers mm/cm/inch, read-only activation, label drag/keyboard placement, geometry edits, deletion recovery, undo/redo, saved reopen and phone controls. Composite/datum targets (S02-D), horizontal/vertical distance variants and a separate display-unit selector remain open; no schema bump is introduced beyond the merged optional-field envelope. Comparison slice: a distance-type badge (absolute/horizontal/vertical) on length dimensions, decided with the driven/driving semantics. S02-E projected reference distances (PR #797): Horizontal and Vertical modes measure sketch-local separation, including valid zero projections, while omitted modes retain legacy absolute distance. Stored identities and label positions survive edits, undo/redo, replay and reopen. Integration assigns distance modes schema v35 after drawing annotations v31, reference images v32, sketch projection v33 and object folders v34; composite targets and conversion provenance follow separately. Current, readable-history and replay carriers must satisfy the field's own introduction version. Acceptance evidence and remaining composite/datum/display-unit scope are recorded in the supporting sketch-reference-dimensions plan. Combined integration coverage uses actual linked projections and folder edits with retained drawing/image/PNG history: absolute and horizontal distances follow the source width while vertical stays fixed, with exact geometry, undo, native backup and replay unchanged (`test/schema-distance-folders-retention.test.ts`). Collaboration HTTP/WebSocket boundaries refuse backdated distance carriers before storage (`test/schema-distance-mode-compatibility.test.ts`). S02-D datum consumers (schema v129, building on v126 from F01's #1846; it was claimed as v126 and renumbered, and v127 and v128 are claimed by F01's #1847 and #1851). Reference distances measure from the sketch origin, and reference angles measure against its X and Y axes (`sketch-origin`, `sketch-axis`), in the sketch-local frame that R01 resolves for a datum-backed sketch. The value is unchanged by a plane offset. The targets carry no object and never block a deletion. Same-datum pairs refuse, and so do pre-v129 carriers. Contract: [datum section](docs/plans/sketch-reference-dimensions-plan.md#sketch-origin-and-axes-s02-d). Evidence: `test/sketch-datum-reference-targets.test.ts`, `SketchReferenceDimensions.test.tsx` and `test/e2e/sketch-datum-reference-targets.spec.ts` (`@ci-smoke`). With S02-D composite consumers (v125) and the S02-F display-unit selector, the row's remaining composite, datum and display-unit scope is delivered. The earlier sentence saying these "remain open" is superseded. Driving-distance modes stay outside S02. S02-F, a separate display-unit selector (no schema change). Reference lengths have their own per-user display unit: a new `sketching.referenceDisplayUnit` app setting, either `document` (the default) or mm/cm/m/in. The reference palette (Show lengths in) and Settings → Sketching both set it. It converts only the rendered reference labels and palette rows. Driving dimensions, document units, expressions and saved references are untouched. An older client's save keeps the stored unit, and a newer client's unknown unit falls back to `document`. Contract: [S02-F section](docs/plans/sketch-reference-dimensions-plan.md#reference-display-units-s02-f). Evidence: `dimensionAnnotations.test.ts`, `SketchReferenceDimensions.test.tsx`, `test/app-settings.test.ts`, `test/api-routes.test.ts` and `test/e2e/sketch-reference-display-units.spec.ts` (`@ci-smoke`: 50.8 mm reads 2 in, the exported sketch is unchanged, and the choice survives reload). S02-D composite consumers (schema v125, building on v121; v122–v124 are claimed by the open F01 PRs). Reference distances and angles now measure a held-line shape's centre (`composite-center`, with the S04-B topology witness) and an unconverted legacy rectangle's or regular polygon's corners, sides and centre (`shape-corner`, `shape-side`, `shape-center`), mixed with primitive and S04-B targets, in every distance mode. Legacy corners use the region and boundary-token order, and the shape is never converted or rewritten. Same-target aliases refuse. So do deletion and explicit conversion of a measured shape, and a direct kind or side-count edit. A parameter-driven count change leaves the reference unresolved by name and never re-indexes it. Pre-v125 carriers refuse the new targets. Helper snaps on a shape's centre are named as that centre. Contract: [S02-D section](docs/plans/sketch-reference-dimensions-plan.md#shape-centres-and-legacy-shapes-s02-d). Evidence: `test/sketch-shape-reference-targets.test.ts`, `SketchReferenceDimensions.test.tsx`, `compositeTargets.test.ts` and `test/e2e/sketch-shape-reference-targets.spec.ts` (`@ci-smoke`). Datum targets and a display-unit selector remain open. | Explicit dimension schema/semantics; product S-2. Separate from S01. Coordinate one future additive schema envelope with S01/S04/R01, selecting its version from the actual merged base; S01 remains additive in the current schema. |
| <a id="s03"></a>S03 | Complete | Truthful DOF/under/over-constrained status, entity highlighting and removable-constraint diagnostics; conflicting edits leave the prior solved sketch intact. Delivered slices: conflict refusal names top residual constraints and preserves the solved sketch; the solve pill reports kernel classification/DOF; residual-backed constraint rows are marked removable and only entities named by non-zero residuals are highlighted in the sketch viewport (#350, this PR, `sketch/constraints.test.ts`, `control-reference.test.ts`). Rank redundancy remains intentionally unattributed when the kernel supplies no residual culprit. Sketch-wide defined colouring is delivered: the pinned GCS reports one DOF scalar and a classification with no per-entity freedom, so `sketchDefinedState` paints all non-construction geometry and its snap-point dots in the success colour only on a converged, zero-DOF, solved sketch, and the pill, constraint rows and viewport read one derivation (this PR; `sketch/constraints.test.ts`, `viewer/sketchModeController.test.ts`, `SketchToolRail.test.tsx`). Per-entity under-defined colouring stays open until the solver supplies per-entity evidence. Actionable inspection and recovery now expose a Review constraints route from solve warnings, focus the existing constraint list, and let every stored relation reveal its current referenced geometry for selection in the normal entity editor. Navigation writes no history; measured residual rows remain the only attributed conflicts, while unqualified solver refusals explicitly say that no conflicting relation was identified. Regression evidence: `sketch/constraintInspection.test.ts`, `SketchToolRail.test.tsx` and `test/e2e/sketch-constraint-inspection.spec.ts` cover stale references, keyboard inspection, live geometry selection, compact-window action reachability, refusal preservation, deletion, undo/redo and saved reopen. The anchored-entity setting remains open. Comparison slices: fully-defined/under-defined colour states on sketch geometry and points, and an anchored-entity rule (first or last selected stays put when a relation moves things) exposed as a setting. Add a navigable remaining-DOF inspection path only where qualified solver evidence identifies relevant entities; numeric status, geometry highlighting and constraint-list state must agree. Keep attribution explicitly unknown when the solver cannot identify a culprit ([FreeCAD E03](docs/reviews/freecad-comparison-2026-09-30.md#hands-on-evidence)). [Production QA remediation](docs/plans/production-qa-fixes-2026-10-05.md) proposes the confirmed 5 October fixes; local regression and build evidence belong to its implementation PR, with production retest pending deployment. 14 October review follow-up UX-2 is delivered: rectangles and polygons drawn since S04 are lines whose corners carry point references, and every point-taking tool (Distance, Coincident, Midpoint, Symmetric, Point on curve, Lock, Disconnect) now refuses a snap without one by name instead of repeating its hint — a parametric rectangle's corners, centre or side midpoints and a parametric polygon's centre (redraw with the Rectangle or Polygon tool), a text origin, a midpoint, quadrant, intersection or the sketch origin; grid and axis-alignment snaps keep the hint (this PR; `sketch/constraints.test.ts` against the real snap generator, `test/e2e/sketch-constraint-set.spec.ts`). Explicit conversion of legacy parametric shapes remains S04. Viewport relation glyphs (ZC-039) are delivered: every non-dimensional relation draws a glyph on the curve or point it holds (curve relations at a line's middle, a circle's 45° point or an arc's mid-sweep; point relations clustered per coincident point), relations the last solve named as conflicting are marked. A presentation-only `sketching.relationGlyphs` preference (Always · Selected · Hidden, default Selected, so a sketch shows the relations of the entity being worked on) sits in the sketch palette and Settings → Sketching; older clients and stored settings default it (this PR; `sketch/relationGlyphs.test.ts`, `viewer/sketchRelations.test.ts`, `test/app-settings.test.ts`, `test/e2e/sketch-relation-glyphs.spec.ts`). Research-audit Stage 2e completes the glyphs: each chip now stands off its geometry in screen space — a curve's chip on the side away from the sketch's centre (so a closed profile's chips sit outside it), a circle's outward, a point's chip in the widest gap between the curves meeting there — and chips claim space in a fixed anchor order (point chips first, then curve chips, by position), sliding along their curve or swinging round their point, then stepping out a ring, so none covers its own anchor, any other anchor or another chip, and none leaves the viewport; a chip with no room within that bounded search (a dense sketch zoomed far out) is hidden until zooming makes room. Screen directions are the projection's exact clip-space derivative, so they hold at any coordinate magnitude. The layout runs on every drawn frame from typed arrays and a screen grid, so it costs about linear time, allocates nothing and writes no style on a still frame; chips stay one CSS2D label per cluster. Because glyphs no longer sit on geometry they take clicks: a click selects that relation (shown on every chip it draws on; the entity selection stays, an armed pick tool gives way to Select), Delete removes it in one undoable edit before it would remove the entity, and Escape clears both in one press. A per-kind show/hide filter (`sketching.hiddenRelationGlyphs`, presentation only, no document schema change) sits in the sketch palette and Settings → Sketching; unknown kinds drop out and a save from an older client keeps the stored filter (this PR; `sketch/relationGlyphLayout.test.ts`, `sketch/relationGlyphs.test.ts`, `viewer/sketchRelations.test.ts`, `interaction/machine.test.ts`, `test/app-settings.test.ts`, `test/api-routes.test.ts`, `test/e2e/sketch-relation-glyphs.spec.ts`). The anchored-entity rule (ZC-041) is delivered as decided: a relation now applies as it is added, in one undoable edit with its solved geometry, and its first pick stays put — a point pick holds that point, an object pick the whole object (a circle also keeps its radius) — so reversing the picks of Equal reverses which side keeps its size. Single-pick relations solve free. When holding the first pick makes the relation unsatisfiable the solve falls back to free and says so; when it cannot solve at all, nothing changes and the culprit is named, as for a driving dimension. A held solve's DOF counts held points as fixed, so it is not shown as the sketch status (this PR; `test/sketch-anchored-solve.test.ts` on the pinned kernel, `test/e2e/sketch-constraint-set.spec.ts`). Per-entity under-defined colouring and a navigable remaining-DOF inspection path, from qualified solver evidence. The pinned GCS reports one DOF scalar, but it solves with an entity held (its points fixed, a circle's radius and an ellipse's shape kept), and the DOF that drops is exactly that entity's remaining freedom: the dimension of the sketch's free motions on its own parameters. After an under-defined solve settles, the geometry worker measures every solver entity this way in one request (`sketch-entity-freedom`, `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/entityFreedom.ts`). The viewport then paints only entities with zero freedom in the defined colour, a relation row reads defined when everything it references is fixed, and the palette lists "Can still move" with each free entity's count (Line 2 · 4 DOF), which selects it through the normal entity editor without writing history. The sketch-wide DOF stays the pill's number. Splines (never held), non-solver geometry and held solves that do not settle stay unmeasured and uncoloured; an unsettled sketch, or one with more than 60 solver entities (each hold is a full solve, so a reading grows with the square of the sketch: 150 related lines took about 2.3 s, 60 about 0.4 s), keeps the sketch-wide explanation. A newer edit or solve cancels a reading, and a reading for a stale evidence key is dropped. Rank redundancy stays unattributed where no residual names a culprit, as before. Evidence: `test/sketch-entity-freedom.test.ts` (on the pinned kernel: free line 4, circle 3, arc 5, point 2; a locked line 0 beside a horizontal joined line 1 and a centre-locked circle 1; a shared joint counted on both lines; zero-DOF and over-limit sketches; splines unmeasured), `SketchToolRail.test.tsx` and `test/e2e/sketch-entity-freedom.spec.ts` (two locks, Solve, the free line listed with 4 DOF and selected). | Existing GCS detailed results; product S-3 and execution Slice D. |
| <a id="s04"></a>S04 | Complete | Give rectangles/polygons stable point identity or a defined decomposition path; constraints and downstream region references survive revision and replay. Delivered for new drawings (research-audit Stage 2c, ZC-006/007/008/011; schema v26): by the owner's decision recorded in the [composite identity design](docs/plans/sketch-composite-identity-plan.md), the Rectangle tool's corner, centre and three-point types and a new viewport Polygon tool (P, sides 3–64, circumradius convention) draw real lines held by persisted relations. Those are corner coincidents plus horizontal/vertical sides, perpendicular/parallel sides for the rotated three-point type, or vertices on a construction circumcircle with equal sides. An additive `SketchNode.composites` record names the lines as one object, with ordered corners and sides by index. Each shape commits as one undoable transaction with pre-assigned ids. Selecting a side opens the shape's resize card, which moves every member in one solved edit, keeping the centre (centre rectangle, polygon) or corner 1. Deleting a member dissolves the record and leaves the lines. The record never enters GCS or the exact-history digest. Saved parametric rectangle/polygon objects load unchanged and are never decomposed. Evidence: `test/sketch-composites.test.ts` (pinned-kernel solves are non-redundant with exactly 4/4/5/4 free DOF; (0,0)–(20,12) bounds 240 with perimeter 64; centre (5,7) 20×12 has corners (−5,1)/(15,13) and keeps its centre on resize; a hexagon of circumradius 10 has sides of 10; collinear third-point refusal; replay, reload, undo and delete-dissolve), `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/composites.test.ts`, `test/e2e/sketch-composite-tools.spec.ts`. Open: composite corner/side targets for snaps and S02-D dimensions; region-provenance tokens and explicit conversion for legacy parametric objects. S04-B named composite targets (PR #800) retain an explicit composite identity, corner/side index and complete ordered topology witness for new held-line rectangles and polygons. References and snaps fail by name on missing, ambiguous or changed ownership. Mixed primitive/composite distances and angles, projected modes, raw expressions and label placement survive resize, replay, undo/redo and reopen. Integration introduces targets at schema v36, after reference-distance modes v35, with independent positive carrier-version checks over current, readable-history and saved/nested replay data. Drawing annotations and reference images remain authored data. Legacy conversion and boundary provenance follow in the next slice. The combined retained-content regression uses actual linked projected edges, named composite corners/sides and all distance modes, plus drawing/image/PNG/folder history. A source resize and composite reshape preserve target identities and exact profiles through undo, replay and native backup (`test/schema-composite-folders-retention.test.ts`). Integer schema proof and collaboration HTTP/WebSocket refusal/acceptance are covered in `test/sketch-composite-reference-commands.test.ts`. Live reference Add validates canonical target identities and measures the matching project/version-qualified solved sketch view. It appends only an annotation; authored geometry, expressions and feature geometry digests stay unchanged. Composite command replay restores the same strict target identities without requiring transient solver output, so an annotation may remain unresolved until the exact build completes, as with saved annotations. Primitive-only replay keeps numerical validation. No payload flag bypasses live qualification. `test/sketch-composite-reference-resolved.test.ts` uses a real linked edge and mixed expression/numeric seeds whose solved width is 32 while canonical seeds remain 20. Add, full JSON command replay, undo/redo and native reopen preserve the 32-unit reference, exact volume 576, all authored inputs and feature digests. Missing, stale and foreign geometry proof refuses live Add. `test/sketch-composite-reference-restoration.test.ts` preserves nonmeeting live refusals and rejects malformed, reordered, foreign, missing, aliased or ambiguously owned targets, duplicate annotations, driving values and older schema carriers. S04-C explicit legacy conversion and boundary provenance (PR #801): converting rectangles or regular polygons creates independent owned primitive constraints, stable aliases and inert authored witnesses in one undoable command. Release shape constraints retains user constraints and aliases. Complete outer/hole boundary loops retain cyclic order and split-edge tokens; precise matching precedes legacy fallback. Conversion preserves selected and first-object consumers, source lineage, raw expressions and native replay/backup behavior. Integration introduces these fields at schema v37, preserving distance modes v35 and composite targets v36 as separate carrier boundaries. Current, readable-history and saved/nested replay copies require supported ownership and version proof; opening legacy documents performs no conversion and synthesizes no provenance. The supported S04 contract and named refusal boundaries are recorded in the composite-identity plan; ROADMAP.md remains the delivery-status ledger. Actual predecessor integration is covered by `test/schema-provenance-folders-retention.test.ts`: mixed-unit legacy conversion inside a linked projected outline preserves p1/b1 boundaries, aliases and raw source expressions. Source resize follows exact volume576→936 and an attached boss from x10→16 while v35/v36 measurements, folders, drawing/image/PNG/history content, undo, native backup and replay remain consistent. Collaboration HTTP/WebSocket refusal and acceptance use the same v37 proof (`sketch-provenance-validation.test.ts`). Conversion of a legacy shape beside source-driven ordinary geometry qualifies selected profiles against the current exact read view. A versioned command witness carries only changed numeric analysis geometry and a compact digest of authored model inputs. It is used only during profile analysis; source witnesses, neighboring expressions, geometry tolerances and persistent nodes are not replaced with solved coordinates. Live preparation requires current exact projection verdicts. Missing/stale/broken proof, changed model inputs, foreign entities, malformed numeric fields and topology mismatches refuse atomically. The existing resolver still enforces ordered outer/hole provenance and repeated boundary occurrences. Payloads without this optional witness keep their path. Native copies rebind project identity and artifact locations without changing model geometry, so those locations are excluded from the stale-input digest. Source checksums, modeling identities, parameters, constraints, consumer references and units remain bound. The digest is a content guard, not authentication of solver coordinates. `test/sketch-conversion-qualification-exact.test.ts` verifies the real solved 32-unit outline containing a 2x2 hole at x24..26: exact volume564 survives conversion, full replay, native backup import, copied-project replay and undo/redo while raw neighboring `2 cm` and `h` remain intact. Editing typed height afterward produces volume660 and replays identically. The browser regression in `test/e2e/sketch-legacy-conversion.spec.ts` drives the actual action and checks unchanged typed neighbors, volume, history and reopen. Status check on main at schema v120 (2026-11-21): the items this row lists under "Open" (composite corner/side targets for snaps and S02-D dimensions, region-provenance tokens and explicit legacy conversion) landed in S04-B (#800) and S04-C (#801), recorded above. The supported subset and the refusal boundary that the [composite identity design](docs/plans/sketch-composite-identity-plan.md) requires are covered. The twelve S04 suites pass locally (139 tests: `test/sketch-composites.test.ts`, the three `test/sketch-composite-reference-*.test.ts` suites, `test/sketch-composite-targets-exact.test.ts`, `test/sketch-boundary-provenance.test.ts`, the three `test/sketch-legacy-conversion*.test.ts` suites, `test/sketch-conversion-qualification-exact.test.ts` and both schema retention suites). Snapping is covered by `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/compositeTargets.test.ts`, and browser coverage by `test/e2e/sketch-composite-targets.spec.ts`, `test/e2e/sketch-composite-tools.spec.ts` and `test/e2e/sketch-legacy-conversion.spec.ts`. Composite centres, references on unconverted legacy shapes and datum targets are S02-D consumers and are tracked in the S02 row. | Design/schema review first; product S-4. No silent conversion of legacy entities. |
| <a id="s05"></a>S05 | Partial | Trim/extend, offset, sketch fillet/chamfer, mirror and linear/circular patterns; each tool preserves constraint/refusal/undo contracts. Deliver each tool as a slice. Delivered: Fillet and Chamfer on the kernel's 2D operations for line pairs (Offset first shipped there for closed line loops), each one undoable transaction, with fillets writing coincidence, line-to-arc tangency and radius constraints (#333; `packages/kernel-adapter/src/sketch-2d-ops.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/edits.test.ts`). Delivered (research-audit Stage 2b, ZC-018/019/021/022): Trim, Mirror, Linear pattern and Circular pattern, app-side exact geometry folded with Offset into one "More modify tools" rail slot, each one undoable transaction. Trim cuts a line, arc or circle back to the nearest crossings either side of the click (deleting it when nothing crosses), keeps the entity's identity where it can, moves relations on a split's far end to the new piece, drops only those on removed points, and holds each cut end on its cutter. Mirror links the image with symmetric relations (coincident on the axis; equal radius for rounds); an on-axis joint's hold on the axis is kept only when a solve of the candidate on the pinned kernel shows it adds rank, so locks, coincidence chains and axes fixed through dimensions leave no redundant relation (`settleOptionalRelations`). Patterns take spacing and count, or count and total angle (rotated orientation), and tie each instance to the source with equal (plus parallel for linear lines). Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/modify.test.ts`, `test/sketch-modify-tools.test.ts` (solved on the pinned kernel), `test/e2e/sketch-modify-tools.spec.ts`. Delivered (research-audit Stage 2b, ZC-014/015/024): Offset is now app-side exact geometry for a circle (radius r ± d; an inward distance that reaches the radius is refused with nothing changed), one line or arc, or, with Chain on, the run of lines and arcs joined end to end with the pick (closed or open, stopping at a branch; construction and regular curves walk separately, so a centre rectangle's diagonals never break its outline). A Miter or Round join is chosen on a strip beside the rail while Offset is armed (Miter default; a corner next to an arc is rounded); overlapping neighbours are trimmed to their crossing. Positive is outward for a closed loop, a circle or a lone arc, otherwise towards the side of the picked curve the click landed on. Every result is witnessed (no point nearer the source than the distance, no self-crossing) or refused. The copies join by coincidence and link back as optional relations (parallel, concentric, round-join centre on the source corner and tangent to its lines) kept only where a solve says they add rank. A 20 × 12 rectangle offset outward by 1 gives exactly 22 × 14 with Miter and radius-1 corner arcs with Round, in one undo step. Make construction / make regular: a Construction toggle on the entity editor flips one existing entity in one undo step; marking one side of a rectangle removes its profile and undo restores it. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/offset.test.ts`, `test/sketch-modify-tools.test.ts` (offset links solve without redundancy on the pinned kernel), `test/e2e/sketch-modify-tools.spec.ts`. Open: extend, pattern by total extent, uniform orientation, a persistent pattern relation that also holds instance positions, multi-entity mirror/pattern/construction selections, a driving offset dimension, arc/circle inputs to Fillet/Chamfer, browser coverage of Fillet/Chamfer. Review follow-up: construction-toggle validation failures report the named refusal, preserve the last valid document, release the busy state and leave subsequent edits undoable (`test/e2e/sketch-delete-dependents.spec.ts`). Offset clearance now uses analytic minima over complete finite line/arc pairs; `offset.test.ts` covers narrow non-adjacent line and arc intrusions between the former sample locations while preserving valid smaller offsets. Delivered: Extend, Trim's counterpart in the "More modify tools" fold (and an unbound `sketch-extend` shortcut). A click on a line or arc lengthens it from the end nearer the click to the first curve its continuation meets: a line along its own direction, an arc round its own circle (either end). It keeps the entity's identity and its far end, is one undoable transaction per click and stays armed like Trim. The moved end drops the relations that held it where it was (coincident, lock, distance, a line's equal-length and midpoint relations), and the status line counts them. Untouched relations stay, and the new end is held on the curve it reached. It refuses by name, changing nothing: a circle or full arc (no end), nothing beyond the end, composites, text, ellipses, splines, linked projections, and an end a reference dimension measures. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/modify.test.ts` (line end and start, arc both ways against closed-form angles, dropped-relation count, every refusal, undo), `test/sketch-modify-tools.test.ts` (on the pinned kernel the extended line and arc ends follow their boundaries when those move), `edits.test.ts`, `SketchToolRail.test.tsx`, and `test/e2e/sketch-modify-tools.spec.ts` (two clicks extend a line past one vertical and then to the next, the bare end is refused, and two undos restore the original). Sketch Fillet previews multiple line junctions at one radius. Corners can be picked and toggled directly or identified by picking their two lines. One validated transaction trims each shared line once and adds the tangent arcs and their driving radii. Trims that would consume or overlap on a shared edge leave the picks in place for a smaller radius. One Undo restores the original sketch. The slotted-plate browser journey covers the four-corner edit before Slot, Extrude, and solid Fillet. A touch case covers radius refusal and retry (`apps/web/src/lib/sketch/filletBatch.test.ts`, `test/e2e/u11-slotted-plate.spec.ts`). Sketch entities select several at a time, as bodies do: under Select a drag sweeps a box (left to right takes what lies wholly inside, right to left also what it touches) and Shift or Cmd/Ctrl-click adds or drops one; Cmd/Ctrl+A selects every entity. Mirror armed over a selection asks only for the axis and mirrors each entity about it in one undoable transaction, leaving out and counting any it cannot mirror. Delete removes the whole selection in one step. Cmd/Ctrl+C and V copy and paste entities as new, unrelated geometry stepped clear of the originals; relations are not copied, and a linked projection pastes unlinked (`machine.test.ts`, `modify.test.ts`, `boxSelect.test.ts`, `clipboard.test.ts`, `test/e2e/sketch-modify-tools.spec.ts` "a box selects several entities"). Moving a multi-selection by drag is not part of this slice. Arc and circle inputs to Fillet and Chamfer (schema v141). The Fillet tool's corner markers now also mark where a line meets an arc or two arcs meet end to end (a smooth continuation offers none), and where a free line or arc end lies on a circle — two markers there, one each way round. The fillet is the exact circle of the asked radius tangent to both legs: its centre is the intersection of the legs' offsets (parallel lines at ±r, concentric circles at R ± r) whose two tangent points lie on the legs themselves, nearest the joint. Arc legs are cut back to their tangencies in the same one-transaction batch as lines, with their preview; a circle stays whole, and what held the cut end on it is released. Picking both sides of one joint is refused by name. The fillet records its tangency to each leg: a line as before, an arc or a circle through a new curve-to-curve `tangent` form (two arcs joined end to end touch where the shared point is in line with both centres; an arc end on a circle is on the circle and in line with both centres), so the next solve keeps it tangent. Chamfer takes a line or an arc against an arc, cut back by the same straight setback (a chord on an arc), or against a circle on the side that was clicked, the bevel held on the circle. Builds on v137 (#1900, on main); v138–v140 are claimed by the S07 entity lock (#1913) and the S05 pattern relation and offset dimension. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/filletCurved.test.ts` (line–arc and arc–arc fillets against closed-form tangency, no corner on a smooth joint, a line into a circle either side; each committed candidate solves on the pinned kernel with rank equal to equations and nothing moving), `curveChamfer.test.ts` (line–arc, arc–arc either pick order, a circle on the clicked side solved on the pinned kernel, refusals), `test/sketch-curved-tangent.test.ts` (inside and outside arc tangency and an arc on a circle against closed form, the join requirement, the v140 refusal) and `test/e2e/sketch-modify-tools.spec.ts` ("Fillet and Chamfer meet a circle a line ends on, keeping the circle whole": the two corner markers picked on the canvas, radius and setback entered, undo between). Sketch Fillet keeps only unique-neighbor state and refuses excessive endpoint candidate work before producing a catalog. The editor presents that refusal without publishing a partial set of corners. Focused regressions preserve ordinary corners beside ambiguous joints, resolved geometry, and recovery on a different sketch (`filletBatch.test.ts`, `useSketchFilletDraft.test.tsx`). Multi-entity pattern and construction selections. Linear and Circular pattern armed over a selection (box, Shift/Cmd-click or Cmd/Ctrl+A), like Mirror, ask only for the direction line or the pivot, then the same values and settings, and repeat every selected entity in one undoable transaction. Each entity is planned as the single-entity pattern would plan it, against the sketch with the instances already planned for the entities before it, so no entity's instances land on another's; an entity the pattern cannot repeat is left out and counted, the direction line is skipped if selected, and only a selection with nothing repeatable is refused. The entity editor's Construction toggle acts on the whole selection when several entities are selected (labelled with the count): all become construction unless every one already is, then all become regular again, in one undo step. No schema change. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/modify.test.ts` (a selection repeated by extent with its links, sequential planning and counted refusals, a uniform circular selection, a mixed construction selection both ways and undo, the selection flow from pick to transaction), `machine.test.ts`, `SketchEntityEditor.test.tsx` and `test/e2e/sketch-modify-tools.spec.ts` ("a pattern and Construction act on a box selection in one step each"). Pattern by total extent and uniform orientation. While Linear pattern is armed, a Spacing · Extent choice rides beside its slot: Extent reads the typed distance as running from the source to the last instance, so the instances share it evenly (an extent prefilled with the direction line's length spreads them along it). While Circular pattern is armed, a Rotate · Uniform choice does the same: Uniform carries each instance round the pivot without turning it, so a line's midpoint or a circle's or arc's centre lands where the turn takes the source's, and a uniform line is held parallel as well as equal to its source. The setting is read when the picks complete and travels with the values, so a change mid-entry cannot reinterpret them; absent settings are the step-spaced, rotated patterns of before. Refusals are by name: a zero extent, an extent too short for its steps, overlapping steps, and a uniform pattern anchored on the pivot. No schema change. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/modify.test.ts` (extent steps, refusals, uniform lines and arcs against closed-form positions, the setting carried through both value steps), `test/sketch-modify-tools.test.ts` (on the pinned kernel uniform instances follow the source's turn and length without redundancy; an extent pattern solves like its step equivalent), `SketchToolRail.test.tsx`, `machine.test.ts` and `test/e2e/sketch-modify-tools.spec.ts` ("Linear pattern spreads its instances over an extent"). Extend and multi-entity Mirror were already delivered (see this row); multi-entity pattern and construction selections remain open. A persistent pattern relation that also holds instance positions (schema v145). Linear and Circular pattern now write one `pattern` relation per source instead of per-instance equal/parallel links: it names the source, each instance with its step, and the layout — the direction line and the spacing, or the pivot (a stored point: a circle's or arc's centre, a line or arc end, a sketch point), the step angle and Rotate or Uniform. On the solver each instance is stated against the source directly, with exactly its own freedoms: two for its anchor (a helper line from the source's anchor parallel to the direction at step × spacing; or from the pivot, as long as the source's and turned step × the step angle — the signed GCS angle), the rest for size and turn (equal length and the same or turned direction for a line, equal radius for a round, equal radius and turned or kept centre-to-end directions for an arc). So a pattern adds no freedom, moving or resizing the source carries every instance to its own step, and an instance moved alone conflicts. Deleting an instance leaves the others held at their own steps; deleting the source, the direction line or the pivot's owner removes the relation, and Trim/Extend drop the instance they reshape. Validation refuses mixed kinds, repeated entities or steps, a non-line direction and an invalid pivot. A circular pivot given only as coordinates keeps the earlier per-instance links. The relation is listed with the sketch's constraints ("Linear pattern 5 · Circle 1 ×3 along Line 1") and draws no glyph. Builds on v141 (#1911, on main); v142 to v144 are claimed by #1914, #1915 and the S07 entity lock (#1913). The solver's equations also admit mirror branches (a copy on the source's other side, a reversed line, the opposite ray from the pivot), so a solve starts each free instance at its step and reads every instance back against the source carried by its step, refusing a mirror by name; a uniform line pattern is carried by its midpoint, as the tool places it; trimming an instance into an arc keeps one relation; a pivot on a spline end is refused. Dragging or editing the source holds it where it was put and carries every instance with it (an edit whose free solve would undo a value the user set retries once with the edited entities held; a real conflict is still refused). Evidence: `test/sketch-pattern-relation.test.ts` (an edit moving the source carries each instance; a copy stays on its own side for either spacing sign and a wrong-side copy is refused; a uniform line keeps the tool's midpoint placement; a trimmed instance leaves one relation; a spline pivot is refused; on the pinned kernel: no added DOF; a moved, resized source carries each circle to its step; rotated and uniform line patterns follow a turned source to closed-form positions; a rotated arc pattern keeps radius and sweep at each 120° step; an instance pulled off its step conflicts; deleting one keeps the others at steps 2 and 3; the v144 refusal), `modify.test.ts`, `constraints.test.ts` and `test/e2e/sketch-modify-tools.spec.ts` ("dragging a linear pattern’s source carries every instance with it"). A driving offset dimension (schema v146). Offset now writes one `offset` relation alongside its copies: every source/copy pair with its side (a line's left or right of its stored direction, a round's outside or inside) and one driving value, the distance as typed (an expression stays parametric). On the solver a line copy keeps that signed distance from its source line at both ends, and a round copy keeps its radius that much from the source's, measured along a ray from the source centre through two helper points that add exactly as many equations as parameters; direction and concentricity stay with the optional links Offset keeps only where a solve says they add rank, so the relation never makes an offset redundant. The relation is listed with the sketch's constraints ("Offset 1 · Line 5 from Line 1 +3") and its value edits like any driving dimension, re-offsetting every copy in one undoable edit, round joins included; a non-positive value is refused by name (the side stays with each copy). Deleting or trimming a copy or its source drops only that pair. Every free copy is started where the relation puts it (a round concentric with its source at the offset radius, a line square onto its offset carrier), so dragging a source carries its copies and a new value moves only the copies. The equations leave a copy's side free, so every solve reads each copy back (a line copy's signed distance at both ends, a round copy's reach past the source radius) and a copy on the wrong side fails the relation; a chain's corner shared by two collinear sources is stated once; a full-turn arc offsets to a circle and pairs with it; renaming a parameter rewrites the offset value and a parameter edit re-solves it; a value whose expression is not positive is refused. Builds on v145 (the S05 pattern relation, #1917). Evidence: `test/sketch-modify-tools.test.ts` (a v145 document holding an offset dimension is refused; a wrong-side round copy is refused; a collinear chain stays non-redundant; a full-turn arc offsets to a circle; a renamed parameter follows and `1 - 3` is refused; on the pinned kernel: mitered and round offsets of a rectangle still solve with rank equal to equations, and re-driven from 1 to 2.5 the copies sit 2.5 out with radius-2.5 joins; an inward circle offset re-driven to 2 has radius 3, a lone arc's to 1.5 keeps its centre), `edits.test.ts`, `constraints.test.ts` and `test/e2e/sketch-modify-tools.spec.ts` ("the Offset distance stays a driving dimension that re-offsets every copy" and the pick-and-drag "dragging an offset source circle carries its copy at the offset distance"). Linear and circular sketch patterns keep the expression their spacing or angle was typed with: a plain numeral still stores its number, an expression stores the text (a linear extent and a circular total divided into steps as the number was: `(E) / (count - 1)`, or `(E) / count` for a full turn), so a parameter-driven pattern follows renames, F05 checks and re-evaluation; the constraint list labels the evaluated value (`test/sketch-pattern-expressions.test.ts`). | S04 where composite identity is required; product S-5, design-review W15/phase 4. Research-audit Stage 0: deleting sketch geometry runs the same downstream rebuild check as an entity edit and refuses by name when a dependent feature would break (`test/e2e/sketch-delete-dependents.spec.ts`). |
| <a id="s06"></a>S06 | Partial | Finish sketch entry/re-entry, normal-to-sketch, hint/snap visibility, Finish/Enter and body-pick behavior. Comparison slices: hover a face or plane and press a key to start a sketch on it, three world-plane tiles at the origin as the idle sketch prompt, and continuing on the same plane editing the same sketch while anything else starts a new one. Include cursor-adjacent numeric drawing inputs in the existing prototype comparison: normal-to-plane entry, pointer/keyboard agreement, unit-aware input, focus/cancel behavior, and measured task steps/errors before adoption. Keep inference visibility/removal with S08 and the current quiet-stage mode boundaries ([FreeCAD E02–E03](docs/reviews/freecad-comparison-2026-09-30.md#hands-on-evidence)). Face-on sketch entry preserves the chosen view instead of rolling to the stored plane axes; exit restores the visible screen-up along with position, target and projection. Oblique entries retain plane-axis alignment and stored sketch bases are unchanged. Regression evidence: `sketchCamera.test.ts`, `SelectionManager.test.ts`, and the frame-sampled `sketch-transition-continuity.spec.ts` in normal/reduced motion, alongside the existing seven `sketch-face-orientation.spec.ts` cases. Browser verification uses the production renderer. This is a bounded S06 slice, not completion of the row. Normal to sketch returns the camera to the active sketch plane at its current zoom and target. It uses the sketch plane's standard roll and keeps the active tool and draft gesture. Chromium covers the projection switch during entry, preserving perspective after a cancelled glide, and finishing a circle after changing views and returning to the sketch plane (`test/e2e/sketch-face-orientation.spec.ts`). A sketch stays flat after its entry glide: the camera controller now holds the sketch's rotation lock across the orthographic switch, which rebuilds the orbit controls and used to turn canvas rotation back on, so a middle-drag set to Orbit (or a touch orbit) no longer turns the sketch out of its plane. The view cube still orbits on purpose (`CameraController.test.ts`, `test/e2e/viewport.spec.ts` "a sketch stays flat"). | U01/U03; [camera record](docs/plans/sketch-flow-camera-plan-2026-08-26.md), UI row 10, design B1/B4. Camera glide/ghost planes/consumed-sketch visibility already exist. Face-sketch entry rolls the camera to the plane's v axis, so +u reads rightward and +v upward on a top face as on Top (XY) (`sketch-face-orientation.spec.ts`); New Front (XZ) sketches record canonical basis revision 2 (u = +X, v = +Z, normal -Y) and enter from the Front view reading +u rightward and +v upward; saved unmarked XZ sketches keep revision 1 and their behind-the-model view rather than being rewritten. Inspector changes from XY/YZ to Front serialize revision 2, same-plane edits retain their saved basis, and old partial-plane commands still replay revision 1 (`test/command-system.test.ts`, `test/e2e/sketch-face-orientation.spec.ts`) (this PR; `test/geometry.test.ts`, `sketch/session.test.ts`, `exact-kernel-adapter.test.ts`, `sketch-face-orientation.spec.ts`). Integration review: new AI `add_sketch` Front sketches use the same revision-2 basis as the plane picker; exact-kernel and replay coverage preserves revision-1 serialized commands (`ai-model-generation.test.ts`). |
| <a id="s07"></a>S07 | Partial | Constraint set completion: symmetry (two similar entities about a line), lock/unlock (fix a point or entity) and disconnect (remove the coincident/midpoint relations on a point), each with solver classification, refusal, undo and a place in `CONSTRAINT_TOOL_SPECS` order; dragging a point onto a point, line or midpoint creates the relation. Whole-entity symmetry (two lines, circles or arcs about a line in one pick sequence). Symmetric now takes two snap points as before, or two whole entities of one kind picked away from their points (the first pick decides, the second follows), then the axis. It writes the existing relations that fix the second entity exactly once on the solver, in one undoable edit that holds the first pick: two lines pair each end with the end nearer its image (two point symmetries), two circles mirror their centres and share a radius, and two arcs mirror the first's start to the second's end and its end to the second's start (reflection reverses an arc) with equal radii, five equations for an arc's five freedoms. Mixed kinds, ellipses, splines, composites, an axis among the mirrored entities and a point after an entity are refused by name. No schema change. Evidence: `test/sketch-constraint-set.test.ts` (on the pinned kernel each set solves without a redundant equation and lands on the closed-form image: a reversed line without flipping, a circle's centre and radius, a quarter arc's reversed sweep), `lib/sketch/constraints.test.ts` (pick kinds, refusals, end pairing) and `test/e2e/sketch-constraint-set.spec.ts` ("Symmetric mirrors a whole line about an axis in one edit", undone in one step). Lock/unlock a whole entity (schema v144). Lock now takes a snap point as before, or a line, circle or arc picked away from its points, and records that entity's resolved geometry in a new `lock-entity` relation; a second Lock on it unlocks it, and locking an entity takes over any point lock already on it in the same undoable edit. On the solver it is exactly as many equations as the entity has freedoms, so it is never redundant with itself: a line's two ends, a circle's centre and radius, an arc's centre and start plus the one end coordinate its own centre–end tie leaves free (chosen by where the end lies, so the five stay independent). Document validation refuses a lock on anything but a line, circle or arc, recorded geometry of the wrong length or with a non-positive radius, a second lock, a whole lock beside a point lock, and a point lock on a locked entity; A linked projection cannot be locked (it follows its model edge), a solve whose locked arc settles on the mirror sweep its five equations also allow is reported unsatisfied, a stored lock whose recorded values do not fit its entity is refused at solve, and a parameter edit that would move a whole-locked entity is refused by name. Trim and Extend drop the lock of the entity they reshape (counted with the other dropped relations) and Fillet refuses a corner on a locked line. Clicking a line at its midpoint now locks the line; the UX-2 midpoint refusal is shown by the point-only tools. Builds on v141 (#1911, on main); v142 and v143 are claimed by #1914 and #1915. Evidence: `test/sketch-constraint-set.test.ts` (on the pinned kernel a locked line, circle and three arcs covering both end-coordinate branches solve at zero DOF with rank equal to equations and one residual row; a radius edit on a locked circle is refused; a locked arc stays put while a joined line moves to it; validation and the v143 refusal; the mirror-sweep, malformed-record, linked-projection and parameter refusals), `lib/sketch/constraints.test.ts`, `filletBatch.test.ts` and `test/e2e/sketch-constraint-set.spec.ts` ("Lock holds a whole line, replacing its point lock, and unlocks it", undone step by step). | S03 diagnostics; the pinned GCS already has `Symmetric`, `SymmetricAboutPoint` and `FixX`/`FixY`, so this is app-side (C8); [comparison §2.3](docs/reviews/reference-cad-comparison-2026-09-26.md). First slice (research audit ZC-028/034–037): Symmetric (two points about a line), Point on curve (line, circle or arc), Lock/unlock (a recorded position held by `fixX`/`fixY`; a second Lock removes it) and Disconnect (removes the coincident, midpoint and on-curve relations on one point, one undoable step), each validated in document-core, mapped onto existing GCS constraints and armed from the relations rail (`test/sketch-constraint-set.test.ts` closed-form solves, `lib/sketch/constraints.test.ts`, `test/e2e/sketch-constraint-set.spec.ts`). The four point relations share one rail slot that opens a flyout, so the relations column grows by one icon and keeps its layout. Remaining: whole-entity symmetry (two lines or circles in one pick), drag-to-create relations, arc tangency and a diameter dimension. |
| <a id="s08"></a>S08 | Complete | Inference while drawing: optional auto-constraining (horizontal/vertical, perpendicular, tangent from an endpoint, coincident) behind a setting, and with it off only connected endpoints/midpoints; guideline extensions that show where coincidence or tangency is reachable; guidepoints (endpoints, midpoints, centers, intersections, face and hole centers) and off-plane geometry projected in while orthogonal; optional text hints naming the snap. Every inferred relation is visible and deletable. First slice: inference engine and Auto-constrain setting. A drawing commit (line, arc, circle, ellipse, spline, rectangle/polygon composite) adds the relations its placement satisfies exactly, in the same undo step as the entity. Always kept: an end landing on another curve's end is joined (coincident) and an end on a line's midpoint is held there (midpoint). With the new `sketching.autoConstrain` setting on (Settings and the sketch palette; off by default, absent in older settings reads off): horizontal/vertical lines, a line perpendicular to the oblique line it continues, a line leaving an arc end along its tangent and an arc leaving a line end tangentially (`tangent` with the arc's contact point), an end or centre on a centre (coincident), an end snapped to the origin (locked at 0,0) and an end snapped to a line crossing (held on both lines). A hold on a curve or the origin is proposed only from the snap the pointer engaged, never from a grid point that merely lines up. While drawing, Automatic inferencing now also locks a line to the perpendicular of a line ending at its anchor and to the tangent of an arc ending there, and slides an arc's centre onto a line's normal when its start lands on that line's end; an exact snap wins over every lock. Optional relations are kept only where a solve on the pinned kernel says they add rank (`settleOptionalRelations`), so inference never makes a sketch redundant and never moves geometry. No silent constraints: the status line names every inferred relation, the last commit's inferred glyphs are drawn even when glyphs show only the selection's, and each is an ordinary relation that a glyph click and Delete (or Undo) removes. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/inference.test.ts` (rules, locks, refusals of unhinted holds, composite corner deduplication), `test/sketch-inference.test.ts` (pinned-kernel solves: a hand-drawn rectangle is joined and squared at 2 DOF, a rotated rectangle drawn by perpendicular locks keeps one closing perpendicular at 5 DOF, a restated horizontal is dropped, arc-tangent lines stay put, setting-off keeps only joins and midpoints, undo and delete), `relationGlyphs.test.ts`, `test/app-settings.test.ts`, and `test/e2e/sketch-inference.spec.ts` (a chain drawn by clicks keeps coincident, horizontal and vertical relations, a glyph click and Delete removes one, Undo restores it and then the closing line with both relations, setting off keeps only a midpoint). Second slice: guidelines, guidepoints, off-plane projection and snap hints. Guideline extensions: past either end of a line, the pointer snaps onto its extension and a guideline is drawn from that end. A point placed there is held on the line (an on-curve relation, with Auto-constrain on). Tangent guidelines: while a line is drawn, the two points where it would touch each circle are offered from its anchor, with the guideline drawn. A line ending there keeps a tangent relation. On the pinned kernel a hold on the circle at the exact tangent point adds no rank (its gradient coincides with the tangent's), so the settle solve leaves it out rather than adding a redundant relation. Guidepoints: besides the sketch's own endpoints, midpoints, centres and line crossings, the model's vertices, edge midpoints, circle centres (hole and boss centres) and planar face centres are lifted into the sketch plane as named snaps. They carry no stored point, so they are positions to land on, not relations. Off-plane geometry: model guidepoints on the plane are always offered; those in front of or behind it only while the view looks within 1° of the sketch normal, named "(projected)". Snap hints: a new `sketching.snapHints` setting (Settings and the sketch palette; on by default, as the labels always were) names the engaged snap beside its glyph; off keeps the glyph and the snap. A new sketch's first entity commits without a settling solve, since with nothing drawn before it nothing it infers can be redundant. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/guides.test.ts` (extension feet and guideline segments past either end only, tangent points square to the radius, none for arcs, none from inside, on-curve and tangent inference from guide snaps, on-plane versus projected model guidepoints and their labels, orthogonal-view test), `test/sketch-inference.test.ts` (pinned kernel: an extension hold and a tangent-point line solve without redundancy and without moving), `test/app-settings.test.ts`, `test/settings-search-index.test.ts`, and `test/e2e/sketch-guides.spec.ts` (a line started on another's extension is held on it and vertical; a line clicked onto a circle's tangent guide is tangent to it; with a box below the plane, its corner is projected in as "Model vertex (projected)", a line starts exactly on it, and Snap hints off drops the label but keeps the snap). Open: tangent guides on an arc's interior, which needs a tangent relation at an interior contact point (the arc form holds only at its start or end). | S07; S06 for entry flow; the sketch session's existing snapping; [comparison §2.3](docs/reviews/reference-cad-comparison-2026-09-26.md). No silent constraints. |
