Design the representation and the kernel path for curve-only results before any UI. | K09 wire bodies; M04 sweep/loft consumers; R01 reference picks. |
| <a id="r01"></a>R01 | Complete | Datum planes, axes, points and named coordinate systems; define document nodes, source references and failure behavior before implementation. Test upstream edits/deletions and every consuming feature. The 2026-10-16 design defines Offset Plane (canonical/datum/qualified planar-face sources) and parallel Midplane, with Sketch, Split and Mirror consumers, explicit source repair and acceptance tests. Document and exact-build slice (R01-A/B, this PR): schema v19 adds a `datum-plane` feature kind in `featureOrder` (a recorded deviation from the design's separate history union; every other contract stands) with offset and parallel-midplane definitions, `reverseNormal`, and canonical, earlier-datum and lineage-plus-centroid face sources; sketches, Split and Mirror take a datum reference resolved at their history position by both the Remus build and the OCCT parity reference through one pure construction module, failing closed with the datum named; deleting a used datum, pointing at a later one, and a reorder that would replay a sketch before its datum are refused. Regression evidence: `datum-plane-geometry.test.ts`, `planeBasis.test.ts`, the datum cases in `affectedFeatureTargets.test.ts` and `boolean-evolution-lazy-probe.test.ts`, and `datum-plane-feature.test.ts` (derived frames, offset 10/0/−4, chained and reversed datums, midplane, nonparallel refusal, split and mirror volumes, edits through the prefix cache, suppression, delete/edit/reorder refusals, v19 boundary). The exact build publishes each resolved datum frame in the derived state, which the browser uses to display and re-enter datum-attached sketches, and edit preflight walks datum dependents. Interaction slice (R01-C): a Plane tool (Create fold) opens an Offset Plane / Midplane card with named source selects (principal planes, earlier datums, the picked qualified planar face), an expression offset and Flip normal; Done commits one `feature.datum-plane` command only after an exact preflight shows the datum's frame resolved, and reopening a datum from History or Items edits it in place, validated against every sketch, Split and Mirror built on it. Split and Mirror cards offer a Datum plane picker that stores the id (numeric fields hidden while one is chosen); the sketch plane prompt offers each resolved datum; Items gains a Planes filter with eye toggles, source and dependent counts; History rows show the offset or midplane, and History details say what the plane is built from; the viewport draws each resolved, shown datum as a non-pickable translucent quad. Regression evidence: `datumPlanes.test.ts`, `DatumPlaneForm.test.tsx`, the replay and forward-reference cases in `datum-plane-feature.test.ts`, and `datum-planes.spec.ts` (create at 8, split halves z 0–8/8–20, mirror and sketch by id, edit to 12 moves the split, undo/redo, reload). AI operations slice (R01-D, this PR): behind the dark `AI_PATCH_DATUM_PLANE_ENABLED` flag the assistant gains `add_datum_plane` (offset plane or midplane from principal planes, digest datums or same-proposal `$datum` aliases), `add_datum_sketch` and `add_datum_mirror`, compiled to the same datum, sketch and mirror commands the Plane tool uses with ids minted up front so later operations reference a datum before it runs; plain datum ids must name a live datum plane in the digest. Regression evidence: `ai-datum-plane-ops.test.ts` (strict-output parsing, four refusals, digest check, a parameter-driven datum padded and mirrored through the exact build, parameter edit moving the pad, one-step undo), the datum cases in `assistant.test.ts` and `describe.test.ts`, and the pinned flag list. Viewport slice (this PR): clicking a datum plane where nothing in the model is under the pointer selects it (a translucent quad through the part never takes a click meant for a face behind it); a selected offset datum with a plain-number offset shows the face-offset pin on its resolved origin along its resolved normal, and dragging it fills the open Plane card's Offset (signed for a reversed datum, expressions left alone) for the card's own validated Apply; an offset plane whose source face no longer resolves offers "Re-pick source face…" in its history menu, which re-attaches it to the picked planar face, keeping its distance and direction, through one validated, undoable edit. Regression evidence: `datumPlaneRig.test.ts`, `datumPlanes.test.ts`, `DatumPlaneForm.test.tsx`, `datum-face-repair.test.ts` (a datum broken by deleting its block, re-picked onto another block's top) and `datum-plane-viewport.spec.ts` (drag, Apply, viewport pick, undo). Follow-up slice (this PR): the selected plane and its pin are drawn where the open Plane card's Offset would put them, following the pin during a drag, staying there after release and moving with a typed number until Apply rebuilds it (an expression, a flipped normal or a changed source falls back to the built frame); a midplane whose face source no longer resolves offers "Re-pick source face A…" and "B…" for each face source, re-attaching only that one through the same validated, undoable edit (a re-pick that would collapse the midplane onto its other source is refused); and behind the same dark flag the assistant gains `add_datum_split`, cutting a body at a datum with `localId`/`secondLocalId` aliases for the two halves, compiled to the Split command the card uses. Regression evidence: the draft-offset case in `DatumPlaneForm.test.tsx`, the slot refusal in `datumPlanes.test.ts`, the midplane case in `datum-face-repair.test.ts`, the split cases in `ai-datum-plane-ops.test.ts` (halves z 0–8/8–20, the back half mirrored by alias, a double split refused), the datum cases in `describe.test.ts` and `assistant.test.ts`, and the live-follow steps in `datum-plane-viewport.spec.ts`. Point-plane slice (#740, schema v25): Three points and Parallel at point planes from typed points or edge-end vertices by lineage, with collinear/coincident refusals; evidence in `datum-plane-point-constructors.test.ts`, `datum-point-planes` parity and e2e specs. Datum-axis slice (this PR, schema v28, additive): a `datum-axis` feature kind resolved once at its history position into a line published as `datumAxes`, with four constructors — through two points (typed or vertex), along a straight edge by lineage (end 0 → end 1), the axis of a cylinder or cone face by lineage (canonical, kernel-neutral sense) and where two planes meet (nA × nB through the point nearest A's origin) — each with Reverse direction; coincident points, curved/closed edges, non-revolution faces, missing sources and parallel planes are refused by name with no line published. Consumers hold the axis by id: a revolve's `axisRef` (axis must lie in the sketch plane, else refused), a circular pattern's `axisRef` (copies turn about the datum line) and a Move's `axisRotation`, created from the new Rotate about axis card and editable on every Move; the Revolve, Circular pattern and Move cards list earlier axes. An Axis tool (Create fold) opens a card that takes typed points, picked edge ends, a picked straight edge, a picked cylinder or two planes, commits only after an exact preflight shows the line resolved, and reopens from History; Items gains an Axes filter, History rows say how each axis is built, the viewport draws resolved axes as non-pickable dashed lines, deleting a used axis (or a plane an axis is built from) is refused with its dependents named, edits walk axis dependents, and a lost edge or face source offers "Re-pick source edge/face…". The Remus build and the OCCT parity reference share the construction rules (`packages/geometry`) and source resolvers (`face-attachment.ts`). Regression evidence: `datum-axis-geometry.test.ts` (two points, edge, canonical surface sense, XZ∩YZ = +Z, offset and oblique intersections on their analytic lines, parallel/antiparallel refusals on both sides of the tolerance, rotation about an axis, revolve-axis-in-plane rule), `datum-axis-feature.test.ts` (v28 round trip and v26 document unchanged, structural refusals, expression points, plane-intersection lines, parallel and coincident refusals, an edge axis following a block edit, a cylinder axis, planar-face and lost-source refusals by name, revolve annuli about axes at x = 0 and x = 20, the out-of-plane refusal, circular patterns about x = 10 (full ring and 90°), a Move turned 180° before its translation and reversed by Reverse direction, an axis edit moving its Move, delete refusals), `datum-axis-sources.test.ts`, the axis case in `boolean-evolution-lazy-probe.test.ts`, `test/parity/datum-axes.spec.ts` (revolves, an edge-axis pattern, a cylinder-axis Move and the parallel refusal in both kernels), `datumAxes.test.ts`, `DatumAxisForm.test.tsx`, `FeatureForms.datumAxes.test.tsx`, `datumAxisRig.test.ts` and `test/e2e/datum-axes.spec.ts` (a Two planes axis revolving a circle, a typed two-point axis turning a block 180° with Rotate about axis and ringing it with a circular pattern, an axis edit moving both, a coincident refusal creating nothing, undo). AI datum geometry slice (this PR): separately negotiated `add_datum_plane_v2` supports qualified face, typed-point and edge-vertex sources for all four shipped plane definitions; `add_datum_axis` supports all four shipped axis constructors. Full-definition plane/axis updates retain feature identity, and datum-axis revolve, circular-pattern and Move operations share the manual commands. Plane/axis aliases are typed and ordered; exact digest source proof, retained historical-source checks, own datum resolution and affected-consumer preflight refuse stale, ambiguous, forward and degenerate proposals atomically. Legacy plane/numeric operation contracts remain unchanged. Evidence: `packages/ai-contracts/src/datum-geometry.test.ts`, `packages/command-system/src/ai-datum-operations.test.ts`, `test/ai-datum-geometry-exact.test.ts`, `aiPatchPreflight.test.ts`, capability negotiation in `assistant.test.ts`, and `test/e2e/ai-datum-geometry.spec.ts` for Preview/Apply, refusal, undo/redo and reopen. Vertex-source recovery is separately in review (#802); broader reference constructors below remain open. Target catalogue from the comparison: planes offset from a face, midplane, through three points, on a curve at a point, perpendicular to an edge at a point, through an edge at an angle, parallel to a face at a point; axes through two points, at two planes' intersection, of a cylinder/cone, along an edge, perpendicular to a face at a point. Open: planes on a curve, perpendicular to an edge and through an edge at an angle; the axis perpendicular to a face at a point; datum points and coordinate systems. For supported attached datums, expose the defining source and qualified dependents through U06; upstream deletion or ambiguity keeps a named broken reference and explicit repair path. No proximity-based rebinding. Datum planes and axes draw in the "solid frame" look: a 32% fill and 2.5 px frame per plane, a 3 px line per axis, a dashed continuation through the part, a name chip on each, the selection hue when selected and a per-theme `--color-datum`; the world axes and origin planes are unchanged (`datumPlaneRig.test.ts`, `datumAxisRig.test.ts`, `stagePalette.test.ts`). Datum vertex repair (PR #802): History re-picks individual or all stored edge-vertex slots of three-point/parallel-at-point planes and two-point axes without changing datum identity, typed points, raw expressions, direction flags or untouched sources. Multiple lost slots stage before one exact-preflighted, undoable feature update. Invalid, ambiguous or closed-edge picks, degenerate geometry, failed affected consumers and stale/cancelled sessions cannot publish partial changes. Evidence: `test/datum-vertex-repair.test.ts`, `test/datum-vertex-repair-exact.test.ts` and `test/e2e/datum-vertex-repair.spec.ts`, covering downstream exact geometry, retry/cancellation, undo/replay/backup/reopen and compact controls. Boundary provenance recovery remains separate. Datum points and named coordinate systems (schema v99). Document: `datum-point` features place a point at an offset from a typed position, a model vertex or an earlier point, at a straight edge's midpoint, at a circle's or arc's centre (both by lineage), or where an earlier datum axis pierces a plane source. `datum-csys` features place a right-handed system through three points (origin, X, XY plane), on a plane source's own frame (origin moved to a point's foot), or at a point turned about world X, Y and Z. Consumers hold them by id: every point source (three-point and parallel planes, two-point axes, systems, later points) takes `{ type: 'datum-point' }`; a system's XY, YZ and ZX planes are datum plane sources (`{ type: 'datum-csys', plane }`), so sketches, Split and Mirror reach them through a plane; a `csys-axis` datum axis runs along its X, Y or Z for revolves, circular patterns and Moves; and a Move's `csysAlign` carries a body from one system (or the world) onto another. Each resolves once at its history position and is published in `derived.datumPoints` / `derived.datumCsys`. Refused by name, with nothing published: a curved edge's midpoint, a straight edge's centre, an axis parallel to its plane, coincident or collinear system points, and any consumer of a point or system that failed, is suppressed or comes later. Deleting a point or system something is built on is refused with its dependents named; edits walk their dependents. In place, Point (Create fold) drags X, Y and Z arrows from the world origin or a picked edge end, or takes a picked edge's midpoint or an arc's centre; Coordinate system drags its origin and turns its rings, or takes three picked points or a plane; both reopen from History. The viewport draws points as crosses and systems as X/Y/Z triads; the Plane and Axis cards offer earlier points, and the Axis card a System axis mode. Evidence: `test/datum-point-csys.test.ts` (the v99 round trip and v91 refusal, reference rules, delete refusals, typed and chained points moving every plane, axis and system built on them, edge midpoint and arc centre by lineage, an axis piercing a plane and the parallel refusal, a system's planes, axis and an aligned Move landing a block at the closed-form box, a failed system refusing its axis) and `test/e2e/datum-points-csys.spec.ts` (a point dragged out along X, a midpoint picked on an edge, a system dragged and placed, the first point reopened and dragged along Y). Open: planes on a curve, perpendicular to an edge and through an edge at an angle; the axis perpendicular to a face at a point. Reference constructors (schema v108), completing the target catalogue with the points and systems of schema v99. A `normal-to-edge` datum plane stands square to a model edge, its normal the edge's tangent from end 0 toward end 1: at a fraction along the edge (of the length on a line or arc, of the parameter span on any other curve; the kernel evaluates the edge exactly there, and an arc gives the plane's u toward its centre), which covers a plane on a curve at a point, or through a point when the edge is straight, a plane perpendicular to an edge at a point. An `edge-angle` plane runs through a straight edge, turned by an angle about it from the plane through the edge parallel to a reference plane source. A `face-normal` datum axis stands square to any face where a point drops onto it: the kernel carries the point to the nearest point of the face's surface and evaluates the outward normal there. Edges and faces are held by verified lineage only. Refused by name, with nothing published: a closed edge, a fraction outside 0 to 1, a point or an angle on a curved edge, an edge not parallel to the reference plane, a foot that falls off the face, and a face with no normal there. A plane whose edge was lost, or an axis whose face was, offers Re-pick source edge or face from History; only the source changes. In place, the Plane tool takes an edge clicked away from its ends (its position dragged along the edge), the edge and a point, or a straight edge and a plane (its angle turned on a ring); the Axis tool takes a face and an edge end, or a curved face at the click. Both reopen in their cards. Evidence: `test/datum-reference-constructors.test.ts` (the v108 round trip and v107 refusal; a plane at a quarter of an upright edge splitting a block 5 and 15 deep, an edit to a half moving the split, and a plane through a typed point; a plane at the middle of an arc with its normal the tangent and u toward the centre, and a point on that arc refused; planes through a top edge at 0 and 45° from XY, and an upright edge refused as not parallel; axes square to a block's top, a cylinder's side and a top corner, and a foot off the face refused; a plane on a deleted block's edge re-attached to another block's edge) and `test/e2e/datum-reference-constructors.spec.ts` (an upright edge picked and the position dragged, a top edge and the top face picked and the ring turned, and the top face and a corner picked for an axis, each checked where the exact build drew it). | [Datum geometry plan](docs/plans/datum-geometry-plan.md); Product R-1; K05 for topology-derived references. Canonical datum definitions can proceed independently. |
| <a id="f01"></a>F01 | Partial | Review follow-up: the to-face editor checks a separate exact replay at the extrusion's history position, discards stale replies, and keeps Apply disabled while the check is pending. Downstream translation/rotation, immutable-source and browser edit regressions cover this contract (`test/extrude-to-face-draft.test.ts`, `test/e2e/extrude-to-face-draft.spec.ts`). Extrude to-face/to-next/through-all/offset-from-face, start-from-plane and start/end offsets, and per-side draft as bounded slices; extend the inferred add/cut rule to revolve, sweep and loft with one override badge (U01 owns the badge). Preserve blind/two-sided/symmetric replay and explicit operation intent; test moving/deleted targets. Qualification design and pinned-kernel support-function probe for the through-all slice: [F01 through-all qualification](docs/plans/f01-through-all-qualification.md). Through-all slice (this PR): schema v20 adds `endCondition: 'through-all'` with a required `throughAllSide` (forward, backward or both) for a cut with exactly one explicit target; absent stays blind byte-for-byte, and new-body/add, missing target, symmetric or back-distance combinations are refused at entry. Each rebuild projects the target's exact bounds on the resolved sketch normal into a finite span and sweeps it through the existing cut, overlap and coplanar-retry path, so resizing or moving the target moves the cut and nothing derived is stored; a side that misses the target, or a deleted target, refuses by name. The extrude editor offers Extent (Distance / Through all) and Direction for an existing cut, and the history row reads "Through all". Regression evidence: `extrude-through-all.test.ts` (forward/backward/both volumes, a grown and a rotated target, refusals, blind round trip and undo), `ExtrudeForm.test.tsx`, `featureSummary.test.ts` and `extrude-through-all.spec.ts`. Create slice (this PR): choosing Cut while creating an extrude offers the same Extent and Direction, commits with a dormant distance (no distance needs typing) and reports "Cut the region through all of the target."; the operation is never inferred for a through-all cut. Regression evidence: `ExtrudeForm.test.tsx`, `extrudeInference.test.ts` and the create flow in `extrude-through-all.spec.ts`. Intersect slice (ZC-060, this PR): schema v24 adds `operation: 'intersect'`, which keeps only the material the extrusion shares with its one explicit target and consumes that target; it is always explicit, never inferred, and through-all stays cut-only. An unknown stored operation now refuses by name on create, edit and rebuild instead of falling through to the cut arm. The exact builder takes the typed exact intersect, refuses an extrusion that shares nothing (or would leave nothing) by name, and never pierces it; the OCCT parity reference models it as `common` with its target. The extrude card offers Intersect beside Add and Cut. Regression evidence: `solid-result-options.test.ts` (10 × 10 profile through a 10-deep block = 1000 by closed form, consumed target, refusals), `occt-solid-result-options.spec.ts` (both kernels against the same closed form), `ExtrudeForm.test.tsx` and `solid-result-options.spec.ts` (2000 → 1000 with the block consumed). To-face and draft slice (ZC-062, ZC-064, this PR): schema v29 adds `endCondition: 'to-face'` with a required `toFace` (a planar face named by verified lineage, the R01 face-source shape) and an optional `draftAngleDeg`; absent fields are the v28 extrude and load byte for byte. Each rebuild re-finds the face at the feature's history position and derives the distance from the sketch plane to it, so moving the face moves the extent and nothing derived is stored; a deleted, ambiguous, non-planar or no-longer-parallel face, or one lying on the sketch plane, refuses as "Target face is unresolved:" and the feature is skipped. That explicit state shows in the card and the history warning, and a History menu "Re-pick face…" (or the card's Re-pick face) replaces only the face through one validated, undoable edit. Draft tapers a one-sided blind or to-face extrusion about the sketch plane (positive narrows away from it): planar walls through the kernel draft, a single round wall as the exact cone loft between tapered cap circles; mixed curved/straight walls, a taper that closes the section, symmetric/two-sided and through-all refuse by name. A drafted extrude publishes hash-only references. The extrude card offers Extent → To face (any operation) with a viewport face pick, and a Draft angle; dragging the viewport arrow returns the extent to Distance, and the history row reads "To face · 2° draft". The OCCT parity reference resolves the same face through its own lineage and builds OCCT's drafted prism. Regression evidence: `extrude-to-face-draft.test.ts` (closed-form prismatoid, cone frustum, 19.1251 cap, moved/tilted/deleted face, re-pick repair, byte-identical load, edit command), `occt-extrude-options.spec.ts` (both kernels against the closed forms), `stage4-analytic-oracles.test.ts` (swept circle 80π, ruled loft 3066.67), `ExtrudeForm.test.tsx`, `featureSummary.test.ts` and `extrude-to-face-draft.spec.ts`. Remaining: multi-target and add through-all, to-next/offset-from-face, to-face on a slanted or curved face, start offsets and per-side draft. Review follow-up: unreachable face targets refuse preview and submission for create and edit; an armed edit pick ends with its edit-card session (`ExtrudeForm.test.tsx`, Cancel and Close panel cases in `extrude-to-face-draft.spec.ts`). Offset-from-face slice: schema v113 adds `toFaceOffset` (a length or expression) to a to-face extrude. Positive stops the extrusion that far short of the face, negative carries it past; absent or zero ends on the face, and a v29 to-face extrude loads byte for byte. The offset is applied on every rebuild to the distance the face resolves to at the feature's history position, so a moved face or a changed parameter moves the end and nothing derived is stored. An offset that reaches or crosses the sketch plane refuses by name ("Offset from face … reaches the sketch plane"), distinct from the unresolved-face state a re-pick repairs; a deleted face is still unresolved. An offset beside any other extent is refused at entry, and switching to Distance clears it with the face. The Extrude bar offers "Offset from face" on its keypad with To face, the editor refuses an offset past the sketch plane before Done, and the history row reads "To face · 5 mm short" (or "past"). Regression evidence: `test/extrude-offset-from-face.test.ts` (16 × 20 short, 16 × 28 past, a face below the sketch, a parameter offset and a moved face, a pocket stopping 1.5 short of its floor, a drafted offset prismatoid, refusals, edit round trip and undo), `ExtrudeCommand.test.tsx` and the `@ci-smoke` case in `test/e2e/extrude-to-face-draft.spec.ts` (400 → 320 → refused → undo to 400). Remaining: multi-target and add through-all, to-next, to-face on a slanted or curved face, start offsets and per-side draft. Per-side draft slice: schema v126 lets a symmetric or two-sided blind extrude take `draftAngleDeg` for the side its distance runs to, and adds `backDraftAngleDeg` for its other side. Each side is swept one-sided from the start plane, tapered by its own angle about it (positive narrows as it leaves; planar walls through the kernel draft, a round wall as the exact cone loft), and the two halves are fused exactly into one solid per profile cell. A side at zero keeps straight walls. A back draft without a back side, and any draft on a through-all cut, refuse at entry, edit and rebuild. A taper that closes either side refuses by name for that side. Every one-sided extrude builds as before, and older documents load byte for byte. The Extrude bar offers "Back draft angle" on its keypad when the extrude is symmetric or has a back distance, and the draft rig now drafts a two-sided extrude's front side. The History row reads "10 mm symmetric · 2° draft · 3° back draft". Regression evidence: `test/extrude-per-side-draft.test.ts` (prismatoid and frustum closed forms for symmetric front-only, both-sides and back-only drafts, a two-sided taper and flare, a reversed two-sided extrusion, sides about a start plane, a round profile, a parameter back draft, a closing back taper refused by side, a drafted pocket cut, entry refusals, rename, edit round trip and undo, editor rules and the History row), `test/extrude-to-face-draft.test.ts` (a symmetric draft parameter leaving 0 now tapers the front half) and the `@ci-smoke` case in `test/e2e/extrude-to-face-draft.spec.ts` (160 → 153.2 → 146.4 → undo). Remaining: multi-target and add through-all, to-next, and to-face on a slanted or curved face. Start-offset slice: schema v121 adds `startOffset` (a signed length or expression along the sketch normal) to a blind or to-face extrude. The extrusion is built on its start plane, the sketch plane moved by the offset, so a blind distance, a back distance and a symmetric span run from it and a draft hinges on it; a to-face extrude runs from it to the face, less any offset from the face, whichever side of the start the face lies on (a start beyond the face runs back to it, and a face on the sketch plane is reached from a start off it). Absent or zero starts on the sketch plane, and every older extrude loads byte for byte. The offset is resolved on every rebuild, so a changed parameter moves the start and nothing derived is stored. One shared rule (`toFaceExtent`) decides the build and the editor: a start on the face, or an offset from the face that reaches the start, refuses by name, distinct from the unresolved-face state; through all refuses a start offset at entry, edit and rebuild, and a feature pattern refuses a through-all copy its source refuses. The pierce retry tests the start cap where it actually lies. The Extrude bar offers "Start offset" on its keypad except through all, its region arrow, ghost, back arrow and draft rig stand on the start plane, the editor refuses a start on the face before Done, and the History row reads "10 mm · starts 5 mm off". Regression evidence: `test/extrude-start-offset.test.ts` (16 × 10 from z = 5, a negative distance, a start below the sketch, symmetric and back-distance spans about the start plane, a parameter start, to-face 16 × 20, 16 × 25 with an offset from the face, a start beyond the face running back to it, a face on the sketch plane reached from a start offset, named refusals, a boss whose offset start cap lies on the target face, a pattern refusing a through-all copy, a cut pocket that starts inside the block, a drafted prismatoid hinged on the start plane, entry refusals, rename, edit round trip and undo, the editor rules and the History row) and the `@ci-smoke` case in `test/e2e/extrude-to-face-draft.spec.ts` (400 → 320 → refused → undo to 400). Remaining: multi-target and add through-all, to-next, to-face on a slanted or curved face and per-side draft. Add-through-all slice: through all now takes an add as well as a cut, with its one explicit target and a side. It adds no stored field and no schema bump, and an older client refuses an add through all by name. The extrusion fills the target's exact span along the sketch normal (forward, backward or both) and joins the target, so a tab sketched off the target's edge grows through its whole thickness. Resizing or moving the target changes the span, and nothing derived is stored. New-body and intersect through all stay refused at entry, and a side the target does not reach refuses by name. The Extrude bar offers Through all (and its Direction) on Add, and the status line reads "Extruded the region through all of the target, added to the body." Regression evidence: `test/extrude-through-all-add.test.ts` (both sides 16000 + 80, forward and backward 16000 + 40, a thicker plate followed, a missed side refused, entry refusals, the editor and the status line) and the `@ci-smoke` case in `test/e2e/extrude-through-all.spec.ts` (16016 → 16080 → undo). Remaining: multi-target through all and to-face on a curved face. Multi-target through-all slice: a new through-all cut can go through more bodies than its target. The result-body and consumption contract is one through-all cut per body: each extra body becomes its own through-all extrude of the same profile and side, consuming and replacing that body exactly as a single-target through-all cut does (ADR-017 pass-through and ADR-025 single replacement are unchanged). All of them are created and undone as one step (`composeCommands`). No stored field or schema changes: each feature is the through-all cut every client since v20 builds, and editing one later changes that body's cut only. The explicit cut choice carries the extra bodies (`alsoTargetBodyIds`) through the preview, which holds every cut, and the commit. A body named twice, the target named again, or a body the profile misses refuses the whole step by name. The Extrude bar offers "Also cut" rows (one per extra body, another appearing until none is left) under a through-all cut being created, and the status line reads "Cut the region through all of 3 bodies." Regression evidence: `test/extrude-through-all-multi.test.ts` (three plates each cut 4000 − 160 by their own through-all feature, a preview holding all three, one undo removing all, refusals, no extra cut off through all, editor rules) and the `@ci-smoke` case in `test/e2e/extrude-through-all.spec.ts` (target and Also cut picked in the bar, both plates 15000, one undo). Remaining: to-face on a curved face. Curved to-face slice: schema v131 adds `toSurface` beside `toFace` on a to-face extrude (exactly one of the two). It names a cylindrical face by verified lineage, as a wrap names its face, with a pick-time centroid the editor reads before a build. Each rebuild re-finds the face with the wrap's lineage resolver and reads its exact cylinder, then sweeps from the start plane past the face's body. It keeps the near side of the cylinder: from outside, the regions left when an exact solid cylinder is cut away; from inside, the regions common with it; either way only those standing on the start plane. The way to the face is the side its middle lies on; from inside a whole cylinder, where that is level with the start, the stored distance's sign (Reverse) decides. An offset from the face changes the cylinder's radius toward the start, and a start offset and a draft apply. A deleted, ambiguous or non-cylindrical face (a cone), or a cylinder whose axis runs along the extrusion, reads as unresolved, which a re-pick repairs; a re-pick can also swap a planar face for a curved one and back. A profile that passes the cylinder by refuses by name. The viewport's to-face pick now takes a cylindrical face, and the prompts ask for a face facing the sketch. Regression evidence: `test/extrude-to-face-curved.test.ts` (closed forms 4 · (120 − ∫√(100 − x²)) under a radius-10 log, a radius-12 offset, a start offset, from inside the log, a moved log followed, a profile passing it by, a deleted log unresolved, a cradle cut into a block; entry refusals and a byte-identical load) and the `@ci-smoke` case in `test/e2e/extrude-to-face-draft.spec.ts` (the log's wall picked in the viewport, 321.07). Slanted to-face slice: a to-face extrude now reaches a planar face that leans off parallel to the sketch, within about 84° (`TO_FACE_SLANT_COSINE_LIMIT`). It adds no stored field and no schema bump, and an older client still refuses such a target as unresolved. A parallel face keeps the derived blind distance it always had. For a slanted face, each rebuild re-finds the face by its verified lineage and reads the profile's footprint from a unit probe sweep's exact bounds. It sweeps from the start plane past where the face plane meets that footprint, then splits each swept solid with the kernel's exact plane `split`, keeping the half that stands on the start plane. An offset from the face moves the plane square to the face, and a start offset and a draft apply. A face nearly perpendicular to the sketch, or one whose middle lies on the start plane, reads as unresolved (a re-pick repairs it). A face plane that crosses the profile where it starts, or a draft that flares past the face, refuses by name. The extrude card and the pick prompts now ask for a face facing the sketch, and the offset and start checks wait for the build on a slanted face. Regression evidence: `test/extrude-to-face-slanted.test.ts` (closed forms for a ceiling turned 10°, −25° and 40° (16 × 25, top at 25 + 2 tan θ), an offset 3 / cos 30° lower, a start offset, a slanted face below the sketch, a cut down to a slanted floor on another body, draft bounds, a plane crossing the profile, a turned face followed, and a face turned 87° unresolved; editor rules), `test/extrude-to-face-draft.test.ts` (an 88° turn is unresolved) and the `@ci-smoke` case in `test/e2e/extrude-to-face-draft.spec.ts` (400 on a face turned 10°, 351.26 with 3 mm short, undo to 400). A round profile up to a slanted face builds its elliptical section on Remus abf4359 (π · 1.5² · tan 30° · 7 for a circle under a face turned −30°). Remaining: multi-target and add through-all, and to-face on a curved face. Up-to-next slice: schema v130 adds `endCondition: 'to-next'` for an add or a cut with one explicit target. The stored distance's sign gives the direction and its size is dormant. Each rebuild sweeps the profile from its start plane past the target's exact bounds and splits that sweep with the target into exact regions (`booleanRegions`). An add keeps the regions outside the target that stand on the start plane, so it grows until it meets the target. A cut keeps the regions inside the target that stand on the start plane, so it removes material only until the profile first leaves it, at a cavity or the far side. Moving the target moves the end, and nothing derived is stored. A profile that does not meet the target everywhere, an add that starts inside the target, a cut that starts outside it, a target behind the start, and a deleted target refuse by name. Symmetric, two-sided, through-all-side and new-body or intersect combinations are refused at entry, and a feature pattern does not repeat it yet. A start offset and a draft apply. The Extrude bar offers Extent → Up to next for an explicit add or cut, with Reverse for the direction. With Cut's four extents the row shows as a menu. The History row reads "Up to next". Regression evidence: `test/extrude-to-next.test.ts` (a hollow box: an add up to its underside and, reversed, onto its top; a cut from the top stopping at the cavity's roof; a start offset and a drafted prismatoid; a moved target; five named refusals; entry refusals; edit round trip and undo; editor rules) and the `@ci-smoke` case in `test/e2e/extrude-to-next.spec.ts` (22700 → 22500 → undo). The e2e fixtures' `chooseBarOption` now also picks from an Options row that shows as a menu. Remaining: multi-target and add through-all, and to-face on a slanted or curved face. Status correction (2026-11-22 ledger review): Open → Partial. This row records merged slices: through-all (schema v20), intersect (v24), add and multi-target through-all, start offset (v121), per-side draft (v126), up-to-next (v130) and curved to-face (v131). "Open" means nothing has merged. Remaining: the inferred add/cut rule with one override badge for revolve, sweep and loft; start-from-plane; to-face on curved faces beyond cylinders. | Product F-1; existing boolean/draft APIs; topology-dependent conditions require K05's verified references. |

| <a id="f03"></a>F03 | Partial | Extend the AI vocabulary to shipped hole/split/loft/sweep/helical-sweep/draft/thicken operations, then dimension edits; each uses the manual command's exact preflight and one atomic proposal transaction. AI preflight now shares the manual exact-warning gate and the local body scope consumes hole/split (#349); auto-parameterize's body scope now walks through Hole and Split (either half) to the features they consume, with an exhaustive feature-kind switch (`test/auto-parameterize.test.ts`, 1 October review follow-up to #514); recognized read-only features are excluded from edit binding (#332). Vocabulary extension itself has not started. A provider-free whole-part finishing slice accepts "Fillet all edges" (optional radius/units), resolves the named/selected/sole part from complete local topology, and exact-preflights one reviewable transaction; `test/all-edge-fillets.test.ts` and `test/e2e/all-edge-fillets.spec.ts` cover full inventories, seams, units, ambiguity, STEP round-trip and atomic refusal; `test/hook-all-edge-fillet.test.ts` and `test/e2e/hook-all-edge-fillet.spec.ts` prove the 46-edge (49 STEP-imported) hook through both visible UI paths with edit, cancel, oversized refusal, undo/redo, autosave reload, and UI STEP export/reimport. Vocabulary extension started with Hole. The AI patch vocabulary now has an `add_hole` operation: one simple blind or through hole in one exact planar face of an existing body, positioned in the face frame from its area centroid. `commandsForCadPatch` maps it to the manual Hole command (`feature.hole`, `validateHoleInput`) with the payload the Hole card builds, so `preflightCadPatch` runs that command's own refusals and the shared exact warning gate. Apply is one `Apply AI patch` transaction that one undo reverts. A provider reply must copy a current planar digest face (hash and reference), and its target cannot be a body created in the same proposal. Provider exposure is a dark rollout flag (`AI_PATCH_HOLE_ENABLED`, unset in every deployment) and is offered only to clients that declare `add_hole`. A provider-free slice follows the "Fillet all edges" precedent: "Drill a 6 mm hole", "… through hole" and "… hole 8 mm deep" on the one selected flat face use the Hole tool's defaults (⌀6, through, 10 deep) and document units, and say why when no single flat face is selected. Regression evidence: `test/selected-face-hole.test.ts` (exact through and blind volumes, payload equal to the Hole card's submission, single-undo transaction, refusals with the manual command's own reasons, digest stale/curved-face refusals), `AssistantPanel.test.tsx` (local preview without a provider; a refused preview never reaches Apply), `test/assistant.test.ts` (flag and client negotiation) and the `@ci-smoke` browser case `test/e2e/selected-face-hole.spec.ts` (select face, preview, Apply, undo, redo, reload). Counterbore/countersink, standard and tapped holes, sketch-point holes, split, loft, sweep, helical sweep, draft, thicken and dimension edits remain. | Product AI-1/AI-2; S01–S03 as applicable. Keep digest/refusal contracts and rollout flags explicit. |

Existing archival authorization and transport cancellation boundaries are unchanged; production verification remains separate. 2026-10-14 review fixes (this PR): STL imports adopt their millimetres in document units through the shared `importedMeshCommand` again (a 25.4 mm cube had landed as 25.4 in in an inch project since #353), ASCII STL is read by facet structure and refuses unreadable vertices, the OBJ triangle budget is charged before the translator, and a cloud save refused at the document value ceiling reports refused instead of offline (`importedMeshCommand.test.ts`, `test/io.test.ts`, `test/mesh-import.test.ts`, `test/cloud-project-autosave.test.ts`). 14 October review follow-ups IO-2/PERF-1: mesh payload references and cloud/backup recovery are in PR #676; IO-4 PLY declared property names and types (a legal `z y x` file imports mirrored), IO-7 STEP header privacy and IO-8 supported-reader variants remain open in the [project review](docs/reviews/project-review-2026-10-14.md). Also open from the same review: IO-3, the 3MF placement scan reads build items inside comments/CDATA and can take units and placements from a different root part than the translator reads; a mesh import whose file read is still pending when the user switches projects lands in the newly open project (all mesh formats); and a padded binary STL whose 80-byte header reads like an ASCII `vertex` statement is routed to the ASCII reader, so binary plausibility should outrank that match ([project review](docs/reviews/project-review-2026-10-14.md)). Mesh payloads by content reference ([#676](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/pull/676), review IO-2/PERF-1): a mesh import stores its document-unit triangles once as a canonical `OZMESH` payload in the source blob store and archives it as a `mesh-payload` artifact; the feature, its `import.mesh` command and its undo entry carry only a SHA-256 `meshPayloadRef`, resolved by the geometry worker like STEP sources. A 25k-triangle STL now saves to the account and its backup restores; a 100k-triangle import keeps its undo entry; legacy embedded meshes replay unchanged and storage-denied sessions still embed (`test/imported-mesh-payload.test.ts`, `importedMeshPayload.test.ts`, `importArchival.test.ts`, `exactSourceResolver.test.ts`). Review follow-up: shared copies rearchive referenced mesh and STEP bytes under their own account project, including sources retained by undo/redo; a fresh-device resolver regression verifies destination ownership and preserved local bytes. Internal payload filenames stay within the upload limit for accepted 249–255-character source names. The current STL parser preserves multiline solid names and refuses malformed first facets in one-line files (`accountImportSources.test.ts`, `exactSourceResolver.test.ts`, `importArchival.test.ts`, `test/io.test.ts`). Production verification pending deployment. Referenced-mesh decoding owns its bytes for both Uint8Array and Node Buffer views, preserving offset views and decoded data after the input changes (`test/imported-mesh-payload.test.ts`). Ordinary STEP imports now sanitize the FILE_NAME path before local storage, embedded fallback, worker inspection and cloud archival; behavioral tests assert identical sanitized source bytes through the real upload loop (`test/step-import-privacy.test.ts`). Existing model labels and historical source references remain compatible. Review regression: complete STEP headers without `FILE_NAME` retain their original bytes through local storage, inspection, embedding and archival (`lemon-torus-band.step` fixture); malformed or duplicate records still refuse. STL compatibility: sewing retries below the shortest edge after a bounds-tolerance refusal; source facets that cannot become a B-rep remain mesh references with advisory attribution, no solid quantities or STEP export, and direct ASCII/binary STL export and Move/scale. Synthetic coverage includes tiny edges, single facets, non-manifold/degenerate meshes, mixed exports and reopen (`import-mesh-solid.test.ts`, `test/mesh-reference.test.ts`, `stl-mesh-compat.spec.ts`). Above 200,000 triangles, the imported mesh replays directly as a stored mesh reference and keeps its source facets for binary STL export. The viewport draws large references from their facet buffers, with facet wireframe available on demand. The 3MF, OBJ, GLB and PLY limits remain at 200,000 triangles. Parser and replay boundary checks live in `test/io.test.ts` and `test/mesh-reference.test.ts`. The web payload suite covers refusal when browser storage fails and backup restore with 500,000 triangles. `stl-mesh-compat.spec.ts` covers the large file picker, undo, redo, reload and binary export flow.
