Product F-4/design W2; overlap regression is already positive. |
| <a id="m03"></a>M03 | Partial | Variable-radius fillets and asymmetric chamfers with explicit per-vertex controls and failure boundaries; creating, editing and export agree. Delivered: start/end radius under Linear or S-curve laws, and two-setback chamfers, as optional fields in the existing editors behind a maturity gate; unqualified stored configurations refuse by name (#334; `exact-variable-blends.test.ts`, `test/variable-blend-edit.test.ts`). The fillet ladder is seeded from the kernel's measured ceiling, a bevel returned under a fillet's name is refused, and angled chamfers probe at their own angle (#335; `edge-modifier-diagnosis.test.ts`). Open: per-vertex controls, other laws, export/reimport agreement beyond the volume/area oracles; comparison slices, each kernel-qualified before exposure: chord-width fillets, G2 continuity, setback corners, tangent-edge chain selection, overflow handling. 2026-11-03 chamfer qualification: exact analytic cuts preserve earlier rounds, join shared tangent rails, handle closed circular rims and complete concave corners; all-edge chamfer selection excludes already-smooth boundaries. The rounded-block regression covers every sharp edge individually and together at C0.1/C1, preview/commit, undo/redo/reload and exact STEP export (`test/chamfer-rounded-block.test.ts`, `test/e2e/chamfer-rounded-block.spec.ts`; [Remus #1518](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/pull/1518)). The complete 33.712389 mm line–R3 arc–line chain is qualified separately at C1, C0.5 and C0.1. Preview/commit parity, strict validity, unchanged source, Undo/Redo, saved replay and STEP reimport remain asserted in `test/chamfer-curved-chain.test.ts` and the tagged browser regression `test/e2e/chamfer-curved-chain.spec.ts`. Independent OCCT measurements of all three STEP exports match the closed-form bevel integral within 1e-6 mm³, with one retained cylinder and one conical bevel. The original excluded full-support setback contract remains enforced by the analytic fallback. Remus 2026.1.119 covers straight ridges between rational spline extrusion walls and monotone curved spline rims with normal planar ends. `test/e2e/chamfer-freeform.spec.ts` selects both kinds on a genuine imported cubic spline prism and checks preview/commit, Undo/Redo and saved replay with the full renderer. The paired kernel retains source carriers and adds whole-interval contact and parameter certificates; it also preserves distant bores and refuses exhausted or intersecting requests transactionally. The paired kernel and translator packages are pinned to immutable source `831eafb073295b0511dc0a609c2476324b440eb8` from [Remus #1519](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/pull/1519). Before the update, both browser cases retained six faces after the chamfer preview; the new package produces seven faces. The regressions also export STEP through the File menu, reimport the download, and require one valid seven-face solid with its spline surfaces retained. Native kernel coverage includes quadratic and multispan profiles, concave supports, rational weights, transformed and scaled models, asymmetric setbacks, both rims, all longitudinal edges, and distant/intersecting bores. An independent solid comparison found no material difference for seven synthetic cases. Normal-offset checks and a converged section integral also qualify the curved rim independently of spline mass-property quadrature. Arbitrary doubly curved supports, closed spline rims, non-normal ends, unequal endpoint weight gauges and general interacting freeform corners remain unqualified. M03 remains Partial; this evidence qualifies only the two support families described above. 2026-11-15 qualification on the Remus 2026.1.138 pin (`66dbbf1`): variable-radius fillets (linear 1→2, S-curve 2→1) and the 1/2 asymmetric chamfer on every edge of four bodies (a box, a cylinder, a box with a united cylindrical boss, a box with a blind bore), through the document path: 135 cases. 65 built, all exact with closed, consistently oriented display meshes. The asymmetric chamfer built on every straight and circular edge, and the variable fillet on every edge of the plain box. 70 refused, and every refusal now carries a typed reason: the variable-blend failure path raises a `blend` `KernelRefusal` from the kernel's `blend_failure_code` prefix, filing a report with no code as `blend-failed` (`variableBlendRefusal` in `packages/kernel-adapter/src/exact-variable-blends.ts`; the sentence is unchanged). Regressions in `packages/kernel-adapter/src/exact-variable-blends.test.ts` pin the typed refusal of a variable fillet on a box carrying a boss, while the constant fillet and the asymmetric chamfer build on the same edge, and of a variable fillet around a closed cylinder rim. Remaining: a variable fillet refuses on any body with a circular edge, even on an unrelated straight edge, because the kernel's variable engine rebuilds faces from their outer-wire polygons (esaueng/remus#1789; the kernel files it under `invalid-input`). A variable radius around a closed rim and on a cylinder's seam line refuse by design. Per-vertex controls, other laws, curved-spine setbacks and the comparison slices listed in the row are still open. 2026-11-22 overflow handling and typed reasons. The variable-blend refusal path now knows every current Remus `blend_failure_code` (`packages/kernel-adapter/src/exact-variable-blends.ts`). Nine codes used to fall back to `blend-failed` without the kernel's sentence, among them `postcondition-failed`, `cliff-encountered`, `unsupported-variable-boundary` and `unsupported-seam-crossing`. They now keep that sentence and a category. The table already lists `unsupported-variable-support`, which esaueng/remus#1939 introduces for curved spines, curved supports and concave edges. An overflow, a radius that would run the band off its support face, is refused rather than rolled over onto the neighbouring face: no exact band can leave its support. It is a typed `cliff-encountered` (`invalid_input`) refusal, and its sentence carries the width the kernel measured. The adapter relays that sentence and does not parse it. Regressions in `exact-variable-blends.test.ts`: - every code keeps its sentence and category; - a 1→45 fillet on the 60 mm edge of a 60 × 40 × 20 box reaches the document path as a typed `cliff-encountered` refusal whose sentence names the width. #1789 status: fixed upstream by esaueng/remus#1809 and adopted on the 2026.1.146 pin (#1717). `builds a variable fillet on a box that carries a boss` pins it. 2026-11-22 tangent-edge chain selection. The in-place Fillet and Chamfer bars offer "Add the tangent chain (N more edges)" once an edge is picked. It adds the smooth run through the last picked edge, in run order (`tangentChainAdditions` in `apps/web/src/lib/interaction/chamferChain.ts`). Edges already picked, seams and, for a chamfer, already-smooth boundaries are left out. The chain is offered, never applied for the user. Each build stays on the exact route: - A constant fillet along the top outline of a box with R5 corners (four lines and four arcs) builds exactly. It measures the closed form, (1 − π/4)·r² along the lines plus Pappus around each corner arc at the section centroid's inset (10 − 3π)/(3(4 − π))·r (`test/fillet-tangent-chain.test.ts`, to 1e-6). - A variable radius along that chain is refused by type, because the arcs are curved spines. Pick-and-drag browser spec `test/e2e/fillet-tangent-chain.spec.ts`. One top line is picked on the canvas, the chain is added (8 edges), and the radius arrow is dragged. The committed round has no warning, and its rebuild matches the closed form for the dragged radius. 2026-11-22 per-vertex radius controls (schema v151). A variable-radius fillet can store `vertexRadii`: the radius at each end of each selected edge. Each entry names its end by the edge's lineage witness endpoint, so the end is the one where the edge was picked, whatever direction the kernel builds the edge in. The adapter compares the resolved edge's direction with its witness. An edge that now runs across the direction it was picked in is refused by name (`fillet_vertex_radius_unmatched`). A pattern refuses to repeat a per-vertex fillet. The kernel builds the result exactly, within the qualified straight-edge, planar-support family; anything else stays a typed refusal. The in-place Fillet tool offers Radius at: Each corner under Linear or Eased. Each distinct corner of the picked edges gets its own arrow and value (R1…R8); two picked edges that share a corner share its radius. Reopening a fillet from History restores the corners. Tests: - `test/variable-fillet-vertex-radii.test.ts`, through the document path on a 60 × 40 × 20 box: - corners 1/3 and 3/1 build; - each end cap loses exactly (1 − π/4)·r² at the radius set at that end; - the volume matches the closed form; - malformed lists are refused; - the v151 guard; - an edit back to a constant radius drops the corners. - Tool, edit and summary tests in `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/tools.test.ts`, `edit.test.ts` and `edgeModifierEdit.test.ts`. - Pick-and-drag browser spec `test/e2e/variable-fillet-vertex-radii.spec.ts`. An edge is picked on the canvas and Linear and Each corner are chosen. One corner's arrow is dragged, and the other corner keeps its radius. The exported fillet stores the dragged radius at that end, and an exact rebuild matches (1 − π/4)·L·(r0² + r0·r1 + r1²)/3. 2026-11-22 further radius laws: interior radius stations (schema v152). A variable-radius fillet can store `radiusStations`: interior radii at fractions along every selected edge, measured from where the edge was picked (its witness end 0). The fillet's law, Linear or Eased, runs between neighbouring stations. The kernel builds this exactly with its station law (esaueng/remus#1939): each piece is a cubic Hermite row, so the band is one exact Bézier piece per segment. Like per-vertex radii, stations record the way each edge ran when they were set (`edgeRuns`). An edge that runs the other way after an upstream change is refused by code (`fillet_vertex_radius_unmatched`), not built with its stations reversed. The pin before 2026.1.158 ignored the stations, so the field is exposed only on a pin that builds them. The in-place Fillet tool offers Radius at: Along the edge. A Mid R arrow stands partway along the first picked edge, its position set by Mid at (%). A stored list of more stations reopens with a notice rather than being cut silently. Tests: - `test/variable-fillet-radius-stations.test.ts`: a 1 → 3 at 25 % → 2 round under both laws matches (1 − π/4)·L·∫r² by Gauss–Legendre; it also covers the refused lists and the v152 guard. - Tool and edit tests in `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/tools.test.ts` and `apps/web/src/lib/edgeModifierEdit.test.ts`. - Pick-and-drag browser spec `test/e2e/variable-fillet-radius-stations.spec.ts`: the middle arrow is dragged on a picked edge, the saved station has the dragged radius, and an exact rebuild matches the closed form. 2026-11-22 setback corners and overflow handling on the exact route. A variable-radius fillet now asks the kernel for tangent-ball corners (`ballCorners: true`, esaueng/remus#1942). Where three filleted edges meet at a vertex, the kernel closes the corner with an exact sphere octant, but only when it can prove the result is exact: - each meeting law has exactly zero slope at the vertex; - the radii agree there to within the linear tolerance; - no other face reaches into the corner ball. Any other corner is refused by code (`unsupported-setback-corner`). It is never patched with an approximate setback surface, and no setback is written into the document. The adapter calls `filletVariableDetailed` with exact-only acceptance, so a result the kernel would only disclose as approximate is refused as well. A radius larger than the faces along an edge can carry is refused by type (`cliff-encountered`). The refusal relays the largest radius the kernel measured: "The faces along the edge carry a radius of at most N." The previous refusal was a bare build failure. Tests: - `packages/kernel-adapter/src/exact-variable-blends.test.ts`: - a three-edge corner closes with the tangent ball and matches the closed form; - a corner whose radii do not settle is refused by code; - an overflow is refused with the limit as data; - the request carries `ballCorners` and never a setback. - `test/variable-blend-edit.test.ts`: an exact rebuild carries no approximate advisory. - Pick-and-drag browser spec `test/e2e/variable-fillet-ball-corners.spec.ts`: - all 12 box edges under Eased, with the radius arrow dragged, rebuild exactly to the closed form (1 − π/4)·r²·(L − 24r) + 8r³(1 − π/6); - an oversized radius is refused with the limit shown. Export/reimport agreement beyond the volume oracle: - `test/variable-fillet-step-roundtrip.test.ts`: a 1 → 3 round under each law, exported to STEP and reimported, keeps its face count and its closed-form volume within 1e-6; - an all-edge round's eight tangent-ball spheres survive the same round trip. Independent check: on a 60 × 40 × 20 box rounded at 3 on all twelve edges, OCCT measured the STEP exports of the variable fillet and the constant one (cylinders and spheres) at 47109.07945 mm³ each, within 6e-5 mm³ of the closed form 47109.07939. The kernel's own volume integral over the corner-trimmed faces differs by about 5e-7 relative in both, so the browser spec bounds the volume to 5e-6 relative. | K08 qualified support families; product F-2; Remus B72 for chord-width, G2 and tangent-chain (see [C8](docs/kernel-roadmap-remus.md#c8-consumer-asks-from-the-2026-09-26-reference-cad-comparison)). |
| <a id="m04"></a>M04 | Partial | Sweep guide rails/twist and loft guides/end tangency, exposed as the corresponding pinned APIs qualify. Delivered: loft to an apex point (proved by measurement to close the section run) and sweep along a sketch guide rail, both editable and tracked as sketch dependencies; a rail on a smooth sweep is refused rather than resurfaced (#339; `test/advanced-modeling-features.test.ts`, `modelingOperationsEdit.test.ts`). Open: sweep twist, scale along the path, profile position (auto, path intersection, closest point, closest endpoint), orientation (normal to path or parallel to profile) and corner type (mitre/round); loft guide curves, connection-point vertex mapping, periodic lofts and end tangency (none/G1/G2 with magnitude). Production QA CAD-003 (11 October): a sweep along a curved path, and every helical sweep, is built from flat facets on the pin with nothing said — a circle r = 3 swept 270° about r = 30 is 902 planes (−2.16% against the exact torus segment) in Standard and Smooth alike, a six-turn spring 2 838 planes plus 656 bilinear patches (−2.63%), and STEP exports the facets. Diagnostics now names such a sweep a faceted approximation, measured from the body's topology in the app (the kernel worker chunk has no size budget left): curved path with no analytic curved surface in the result, plus the end-cap area shortfall for a circle profile (2.02%), plus a note when Smooth could not apply (Smooth only raises the station count of a single-edge sweep; an arc over a quarter turn is split into several edges); the Sweep card says what Smooth does (`sweepFaceting.ts`, `test/sweep-faceting-disclosure.test.ts`, `test/e2e/sweep-faceting.spec.ts`). Geometry, defaults and saved values are unchanged. Open: an exact surfacing route — on the pin `revolve`/`revolveDetailed` about the arc's axis builds a circle-along-arc sweep as one torus face plus two planes (0% volume error) when the profile plane holds the axis, the profile centroid is the path start and the profile normal matches the start tangent (the opposite orientation validates invalid); it changes face count and references, so it needs an explicit, versioned opt-in rather than a silent rebuild of saved sweeps. A helix has no exact or tolerance-reporting sweep on the pin (Remus 7.1 request). 2026-10-11 queue integration: Review correction: faceting diagnostics inspect the active preview or staged topology. The end-cap area percentage is omitted because the projection does not identify the caps; similarly sized wall facets must not be reported as cap measurements. Coverage: sweep-faceting-disclosure and sweep-faceting. Exact curved and helical sweeps (CAD-003), with no faceted fallback. A new Sweep is built by the kernel's exact chain sweep (`sweepExact`, Remus `exact_sweep`) and saved with `construction: 'in-place'`: the profile stays where it is and is carried by the rigid motions of its path, a line moving it along the line and an arc turning it about the arc's axis, which along a tangent-continuous chain is exactly the rotation-minimizing frame. Each profile edge sweeps into one face per path segment, a plane, cylinder, cone or torus where one exists and otherwise an exact rational NURBS; nothing is sampled. A corner between two straight runs is mitred exactly on the bisector plane (corner type: mitre). A closed chain that brings the profile back builds a ring. Refused by name, building nothing and never falling back to facets: a path curve other than a line or arc, a corner next to an arc or one that turns back, a run too short for its mitres, a profile that reaches an arc's axis, one that is not transversal to the path or that would meet itself, and a guide rail (a rail has no exact construction). `solid_volume` reads these bodies off certified face integrals. A new helical sweep was already wound in place (`helicalSweepInPlace`). Saved sweeps and helices keep replaying their original sampled call, unchanged; Diagnostics still names them faceted and now says how to rebuild them, and Edit offers Build: Exact on exactly those, which rewinds or resweeps them from exact surfaces (a helix's coil then starts at the profile). Evidence: `test/exact-sweep.test.ts` (the QA elbow as two planes and one torus at the torus-segment volume with no faceting advisory, a line–arc–line bend that is cylinder, torus, cylinder in any pick order, a rectangle kept flat and round through the bend and round-tripped through STEP with its cylinders intact, a round tube mitred at a square corner, its two cylinders meeting on an exact ellipse, at area × centre-line length, the corner-next-to-an-arc refusal, a bend tighter than the profile refused, the guide refused), `test/sweep-faceting-disclosure.test.ts` (in-place sweeps and helices are not called faceted; saved sampled ones name the rebuild) and `test/e2e/exact-sweeps.spec.ts` (a profile and an L path picked in place build two cylinders and two planes at π·4·35 mm³; a saved sampled helix reopened, switched to Build: Exact and its height dragged, rebuilt in place at its Pappus volume). Open: sweep twist, scale along the path, profile position and orientation options, a round corner, splines in the path, a corner next to an arc, guide rails on the exact route; loft guide curves, connection-point mapping, periodic lofts and end tangency. History edits on sampled sweeps keep their stored construction and can still add or remove a guide rail. An explicit Exact rebuild writes the in-place construction. Covered by the in-place edit tests and the authored sweep edit, cancel, undo and redo case in the [profile feature browser suite](test/e2e/profile-feature-edit.spec.ts). Loft guide curves and connections, on the exact route (schema v133). **Connections.** A loft stores `connections`: one vertex per section, a line's or arc's end or a rectangle's or polygon's corner, named by identity. Each section's correspondence then starts there, through the kernel's `loftWithOptions` `connections` (Remus #1918). The kernel re-enters each section's outer loop at that vertex. Nothing moves. Straight-edged sections take the exact polygon loft, and curved ones the curve-preserving loft, or the loft is refused: a connection never sends a loft to the chording fallback. **Guide.** A loft stores `guides`: one straight sketch line through a vertex of every section, on a ruled loft. The line fixes the connections where it meets each section's plane, and the ruled edge between those vertices is exactly the guide. **Refused by name:** - a point that is not a section vertex; - a curved guide, more than one guide, or a guide on a smooth loft; - connections or a guide with an apex point; - a section that is one closed curve. **Card.** The Loft card picks a guide line after its sections. **Evidence:** - `test/loft-connections.test.ts`: matching corners give the plain loft at 40. A start shifted by one corner gives the quarter-turn loft at the exact prismatoid 10/6·(4 + 8 + 4), and so does a guide from a bottom corner to the next top corner. Also the refusals. - `test/e2e/exact-sweeps.spec.ts`: a loft picked with a guide line. **Remaining:** a curved guide, which would need a guided surface, has no exact construction yet. Periodic lofts and end tangency are outside this slice. Curved loft guides on the exact route. The guide can be a line, an arc or an open spline, on a ruled or a smooth loft. Remus's guided loft (`loftWithOptions` `guide`, Remus #1932) follows it exactly. Every section corner rides a rail offset from the guide, every wall is a straight blend between two rails, and the guide lies on the solid. A straight guide on a ruled loft keeps the previous route: it is turned into section connections, so curved sections still take it. These are refused by name as a typed `modeling` refusal (`loft_guide_refused`, or `loft-guide:not-one-curve` for a guide that is not one curve of its sketch), never faceted: - a curved guide between curved sections, or between sections with different corner counts; - a guide that misses a section corner; - a guide that doubles back; - a guide that crosses a section backwards. The Loft card offers lines, arcs and open splines as guide picks once two sections are picked. Evidence: - `test/loft-connections.test.ts`: - an arc guide between 2 × 2 squares 10 apart, ruled and smooth: volume 40 (area × height), reaching out to the arc's bulge; - a straight guide on a smooth loft: the quarter-turn prismatoid; - a curved guide between discs: a typed refusal. - `test/e2e/exact-sweeps.spec.ts`: a loft picked with a curved guide arc on the canvas, rebuilt at volume 40. Loft guide and connection refusals are now typed. A curved guide (`loft-guide:curved`), a guide that is not one curve of its sketch (`loft-guide:not-one-curve`), a guide that does not reach every section (`loft-guide:short`), and the kernel's refusal of a connection that misses a section vertex (`loft_connections_refused`) each arrive as a typed `modeling` refusal. Before, each was a plain build failure. A curved guide still has no exact construction on the pinned kernel, so it is refused by name and never faceted. The sentences are unchanged. Evidence: `test/loft-connections.test.ts` asserts each refusal's `kernelRefusal` record. Guide rails on the exact route. An in-place sweep now follows a `guide` rail when the rail is one straight line beside a straight path, in one plane with it, running from the profile's plane to the path's far end. The rail's distance from the path then changes linearly, so the profile scales about the path to follow it exactly. The end scale is the rail's far distance over its near one, built by the kernel's exact `sweepExactWithOptions`. Every point at the rail's distance stays on the rail. Each of these is refused by name and builds nothing, never sampled: - a curved rail; - a rail on a path that bends; - a rail that does not run the length of the path; - a rail that crosses the path or turns about it; - a rail together with a twist or end scale. On Exact, the Sweep card offers Rail picking; the twist and scale rows are not written with a rail. The rail adds no stored field, so the schema is unchanged. Evidence: - `test/exact-sweep-guide-rail.test.ts`: a square scaled to half by a rail from 3 to 1.5 off the path, six planes at the Cavalieri volume; a disc and a rail drawn the other way round, one cone; the four refusals; entry validation. - `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/edit.test.ts`: the rail request. - `test/e2e/exact-sweeps.spec.ts`: a square picked, then a path and a rail clicked on the canvas, builds the scaled sweep. - `test/exact-sweep-twist-scale.test.ts`: a stored twist in a document older than v132 is refused (the guard previously covered replayed commands only). Remaining: a rail that turns about the path (a helix) has no exact construction yet. Sweep orientation and splines in the path, on the exact route (schema v134). An in-place sweep stores `orientation: 'parallel'`. The profile is then translated along the path, never turned, through the kernel's `sweepExactWithOptions` (Remus #1915). Every face is the exact translational surface of a profile edge along a path edge, with control points summed and weights multiplied. - **Path curves:** a path may hold sketch splines, which join the exact path as one exact NURBS edge each, as well as lines and arcs. - **Corners:** corners, including one next to an arc, crease the body instead of being mitred. - **Self-clearance:** the path must run steadily away from the profile's plane, certified on its Bézier spans. A path that turns back, runs along the plane or closes is refused by name. So is a spline on a sweep that turns with its path, whose sentence points to Orientation: Parallel. - **Card:** the Sweep card offers Orientation (Normal to path / Parallel) on Exact. Evidence: - `test/exact-sweep-orientation.test.ts`: a square kept parallel through a corner, at the area times its rise; a disc along a cubic spline, at π·4·20; the refusals. - `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/edit.test.ts`. - `test/e2e/exact-sweeps.spec.ts`: a picked spline path swept parallel, rebuilt exactly. Profile position on the exact route (schema v136). An in-place sweep stores `profilePosition`: `auto`, `intersection`, `closest-point` or `closest-endpoint`. It picks the anchor on the path where the profile stands, measured from the profile's area centroid, through the kernel's `sweepExactWithOptions` (Remus #1917). The profile never moves. From inside the path, the sweep runs both ways. The profile is carried rigidly back to the path's start and swept forward, so the result is one exact body with no boolean. Absent keeps the previous rule: start from the path end nearer the profile's plane. Refused by name: - a crossing the path never makes; - a profile standing on a mitred corner; - a closed path; - a position with a twist, scale, rail or parallel orientation. The Sweep card offers "Profile at" (Path end, Auto, Crossing, Closest point, Closest end) on Exact. Evidence: - `test/exact-sweep-profile-position.test.ts`: both ways from the crossing over the whole path, with bounds on both sides; the closest end; the refusals. - `test/e2e/exact-sweeps.spec.ts`: a path picked through the profile and swept both ways from the crossing. Round corners, and corners next to an arc, on the exact route (schema v135). An in-place sweep stores `cornerRadius`. Each corner of the path is replaced by the arc of that radius tangent to both sides, through the kernel's `sweepExactWithOptions` (Remus #1916). That covers a corner between straight runs, and one beside an arc in the corner's plane. The arc's centre is where the two sides, offset by the radius, meet: lines, a line and a circle, or two circles. The profile then turns about the fillet's axis as exact tori and cylinders. A mitre next to an arc stays refused, because its two bodies do not meet on a plane; a round corner takes it instead. Refused by name: - a radius that does not fit, or lies inside the profile; - a corner out of the arc's plane; - round corners with a twist, scale, rail or parallel orientation. The Sweep card offers Corners (Mitre / Round) with a corner radius on Exact. Evidence: - `test/exact-sweep-round-corners.test.ts`: a tube rounded through one torus band, at Pappus; a line meeting an arc square, rounded at its closed-form centre line; the refusals. - `test/e2e/exact-sweeps.spec.ts`: a picked L path given a typed radius-5 corner, rebuilt as two cylinders and a torus at π·(30 + 5π/2). Sweep twist and scale along the path, on the exact route (schema v132). **Schema.** An in-place sweep stores `twistDeg`, the profile's total turn about the path in degrees, and `endScale`, its scale at the far end about the point where the path crosses it. Both are literals or expressions, linear in the distance travelled. A zero twist and a unit scale are never written, so every older sweep replays unchanged. The sampled sweep refuses both at entry, at edit and at rebuild. **Kernel.** The kernel's `sweepExactWithOptions` builds them (Remus #1913, `exact_sweep::sweep_exact_with_options`) along a straight path with the profile square to it. A twist alone is the screw-fitted twisted extrusion. A scale is the new similar extrusion, where every control point rides the conical screw motion with its own offset from the path's line, so each section is the profile exactly scaled and turned. Without a twist the walls are exact planes, cones and ruled NURBS. With one they are certified fits, whose proven bound is recorded as the edge tolerance. Nothing is faceted. **Refusals.** Each is by name, builds nothing, and is never sampled in its place: an arc or a corner in the path, a closed path, a profile tilted from square, a full circle off the path, and a trimmed spline, ellipse or conic in the profile. **Sweep card.** On Exact, the card offers Twist and End scale, each on a chip beside the profile. An edit reopens them as stored, expressions and all, and setting them back to none clears them. **Evidence.** - `test/exact-sweep-twist-scale.test.ts`: - a scaled square as six planes at the Cavalieri volume A·L·(1 + σ + σ²)/3, which survives a STEP round trip; - a twisted, scaled circle as one cone; - a bar twisted by a typed expression; - the twist and scale cleared in an edit; - entry refusals; - the arc, corner and tilt refusals, in their plain sentences. - `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/edit.test.ts`: the reopen, clear and card cases. - `test/e2e/exact-sweeps.spec.ts`: a picked straight sweep given a typed twist of 90° and an end scale of 0.5, rebuilt exactly at 16 × 20 × 1.75 / 3. **Remaining.** Profile position, orientation (normal to path or parallel to profile), a round corner, splines in the path, a corner next to an arc and guide rails on the exact route; loft guide curves and connections. Profile position, on the exact route, now covers a path that ends on the profile's plane and also crosses it. Remus #1936 offers every point where the path meets the plane, its ends and the crossings inside an arc alike, and stands the profile at the one nearest it. OpenZCAD drops its `sweep-position:ambiguous-crossing` refusal: Auto and Path intersection build such a path, swept both ways from the profile. Evidence: - Remus `exact_sweep::position_tests`: - an arc ending on the plane anchors at its crossing by the profile, with the exact Pappus volume; - an end within tolerance of the plane does not hide that crossing; - two close crossings of a near-tangent arc both stay candidates. - `test/exact-sweep-profile-position.test.ts`: a 210° arc that crosses the plane under a radius-1 disc and ends on the plane, exactly or a hair off it within the kernel tolerance. Auto and Path intersection each sweep the whole arc both ways from the disc, at volume π · 10 · angle. - `test/e2e/exact-sweeps.spec.ts` "a picked arc that ends on the profile plane stands the profile at its crossing": the arc is picked on the canvas and Profile at → Crossing is chosen. It rebuilds with no warning at the 210° Pappus volume, passing under the disc and over the top. Corners next to arcs now have their own canvas pick. A new case in `test/e2e/exact-sweeps.spec.ts`, "a picked line and arc take a round corner beside the arc, exactly", works through the Sweep card: 1. Choose Round, then pick a line and a square-leaving radius-10 arc on the canvas. 2. Switch to Mitre. The card refuses it by name ("A mitre is cut only between straight lines"). 3. Switch back to Round and type a corner radius of 3, which is not the default. | <a id="m06"></a>M06 | Complete | Rib/web from an open profile with explicit thickness/end conditions, reliable boolean join/refusal and revision tests. Ribs (schema v101): a `rib` feature braces a body from an open chain of sketch lines and arcs, `thickness` centred on the profile or to either side. `parallel` fills from the profile, extended along its end tangents, to the body within the sketch plane, the thickness across the plane; `normal` thickens the profile within the plane (offset arcs, mitred line corners) and runs it along the sketch normal to the body. The material is exactly the region the profile and the body close off: the tool is cut by the body into separate regions (`booleanRegions`) and the one beside the profile is kept only when it closes against the body, then united with it through the checked union a Boolean takes, so the result is one body. By default the rib fills the side (or runs the way) that closes, the profile's left (or along the normal) when both do; Flip takes the other. Refused by name, building nothing: a profile in open space ("does not close against the body"), one inside the body, a closed or branching profile, a curve other than a line or arc, a rib thicker than an arc in its profile is tight. Rib runs in place (Edges & faces): its profile is curve clicks, its body a click (a lone body is taken), its thickness an arrow from the profile's middle along the sketch normal; it reopens from History. Evidence: `test/rib.test.ts` (the v101 round trip and v100 refusal, an exact gusset across a bracket's inside corner at 7 500 + 625 mm³, a thickness edit to 1 250 mm³, the flipped side refused, a half-circle arch over a U bracket kept by its crown at 5 000 + 2 × (π·17²/2 + 150) mm³ with its cylinder face, a gusset whose coordinates are expressions, a normal rib from a sketch above a block down to its top, open-space and buried profiles refused) and `test/e2e/rib.spec.ts` (the profile clicked, the lone bracket taken, the thickness dragged to about 4, one body at 7 500 + 312.5 × thickness mm³). | Existing extrusion/boolean subset; product F-6. |

Move-face journals now count only construction-provenance bindings; the affected faces are hash-only, and references a document saved before the fix stored under those names resolve by the exact stored witness onto an unnamed face (ADR-013 step 2, `partial` lineage). A push into a cylinder's rounded rim remains the kernel's plainly worded collision refusal, not a stale name (`test/cylinder-resize-face-lineage.test.ts`, `topology-lineage.test.ts`). Move-face journals are read by the kernel's own events, not output position: `resolveOperationOutput(op, 'face', i)` is the `i`-th result face and names no source, so the reader had swapped corner-band names across an imported rounded block on a face-distance edit. Sources are now tagged and carried with `propagateAttributesForOp`; boss-resize history, now correctly paired, is still refused because legacy documents store misplaced names for those faces (ADR-013 M07 amendment; `move-faces-journal-face-map.test.ts`, `test/face-distance-lineage.test.ts`, `test/cylinder-resize-face-lineage.test.ts`). Direct edits with no construction history (exact sleeve and blend-ladder wall resizes, analytic cap offsets, blend resizes and removals, face-feature removals, imported hole resizes, and moves the kernel journals only by inference) now carry face names by exact predicted carrier instead of dropping them: untouched faces keep their quantized plane or cylinder, the edited face lands on the carrier the command constructs, and a name crosses only when that carrier is unique among the source faces and in the result. Names the retired positional reader placed elsewhere stay withheld so saved references keep their exact-witness fallback (ADR-013 H01/M07 predicted-carrier amendment; `remus-predicted-carrier-lineage.test.ts`, `test/direct-edit-predicted-carrier-lineage.test.ts`). On a reported 52-feature box ∪ cylinder history, changing an upstream seam fillet from R2 to R3 now fails 4 later features instead of 20. Correction, as of 2026-11-13: the earlier note that boss-resize history "is still refused because legacy documents store misplaced names for those faces" is historical. Since move-face journals count only construction-provenance bindings, references a legacy document stored under those misplaced names resolve by their exact stored witness, and a later edit on such a document replays (`test/cylinder-resize-face-lineage.test.ts`, "still replays a later edit that stored a misplaced name"). Openings close two ways. Pushing a planar single-loop face into the wall opposite it now adds the prism the face sweeps through, when every face beside it is a plane parallel to the push and the push stays in the body: a window's floor pushed to its top or past it into wall closes the window exactly, and a push that would leave the body still refuses. A new replayable `remove-faces` direct edit deletes several faces of one body together, with every face pinned by its hash, surface type, area and centre. Selecting the four walls of a window closes it. Selecting three of them refuses, and the refusal says to select every face around the opening. In the workspace, Shift-click on faces of one body builds a face set, and Delete removes the set as one undoable edit (`test/close-opening-direct-edit.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/faceSetSelection.test.ts`). Pushing a window's floor across the opening now also closes a window whose floor is not convex, such as an L- or T-shaped floor where the window is cut across a rib or rail band that thickens the wall. Between sides parallel to the push, the fill is built as the union of convex pieces of the floor. Each piece is bounded by the floor's own exact side planes, and the result is the same as a push on a rectangular floor. Leaning sides or blends beside a reflex corner still refuse (`test/close-opening-direct-edit.test.ts`). Pushing an opening's floor across it now closes windows with rounded corners and drafted sides too. The swept region is built exactly as a box split by the face's planes, which reproduces the kernel's re-limited move for any side tilt on a convex face. The floor's own fillets travel with it, and blends the sweep swallows are removed with the kernel's defeature before the union, because the exact union refuses tangent contact. The fallback declines a push that would grow the body past its own extent, or one shorter than its blends. Delete on the wall of a hole a subtract cut (a round port) heals the face in place, as it does on an imported part, instead of deleting the cutting tool the subtract needs (`test/close-opening-direct-edit.test.ts`). QA 2026-11-14 finding #9, owner decision: the face-offset chip and its typed field start on Offset, the signed distance the face moves (+ out, − in), on every face, so a typed 5 on a 24 mm body's top face moves it +5 instead of −19. Total, the size the face sets, stays one switch away on the chip's tag wherever a span is known, keeps its baseline and meaning line, and stays with the face it was chosen on, through that face's own commit; any other face, even one facing the same way, starts on Offset again. Stored feature values are unchanged (`offsetChip.test.ts`, `opposingPlaneSpan.test.tsx`, `prompt.test.ts`, `face-offset-total.spec.ts`, `shoulder-cap-offset.spec.ts`). Solid offset, which only grows a body, no longer applies the inward wall cap of half the thinnest extent; the kernel keeps its own refusals (`remus-modeling-operations.test.ts`). A decimal comma (`2,5`) names the decimal point (`expression-audit.test.ts`). A second click on the same spot still steps to the edge (`apps/web/src/components/viewer/faceSetPick.test.ts`). Picked chamfer, fillet and hole faces stay owned by their feature under later edits: a later fillet or chamfer carries each support it re-trims, re-witnessed, and edits with no construction history (blend removal, through-hole resize) carry cone, torus and sphere faces by their exact kernel carrier. Deleting a detail that later details or blend edits were built on offers to delete them too; Delete on a face no step owns heals it in place, as on a STEP part; the viewport menu offers Delete; single-face removal no longer refuses curved bodies up front (`test/picked-chamfer-under-later-edits.test.ts`, `remus-predicted-carrier-lineage.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/DeleteDependentsDialog.test.tsx`). The adapter chains one exact journaled move per plane, each along its own normal, in a geometry-only order (direction classes by descending normal z, y, x; leading plane first within a class), so each shared edge ends where the moved planes meet. Two faces with opposite normals are still refused, as is a later step that would change the body's topology. No schema bump, no new Remus API, no workspace change. Offset arms the one shared handle on the last-picked face, each face moves along its own normal by the one distance, and a small direction tick on every face of the set shows which way it moves. While dragging, the chain's leading plane is previewed display-only every frame from the kernel's prepared move, and the whole chain at a coarse rate (250 ms). Release, a typed value and a reopened History row all replay the whole set with full checks, and kernel refusals are named on the card as on the single-face path. A set whose faces face different ways, several bodies, a curved face and more than 64 faces are still refused before any handle appears. `apps/web/src/lib/faceSetOffset.test.ts`, `packages/viewport/src/gizmo/faceSetTicks.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/livePreview.test.ts`, `test/offset-faces-workspace.test.ts`, `test/e2e/offset-face-set.spec.ts`. Every face is pinned like `remove-faces`; a re-pick repairs the first face, and a drifted other face refuses with "Select the faces again". Face ranges are exact where curvature is constant on the surface type (plane, cylinder, sphere) and sampled on cones, tori and freeform faces; the panel counts the exact ones. Zebra reflects stripes from the kernel's exact outward normals (`evaluateSurfaceNormal`), sampled four times as densely, in a clipped shader that moves with the view; the stripe count only redraws. Limits stated on screen: curvature along edges and where undefined (a cone's apex, a freeform pole) is left grey and listed; the scale spans the largest value found; zebra is read by eye at the sample density and is not a certified G1/G2 check. Evidence: `test/surface-analysis.test.ts` (sphere k = 1/r exact in all three measures with its 5 mm radius in closed form; a bored plate's bore mean −1/12 concave, Gaussian 0, maximum 1/6, radius 6 exact, its planes flat; a torus sampled with Gaussian spanning both signs within ±1/21; zebra unit outward normals, denser than draft's samples), `packages/viewport/src/scene/analysisOverlay.test.ts` (zebra shader, uniforms, clipping), `apps/web/src/lib/analysis/surfaceAnalysisUi.test.ts`, `AnalysisPanel.test.tsx`, `DisplayModeMenu.test.tsx` and the CI smoke case `test/e2e/analysis-curvature-zebra.spec.ts` (a block and an 8 mm ball: mean 0 to 0.125 1/mm with the 8 mm exact radius, Gaussian 0.0156 1/mm², zebra on all 8 faces, cleared on Turn off). Draft analysis overlay (view state only, no schema change): Display › Analysis › Draft colours every body on screen against a pull direction (±X/±Y/±Z) and a minimum draft: green drafts toward the pull, amber lies within ±minimum, red drafts away (an undercut against this pull), grey was not evaluated. It is one read-only query on the M09 clash query's worker path and build (`surface-analysis` request, `ExactKernelAdapter.surfaceAnalysis`, `packages/kernel-adapter/src/exact-surface-analysis.ts`), never the display mesh: each face is tessellated on its own, every vertex is projected onto the face's exact surface, and the kernel's `getFaceDraftAngle` gives the value there; a projection that lands off the tessellation by more than the chord tolerance leaves the sample unevaluated rather than coloured. The minimum only recolours (`draftBand`/`draftVerdict` in `@openzcad/shared`); a new pull measures again. The panel states its provenance (exact draft at samples; a plane's range is exact, a curved face's is its sampled range, with the count of exact face ranges) and its limits (not a parting-line or mouldability check; refused faces, partly evaluated faces, bodies over 150,000 samples and bodies with no exact geometry are listed by name, never given a value), and withholds the overlay while a preview stands in for the build. The overlay is a child of each body's mesh, so it follows assembly poses and the section cut, and is never picked; it is drawn as the body's faces (their material is switched off while it meshes every face, so the coarser samples are never hidden behind the finer display mesh), and the panel, colouring and overlay code load with the first analysis, outside the entry chunk. Evidence: `test/surface-analysis.test.ts` (box ±90°/0° exact with values matching the exact normals, a non-unit X pull, a cylinder wall at 0° sampled not exact, sphere halves crossing bands, a tilted body at ±30°/±60° where the build put it, a missing body, a zero pull refused), `packages/viewport/src/scene/analysisOverlay.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/analysis/surfaceAnalysisUi.test.ts`, `AnalysisPanel.test.tsx`, `DisplayModeMenu.test.tsx` and the CI smoke case `test/e2e/analysis-overlays.spec.ts` (block and pin under +Z then +X, the legend recoloured at 5°, the overlay cleared on Turn off). Wall-thickness analysis (view state only, no schema change), on the same `surface-analysis` query path and overlay as draft, curvature and zebra (`exact-surface-analysis.ts`; one engine with the clash query), for 3D-print and moulding checks. Display › Analysis › Thickness measures by the ray method: from the centre of each triangle of a per-face mesh subdivided by longest-edge bisection (so samples stand away from edges), projected onto the exact surface, along the inward exact normal to the first wall the ray meets. Planes, cylinders and spheres are crossed in closed form (`getAnalyticSurfaceParams`), each crossing confirmed on the trimmed face by the kernel's exact face distance; cones, tori and NURBS are sphere-traced on exact point-to-face distances (a step that size cannot cross a wall) up to the nearest closed-form crossing, and a trace that cannot rule out an earlier wall refuses the sample rather than report the crossing. Every figure is labelled approximate, with the body's resolution (1e-4 of its size) shown, except a flat face whose every sample lands on a parallel opposing plane (`getOpposingPlanarFacePairs`), which reads as their exact separation; the panel counts exact face ranges and names the thinnest wall, exact or approximate, and the faces under the minimum wall (default 1 mm in the document's units; the minimum only recolours: red under it, amber under twice it, green above). Limits stated on screen: thin spots between samples, thickness other than along the normal (the ray method reads sharp inside corners thick), and samples a ray cannot start or finish (knife edges, shown grey and listed); bodies over 40,000 samples are refused by name. With this slice M09's row contract is met: exact clash/clearance with witnesses (AS04), draft, curvature and zebra overlays and thickness analysis, each with its provenance and unsupported cases on screen; clearance thresholds and a clash command outside Assemble remain follow-ups. Evidence: `test/surface-analysis.test.ts` (a 40 × 30 × 2 plate exact at 2/30/40; a cylinder's wall 10 to the resolution and its ends exactly 20; a bored plate's 3 mm wall beside the bore found and not called exact; a torus tube traced to its 6 mm diameter), `packages/kernel-adapter/src/exact-surface-analysis.test.ts` (closed-form plane, cylinder and sphere crossings; sliver bisection and the sample budget), `apps/web/src/lib/analysis/surfaceAnalysisUi.test.ts`, `AnalysisPanel.test.tsx`, `DisplayModeMenu.test.tsx` and the CI smoke case `test/e2e/analysis-thickness.spec.ts` (a 2 mm plate and a pin: thinnest 2 mm exact, 8 of 9 face ranges exact, two faces under a 3 mm minimum, cleared on Turn off). | Qualified read-only kernel queries (Remus 7.5, C8); product A-2/A-4. AS04 reuses this rather than adding a second engine. |
| <a id="m10"></a>M10 | Partial | Transform depth: copy badges on move/rotate, scale and rotate-about-axis (repeated stamps, linked or as separate steps), non-uniform scale about a movable center, rotate about a model edge or datum axis, translate by two picked points, and Align (a body's face, edge or axis onto a target face, plane, axis or circular edge with snap positions, flip and gizmo adjustment). Each is one transaction. Existing rigid-transform lineage covers rigid moves and rotations; non-uniform scale needs a qualified affine lineage path that preserves topology references or reports their loss explicitly. Pivot slice (Stage 1b, ZC-083/085/087, PR #732): schema v22 adds an optional `pivot` to every Move, the point rotation and uniform scale turn about (absent is the world origin, so older documents are unchanged). The exact build and the OCCT parity reference fold it into one matrix (t + p − L·p); the Move gizmo now stores the body's centre as the pivot instead of folding it into the translation, the numeric edit card shows and edits it (pX/pY/pZ), and Apply keeps it, so a later rotation edit turns the body in place rather than about the origin. Reference-lineage follow-up: preserve the oriented plane normal until canonicalizing its offset, so rotated and scaled Moves keep all six face and twelve edge references; signed-plane regressions include translation and 90/180/270-degree turns in `test/parity/transform-pivot-scale.spec.ts`. Regression evidence: `test/transform-pivot.test.ts` (+10 then −10 restores the bounds exactly; 90° about the centre and its inverse; the same turn about the origin lands elsewhere; ×2 about the pivot gives ×8 volume with the centre fixed; an edited angle keeps the pivot), `TransformForm.test.tsx` and `transform-pivot.spec.ts` (gizmo rotate → card shows the centre → edit 45° to 90° keeps it). Copy slice (ZC-084, this PR): schema v23 adds `copy: true` to a transform feature, which then owns a `bodyId` and body node of its own (as a Mirror does) and leaves its target live and unmoved; the exact build publishes the rigid-transform copy (verified lineage, so edges on the copy stay addressable) under the new body and never consumes the source. A shared `isInPlaceTransform` guard keeps a copy out of every rule that reads "the transform of body X" as an in-place edit — selection, the primitive placement Move, face-offset placement, cylinder ancestry, history ownership, imported-source placement, the growing-holder recipe and auto-parameterize — and `copy` is not patchable, so a stored Move cannot change identity. The Move gizmo card and the create card carry a Copy box (a copy applies even at zero, as a coincident duplicate); the edit card names an existing copy. Regression evidence: `test/transform-copy.test.ts` (source bounds and volume unchanged; copy at +30 mm with equal volume and its own BodyId; rotate 90° and ×2 about the pivot; both bodies export as two STEP solids; undo/redo/replay keep the BodyId; a source edit reaches the copy and a later source Move does not; a fillet on the copy consumes only the copy; `copy` refused as a patch), `transformCopy.test.ts` (selection, placement, history graph), `TransformForm.test.tsx` and `transform-copy.spec.ts` (gizmo copy → two Items rows, one History row, undo/redo). Copy review follow-up: preserve resolved default/chosen colours and source opacity without treating a feature-colour stamp as a choice. Result body and feature IDs must be unused in their own namespaces, and result node IDs must be unused and distinct, with collisions refused before document mutation. The OCCT reference publishes copies under their result body while keeping the source; repeated copies use distinct numbered body names, persisted by both web creation paths. Explicit feature IDs are retained and body IDs are serialized before replay; coincident copies participate in Apply/Discard guards; the viewport draws an independent copy projection while preserving the source and section validity guards. Regressions: dependent-copy redo/replay in `test/transform-copy.test.ts`, preview lifecycle/resource ownership in `packages/viewport/src/scene/moveCopyPreview.test.ts`, and all four guarded transitions plus a delayed-worker Apply hold in `test/e2e/transform-copy.spec.ts`. The applied copy stays visible until exact geometry replaces its temporary projection. History Duplicate preserves a copied transform's new-body ownership, with source bounds, undo/redo/replay and browser coverage. Remaining: linked copies and repeated stamps, a movable pivot handle in the gizmo, and translate-by-two-points. A movable pivot handle in the gizmo (no schema or kernel change: the Move's existing v22 `pivot`). The Move bar gains a Pivot toggle. While it is on, dragging an arrow or the centre ball carries the point the rings turn about and leaves the body where it is: the drag splits into the pivot's own move Rᵀ·Δ and the translation that keeps the body still, so the gizmo lands where it was dragged, and the centre ball snaps to the moving body's own corners, midpoints and centres (read where the preview shows the body) as well as other bodies'. Options show the pivot (pX/pY/pZ, typed values keep the body still by t′ = t + (R − I)(p′ − p)) and "Pivot to body centre"; Done stores the placed pivot, so the turn and any later edit go about it; switching the target body clears it. Evidence: `packages/viewport/src/gizmo/moveTransform.test.ts` (relocating a pivot under no, a 90° and a compound turn leaves three body corners in place; a pivot drag moves the gizmo by exactly the drag and keeps the body still), `DirectModelingOverlays.test.tsx` and the Chromium smoke case "the pivot handle places the turn centre without moving the body" in `test/e2e/transform-pivot.spec.ts` (drag the centre ball under Pivot, Done stays off with Move X/Y at 0, then a 90° turn stores the dragged pivot in History). Linked copies and repeated stamps (schema v137, up from v136; no kernel change). A transform copy can store `stampBodyIds`: each id names one more copy, stamped by the same transform from the copy before it, so stamp k lands where the transform applied k times puts the source (rotations about the stored pivot included), each through the same verified-lineage copy, so its edges stay addressable. Linked, the stamps are one History step owning every copy ("× 3 copies"); an edit to its transform moves every stamp, and the stamps are body wiring, never patchable. As separate steps, each stamp is its own transform copy of the one before. Either way one Done is one transaction and one Undo removes every stamp. The Move bar's Options gain Copies (1–50, while Keep original is on) and "Link copies (one History step)"; the viewport previews every stamp before Done. History Duplicate stamps as many new bodies. A content gate refuses stamps in a document or replayed command that declares v136 or older. Evidence: `test/transform-copy-stamps.test.ts` (closed-form bounds and volumes for three stamps along X and for 90°, 180° and 270° turns about the pivot, four STEP solids, undo/redo/replay under the same BodyIds, an edited step moving every stamp, a fillet on one stamp leaving the rest, separate steps landing where linked stamps land, Duplicate, count refusals and the v137 gate), `moveCopyPreview.test.ts`, `DirectModelingOverlays.test.tsx`, `featureSummary.test.ts` and the Chromium smoke case "the Move card stamps linked copies or separate steps" in `test/e2e/transform-copy.spec.ts`. Translate by two picked points (no schema or kernel change). The Move bar gains Two points. It starts the pending move again from nothing and hides the gizmo, so picks read the model where it rests. Each click on the model takes the point its snap glyph names (an endpoint, midpoint or centre, as Measure snaps them) or else the surface point, and selects nothing. The first pick names the point to move from ("From Endpoint · pick the point to move it to"); the second sets the translation to the difference and ends the pick, and Done commits it as one Move (with its pivot, as every gizmo Move). The viewer's new `onPointPick` host callback carries the snapped point and its kind. Evidence: `DirectModelingOverlays.test.tsx`, `ModelViewer.lifecycle.test.tsx` and the Chromium smoke case "Move by two points carries the first pick onto the second" in `test/e2e/transform-pivot.spec.ts` (two located edge picks under Two points, no apply-first question, a non-zero move in Options, one Move in History with no warnings). | R01 for axes; U01 gizmo contract; existing rigid-transform lineage; Remus B74 before non-uniform scale (C8); [comparison §2.6](docs/reviews/reference-cad-comparison-2026-09-26.md). |

| <a id="m12"></a>M12 | Complete | Wrap/emboss: map sketch regions onto a cylindrical or conical face preserving arc length, with emboss or engrave depth, rotation and center. Design the representation and qualify the kernel path before any UI. Wrap, emboss and engrave (schema v111): a `wrap` feature wraps regions of a sketch drawn on a plane parallel to the axis of a cylindrical or conical face onto that face, preserving arc length, and embosses them out of it or engraves them into it by `depth`, turned about the axis by `rotationDeg` and slid along it by `axialOffset`; the result is one body. Out of the face is away from its material, so in a bore an emboss stands toward the axis and an engrave cuts into the wall. The kernel's `wrapProfileOnCylinder` (Remus) builds the wrapped material as the solid between two radii: exact cylinders inside and out, exact planes for edges along or across the axis, and for every other edge (an oblique line wraps to a helix, an arc or spline to a curve with no rational form) an exact ruled wall between certified cubic fits whose distance from the exact wrapped curve is proven within 1e-7 of the wrap's scale by the Hermite remainder. The emboss reaches a quarter of its depth into the cylinder and the engrave a quarter out of it, so the union or cut meets the face across a margin rather than a coincident surface. On a cone, the kernel's `wrapProfileOnCone` reads the regions in the cone's development (arc length across, length along the generator from the circle at the sketch's origin) and builds the solid between two normal offsets: exact cones inside and out (a cone offset along its normal is a cone), every edge a certified fit from a closed-form fourth-derivative bound of the development map, and walls ruled along the normal between an edge's two equal-span fits; an annular sector of the development lands within 1e-6 of its closed form. Arc length is read along the sketch plane itself, so Turn moves a wrap round the axis without scaling it and a region lands under where it is drawn whichever way the sketch faces. Refused by name: a sketch not parallel to the axis, a face that is neither a cylinder nor a cone, a depth past the axis or an offset past a cone's apex, a region reaching the apex or spanning a full turn, a rational curve other than a circle or ellipse. Known limit: Remus reads the volume of a cone or cylinder face trimmed by a curved boundary to its face integrator's boundary chording (a wrapped disc 1.3e-4 low on a cone, 4e-4 high engraved in a radius-10 post, which exact mass properties do not show), so a body wrapped with curved regions reports a volume carrying that error. Wrap runs in place (Edges & faces): regions are clicks, the cylinder or cone a face click, depth an arrow out of (or into) the face, turn a ring about the axis, offset an arrow along it; it reopens from History. Evidence: `test/wrap.test.ts` (the v111 round trip and older-schema refusal; a rectangle embossed at the post's volume + A·(r2² − r1²)/2R; a disc engraved; a quarter turn and offset; a sketch not parallel to the axis refused; a label embossed toward the axis and engraved into the wall of a bore at the closed-form volumes; a label embossed and engraved on a frustum through its development, one body each with exact cone faces, within the integrator's chording of the developed volume) and `test/e2e/wrap.spec.ts` (region and face clicks, the depth dragged, one body at the closed-form volume). | K07/K09 kernel qualification; Remus B73 after 7.4 (C8); [comparison §2.5](docs/reviews/reference-cad-comparison-2026-09-26.md). Not before D01. |
| <a id="m13"></a>M13 | Partial | Measure depth: point-to-point between notable points (endpoints, midpoints, centers, intersections, face and hole centers) and 3-point angle; maximum, central (from a center or axis) and parallel distances including the radial difference of concentric items; hover-to-highlight the measured entity. Preserve existing X/Y/Z deltas and show/hide viewport annotations as new measurement modes are added. Every value keeps its exactness provenance label. Core slice delivered: `notablePointTarget` resolves vertices, line and arc trimmed-range midpoints (on the arc, not the chord), circle/hole centres and area centroids (never the rim mean), point-to-point distances keep X/Y/Z deltas, and `createThreePointAngle` reports the included 0–180° angle; each value is exact only when every input is, and a centroid is exact only when the integrator walked its boundary exactly (this PR; `test/notable-point-measure.test.ts`). Measure is reachable from Build too (instrument rail and palette; 1 October review F13, `build-reach.spec.ts`). Open: viewport picks that choose a point kind, maximum/central/parallel distances and hover highlighting. A Distance pick under a measure snap glyph records the point the glyph names (QA15-2E-01: "Endpoint" had recorded the edge's midpoint, 57.31 mm measured for 56). Each topology snap candidate now carries its source edge and role, the click passes it in `PickDetail.snap`, and `measurementTargetFromSnap` records the matching vertex, midpoint or circle centre by identity, refusing a source that no longer lands on the marked point (`test/measure-snap-pick.test.ts`, `packages/viewport/src/snap/topologySnaps.test.ts`, `test/e2e/measure.spec.ts`). Hover-to-highlight the measured entity (no schema or kernel change). Pointing at a Measure row, or giving it keyboard focus, lights the edges and faces that measurement measured on the model, drawn in the pointer hover's own colours (`SELECTION_SEMANTICS.hover`: the face film and the wide hover edge) on each body's object, so a moving preview carries them; leaving the row clears them, and selection is never touched. A whole-body figure lights nothing, since the hover draws entities rather than solids. The row-to-entity mapping (`measurementHighlightTargets`) sits outside the deferred measurement library, and the viewer's new `measureHighlights` prop builds and disposes its overlays per change. Existing X/Y/Z deltas and the show/hide annotation toggle are unchanged. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/measureHighlights.test.ts`, `MeasurementDock.test.tsx` (pointer and focus enter and leave) and the Chromium smoke case "hovering a measurement row highlights the edge it measured" in `test/e2e/measure.spec.ts` (an edge recorded in View, its row hovered and focused lights one overlay, and moving off or blurring clears it). Maximum, central and parallel distances (no schema or kernel change; measurements stay out of the document). Measure gains three modes, each two picks recorded by identity like Distance, with the geometry read from the picks' exact topology at measure and refresh time and solved in closed form (`apps/web/src/lib/measureDistances.ts`). Maximum takes points, straight edges and full circles: over a segment the farthest distance is convex, so it is reached at an end, and from a point to a full circle it is √(h² + (ρ + r)²) at the opposite rim point. Central measures from a circle's centre, or square from a cylinder's or hole's axis, to the other pick. Parallel measures between parallel edges, axes or planar faces and between an edge and a parallel face, and reads two concentric circles or cylinders as their radial difference. Each row draws the span it measured and names itself ("Parallel distance, radial difference"); pairs without a closed form here (an arc's or a circle pair's maximum, a face's maximum, non-parallel pairs, picks with no centre) are refused by name and keep the first pick, never estimated. The kinds `max-distance`, `central-distance` and `parallel-distance` persist and refresh with the other rows. Evidence: `test/entity-distance-measure.test.ts` (13 cases: segment-end, point-to-rim and segment-to-circle maxima; centre and axis central distances; parallel edges, faces, an edge on a face, a radial difference and offset axes; every refusal; a ring wall recorded as its 6 mm radial difference, refreshed to 7 mm when the bore shrinks and kept through the store), `MeasurementDock.test.tsx` and the Chromium smoke case "Maximum records the farthest two edges are apart, and Central says what it needs" in `test/e2e/measure.spec.ts`. Viewport picks that choose a point kind (no schema or kernel change; measurements stay out of the document). In Distance, the Measure dock gains "Point to pick": Auto, Endpoint, Midpoint, Center, Centroid. Auto keeps the existing derivation. A chosen kind records that notable point of each pick through `notablePointTarget`, by identity: an endpoint is the end the snap glyph named, else the end nearer the pick, stored as the edge's start or end; a midpoint lies on the trimmed line or arc; a center is a circle's, an arc's or a cylinder or hole axis's; a centroid is a face's area centroid. A pick without that kind is refused with the reason ("That edge is not a circle or arc, so it has no center.") rather than recorded as another kind. The viewport's measure snaps are scoped to the chosen kind (`measureSnapKinds`), so the glyph only ever names what the click will record; the kinds live in a small module so the workspace scopes snaps without loading the deferred measurement library. Evidence: `test/notable-point-measure.test.ts` ("point-kind picks": nearer and snap-named ends, line and arc midpoints, arc and hole centres, an area centroid, four refusals with reasons, snap scoping, an exact midpoint-to-hole-centre distance), `MeasurementDock.test.tsx` and the Chromium smoke case "a Distance pick asked for midpoints records each edge midpoint" in `test/e2e/measure.spec.ts`. Measure workbench redesign: the six up-front modes give way to a pick session. One pick reads as itself; two picks read every way they relate (parallel, central, distance, maximum, angle), the most specific first and each figure once, and Keep (or Enter) records the reading shown. Units, precision, Ø/R and the Distance point kind sit behind one settings menu (`apps/web/src/lib/measureReadings.ts`, `useMeasurementWorkbench.test.tsx`, `MeasurementDock.test.tsx`, `test/e2e/measure.spec.ts`). | Existing measurement workbench (`apps/web/src/lib/measurements.ts`) and provenance rules; S08 notable points; Remus 7.5 for entity-pair and maximum distances (C8); [comparison §2.7](docs/reviews/reference-cad-comparison-2026-09-26.md). |
| <a id="m14"></a>M14 | Partial | Gear-reference recovery: older gear edits with a live hash beside the original face reference resolve by the verified reference; History edits retain the authored hash/reference pair. Synthetic regressions cover repeated top/bottom cap offsets, exact bounds/volume, undo/redo/reopen, invalid lineage/witness refusal, and hash-only fallback; browser coverage imports the mismatched pair, offsets 81→86 mm, re-edits teeth, commits 56 mm and reloads (`gear-stale-reference.test.ts`, `inPlace/edit.test.ts`, `gear-stale-reference.spec.ts`). Gears: involute spur, internal (ring) and rack gears as one parametric feature, either a new body standing on a plane or teeth cut along the whole length of an existing cylindrical face (outside on a boss, inside in a bore). Size by module, circular pitch or diametral pitch (always per inch) with tooth count, pressure angle, profile shift, backlash, bore, rim and phase; keep-diameter or set-module fit on a cylinder; every face named from its kernel role (tip, flank, root, cap) so later fillets and holes survive edits; refusals (pointed tip, too few teeth, cylinder too small, partial span) and undercut advisories reported by the kernel by code. Remaining after the first slice: helical and herringbone teeth, bevel and worm gears, a meshing-pair helper that places a mate at the right center distance, and partial-span teeth. Straight bevel gears (schema v41): `gearType: 'bevel'` with the mate's tooth count and a shaft angle (default 90°), a Bevel choice in the Gear tool and edit from History, built through Remus `makeBevelGearDetailed`. Module, pressure angle, addendum and dedendum are heel values and the face width runs along the pitch cone; the teeth taper from the heel cap toward the cone apex. Flanks are the kernel's certified spherical-involute fits; profile shift, root fillet, helix and teeth on a cylinder are refused on a bevel gear. Evidence: `test/gear-feature.test.ts` (bevel validation and refusals; a 20:40 right-angle m 2 bevel gear's exact axial height `b·cos δ`, heel tip diameter, volume between the root and tip cone frusta less its bore, every face uniquely named; a face width reaching the apex refused as `gear:invalid-parameter`), `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/tools.test.ts` (a bevel request carries its mate and drops spur-only fields) and `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/edit.test.ts` (an untouched bevel edit writes back as stored; returning to an external gear clears the mate and shaft angle). First slice (schema v40): an additive `gear` feature kind with Gear (create) and Gear teeth (on a cylindrical face) in-place tools, edit from History, and the exact build through Remus `makeGearDetailed` / `cutGearTeethDetailed`. Helical external and internal gears, new or cut into a cylinder, take a helix angle (0–45°, normal-section sizing per ISO 21771) and a hand; a spur gear sends no helix fields, so it still builds on a kernel without helical gears, which refuses a helical spec rather than building a spur. Herringbone gears (`herringbone`, a Helix option) reverse the hand at mid-width; each half's faces are named `….start-half` / `….end-half`. Bevel and worm gears, the meshing-pair helper and partial-span teeth remain. Diametral pitch is read per inch in every unit system; tooth faces carry `gear.face.*` lineage from the kernel's roles; refusals surface as `gear:*` kernel refusals and undercut as an advisory. Evidence: `test/gear-feature.test.ts` (sizing conversions, frame, command validation and refusals, 20-tooth m 2 volume between root and tip discs with every face named, 2.2 in tip diameter for 20 teeth at P 10, undercut advisory, pointed-tip refusal, teeth on a Ø22 cylinder equal to the standalone m 1 gear's volume, cylinder-too-small refusal, helix-angle and hand validation, a 20° helical gear's transverse tip diameter, 40 named flanks and equal volumes for both hands, a herringbone gear's 80 half-named flanks and its volume equal to the single helix's, herringbone teeth on a Ø22 boss keeping its diameter with 80 half-named flanks) and `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/edit.test.ts` (untouched edits of a new gear, of teeth on a body, of a helical and of a herringbone gear write back as stored; returning to spur clears the helix and hand, and to a single helix clears `herringbone`). Double-enveloping wheels (schema v45): `gearType: 'enveloping-wheel'`, its `teeth` its own teeth, `mateTeeth` the starts of the worm that drives it, `diameterFactor` and `wormLength` that worm's, `faceWidth` its face and `outsideDiameter` beside the throat; an Enveloping wheel choice in the Gear tool (Teeth, Starts, Diameter factor, Worm length, Outside diameter) and edit from History. Built through Remus `makeEnvelopingWormWheelDetailed`: the Hindley straight-sided tooth exactly in the mid-plane and, off it, straight sections fitted inside the tooth the worm would hob (sampled, not proven, and disclosed as approximate), creased along the mid-plane line; tips on the throat torus and the outside cylinder. Profile shift, root fillet, helix angle, a throat option and teeth on a cylinder are refused, and a worm length on any other gear. Evidence: `test/gear-feature.test.ts` (validation and thirteen refusals; an m 2, 30-tooth wheel for a single-start q 10, 24 long worm whose volume lies between its root and outside cylinders, every face uniquely named with both flank halves; a throat wider than the face refused as `gear:invalid-parameter`), `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/tools.test.ts` (the request carries the teeth, the worm's starts as its mate, the worm length and outside diameter, without spur-only or throat fields; the count chip is its teeth), `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/edit.test.ts` and `apps/web/src/lib/featureSummary.test.ts`. Double-enveloping worms (schema v44): `gearType: 'enveloping-worm'`, its `teeth` the thread starts, `mateTeeth` the teeth of the wheel it wraps, `diameterFactor` its throat pitch diameter over the module and `faceWidth` its length; an Enveloping worm choice in the Gear tool (Starts, Teeth for the wheel, Diameter factor) and edit from History. Built through Remus `makeEnvelopingWormDetailed`: the Hindley worm whose thread is the surface the wheel's straight-sided teeth sweep, with exact torus lands and root floors and certified ruled flank patches. The wheel it envelops is not built. Profile shift, root fillet, helix angle and teeth on a cylinder are refused. Evidence: `test/gear-feature.test.ts` (validation and eleven refusals; an m 2, q 10 single-start worm for a 30-tooth wheel whose caps span its end tip diameter, volume between its throat root and end tip cylinders, every face uniquely named; a bore reaching the throat root refused as `gear:bore-too-large`), `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/tools.test.ts` (the request carries the starts as teeth and the wheel's teeth as its mate, without spur-only or throat fields; the count chip is its starts), `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/edit.test.ts` and `apps/web/src/lib/featureSummary.test.ts`. Teeth cut along a cylinder leave each end cap as one face: Remus `cutGearTeethDetailed` returns the blank's disc and the toothed ring as two coplanar faces, which the adapter now unifies in place (`unifyFacesChecked`) and names `gear.face.cap.start\|end`. Dragging a gear cap edits the source cylinder's height, which re-cuts the teeth, instead of a local face move the kernel refuses (it cannot extend the NURBS involute flanks). The gear stays opaque to cylinder radius drags. Evidence: `test/gear-cap-height-drag.test.ts` (one named cap per end whose area is the prism's cross-section; top and bottom cap drags plan as cylinder height edits and rebuild with 40 named flanks and the opposite cap fixed; no radius ancestor through the gear). Gear parameter panel (shared by every in-place command): the bar's Options is a compact parameter sheet whose values are typed in place (numbers or expressions, arrow keys and label scrubbing) in their own units, so pressure, helix, phase and shaft angles read in degrees, profile shift in modules and diametral pitch per inch rather than in the document's length unit; a gear's rows show only where they apply (hand and herringbone once a helix angle is set, mate and shaft angle for a bevel, starts and diameter factor for a worm, rim for a ring, no sizing while teeth keep the cylinder's diameter), the tooth form folds away, and a summary gives the resulting module and pitch diameter. Changing the sizing keeps the tooth size (module 2 becomes 12.7 /in), the pitch chip edits the pitch the sizing reads, and the default diametral pitch matches the default module. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/panel.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/InPlaceCommandBar.test.tsx` and `test/e2e/gear.spec.ts` (Gear parameters). Standalone external and internal gear cap edits update the authored face width instead of requesting a local extension of the involute flanks. The near cap also shifts the gear's stored origin so the opposite end stays fixed. Routing requires a current named cap owned by that gear; teeth on a cylinder, bevel/worm gears, stale picks and locally edited results keep their existing routes. Expressions are retained and nonpositive widths are refused before rebuilding. `test/standalone-gear-height-drag.test.ts` covers 40 to 25 mm, unchanged sibling/topology/volume, undo/redo, reopening, STEP round-trip and the near cap of an X-axis gear. `faceOffsetPlan.test.ts` covers expressions, refusals and ancestry boundaries. `standalone-gear-height.spec.ts` exercises the actual Total keypad and checks the exported project still contains one gear feature with a 25 mm face width. Throated worm wheels (schema v43): `throated: true` on a worm wheel, with an optional `outsideDiameter`; a Throat choice (Plain or Throated) and an Outside diameter value under Options in the Gear tool, and edit from History. Built through Remus `makeWormWheelDetailed` with `throated`: across the throat, half way across the face, each tooth's tip is an exact torus patch about the worm's axis at its root clearance, and either side it stands at the outside diameter (kernel default: the throat diameter plus one module). The flanks stay the crossed helical wheel's, so contact is still at points. Throated is refused off a worm wheel, and an outside diameter without it. Evidence: `test/gear-feature.test.ts` (throated validation and refusals; an m 2, q 10, 30-tooth throated wheel at outside diameter 65 builds without warnings with its 65 mm outside diameter, volume bounds, `tooth[k].throat` and tip half faces all uniquely named, a plain wheel without throat faces; an outside diameter below the tips refused by the kernel), `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/tools.test.ts` (a throated wheel sends its throat and outside diameter, 0 leaves the default, a plain wheel or a worm sends neither), `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/edit.test.ts` (an untouched throated edit writes back as stored; making it plain clears both fields) and `apps/web/src/lib/featureSummary.test.ts`. Worms and worm wheels (schema v42): `gearType: 'worm'` (its `teeth` are the thread starts, its face width the length) and `gearType: 'worm-wheel'` (the worm's starts as `mateTeeth`), both with the worm's `diameterFactor`; Worm and Worm wheel choices in the Gear tool with Starts and Diameter factor under Options, and edit from History. Built through Remus `makeWormDetailed` (a cylindrical involute ZI worm, certified involute-helicoid flank fits) and `makeWormWheelDetailed` (the helical wheel of the worm's hand at helix angle equal to the lead angle, transverse module the worm's axial module). The pair is a crossed helical drive with point contact, not a hobbed throated wheel. Lead angles over 45° and profile shift, helix angle, herringbone and teeth on a cylinder are refused.
