Evidence: `test/gear-feature.test.ts` (worm and wheel validation and refusals; a single-start m 2, q 10 worm's exact length, tip diameter and a volume between two and three fifths of its thread annulus over the core, every face uniquely named; its wheel's tip diameter and volume bounds; a bore reaching the worm's root refused as `gear:bore-too-large`), `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/tools.test.ts` (a worm sends its starts as teeth, a wheel as its mate, without spur-only fields; the worm's count chip is its starts) and `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/edit.test.ts` (untouched worm and wheel edits write back as stored; returning to an external gear clears the worm fields). Remaining, as of 2026-11-12: the meshing-pair helper that places a mate at the right center distance, and partial-span teeth. This replaces the earlier "Bevel and worm gears, the meshing-pair helper and partial-span teeth remain" and "Remaining after the first slice" lists above: straight bevel gears (schema v41), worms and worm wheels (v42), throated worm wheels (v43) and double-enveloping worms and wheels (v44, v45) have merged, with the evidence recorded above. Gear edge names (after partial-span teeth, PR #1389; no schema change, no kernel change): every gear body now names its edges as well as its faces, so a fillet or chamfer on a gear edge follows later gear edits instead of being pinned to an edge hash. An edge is named from the two faces it lies between, `gear.edge.between.<face>\|<face>` with the two face names in string order (`gear.edge.between.gear.face.cap.end\|gear.face.collar.end`, `…gear.face.bore\|gear.face.cap.start`, `…gear.face.collar.start\|gear.face.shoulder.start`), the pair read from the built solid's own topology and never from position or output order; it applies to new spur, helical, herringbone, bevel, worm and enveloping gears and to teeth cut on a body, whose carried faces count by their carried names (`packages/kernel-adapter/src/exact-gear-edges.ts`, [ADR-013](docs/adrs/ADR-013-persistent-topology-lineage.md) M14 amendment). Tooth edges are named the same way rather than left out, so they follow the tooth index of their faces (`…tooth[3].flank.plus\|…tooth[3].tip` is tooth 3's edge at any tooth count that has one): the rule is one rule and costs nothing extra. Where one pair of faces shares several edges each is `….piece.<i>.of.<n>`, ordered along the gear's axis, the count part of the name; pieces at one height publish no name, because around the axis no origin survives every edit (the x direction is a parameter and the blank can be turned), so an angular count could hand one piece's name to its neighbour. The one case found is a collar's seam cutting the smooth arc it shares with a flush keep-diameter tooth tip in two (four unnamed half-arcs on a two-collar gear, edges no blend applies to). Edges with an unsupported witness and seams publish no name either. Face hashes and edge hashes are unchanged everywhere, and face names are unchanged but for the one rename below: compared against main for a bored spur, helical, herringbone, bevel, worm and worm-wheel gear and for whole-face and partial teeth of both fits and anchors on a boss, every face name, face hash and edge hash is identical; only edge references are added (482 of a bored 20-tooth spur's 483 edges, the other its bore seam). A neighbouring face is no longer taken for a cap (the one face rename): partial-span teeth named any plane at the picked face's end `gear.face.cap.start\|end`, including a face the cylinder runs into (a flange it ends on), which whole-face teeth leave under the name the body's lineage carries, so a datum on that face, and a fillet on its untouched outer rim, lost their reference when the teeth went from the whole face to part of it. Now only the picked face's own cap, the plane that closes the cylinder and looks away from it along the axis, takes `gear.face.cap.*` (and `.body` where teeth reach it); a plane that looks back along the face keeps its carried name at every span, and teeth that run up to it end at an ordinary `gear.face.shoulder.*` in its plane, the neighbour beside it keeping its name by its exact unchanged witness (the only named face of the body on that plane, exactly one result face with that witness), the name the carrier rule gives the unified face under whole-face teeth. On a Ø22 shaft with a Ø26 flange, for teeth below or above the flange at five spans each, face and edge hashes are identical to before and the only names that change are that flange face (to its carried name), the shoulder beside it and the edges named from them; on a plain boss nothing changes. A document saved since #1389 with a reference to such a neighbour under the name `gear.face.cap.*` refuses by name and needs a re-pick. Resolution is unchanged and stays strict: a stored edge name the body no longer publishes refuses as `reference_missing`, never rebinding (teeth run up to an end remove its collar and that rim; fewer teeth remove the higher tooth indices), and a hash-only edge pick stored on a gear before this takes the existing all-or-nothing upgrade to its name on the next build. Evidence: `packages/kernel-adapter/src/exact-gear-edges.test.ts` (CI unit smoke selection: pair naming whatever order faces and edges are listed in; an index only for several edges of one pair, counted along the axis from either end; no name for pieces at one height, without a frame, beside an unnamed or twice-named face, for seams or for a closed spline), `test/gear-edge-lineage.test.ts` (smoke selection, exact kernel: every pickable edge of a bored spur, of whole-face teeth and of set-module partial teeth named once, each name equal to the pair of faces the kernel's adjacency reports; the same names and hashes on a second kernel instance and after a span edit and back; keep-diameter partial teeth named but for the four smooth seam-cut half-arcs; a fillet and a chamfer on the top cap's rim, teeth anchored at the start and at the end, each still taking exactly the analytic volume off the Ø22 rim after a length, an offset, a length-and-offset, a tooth-count (20 → 18), a module (0.9 → 0.8) and a blank-height (12 → 14, the blend following to z 14) edit; a fillet stored by hash alone resolving, being offered its name, and with it following an edit that loses the hash-only one; the rim's fillet refusing by `reference_missing` with the gear passed through unrounded once teeth reach that end or run the whole face, and resolving again when the collar returns), `test/gear-neighbour-face-lineage.test.ts` (smoke selection, exact kernel, each case failing before the cap rule: on the flanged shaft with set-module teeth below the flange, through part of the face, the whole face, up to the flange, set off from it, from the free end and the whole face again, the flange's underside, top and wall keep their carried names, no `gear.face.cap.end` appears, the rim keeps its name and hash, teeth up to the flange end at `gear.face.shoulder.end` beside the flange face left at its full annulus, and the whole-face body returns with identical names and hashes; a fillet and a chamfer on the flange's outer rim each still take exactly the analytic volume off the Ø26 rim at every one of those spans; a datum plane on the flange face stays at z 14 for teeth below it and z 18 for teeth above it through whole, part, up to the flange and whole) and `test/gear-edge-lineage-extended.test.ts` (full suite: a fillet on the shoulder's rim, `collar.start\|shoulder.start`, following length, offset, tooth-count and height edits at both anchors with the blend at the shoulder's new height; a fillet at one tooth space of a keep-diameter shoulder, `root[3]\|shoulder.end[3]`, through a span and a tooth-count edit at both anchors; a fillet and a chamfer on a whole-face gear's bore rim through tooth-count, module, face-width (its cap drag) and bore-diameter edits; a tooth-tip edge found by its tooth from whole-face teeth to part of the face and back, refused by `reference_missing` at 18 teeth and found again at 22). Kernel limits, not naming ones, recorded as found: it refuses a chamfer on a shoulder's rim and at a split shoulder's tooth space ("could not be chamfered on this body"), refuses both blends on a cap's edge at one tooth and on a narrow (1.1 wide) shoulder's rim, and builds a tooth-tip fillet on some teeth and spans only, so those cases are fillets and the tooth-tip case asserts that the edge is found (built, or the kernel's own geometric refusal, never a lost reference). M14 stays Partial. Both listed items (the meshing-pair helper, PR #1373, and partial-span teeth, PR #1389) have landed and gear edges are named, but "later fillets and holes survive edits" does not yet hold for every reference: (1) the body's part of the picked face's own split end cap, `gear.face.cap.start\|end.body`, exists only while partial teeth reach that end, so a reference to it, and to the one edge beside it (its seam with the toothed ring), refuses by name once the caps rejoin (teeth along the whole face, or stopping short of that end); following it into the rejoined cap needs rejoin provenance, unchanged from #1389; (2) where partial teeth run up to a neighbouring face, the edges at which the teeth meet that plane are named from the shoulder there, and under whole-face teeth, which unify the shoulder into the neighbour, from the neighbour, so a reference to one of those edges does not cross between the whole face and teeth that reach the neighbour (the neighbour face and its other edges do); (3) the smooth half-arcs a collar seam cuts, and seams, carry no name, so a reference to one stays hash-only. The row can close when (1) and (2) are followed or the owner narrows the criterion. Meshing-pair helper (no schema change): Place gears in Assemble's Joints section (shown once two parts are gears) opens its placement card, which takes the gear that stays put and the one that meshes with it, shows the working centre distance, working pressure angle, calculated turns per base turn (rack: calculated travel per turn) and backlash, and Place saves an ordinary rigid joint that holds the meshed pose (a floating base is fixed first, as Set up motion does). Covers external spur and helical pairs (equal module, pressure angle and helix angle, opposite hands), herringbone pairs (opposite stored hands facing the same way, the same stored hand when one is turned end for end, since it then meets its mate with its other half; a ring pair the reverse), a pinion inside an internal gear (same hand) and a spur pinion on a rack. Placements are read the way the kernel builds them from the same values: the axis as a direction and the stored x direction with its part along the axis removed, never a substituted world direction. A gear whose x direction's remainder off the axis is under 1e-6 of its length, where rounding alone decides the tooth phase, is refused as `gear-mesh:invalid-placement` (re-pick its reference direction), even where the kernel would build it. The centre distance is the working one with profile shift (ISO 21771 `inv α_wt = inv α_t + 2 tan α_n Σx / Σz`, internal pairs by `x₂ − x₁` over `z₂ − z₁`; a pinion stands `r + x·m` off the rack's pitch line); backlash is the gears' own tooth thinning, so the centres stay at the zero-backlash distance and the play is split either side of each tooth. The tooth phase follows the kernel's tooth placement (tooth 0 on the x direction turned by phase, helical teeth turning `h·tan β / r` by height, rack teeth at `(k + ½)·p`) with mid-face planes level. Refused by code (`gear-mesh:*`): bevel and worm pairs (intersecting or crossed axes, not placed yet), two racks, two internal gears, a rack with an internal or helical gear, mismatched module, pressure angle or helix angle, same-hand external or opposite-hand internal helical pairs, herringbone with single helix, a ring with no more teeth than its pinion, shifts that leave no working pressure angle, tips that would reach past the mate's root circle at the working distance (`gear-mesh:tip-clearance`, from the addendum, dedendum and shift, its overlap shown in the document's length unit; the kernel does not shorten tips; involute/trochoid interference below the base circle is not checked), teeth cut into a cylinder, a gear moved after it was made, and parts that are not gears. Gear-ratio coupling between joints and partial-span teeth remain. Evidence: `test/gear-mesh.test.ts` (in the CI unit smoke selection; exact centre distances and working pressure angles for standard, shifted, helical, internal and rack pairs; phase interleaving at arbitrary phases, hands and orientations; herringbone and single-helix hand rules with the axis turned end for end; tip-clearance refusals for external, internal and rack pairs; every refusal code; document resolution in inch units and the moved-gear refusal; the saved joint landing the wheel at the helper's frame (also with a non-unit stored axis) and holding it fixed against attempted independent rotation; tip and root circles equal to the kernel's reported diameters; placement frames following the kernel's projection, an x direction stored 1e-5 off the axis phased against the kernel's own teeth, and an ill-conditioned tilted placement the kernel builds refused on the reader, pair and joint paths; a rack pair on the kernel), `test/gear-mesh-kernel.test.ts` (full suite: on the exact kernel, grids of points across the pitch point showing spur, helical, internal and end-for-end single-helix and herringbone pairs (both halves sampled) built where the helper puts them never overlap, while a turn past the play, half a pitch or the wrong hand does), `apps/web/src/components/assembly/GearMeshCard.test.tsx` (smoke selection; refusals shown with the document unit) and `test/e2e/gear-mesh.spec.ts` (Place gears opens the card at 60 mm with 0.2 mm play, Place fixes the pinion and adds Gear placement, one undo removes it). Approved placement-only slice (PR #1373): the pair is rigid, with no motion values or limits. The card explicitly says linked rotation is unavailable; ratio/travel readouts are calculated values. Place gears lives in Joints, and selecting exactly two gear parts offers Place selected gears with that pair preselected (first selected as base unless one is fixed). Ctrl/Command click adds or removes parts in the list or viewport without starting a drag; all selected parts remain highlighted, and only the active part opens its details. Selection is transient, with no schema change. Gear-ratio coupling remains deferred. Regression coverage includes rigid solver overrides, selected/non-gear/third-part/deselection cases, viewport modifiers, and both Joints-entry and selected-pair browser journeys. Queue integration with U11: the combined entry bundle reproduced the required Cloudflare build failure at 1,050,503 bytes against the unchanged 1,048,576-byte budget. The assembly sidebar is now its own chunk. Its module is imported when the pointer or keyboard focus reaches the Assemble switch, or on entry (in production the existing idle prefetcher may already have fetched the file in the background, as it does for every dynamic chunk); until it arrives a sidebar-shaped Assembly panel says it is loading (the workspace's one-time reveal does not cover a load after the project opened), and a chunk that cannot load reports through the stale-chunk notice. An E2E regression holds the chunk after Build is visible, sees the load start on hover, the loading panel on entering Assemble, and the sidebar once it arrives (`gear-mesh.spec.ts`; `TopBar.test.tsx`, `AssemblyPanelPending.test.tsx`). With the loading panel and preload, the production build's entry is 1,015,791 bytes (32,785 bytes below the same limit). The gear browser regression, run on the E2E build where background prefetch is off, verifies no sidebar request in Build, one when opening Assemble, and no second request after returning through Build. Gear placement, selection, BOM and motion behavior are preserved; the exact geometry and joint code are unchanged by this load-boundary fix. Partial-span teeth (schema v55): "partial span" means teeth cut along part of the cylinder's length, the axial band the old `partial span` refusal turned away; sector gears (teeth over part of the circumference) are out of scope. Teeth on a body take an optional `faceWidth` (their length; absent runs from the offset to the face's far end), `offset` and `anchor` (`start`, the face's lower end along its axis, or `end`), which the adapter turns into Remus `cutGearTeethDetailed`'s axial `offset` and `faceWidth`; the cylinder either side stays as collars. A whole-face gear stores none of them, so it still builds as before and older documents need no migration; an older client would cut a partial span along the whole face, so v55 refuses there. Every face is named: the kernel's tooth roles, `gear.face.collar.start\|end` for what is left of the picked face beside the teeth (only coaxial faces on its axis line and within its length, so a same-diameter step elsewhere on the shaft is never taken for a collar; always by role, even where one collar could carry the face's old name, so an offset or length edit that makes one collar two or two one keeps the names and references to them), `gear.face.shoulder.start\|end` at the teeth's ends (`shoulder.start[k]` per tooth space, by the root it borders, where keep-diameter tips stand on the collar and split the shoulder), and `gear.face.cap.start\|end` for the picked face's cap at either end, as the whole-face cut names it, so a reference to a cap holds as the teeth go from the whole face to part of it and back: at an end the teeth stop short of, the plane there that bounds the collar; at an end they reach, the cap is split, the gear's toothed ring keeps `gear.face.cap.*` and the body's part of it, inside (a boss) or outside (a bore) the ring, is `gear.face.cap.*.body`. Known limitation: a reference to the split-off part (`gear.face.cap.*.body`) does not survive the caps rejoining (teeth back along the whole face, or stopping short of that end). It refuses by name, "The selected part of the gear's end cap no longer exists. Re-pick the face.", and never resolves to the ring or the rejoined cap; a later feature that trims or splits that part refuses the same way. Following it into the rejoined cap needs rejoin provenance, a candidate for the gear edge and face naming follow-up. Other faces the body's lineage already names keep that name. Fillets and chamfers on a gear result's edges are pinned to edge hashes: gear results publish face lineage only, no edge lineage (true of every M14 gear body, not just partial spans), so an edge modifier on a shoulder or collar does not follow a span edit; naming gear edges from their two adjacent face names is a separate app-side follow-up PR, which the owner chose over a Remus change. Partial bands skip cap unification: the kernel's whole-solid unify would also merge each tip land into its collar, which it reverts or leaves unmeasurable, so such an end cap stays two named faces. Teeth past the face refuse as `gear:span-outside-face`; a zero length, a negative offset or an unknown anchor are command refusals, and an offset or anchor on a new gear is refused. A cap drag on partial teeth is no longer read as the source cylinder's height, which would slide the stored span. The Gear teeth tool picks its end from the click, as the Thread does, and shares the Thread's length arrow, Offset row and their refusals (Length and Offset under Options, in the document's length unit); History edits round-trip the span and returning to the whole face clears it. Evidence: `test/gear-feature.test.ts` (command validation and refusals; on the exact kernel, 6 of a Ø22 boss 12 long at offset 3 equal to six of the standalone m 1 gear plus two Ø22 × 3 collars, every face uniquely named, 20 shoulder pieces per end at z 3 and 9 and named collars; an end-anchored band at 5..11; a band from the bottom naming `cap.start` and `cap.start.body` with volume 8 of the gear plus a 4 long collar; a set module with one shoulder per end and the collars at Ø22; `gear:span-outside-face`; a datum axis on the set-module collar still resolving as the offset goes 0 → 2 → 0, the collar keeping `gear.face.collar.end`; on a Ø22 shaft with a Ø26 flange, teeth at 3..9 name only the collars 0..3 and 9..14, leaving the Ø22 above the flange as it was), `test/gear-cap-height-drag.test.ts` (a partial gear's cap drag is not a cylinder height edit; datum planes on `gear.face.cap.start` and `gear.face.cap.end` of a set-module gear still resolve at z 0 and 12 through whole → 6 from the start → whole → 6 from the end → whole → 6 at offset 3 → whole, the teeth reaching either end or neither; a datum started on `gear.face.cap.start.body` or `gear.face.cap.end.body` refuses with the re-pick wording once the caps rejoin or the teeth stop short of that end, and resolves again whenever the split returns or persists (a shorter span still reaching that end), the end case keeping its anchor throughout), `packages/kernel-adapter/src/topology-lineage.test.ts` (a missing `.body`, `.body[k]` or boolean-carried `.body` beside a ring named `gear.face.cap.*` refuses under available lineage, also beside an unnamed face with its exact hash; under partial lineage the ring alone is still refused and the existing exact-hash fallback takes an unnamed exact-hash match, unchanged from main), `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/tools.test.ts` (whole face stores no span; dragged short and offset from the top stores `faceWidth`, `offset` and `anchor: 'end'`; the arrow on the free end; length and offset refusals; an offset carrying the length), `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/edit.test.ts` (untouched round trip; whole face clears all three) and `test/e2e/gear.spec.ts` (pick near the top, Length 6 and Offset 2, Done, stored span, History edit unchanged). With the meshing-pair helper (PR #1373) both listed M14 items have landed; M14 stayed Partial with that PR because later fillets and chamfers on gear edges did not yet survive edits (gear edges carried no names); the gear edge-naming follow-up names them. | Remus B262 (`makeGearDetailed`, `cutGearTeethDetailed` with face roles) in the pinned kernel; K05 face lineage. |
| <a id="m15"></a>M15 | Complete | Modeled screw threads on a cylindrical boss or bore, sized and shaped for 3D printing: ISO metric, a printable 45° profile and custom trapezoids, fitted to the face (ISO 261 size and pitch; a bore takes the size its tap drill fits), a printed-fit clearance, a crest the face truncates, multi-start and left-hand threads, partial spans, print advisories (overhang past 45°, crests under a line, a layer-deep thread), cone-chamfered ends at either or both ends of the face, and History edits. Kernel: `cutThreadDetailed` (esaueng/remus#1480, chamfers esaueng/remus#1483). Not yet: tapered (pipe) threads, chamfers on a thread that stops short of the face's end, cosmetic threads and drawing callouts (M01). Thread feature (`featureKind: 'thread'`, schema v53) and in-place Thread tool: closed-form outline-area volume of an ISO M6 boss on the exact kernel, typed `thread:<reason>` refusals, a named refusal on a kernel without the thread generator, size and fit resolution, truncation, print advisories, chamfered ends (the kernel's end spec, and on a chamfer-capable kernel both cones taking 1.5–2.5 mm³ off an M6) and command validation (`test/thread-feature.test.ts`); request, summary and History round-trip (`apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/thread.test.ts`). A thread that stops short of its face keeps its cone chamfer at the end of the face it starts from and stops plain: the in-place Thread tool stores `chamferStart` or `chamferEnd` for each end the span reaches instead of dropping both, and reads "plain where it stops". Which ends a span reaches is read from its resolved offset and length against the face, within the kernel's snap, not from how the length is stored: a length held as an expression or stored by an earlier edit keeps both chamfers where it runs the whole face and the far one where an offset span reaches it, from either end and either axis direction, and such a thread reopened from History and finished untouched is written back as stored; a History edit rewrites the flags only where the edited span stops reaching an end (`apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/thread.test.ts`, `edit.test.ts`, `test/e2e/in-place-thread.spec.ts`). On the exact kernel a 5 mm M6 thread on an 8 mm boss matches its closed-form volume, and its one cone, from either end of the face, takes a volume inside the closed-form bounds of a 45° cone through the end plane between the root and a tenth of the depth below it. A chamfer on the end that stops short of the face now refuses before the kernel is asked, as `thread:chamfer-short-of-face` (`unsupported`), where the kernel answered `thread:invalid-parameter` (`test/thread-feature.test.ts`). Still open, both blocked on the kernel (esaueng/remus `thread.rs` at the pinned v2026.1.135 lists them as not covered, and `cutThreadDetailed` rejects a taper field as unknown): a chamfer on the stopped-short end itself, and tapered (pipe) threads. Runouts and tapered pipe threads (schema v57, on Remus with esaueng/remus#1698 and #1701). A thread end can now run out (`runoutStart` / `runoutEnd`, the kernel's `{ kind: 'runout' }` end, sharing the end angle with chamfers): the in-place Ends row chamfers an end the thread reaches and runs out an end where it stops short, and a chamfer on a stopped-short end is still refused by code with copy that points to the runout. New profiles `'npt'` (ASME B1.20.1, 60°, 0.8P deep, 0.038P flats) and `'bspt'` (ISO 7-1 R on a boss / Rc in a bore, 55°, 0.640327P deep, its rounded crests and roots approximated by P/6 flats, which an advisory says) cut a 1:16 taper through the kernel's `taper` spec. `pipeSize` takes 1/8″–2″ from each standard's table (`packages/shared/src/pipe-threads.ts`), or the size the face is nearest. The fitting end is the end the thread starts at: a boss tapers small there and is gauged L1 (NPT) or the gauge length (R) in, and a bore opens large there and is gauged on its face (NPT) or half a pitch behind it (Rc), all measured from the fitting face, so an offset moves the band and never the gauge plane. The length is the standard's (NPT L2, BSPT minimum useful thread), within the face and stopping where the crest meets the cylinder, with an advisory whenever the band ends there short of the standard (a stored default length included); an offset that leaves no length is refused in plain language. Printed-fit clearance starts at Exact for pipe threads. Parallel Rp/NPS are not covered. Plain-language copy for `thread:ends-overlap` and `thread:taper-out-of-range` (and for the taper's own `cylinder-too-small`, `bore-too-large` and `too-deep`). The build refuses a kernel that does not report the runout (`details.geometry.start\|end.kind`) or the taper (`taperAngle`) it was asked for, and names a thread face from `details.faceRoles` (`thread.face.<role>`) only where a role holds exactly one face (ADR-013). Older documents load unchanged; a runout or pipe thread in a document that declares v56 or older is refused, on opening (saved partial patches included) and on writing. The panel settles each finished end by the build's own placement and snap (`threadSpanOnFace`, shared with the adapter): from the face length the kernel measured: a stored runout stays a runout unless an edit carries that end from short of the face onto its end, where it is chamfered, and every other end is a chamfer where the span reaches the face's end and a runout where it stops short, so it never writes a chamfer the build refuses. Evidence: `test/thread-pipe-runout.test.ts` (NPT and BSPT tables checked against their standards' relations; NPT 1/2 boss and bore, R and Rc 1/2 gauge planes, inch units and clearance; runout end specs; v56 documents opening unchanged and v57 content refused under v56; on the exact kernel a runout on a partial M6, the overlap refusal and 1/4-18 NPT on its pipe), `test/thread-feature.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/thread.test.ts` and `test/e2e/in-place-thread.spec.ts` (runouts stored where a dragged thread stops short). Complete (owner decision, 2026-11-14). Tapered pipe threads are delivered: NPT and BSPT R/Rc, 1/8″–2″ (esaueng/remus#1701, PR #1458). BSPT's rounded crests and roots are approximated by flats. By owner decision, a runout at a stopped-short end satisfies M15's stopped-short-end requirement (esaueng/remus#1698, schema v57, PR #1458); a chamfer at that end remains unsupported and is refused as `thread:chamfer-short-of-face`. OpenZCAD pins the Remus build that has both (PR #1468, Remus 42389c8). This completion supersedes the row's "Not yet" list and the earlier "Still open" evidence. Cosmetic threads and drawing thread callouts remain open under M01. Parallel pipe threads (Rp/NPS) are out of scope. |  |
| <a id="m16"></a>M16 | Partial | Sheet metal, flat first: turn a sketched blank into a sheet of a material and thickness, pick bend lines on it and set each bend (angle, up/down, inside radius, K-factor or DIN 6935, where the bend sits on the line, relief) in one bend table, see the folded part with a fold preview, and hand the shop an exact flat pattern (layered DXF with up/down bend lines and notes) and a bend table. One `sheet-metal` feature owns the blank, the rule and every bend in flat coordinates, so the flat pattern is the source of truth and no unfold is needed; the exact build folds composed kernel pieces (regions moved rigidly, bend zones revolved) with named refusals for zones crossing holes or angled edges, overlapping bends, flanges still attached beside a bend and closed bend loops, and rule-of-thumb advisories (radius below the material minimum, short flanges, holes near bends, narrow relief). Slices: (1) spec, pure bend arithmetic, fold kinematics and material/gauge presets; (2) schema, feature, commands and exact build; (3) Sheet metal and Bend tools; (4) flat-pattern view, layered DXF and bend table. Later: 3D-first flanges and unfold, hems at zero radius, jogs, bend sequencing and brake collision checks. [Specification](docs/plans/sheet-metal-plan.md). Slice 4 (no schema change): the flat-pattern view, layered DXF and bend table. Pure writers in `packages/kernel-adapter/src/sheet-metal-flat-pattern.ts` (leaf subpath `@openzcad/kernel-adapter/sheet-metal-flat-pattern`) turn the adapter's flat pattern into a DXF R12 in the document's units (`$INSUNITS`) with a layer table: `0` the cut outline and holes as the face-outline export writes them (exact ARC and CIRCLE), `BEND_UP` (blue) and `BEND_DOWN` (red) dashed bend centre lines, `BEND_NOTES` one TEXT per bend line such as `UP 90° R2`, centred beside its line and turned along it; and an SVG of the same drawing at 1:1 (up dashed blue, down dash-dot red). The geometry worker answers a `sheet-metal-flat-pattern` request; the lazy `FlatPatternDialog` (from the body's context menu, the Inspector's Flat pattern… on a sheet-metal feature, File › Flat pattern… and the command palette) shows the drawing, the overall flat size and thickness, the bend table (number, direction, angle, inside radius, K, bend allowance, bend deduction with — at 180°, line length; document units, the app's three decimals but at least four significant digits, as the DXF notes write them) and Export DXF, Export SVG and Copy bend table (CSV with a header row), with the plain `changedBy` warning ("2 later features change this body; the flat pattern shows the sheet before them"); the view rebuilds the pattern on every document revision and offers no export until it has, the adapter refuses the flat pattern of a part the build refused (with the build's message), and a requested sheet that is suppressed or rolled back says so instead of opening another. Evidence: `test/sheet-metal-flat-pattern.test.ts` (layer table, the L bracket's 96.5239 × 40 outline on layer 0, one BEND_UP line at x = 48.262 across the width, its BEND_NOTES text, `$INSUNITS` for mm and inch, an up/down Z part on its two layers with SVG groups, a hole kept as a CIRCLE and a hem with no bend deduction, the DXF importing back through `apps/web/src/lib/dxfImport.ts` as the outline, CSV rows, the body-to-sheet lookup through a later hole and its warning), `apps/web/src/components/FlatPatternDialog.test.tsx` (table, size, warning, DXF/SVG export and CSV copy, refusal, feature switch), `test/e2e/sheet-metal-flat-pattern.spec.ts` (imports the bracket, opens File › Flat pattern…, downloads the layered DXF). Remaining: a "Bend table" annotation on drawing sheets — the drawing package has no table annotation kind (only dimensions and notes of at most 200 characters), so it needs a new persisted annotation kind (a schema version), its sheet renderer and the PDF/DXF sheet exports, which is more than this slice; the CSV and the flat-pattern SVG stand in until then. Slice 1 (no schema change, no kernel change): the specification ([docs/plans/sheet-metal-plan.md](docs/plans/sheet-metal-plan.md)) and `packages/shared/src/sheet-metal.ts`: bend allowance, outside/inside setback, bend deduction, DIN 6935 K-factor, bend-zone placement for the four line positions, flat length from outside flanges, fold kinematics (neutral-fibre-preserving zone map, rigid moving-side transform, partial-fold preview radius), bent-zone volume, rule-of-thumb limits, and material and gauge presets. Evidence: `test/sheet-metal-math.test.ts` (hand-computed 90° values; a 50 × 50 outside L bracket from a 96.5239 blank at T 2, R 2, K 0.44 folds its far corners to (50, 50) outside and (48, 50) inside; continuity at both zone edges; neutral-fibre length equal to the bend allowance; down bends, diagonal bend lines and a two-bend U channel; DIN 6935 values and clamps; range refusals; gauge conversions and preset links). Slice 2 (schema v58, up from v57, no kernel change): the `sheet-metal` feature kind with its bend table, `feature.sheet-metal` and `updateSheetMetal` commands with range validation, parameter expressions on every value, a content gate that refuses the feature on a document declaring an older schema, and the exact fold build composed from existing Remus operations (`packages/kernel-adapter/src/exact-sheet-metal.ts`): relief slots, bend zones in the four line positions, a region tree from the fixed point or the largest region, rigid region transforms with revolved bend zones fused pairwise, a result checked against the analytic volume, typed `sheet-metal:*` refusals (zone not clear, bends overlap, not separating, loop, folded overlap, line missing, off-plane or outside), shop advisories, role names for every face and edge, a view-only fold-fraction preview and the flat-pattern API (outline loops, bend centre lines and bend table rows, later features that changed the body). Spike on the pin: `fuseAll` left unmerged contacts in 4 of 58 folds, pairwise `fuse` in none, so no native fold operation was needed; the as-built differences are in [docs/plans/sheet-metal-plan.md](docs/plans/sheet-metal-plan.md). Evidence: `test/sheet-metal-feature.test.ts` (55 cases, six of them review regressions for linked-sketch re-projection and failed solves, obround and exactly decided reliefs, and a fixed point beside a `moving-side` bend: the 96.5239 blank folds to 50 × 50 outside with the analytic volume and every face named once; down, negative-side, U channel, 180°, each line position, fixed point, tab with rectangular and obround relief and its refusal without, holes kept and refused in a zone, a line across a window, loop and overlap refusals, the 135° folded-overlap example, expressions and DIN 6935, advisories, undo, redo and replay, the v58 gate, fold fraction 0 and ½, flat-pattern outline and bend rows). Remaining: the two tools, bend table and fold slider (slice 3); flat-pattern view, layered DXF and bend table export (slice 4). Slice 3 (no schema change): the in-place **Sheet metal** and **Bend** tools. Sheet metal (From sketch, beside Extrude) takes one or more sketch cells as Extrude does and makes the flat sheet with no bends: thickness is a drag handle and chip; side, material, the material's gauge table (a gauge sets the thickness in document units and is recorded only while the thickness still is that gauge), inside radius (follows T until set), K-factor or DIN 6935, and default relief sit under Options, with a readout of the rule and the material's rule-of-thumb minimum radius. Defaults: mild steel, 1.5 mm (16 ga in inch documents), R = T, K 0.44, rectangular relief. Bend (Edges & faces; also what History reopens a sheet-metal feature with) offers the straight lines, construction lines included, of sketches on the blank's plane; a click adds a bend row (90°, up, centre line), a second click or the row's delete removes it; the bend table edits angle, up/down, position, radius, K and relief overrides, with expressions kept as typed; a click on a flat region sets the fixed point; a fold slider (0–100 %) shows the sheet folded that far through the adapter's `sheetMetalFoldPreview` (worker request `sheet-metal-fold`), starting flat; Done commits one `updateSheetMetal`, and refusals and advisories show in the bar. History label "1.5 mm mild steel, 3 bends", History icon, selection filters, palette and keymap reach. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/sheetMetal.test.ts` (requests, gauges, Options rows, untouched edits written back as stored with expressions, row changes, line candidates, fixed-point picks) and `test/e2e/sheet-metal.spec.ts` (blank and line → Sheet metal → Bend → line → Done gives the 50 × 50 L; reopened from History and turned down; undo and redo). Remaining: flat-pattern view, layered DXF and bend table export (slice 4). | Composed Remus extrude/boolean/revolve/transform operations; a native Remus fold only if those are not robust; existing face-outline DXF export; K05 face lineage. |
| <a id="m17"></a>M17 | Complete | Standard parts: parametric ISO and ANSI screws, nuts, washers, threaded inserts, dowel pins and bearings generated from the M15 thread and M14 gear engines rather than imported meshes; inserted into M01 holes with a matched size and length; listed in the AS03 BOM; and drawn with a simplified thread display so many fasteners stay within rendering budgets. Slice 1, catalogue and generators (schema v69, up from v68; no kernel change, Remus 2026.1.141). The `standard-part` feature kind generates a new body from its standard's table (`packages/shared/src/standard-parts.ts`): ISO 4762 / ASME B18.3 socket head cap screws, ISO 4017 / ASME B18.2.1 hex head screws, ISO 10642 / ASME B18.3 countersunk socket screws, ISO 4032 / ASME B18.2.2 and B18.6.3 hex nuts, ISO 7089 / ASME B18.22.1 plain washers, heat-set threaded inserts (typical sizes, labelled so: no standard fixes their outside), ISO 8734 / ASME B18.8.2 dowel pins and ISO 15 / ABMA deep groove ball bearings. Every blank is an exact revolved profile, a chamfered hex prism or a torus-grooved ring; screws, nuts and inserts are threaded by the M15 thread generator through `cutResolvedThread` (ISO 68-1, also the Unified basic profile), and an insert's knurl is herringbone teeth cut by the M14 gear generator (`cutGearTeethOnFace`), keeping its outside diameter. Nothing is imported or meshed. Approximations, labelled in the catalogue: a flat-bottomed hex socket, an ANSI key depth of half the head, dowel chamfers of a quarter radius, and bearing balls of 0.3 (D − d) on the pitch circle in raceways 4% wider; the boundary dimensions are the standards'. A screw is threaded from its tip for the standard's thread length and stops at least two pitches short of its head with a runout. Faces are named by role (`standard-part.face.seat`, `…head.top`, `…tip`, `…bore`, `…outside`) where one face holds it, and thread faces by the thread generator's roles. The Standard part tool (Create group) places a part where a flat face or plane is clicked: a screw, insert or dowel pin goes into the face, a nut, washer or bearing sits on it; Options choose kind, head, system and size; a screw's or dowel pin's length is an arrow along its axis; History reads the designation ("ISO 4762 M6×1 × 20 socket head cap screw") and reopens the part as stored. A content gate refuses the kind on a document that declares v68 or older. Evidence: `test/standard-parts.test.ts` (catalogue consistency across every row; designations; refusals; the v69 gate; command validation, undo, redo, edit and replay; a washer and a dowel pin to their closed-form volumes; an M6 × 20 socket screw to its head and length with its thread inside the major-to-minor band; placement along a downward axis; hex, countersunk and nut dimensions; a knurled, tapped M3 insert; a 608 bearing between its ring and ball bounds; ANSI parts in an inch document; every table's smallest and largest threaded size and every unthreaded row built without a warning), `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/standardPart.test.ts`, and `test/e2e/standard-parts.spec.ts`, which clicks the block's top face, chooses an ISO M6 socket head screw and drags its length arrow from 16 to about 24 mm, then checks the stored part and its History row. Measured on this machine, one threaded part costs one to three seconds of exact rebuild, mostly display tessellation of its helical faces at the 0.02 % chord; the simplified thread display (slice 4) addresses many fasteners. Slice 3, standard parts in the AS03 bill of materials (no schema change). The exact build records each standard part as it placed it (`DerivedState.standardParts`): the length of every part, one per bore of a hole it is in, a hole-matched length included. `deriveAssemblyBom` lists a standard part by its catalogue designation ("ISO 4762 M6×1 × 20 socket head cap screw", "ASME B18.2.2 1/4-20 UNC hex nut"), not its body's name. Parts of one designation share one row whichever feature placed them, and a rigid copy keeps its source's row. A part in a hole counts once per bore, while a scaled or edited part becomes its own part. Parts whose bores came out at different lengths are named "(mixed lengths)". The CSV keeps its columns, and its Part cell carries the designation. Evidence: `test/standard-parts-bom.test.ts` (grouping by designation across features, quantities, CSV, a rigid copy, ANSI parts named in inches, and on the exact kernel one screw per bore of a two-point clearance hole at its matched 20 mm counted twice), and `test/e2e/standard-parts-bom.spec.ts`. That spec places an ISO M6 screw with the Standard part tool by click and length drag in a block that is already an assembly. It adds the screw from Assemble's Not in the assembly list, then checks the BOM row and the CSV name it by its designation at the dragged length. Slice 2, insertion into M01 holes (schema v77, up from v75 (D02 #1650), with v76 claimed by D03 #1651; M17 first claimed v71 and then v74, but clients up to v75 have no `hole`; no kernel change). A standard part may store `hole: { featureId }`, naming an M01 hole earlier in history. It then stands in every bore that hole drills, one solid per bore. Each bore is drilled again, untapped, into the hole's input body and read back as its cylinder. The part sits on the seat (a counterbore's floor, else the entry face; a countersunk head in a countersink sits flush with the entry face) along the bore, and follows the hole when the hole moves. The size is matched (`standardPartForHole`): a screw takes the hole's own ISO or ANSI size, whether clearance or tap drill, and a dowel pin takes the size whose diameter is the hole's. A part with no stored length takes the preferred length matched to its bore (`matchedStandardLengthMm`). Through a clearance hole that is the shortest length that passes the material, a standard nut and two pitches. In a tap drill or blind hole it is the longest length short of the bore's end and of its thread. A dowel pin takes the longest length that fills a blind hole, or the shortest that passes a through hole. A countersunk head's height counts in its length. A hole resized or re-standardized under its part refuses by name ("No ISO dowel pin is 7 mm across."), and so does a part the hole does not take ("Hole 1 takes an ISO M6, not M8."). The hole and its kernel reading share one fit rule (`standardHoleFit`). The dependency graph makes the part a dependent of its hole, so deleting or editing the hole shows it. A content gate refuses `hole` on a document that declares v76 or older. UI: the Standard part tool's Place option offers Into a hole for a screw or dowel pin. A click on a hole's bore sizes the part from the hole and stands its length arrow at the matched length; dragging the arrow stores a length of its own. Evidence: `test/standard-parts-holes.test.ts` (matching rules for clearance, tapped, blind and through holes, dowel pins and countersunk heads; the v77 gate and refusals; on the exact kernel, an M6 screw at its matched 20 mm in a clearance hole, to the volume of the same screw placed by hand; a counterbore seat; a blind tapped hole's 10 mm and the screw following the hole when it moves; one screw in each of a hole's two sketch-point bores, at twice one's volume; a dowel pin filling a blind hole and refusing when the hole is resized), `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/standardPart.test.ts`, and `test/e2e/standard-parts-holes.spec.ts`. That spec drills an ISO M6 clearance hole with the Hole tool, picks its bore with Standard part › Into a hole, checks the matched 30 mm arrow (20 through, a nut and two pitches), drags it longer, and checks the stored part, its hole and its History row. Slice 4 is the simplified thread display (schema v83, up from v81, with v82 claimed by D02 #1662). A screw, nut or threaded insert may store `thread: 'simplified'`, and the Standard part tool's Thread option makes Simplified the default. A simplified part is its plain envelope: the head's form, every size and its length are the modeled part's, but no thread is cut and there is no drive recess, chamfer or knurl. The uncut face is named `standard-part.face.thread`. A socket screw is then one revolve of 5 faces and 6 edges, where the modeled screw has about 465,600 display triangles. It refuses the lengths a modeled screw refuses, and switching back to Modeled clears the field. A standard part on a plane reads no other body, so the history cache's primitive reuse now restores it, derived lengths included, across an unrelated edit. A part in a hole is still rebuilt. Measured on the Active 1 machine with 100 ISO M6 × 20 screws on a quiet host ([record](docs/performance/2026-11-16-m17-fasteners.md)): - **W01**, editing a box before them: Apply → frame **142.7 ms median and 163.5 ms p95**, against the 150/170 ms budget for a 100-feature history. 100 boxes measure 130.4/150.6 ms on the same build. - **H02**, the first edit after a settled reload: **164 ms**, against 400/850 ms. Reload → exact ready is 1.36 s, against 0.69 s for the boxes. Modeled, ten screws are 4.66 M triangles and 17 s to build cold. Evidence: - `test/standard-parts-threads.test.ts`: - the v83 gate, refusals, and the clear; - the socket, hex and countersunk envelopes and the nut to their closed-form volumes; - matching refusals, and fewer triangles than modeled; - reuse through an unrelated edit with derived lengths kept, and a rebuild when a parameter the parts read changes. - `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/standardPart.test.ts`, covering the option, storage and reopen. - The probes `test/perf/standard-parts-display.bench.ts` and `test/e2e/perf-cad-edit.spec.ts` (`CAD_PERF_BROWSER_FASTENERS`). - `test/e2e/standard-parts-threads.spec.ts`. It picks a block's top face with Standard part, chooses an ISO M6 socket screw with Thread: Simplified, drags its length arrow, and checks the stored `thread: 'simplified'`. It also checks that the viewport draws the screw in under 10,000 triangles. `test/e2e/standard-parts.spec.ts` now chooses Modeled, so the cut thread keeps its own pick-and-drag spec. With this slice M17's contract is met on main: ISO and ANSI screws, nuts, washers, threaded inserts, dowel pins and bearings generated from the M15 thread and M14 gear engines (#1623), inserted into M01 holes with matched size and length (#1644), listed in the AS03 BOM by designation (#1660), and drawn simplified within the H02/W01 budgets (#1666). | M01 hole standards and positions; M15; AS01 instances; W01 budgets for many threaded instances. |
| <a id="m18"></a>M18 | Deferred | Surface features: surface bodies with boundary, fill, ruled, offset, extend, trim, knit-to-solid and thicken, G1/G2 continuity options, and curvature and zebra checks reused from M09. Select only after K10 runs clean; it needs the deepest kernel work in the ledger. | K09 sheet/wire bodies; K10 qualification; M09 overlays. Split from L05 on 2026-11-15. |
| <a id="i01"></a>I01 | Partial | Project model edges/silhouettes into sketches, then add spline/ellipse/slot/point entities as individual slices; add offsetting a body edge or edge chain into the active sketch; a projection carries a linked/unlinked choice; preserve source linkage and explicit broken-reference behavior. Expose the projection source and linked/independent role through U06. Repair after an upstream edit/deletion must use verified source identity and an explicit re-pick; preserve the last valid sketch until the replacement passes validation. Delivered: ellipse, elliptical arc, fit spline and control-point spline entities (research-audit Stage 2d, ZC-004/005/010; schema v27). Additive `ellipse` (centre, two radii, rotation, optional parameter range) and `spline` (fit or control method, degree 2/3, points, closed) sketch objects; stored documents are never rewritten. One geometry module derives every curve: a chord-length global interpolation (periodic when closed) and clamped uniform control splines, normalized to exact clamped B-splines with knot insertion for pieces, reversal and Bézier spans. Region analysis splits them against every kind (Newton-refined crossings plus endpoint projection; a tangential touch is not found) with exact Green areas, direction-canonical fingerprints and a self-intersection refusal; regions carry new `ellipse`/`spline` curves. The exact builder lifts true ellipse edges and the computed NURBS through `liftCurve2dToPlane`, cutting a clockwise full ellipse into quarters and a closed spline into halves because the pinned extrude chord-splits any other closed single edge into facets; the OCCT parity reference cuts the same pieces. Viewport: one curve rail slot (O ellipse, S spline, again steps the type) with Ellipse/Arc/Fit/Control tiles; ellipse is centre, axis end, other radius (then arc start and end); spline is clicks, Enter or a second click on the last point to finish, a click on the first point to close. GCS: an ellipse is a kernel ellipse held by its centre (coincident, lock, point-on-ellipse, concentric, equal radii); an open spline exposes its end points; interior points carry no relation. The entity card edits radii/rotation/arc range and each spline point; modify tools, revolve, sweep paths and the assistant refuse the new kinds by name. Sketch SVG writes elliptical-arc and per-span Q/C commands with an exact extent; sketch DXF (R12) refuses both by name, since R12 has no ELLIPSE or SPLINE. Evidence: `test/sketch-curve-entities.test.ts` (fit passes through its points with bit-exact ends, closed fit is C2 through its seam, πab regions rotated or not, half ellipse and quarter segment closed forms, circle hole in an ellipse, line crossing, spline closed/open/split areas, self-intersection, stable fingerprints), `test/sketch-curve-extrude.test.ts` (πab·h, taller-than-wide restatement, half and 220° arcs, ellipse and spline hole loops that a following box subtract accepts, closed spline as two walls), `test/parity/occt-reference/occt-curve-entities.spec.ts` (both kernels against the oracle with equal face counts), `test/sketch-curve-constraints.test.ts` (centre coincident, point on ellipse, concentric+equal, spline ends joined with interior points untouched, refusals, v27 round trip, sketch move), `test/sketch-svg-export.test.ts`, `test/sketch-dxf-export.test.ts`, web unit tests for the tools, card, rail and constraint picking, and `test/e2e/sketch-curve-tools.spec.ts`. Open: spline curve constraints (tangency, point-on-spline; Remus B75), trim/offset/mirror/pattern of the new curves, DXF R2000 ELLIPSE/SPLINE, slots, points and projection. Delivered: sketch projection with a linked/unlinked choice (research-audit Stage 2d, ZC-013; schema v33). An additive `projection` record on line, arc, circle and ellipse objects stores the source body and edge by schema-v5 lineage plus `linked` or `unlinked`; stored documents are never rewritten. The sketch rail's Project tool (U; its strip picks Linked or Unlinked) projects a picked model edge, or every boundary edge of a picked face, along the sketch normal. It follows the Construction toggle like every other drawing tool. Straight edges become lines. Circles and arcs become circles and arcs when parallel to the sketch, and exact ellipses and elliptical arcs when tilted. A line along the normal, a circle seen edge-on and any other curve kind are refused by name. Linked: at the sketch's history position the exact build re-finds each edge through verified lineage only (no hash match, no proximity). Every later feature in that build reads the re-projected geometry, so a downstream extrude follows an upstream resize in the same rebuild. The state is published as `derived.sketchProjections` (current, moved or broken). A move is written back to the object as a normalization with no undo step of its own. Line ends keep their names when an edge comes back reversed. The solver holds linked objects fixed, and drag, trim, fillet and chamfer refuse them until they are unlinked. Broken (source deleted, unavailable at the sketch's place in the history, or no longer the same curve kind): the stored geometry, which is the last valid projection, keeps building, and the sketch carries an advisory. Repair is an explicit Re-pick source on the entity card. It is validated by a rebuild before it commits and keeps the object id. Unlink instead keeps the geometry as an independent copy. A new linked pick is refused when its edge does not exist at the sketch's place in the history. Unlinked: copied once, ordinary editable geometry, source kept as provenance. U06: the Objects sketch row counts its projections and says when one is broken. Each projected entity is listed under it with its source feature and its linked/independent/broken role. The History row of a sketch with a broken projection is flagged. Evidence: `test/sketch-projection.test.ts` (exact kernel: a pick re-projects to exactly what it stored; a linked outline follows w 20→32 with the extrude at 32·10·3 in the same rebuild, then settles current after write-back; reversed line ends; unlinked never moves; a deleted source is broken with the last valid 25·10·3 plate still built and four advisories; an orphaned lineage refuses; history-position refusal; exact rim circle; v33 round trip and malformed-record refusals), `test/sketch-projection-geometry.test.ts` (closed-form line, circle, arc-side, tilted-circle r·cos θ ellipse and elliptical-arc oracles, refusals, tolerance and end-order rules), `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/projection.test.ts`, `ObjectsPanel.projections.test.tsx`, `HistoryTimeline.test.tsx`, `SketchEntityEditor.test.tsx`, `SketchToolRail.test.tsx`, `machine.test.ts`, and `test/e2e/sketch-projection.spec.ts` (projects a box's top face linked, widens the box from History and sees the stored outline follow, deletes the box and sees broken in Objects, History and the card, then unlinks). Remaining: silhouette projection of curved faces, spline and ellipse edge projection, and offsetting a body edge chain into a sketch. Constrained ordinary geometry now follows refreshed linked projections during exact history rebuilds. Every linked entity stays fixed, including current and broken links at their retained positions; only a successful solve publishes overrides. Unsatisfied relations refuse dependent features and affected exports without partial projection write-back (`test/sketch-projection-constraints.test.ts`). Cold rebuild, undo, replay, verified edge lineage, exact-volume and SVG/DXF export regressions cover the lifecycle. Solved ordinary neighbours also reach the sketch viewport through a project/version-qualified session view, so entity cards, endpoint snaps, point measurements and closed-profile picks agree with the rebuilt solid and exports. The view preserves authored values/history, is omitted from persistence, and is retained across geometry-preserving edits only after canonical geometry equivalence is proved. `test/e2e/sketch-projection-constraints.spec.ts` checks these reads after resize and reopen without a manual solve. Unlinking the last projection retains the solved shape in one undoable, replayable transaction; driver releases keep unchanged typed expressions. Exact regressions cover 576 volume after release, rollback without partial writes, undo/redo, cold replay/reopen/export, and an independent typed-height edit producing 672 volume. Saved linked projections demand Boolean lineage independently of transient selection, both through their producer reference and through a descendant source body. Exact-kernel cold reopen and cached rebuild regressions preserve the saved document, source reference and volume, while unlinked and unrelated controls retain lazy behavior (`boolean-evolution-lazy-probe.test.ts`, `sketch-projection.test.ts`). An explicit line re-pick retains the stored start/end orientation and construction role, preserving constrained endpoint identity (`apps/web/src/lib/sketch/projection.test.ts`). Projection source admission now requires the exact resolver's positive, canonical, hash-consistent edge reference; malformed references fail before native changes, account saves or HTTP/WebSocket collaboration storage. `sketch-projection-reference-validation.test.ts` covers zero/mismatched hashes, reversed witness endpoints, invalid closed axes and unsupported closed witnesses, unchanged storage, valid open/closed/null-axis controls and frozen ADR-011 hash values. The existing fingerprint algorithm and signature field order are shared with the kernel rather than changed; no valid document format, tolerance or schema version changes. Open circular edges published with only two tessellation endpoints are refused before a sketch copy is proposed: an endpoint cannot select the short versus complementary arc. Geometry and browser-pick regressions cover shallow parallel and tilted arcs in both directions, genuine interior-sample short-sweep controls, unchanged unlinked documents on refusal, and proven full circles with only closing endpoints. This narrows unsupported input; it does not fit or approximate an arc or change tolerances. CadPatch single and multi-profile extrusions resolve their sample points against the same project/version-qualified solved sketch view advertised in the assistant digest. Only reads use the session view; commands, authored expressions and history keep their original base. Append-only consumers and new or unchanged parameter bindings retain that qualified read snapshot. Qualified reads are tracked per sketch through its earlier feature, body, datum and linked-object dependencies, including in-place Moves. Renames and unrelated sketch/feature dimensions preserve them; edits to the sketch or its transitive geometry sources require an exact rebuild before profile selection. Existing parameter and structural edits conservatively require that rebuild. Exact-kernel regressions cover points outside the old outline, overlapping old/new outlines with independent area/volume oracles, repeated consumers, unchanged authored nodes, undo/redo/replay and wrong-project/old-version refusal controls. Consumers that only need a sketch id keep their existing command semantics and do not require profile-geometry reads. Compatibility controls retain multi-line command compilation and verify legacy single-circle extrusion/revolve with independent cylinder and torus volume formulas. Delivered: point and slot sketch entities (schema v59; v58 is claimed by the M16 sheet-metal change). Stored documents are never rewritten, and an older client refuses a v59 document rather than dropping either entity. A `point` sketch object (`x`, `y`) is one solver point named `center`. Coincident, midpoint, distance, symmetric, point-on-curve, lock and disconnect all take it; curve-only relations refuse it by name. It bounds no profile, splits no curve, is left out of sketch DXF/SVG output like construction geometry, draws as a dot, is selected by a click and edits X/Y on the entity card. A `slot` is a new S04 composite kind (`center-to-center`): side 0 a line, side 1 the half circle about the second centre, side 2 the opposite line, side 3 the half circle about the first centre, counter-clockwise, plus a construction centre line as its one helper. Its relations are four corner joins, four line-to-arc tangents at the joins, equal ends and the centre line held on both centres. They leave exactly position, rotation, centre distance and radius free with nothing redundant, and a radius on an end or a distance on the centre line drives it. The composite resolves only while its arcs are equal half circles, its lines run tangent at the corners and the centre line joins the centres; otherwise it reads as broken. Because its members are ordinary lines and arcs, region analysis, profiles, the exact build, the solver and undo need no new path. Rail: Slot and Point are tiles on the shape strip beside Polygon (Slot: both end centres, then a click off the axis for the radius, with a live outline; Point: one click, which snaps). Evidence: `test/sketch-point-slot.test.ts` (pinned kernel: slot rank equals equations at 5 DOF, radius and centre-distance dimensions drive it, the stadium region has the exact 2rL + πr² area and extrudes to it times the height, one undo removes the whole slot, a record with the wrong side kinds is refused, a point fully defined by a midpoint relation solves to the midpoint with no region, curve-only relations refused, schema v59 required), `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/composites.test.ts`, `SketchToolRail.test.tsx`, and `test/e2e/sketch-point-slot.spec.ts` (three clicks draw a slot as line, arc, line, arc and a centre line under one composite with 6 coincident, 4 tangent and 1 equal relations; one click places a point snapped on the centre line's midpoint; undo removes each in one step). Open: a resize card for the slot (its sizes are driven through ordinary dimensions), arc slots, DXF POINT output. | K05/K06 and sketch identity; product S-6/S-7, design B5/phase 6; Remus B75 for spline solver constraints (the pinned GCS already has ellipse entities) and 4.5/7.4 for projection and imprint (C8). Prototype snapping before dependent constraints. |

File selection through download fell from 7.37 to 5.03 s. Archived imports with a changed artifact ID still rebuild under their committed cache key. STEP import discovers accepted indices and assembly occurrences from exact edge incidence. The validated rebuild retains the full solid diagnostics. | <a id="i03"></a>I03 | Partial | Explicit whole-document unit conversion after creation; convert expressions, geometry, tolerances and persisted references through a reviewed migration contract. Delivered (schema v148, building on v145): switching an existing project's units without rewriting it. File › Units (mm, cm, m, in) and the palette's "Show lengths in …" commands set the document's `displayUnits` through one undoable, replayable, geometry-free `displayUnits.set` command; choosing the project's own unit clears it. Every stored length, persisted reference and export keeps reading `units`, so the model keeps its exact size and nothing rebuilds. Expression fields and the numeric keypad show and take plain numbers in the display unit and hand back stored-unit values (an untouched converted prefill commits the exact stored value); expressions keep their stored-unit meaning and preview in the display unit; sketch dimension labels (driving, and reference labels that follow the document), selection read-outs (lengths, Ø/R, areas, body size, the live diameter during a drag) and the measurement dock follow it. Older schemas refuse the field, its history changes and the command (`assertDisplayUnitsSchema`). Coverage: `test/display-units.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/displayUnits.test.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/sketch/dimensionAnnotations.test.ts`. Remaining: the explicit conversion of stored values (expressions, geometry, tolerances, ADR-013 witnesses) this row names, and the read-outs that still print stored units under their own label (Inspector property grids, status messages, drawing dimensions), and the parameter table, whose values keep their stored-unit meaning. Stored-unit conversion phase 2 (plan `docs/plans/i03-stored-unit-conversion-plan.md` §7 row 2): `convertDocumentUnits` in `packages/document-core/src/units-convert.ts` is a pure, tests-only transform between mm, cm and inch. It scales registry lengths and positions by k and areas by k², rewrites expressions by rule D (suffixed and typed-length text unchanged, unit-free text wrapped as `(E) / 25.4` with exact factors and unwrapped on the way back, numerals scaled to the shortest 15–17-digit value that converts back exactly), converts typed-length parameters and every configuration row, recomputes parameter values and drops `derived`. Every expression, parameter and configuration cell is audited structurally: a length that reaches an operation discontinuous or not equivariant in a length (`round`/`floor`/`ceil`, `mod`, `require_min`/`require_one_of`, `sign`, `atan2`, trigonometry, an unclassified function, `^` with a non-integer or length exponent), a length in a parameter that does not scale, or any count, angle, ratio, fraction or condition field whose value depends on a length (the kernel rounds counts and compares the rest), refuses whatever today's value. A field-level oracle then evaluates every classified field and stored literal before and after: no value may change sign or cross zero, lengths and areas agree at relative 1e-12, unscaled values must be bit-identical unless they are ratios of lengths, whole-number counts stay exact, every stored number equals its canonical scaled value bit for bit, and a non-zero length never underflows to zero or a subnormal. Scaling never overflows when the true result is finite, and an out-of-range result refuses. The active configuration row's stale cells are replaced by the converted live parameters. It refuses `m`, custom features, imported-mesh payloads and non-empty drawings, PMI, assemblies and reference images; the print clearance converts. Round trips mm→cm/inch→mm restore every converting parity scenario, fixture and the import-modeling corpus byte for byte; Scissors (assembly) and the scripting fixture (custom feature) refuse (`test/units-convert.test.ts`, `test/parity/unit-conversion.spec.ts`). Not wired to a command or UI. Stored-unit conversion phase 6 (plan `docs/plans/i03-stored-unit-conversion-plan.md` §5.2–§5.4, §6, §7 row 6, §9): File › Units › "Convert stored units…", under the display-unit choice it does not replace, opens a review (`ConvertUnitsDialog`) for mm, cm or inch other than the stored unit. It checks the conversion in the disposable worker with the committing manager's actor and shows the stored values that change, the rewritten expressions, the residual-risk warnings, every refusal by feature and field, the undo-size warning and what happens to the shown unit. "Convert" commits through `commitUnitsConversion` with the live manager, room and clock getters, keeps the review open with the reason on a refusal, and offers "Check again" when the project moved (`stale`). "Convert a copy" duplicates the project through the existing duplicate flow, opens the copy and converts that. Viewers, Tweak, View, Draw, Assemble and shared-link sessions see the item disabled with the reason; other people in the room are named before anything runs. Browser: `test/e2e/convert-stored-units.spec.ts` (`@ci-smoke`): convert mm → in, read-outs and a reference dimension in inches, undo, redo, reload, then restore "Before unit conversion (mm → in)" back to millimeters; plus Convert a copy. Display units reach the read-outs (no schema change): the Inspector's body measurements (size, volume, centre of mass, unit-density inertia; mass and inertia units follow the display unit), face and edge figures, recognized-feature dimensions and stored direct-edit values; History row values (lengths converted, angles and counts untouched); the parameter table (a length parameter reads converted with its unit, its expression as stored; its minimum too) and the Tweak panel's units note; the viewport rule, which chooses a round step in the display unit; the on-canvas offset, radius, edge-radius and primitive-dimension chips (the primitive callout's inline entry reads and commits in the display unit); and the status messages that report an applied length. Fixed: a refusal's "Try" size in the in-place Options panel was read in the display unit rather than as the stored size it is (`displayEntryText`). Coverage: `apps/web/src/components/displayUnitsReadouts.test.tsx`. Still in the stored unit, correctly labelled: drawing sheets (each has its own Units setting), the direct-edit Diameter and Offset fields in the Inspector, and refusal sizes. Stored-unit conversion phase 3 (`docs/plans/i03-stored-unit-conversion-plan.md` §3.4): an imported mesh's referenced payload may state its own units (`payloadUnits`, schema v153), absent meaning the document's, and the kernel scales the triangles on load and keys the mesh conversion cache by the scale, so a conversion can leave the content-addressed blob alone. Nothing writes the field yet (`imported-mesh-payload-units.test.ts`, `imported-mesh-schema.test.ts`, PR #1966). Stored-unit conversion phase 1: a machine-checked quantity registry (`packages/shared/src/quantity-registry.ts`) classifies every numeric and `ParamValue` field of every feature kind (direct edits included), sketch object, sketch constraint, the sketch node and a parameter's expression; an unclassified numeric field fails `pnpm typecheck`. `rewriteNodeExpressions` is now derived from it, characterized against the hand-written visitor, and a runtime test classifies every numeric leaf of the parity documents and fixtures (`test/quantity-registry.test.ts`, PR #1955). Stored-unit conversion phase 4 (plan `docs/plans/i03-stored-unit-conversion-plan.md` §3.2, §6 steps 2–3, §7 row 4): `computeConversionReferenceMap` in `packages/kernel-adapter/src/units-reference-recapture.ts` re-captures every persisted topology identity of a converted document by rebuilding, never by scaling witness integers. The source document is built cold and each reference lookup is recorded with the entity it resolved to; the converted document is built cold with every lookup replaced by the unique entity whose witness is the recorded one scaled by k within two quanta of the coarser unit, carrying the same lineage name (imported-STEP hash names compared by family) or, for a legacy hash-only pick, none. Schema-v5 face and edge references, legacy `faceHash`/`edgeHashes`/`oppositeFaceHash` and emboss hashes (in their original scheme), and sketch `regionFingerprint`/`profileId` are covered through one hook seam (`reference-recapture-slot.ts`) in the three resolvers. The result is a map keyed by JSON pointer; `listConversionReferenceSites` and the pure `applyReferenceMap` (`packages/document-core/src/units-reference-map.ts`) apply it without a kernel for phase-5 replay. A verification build of the applied document must resolve every identity, propose no repair the source build does not, and match the source per body (bounding box within 1e-6 + 1e-9·scale, volume ×k³ and face area ×k² at 1e-6, face/edge/vertex counts, consumed/exportable flags, exportable set and warnings). Every lookup is keyed by the feature whose build made it, so equal stored hashes on two features never share a resolution, and re-capture must be one-to-one per feature. Edge-end indices (datum vertex `end`, variable-fillet `vertexRadii[].end` and `edgeRuns`) are remapped from the witness ends actually matched, since the quantized sort can swap them; an ambiguous pairing, or an edge whose end order its consumer reads another way, refuses. The oracle also compares every datum plane, axis, point and coordinate system scaled by k. Missing, ambiguous, conflicting, never-resolved or never-looked-up identities and any oracle mismatch refuse, naming the node and field; imported STEP raised lettering refuses as never resolved (known limitation). Parity property (`test/parity/unit-conversion.spec.ts`): of 13 documents (parity scenarios, the visual-selection demo, the import-modeling corpus) 11 convert to cm and to inch and round-trip back to identical witnesses; Scissors refuses in phase 2 (assembly) and `boolean-on-nurbs-import` is pinned as a kernel refusal (its B-spline faces measure volume 7.5e-6 and area 0.8% differently at another scale). The b-unit STEP files keep their physical size through import, conversion and export. No command, UI or schema change. Four expression fields the registry listed as unvisited gaps are now renamed and F05-checked like any other: a tapped hole's thread `length` and `clearance` (lengths) and a sketch pattern's `layout.spacing` (a length) and `layout.stepDeg` (an angle). A sketch promotion's `sourceData` stays deliberately unvisited, because it is the verbatim legacy record S04 keeps. The characterization now pins these four fields as the only visits added to the frozen hand-written visitor (`test/quantity-registry.test.ts`, `packages/document-core/src/skipped-expression-fields.test.ts`). Stored-unit conversion delivered end to end (plan `docs/plans/i03-stored-unit-conversion-plan.md`, owner decisions §9): the plan (PR #1933), the gear `outsideDiameter` tag fix (PR #1935), the quantity registry (PR #1955) and the four skipped expression fields (PR #1962), the pure verified transform (PR #1965), mesh payload units (schema v153, PR #1966), reference re-capture verified by rebuild (PR #1977), the replayable `units.convert` command with a safety checkpoint (schema v154, PR #1990), and File › Units › "Convert stored units…" with "Convert a copy" (PR #1999). A project converts between mm, cm and inch, keeps its exact size, records one undo step and a "Before unit conversion" checkpoint, and refuses with the field named rather than mis-scale. Out of scope by decision: metres, custom-feature scripts and expressions that reach a length-discontinuous operation (`round(width)`). Still refused when they hold data: drawings, PMI, assemblies, reference images, growing-holder metadata, imported STEP raised lettering and per-end variable fillets. Not yet converted: saved views, the remembered section plane, the linear snap setting and comment pins. Stored-unit conversion phase 5 (plan `docs/plans/i03-stored-unit-conversion-plan.md` §5.1–§5.3, §7 row 5): the replayable `units.convert` command (schema v154), payload `{ from, to, factor: [N, D], references }`, each reference entry `{ from, to }`: replay refuses the whole conversion when a place no longer holds its recorded `from`. Applying or replaying it is pure (`applyUnitsConversion`: `convertDocumentUnits`, then `applyReferenceMap` with the recorded map), and older schemas refuse it on the command, the stored log and replay. Authoring needs the kernel: `prepareUnitsConversion` (kernel adapter, run by the disposable `unitsConversionWorker`) runs the transform and field oracle, re-captures references through three cold rebuilds with the geometry and reference oracles, checks that replaying the recorded payload reproduces the verified document byte for byte, and measures the undo entry against `MAX_UNDO_BYTES` with the bound's own measure, refusing a conversion whose undo step the history could not keep even alone (`undo-too-large`) and reporting when it would be the only step left; it returns a preview (counts, rewritten expressions, warnings, undo estimate) or every refusal by node and field, and no command exists until then. `commitUnitsConversion` (web) refuses while other clients are present in the room, for viewers, outside a live room or with an expired or denied edit lease; writes and persists "Before unit conversion (mm → in)" on a fresh revision as a save-state restore does; then re-checks every eligibility condition after that write and executes the command as one undo step. Imported meshes with a referenced payload now convert by recording `payloadUnits` (v153) and leaving the blob alone. The save-state diff shows one "Converted stored units mm → in (n values)" row in place of a row per scaled value. Rounding a ratio of lengths and a pole or domain edge in a sum of lengths are reported as warnings naming the field (0 over 26 conversions of the 15 parity, demo, import-modeling and fixture documents). Parity property (`test/parity/units-convert-command.spec.ts`): the 11 scenarios phase 4 accepts are authored through the kernel and replayed without it to the same document. No UI (phase 6). | Product I-4; no implicit reinterpretation of existing dimensions. |
