Research-audit slice (ZC-199): an all-bodies export now reports viewport-hidden bodies in its scope ("all bodies (3, 1 hidden)") and the mesh dialog offers Include hidden bodies; switched off, mesh and STEP exports write only visible bodies ("visible bodies (2, 1 hidden left out)"). A selected body still exports alone. `lib/exportScope.test.ts`, `ExportDialog.test.tsx`. Exchange units, tolerance and loss-contract slice (ZC-177/183–186/188/200–202, this PR): an STL, OBJ or PLY import, or a DXF without `$INSUNITS`, asks how to read its numbers, showing the size each unit would make and preselecting the old reading (millimetres for a mesh, project units for a DXF); Settings → General → Unitless imports can settle the answer. An Export units preference (default millimetres, so existing exports are unchanged) scales STEP, which declares inches as a 25.4 mm `CONVERSION_BASED_UNIT`; 3MF, through its unit attribute; DXF, through `$INSUNITS`; and the bare numbers of STL, OBJ and PLY. GLB stays in metres and SVG keeps its physical size. The mesh dialog adds Max facet angle: the kernel's 20° default sends nothing new, and a tighter angle re-meshes through the indexed-mesh translator. Its check reports the triangle count and the deviation measured against the exact faces. `docs/exchange-loss-contract.md` states what STEP, STL, 3MF, OBJ, PLY, GLB, DXF and SVG each keep and drop, and `test/exchange-round-trip.test.ts` checks every line through the shared harness `test/support/exchange-harness.ts`, including a 25.4 mm STEP block that round-trips as 1 in. Evidence: `test/mesh-export-tolerance.test.ts` (R10 cylinder at 0.1 and 0.01 mm: deviation within tolerance and more triangles at the finer one; a 5° angle adds facets), `export-units.test.ts`, `importUnits.test.ts`, `ImportUnitsDialog.test.tsx`, `ExportDialog.test.tsx`, `dxf-import.test.ts`, `dxf-writer.test.ts`, `app-settings.test.ts` and `exchange-units.spec.ts`. Recorded drops that stay open: a 3MF import merges its objects into one body; no format writes body names or colours; SVG has no import (I07). |
| <a id="i05"></a>I05 | Partial | Reference images: place PNG/JPG (first PDF page through the existing rasterizer) on a plane or face with a gizmo for scale/move/rotate and an opacity slider, listed as an item, usable as a tracing underlay in sketches and as a split tool later. Stored as an artifact, not in the document body. Research-audit Stage 6 slice (this PR, schema v32): the Create fold's Image tool places a PNG or JPEG (sniffed by content, at most 20 MB and 8192 px a side) on Top (XY), or on the flat face selected when it opens; its card moves it to any principal plane (with offset), a resolved datum plane (followed by id) or the selected face (followed by lineage, or a fixed frame for a face without identity), sets position U/V, rotation, width (height follows the aspect) and opacity as a live draft applied in one undoable edit, and scales it from two points picked on the picture and a known distance. Objects lists images with show/hide (view state, like datums), an Images filter and name search. The record lives in `ProjectDocument.referenceImages` and holds only a SHA-256 reference: the bytes go to the content-addressed source blob store and a `reference-image` artifact (PNG/JPEG only, checked at upload-session creation and finalize), never inline, and a browser without blob storage is refused. Images are never features or bodies, are excluded from the geometry rebuild key, share snapshots and diagnostics, are never raycast and sit outside the PNG export's allowlist; STEP/mesh/drawing exports cannot see them. Backups carry the picture as a source and refuse one without it; blob cleanup keeps every picture the document or its undo history names. A device-only picture (local project, or a failed upload) counts as a local-only source and is archived, with its id rewritten through history, by the next account save or local-project adoption; a duplicate or branch caches the source's pictures and holds them under device-local ids instead of depending on the source project's artifact, and an account duplicate uploads them into the new project and stores the rewritten copy before reporting it made. An insert is validated in full (including the 64-image cap) before any byte is stored or uploaded, and a texture that could not load retries when the image's artifact id changes. Regression evidence: `test/reference-images.test.ts` (schema, commands, undo/replay, validation at load and the account edge, history snapshots, geometry key, share/diagnostics, upload content types, two-point scale), `referenceImageDisplay.test.ts`, `referenceImageRig.test.ts`, `referenceImageInsert.test.ts`, `ReferenceImageForm.test.tsx`, `ObjectsPanel.images.test.tsx`, `projectBackupTransfer.test.ts` and `test/e2e/reference-images.spec.ts`. Remaining: a viewport gizmo for move/rotate/scale (fields only today), PDF first-page import, sketch tracing snaps and split-by-image, share-link delivery of pictures, and a server-side artifact copy for account duplicates (today the duplicating device uploads the pictures). | U06 items; the pdf.js rasterizer already used for assistant ingestion; [comparison §2.9](docs/reviews/reference-cad-comparison-2026-09-26.md). |

## File interoperability

This lane has its own bounded sequence within the delivery milestones above.
[D04](#d04) DXF sketch exchange, [I06](#i06) IGES and [I02](#i02) STEP
fidelity are Complete, and [I07](#i07) is down to SVG import (its export
half has merged). Qualify [I10](#i10) before selecting
native-CAD implementations; the first package is [I11](#i11) Parasolid,
[I12](#i12) ACIS, [I13](#i13) Rhino and [I14](#i14) SolidWorks part import.
Inventor/JT follow demonstrated demand; Creo/NX/CATIA/Solid Edge are retained
as later targets. [I08](#i08) PLY export has merged and needs only its
browser download acceptance. PDF belongs to [D01](#d01), which exports one
sheet as vector PDF and still has to export all sheets as one PDF.
[I09](#i09) USDZ is deferred visualization work.

Import means qualified geometry and supported metadata, not recovered source
feature history. Per-format direction/version claims require fixtures; native
assembly references must resolve explicitly, and mesh-only input cannot become
an exact solid merely by translation. Details and source evidence live in the
[format specification](docs/plans/file-format-interoperability.md); this table
is the only delivery-status ledger. Existing I04 owns export controls; I07 owns
SVG geometry, replacing the previously unnamed SVG slice in D04/I04.

| ID | Status | Outcome and completion evidence | Depends on / supporting detail |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="i06"></a>I06 | Complete | IGES `.igs`/`.iges` import/export: qualify supported solid and trimmed-surface families, units, multiple bodies, malformed input and export/reimport fidelity. Current kernel IGES remains lossy preview; retain that disclosure until a bounded precise-scope decision and its qualification pass. Reject or explicitly classify open/unsupported geometry. Precise-scope decision and qualification landed in Remus O5.7 (esaueng/remus#1820): an additive exact IGES 5.3 B-rep path for type 186 solids over 190–198 analytic and 128 B-spline faces, 110/100/104/126 edges, rigid 124 frames and declared units, read through the strict STEP body reader; surface models (143/144 with no 186 solid), other entity types and non-rigid frames are refused by name, and the legacy preview reader stays a declared lossy preview that OpenZCAD does not use. Eleven independent B-rep files (box, cylinder, cone, sphere, torus, holed plate, cavity and tilted cylinder in mm and inch, B-spline box) read with the independent reader's volumes and centroids and validate; every file written back reads to the same solid, and the independent reader opens every Remus-written file as one valid solid with exact volume and area. OpenZCAD: Import CAD files reads `.igs`/`.iges` as the exact STEP the translator restates it to (stored, archived and replayed under the IGES file's name), and File › Export IGES writes the shown bodies in the export unit. Evidence: `test/iges-exchange.test.ts`, the IGES (B-rep) row of `test/exchange-round-trip.test.ts` and `docs/exchange-loss-contract.md`, `test/e2e/iges-exchange.spec.ts`. | Remus O5.7 ([#883](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/pull/883)); pinned IO APIs are not qualification. K01/K03, K09 for sheet workflows; format specification. |
| <a id="i07"></a>I07 | Partial | SVG sketch/profile import/export: paths/basic shapes, nested transforms, physical scale/viewBox, winding and closed loops; preserve supported curves, name unsupported entities, and require text converted to paths initially. Delivered (export half, ZC-190, with the ZC-192 view PNG): File › Export image… (also in the command palette) writes one saved sketch as SVG through the D04 sketch extraction, so it refuses the same sketches by the same names (text, construction-only, stale attachment…); lines, circles and arcs stay exact `<line>`/`<circle>`/elliptical-arc `<path>` elements (never sampled) in DOCUMENT units, with v negated for SVG's y-down axis, a viewBox over the exact curve extent (arc axis extremes included) plus half a 0.25 mm stroke, and a physical width/height that opens at 1:1 (metres stated in millimetres); construction geometry is left out and a transparent-background option drops the white backing rect. SVG review follow-up: normalize finite arc starting angles before bounded quadrant checks, and retain nearly full turns with two arcs when their formatted endpoints coincide. Browser downloads prove both cases remain visible. PNG rendering preserves per-material tone mapping, including the unmodified backdrop; a native-resolution browser comparison fails by 16 colour steps before the fix and matches within one rounding step afterwards. A second-sketch History selection defaults the dialog to that sketch and downloads its distinct geometry; the browser regression fails against the prior default. Selected-body images also exclude uncommitted Move-copy projections; a viewport regression checks capture scope and restores their visibility afterwards. PNG capture also suspends sketch/profile selection and hover styling, including profile boundaries and pick markers, and restores the exact live styles after success or renderer failure. The same dialog renders the current view as a PNG at a chosen size (View, 2× view, 1920×1080, 3840×2160 or typed, up to 8192 px a side and 40 MP) offscreen on the live renderer — MSAA render target, then the renderer's own tone mapping and sRGB encoding with alpha un-premultiplied — and restores the camera, visibility and clear state in the same synchronous call, so the live canvas never presents a capture frame; a wider/narrower image widens the frame rather than cropping it, gizmos/rigs/measurements and selection glow are left out, the grid is optional, and the background can be transparent. Scope follows #707: a selected body draws alone, otherwise the visible bodies with hidden ones counted as left out. The PNG is written by an in-app encoder (8-bit RGBA, IHDR/IDAT/IEND only, so no metadata) to keep straight alpha exact. Desktop saves accept `.svg`/`.png`. Evidence (this PR): `test/sketch-svg-export.test.ts` (lines/arcs/circles, sweep and large-arc flags, arc bounds, units incl. inch and metre, background, adapter refusals), `apps/web/src/lib/pngEncode.test.ts` (size, colour type, CRCs, exact straight alpha), `apps/web/src/components/viewer/viewportImage.test.ts` (aspect fit keeps the view's extent, restore), `apps/web/src/lib/imageExport.test.ts` (size limits, scope), `ImageExportDialog.test.tsx`, `geometryWorker.test.ts` (svg route) and `image-export.spec.ts` (downloads a 640×360 transparent PNG with transparent corners, opaque body pixels and an unchanged live canvas, an all-opaque PNG, and the imported slot's SVG with exact arcs and 1:1 size). SVG import remains open. | Shared sketch extraction from D04, I01 for additional curve entities; bound XML/path resources and exclude active/external content. |

## 3. Drawings

| ID | Status | Outcome and completion evidence | Depends on / legacy item |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="d01"></a>D01 | Partial | Isolated-body drawing entry (this PR): first entry opens the unique saved sheet matching the visible result bodies, including after a cold rebuild; returning from Build remembers the chosen sheet within the project session. The properties panel names the sheet bodies and explains that Hide/Isolate does not rebind saved views. Canonical drawing and section requests use idle boolean-lineage demand, avoiding an unnecessary probe upgrade and model replay; modeling consumers still demand full lineage when needed. Evidence: `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` and `test/boolean-evolution-demand.test.ts` cover source choice, retained selection/projections, absence of extra probes, canonical output and a later explicit lineage demand. [Drawing MVP design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md) records the contract and this slice's decisions; Peter chose the Draw workspace mode and accepted both departures (2026-10-19): the fingerprint fence with explicit Update instead of checkpoint replay, and worker-side visibility against the display tolerance, labelled approximate, instead of `projectEdges`. Canonical drawing tessellation (follow-up to #753): views no longer project from the viewport's display mesh, which holds an earlier deflection through edits within 25 % of it while a cold reload re-meshes at the nominal value (so a sheet could read ready after a reload yet export different bytes). A `drawing-tessellation` geometry-worker request meshes each sheet body at its nominal display deflection for its own exact bounds and puts faces, triangles, vertices and edge polylines in an order that depends only on their coordinates, so checkpoint replay, a full rebuild and a cold reload give the same arrays; the Draw workspace caches it per fingerprint, so a hidden-line toggle does not mesh again. The fingerprint is unchanged (no tessellation signature, existing sheets stay ready): the canonical mesh is a function of the exact geometry it already fences. The display path is untouched; a drawing request leaves the next sync's checkpoint restore and measurement reuse unchanged. Evidence: `test/drawing-canonical-tessellation.test.ts` (a sheet exported in a session after a held-deflection parameter edit and after a checkpoint-replayed edit, then rebuilt cold in a fresh kernel: byte-identical PDF and SVG, while the display-mesh input demonstrably differs; refusal of a consumed body; permutation invariance), `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (projects from the requested tessellation, meshed once across a hidden-line toggle; a refusal shows after two retries), and `test/e2e/drawings.spec.ts` "exports the same bytes after a reload re-meshes the model" (R20→R21 disc, export, browser reload, export: identical bytes; fails when fed display meshes). Foundation slice delivered (research-audit Stage 7, ZC-139–142, 164; schema v30): `ProjectDocument.drawings` holds sheets with ISO A4–A0 or ANSI A–D paper and orientation, a shared integer scale, first/third projection angle and title-block text (title, drawing number, revision, author, date; scale, sheet, units and projection fill from the sheet), and per-view orientation (six principal views or the true isometric), bodies, paper-mm centre, hidden-line toggle and an aligned base for projected views. A new **Draw** workspace mode (sheets in a left column, the sheet canvas over the stage with the viewer kept mounted, properties on the right; lazy chunk) makes a four-view starter scaled to fit (front base, top and side folded on the projection angle's sides, isometric free), moves views by drag, fields or arrow keys with projected views kept on their base's row/column, and exports a sheet as dependency-free vector PDF (base-14 Helvetica, WinAnsi; other characters become "?") or SVG in millimetres, byte-stable across reload. Projection runs in its own worker from the canonical drawing tessellation: kernel-sampled edge polylines plus curved-face silhouettes, occlusion against every body's triangles (approximate to the display tolerance) — the pinned `projectEdges` tests occlusion only locally and draws no silhouettes, recorded as a diagnostic test. Model fence: each sheet stores a fingerprint of its bodies' persistent topology hashes and extents; an edited model shows a stale state, draws outlines only and refuses export until an explicit Update (undoable). Evidence: `test/drawing-projection.test.ts` (box principal-view bounds, isometric far-corner hidden runs, cylinder silhouettes, U-part and two-body occlusion, drilled-plate bore), `test/drawing-sheets.test.ts` (schema, validation on load and at the account edge, commands with undo/redo/replay, alignment, fingerprint, 1:2 and inch transform, starter layout, PDF/SVG structure and reload byte-stability), `test/e2e/drawings.spec.ts`. Open: checkpoint-snapshot replay of an old model (the fence refuses instead), per-view scale and custom view angles, exact silhouettes (Remus ask, ZC-145), drawing on share links; title-block layouts and D02 annotations stay with D02. Production QA drawing fixes: views are flagged by their whole footprint and export is refused while one runs off the paper, with an undoable "Fit views to sheet"; the composed page and PDF are clipped to the paper; the title block prints a full ISO date; ⌀ and other common symbols print as Latin-1 stand-ins with a warning for the rest; the sheet zooms and pans; view labels are documented as editor-only (`test/drawing-sheets.test.ts`, `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx`). Draw workspace "paper first" layout (design direction A, chosen 2026-10-31): sheet tabs, the sheet's sync state, zoom and Export PDF/SVG over the sheet in place of the left sheet column and the panel-bottom export; annotation tools on a floating icon rail beside the paper with one-key shortcuts (H, V, A, R, D, G, N; Esc backs out of a tool, then a selection); a contextual properties panel (sheet settings, or the selected view's or annotation's); editor view names at a readable on-screen size; while the model rebuilds, the sheet keeps its last drawing faded, with the rebuild's feature progress, and stays unexportable; and an editor-only dark paper toggle (per device) that never reaches PDF or SVG. Evidence: `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (faded last drawing while rebuilding, tool keys taken ahead of the workspace shortcuts and never from a field, dark paper remembered) and the drawing e2e specs updated for the moved controls. A sheet whose model changed keeps its last drawing (views, dimensions on that model, export) for the session behind one compact "Model changed · Update" pill instead of a full-width banner and outlines; undoing an Update restores it (`DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` "keeps the earlier drawing usable after a model change until Update", `drawings.spec.ts`, `drawing-dimensions.spec.ts`). Sheet scales and Auto scale: the scale list gains the ISO 5455 enlargements 50:1 and 20:1 and the common intermediates 4:1, 1:4, 1:8, 1:25 and 1:250 (the starter and "Fit views to sheet" pick from the same list, so they can land on these). A new undoable **Auto scale** action lays the sheet's views out again as an evenly spaced grid at the largest preset at which the grid fits above the title block: views whose centres share a row or column keep sharing it, so projected views stay aligned, but the gaps are set afresh, so views that overlap or sprawl after a scale picked by hand come out clean, and a sheet shrunk by hand grows back to fill the paper. Evidence: `test/drawing-sheets.test.ts` ("auto scales views to the largest preset, spaced evenly in a grid"), `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (preset list, one-command Auto scale with status). Sheet tabs scale to many sheets: one row that scrolls sideways (edge fades mark hidden tabs, a plain wheel scrolls it, the active tab is kept in view) instead of wrapping into rows that pushed the zoom and export controls down; an "All sheets" list once the row overflows (number, name, paper, scale, view and note counts, opened at the active sheet); the paper tag shown only when sheets differ in paper; arrow keys, Home and End move between tabs and only the active tab is a Tab stop. Evidence: `DrawingSheetTabs.test.tsx` (paper tag only on mixed paper, arrow-key selection, list only when overflowing). 13 November design review, Draw (I62–I66 and the Draw half of I2): a sheet tab's menu (right-click, Shift+F10 or the visible Sheet actions button) renames, duplicates, moves and deletes the sheet; F2 or a double-click renames in place and a tab drags to reorder, one undo step each. A move is the existing sheet delete and add commands, so an older client replays it exactly and the document schema is unchanged. A new sheet takes the next unused "Sheet N". The title block's SHEET cell prints "2 / 3 · Details" instead of the paper size. Changing Paper or Orientation lays the views out again on the new paper in the same undo step, so a smaller sheet no longer leaves views off the page and export refused. Add view offers the first orientation the sheet lacks and puts a view with no base in the free space nearest the middle, clear of the others. A plain wheel zooms the sheet as in Build, through Build's wheel classifier, pointer-navigation setting and remembered device. Bill-of-materials rows lose the hover fill that implied an action. Deleting a sheet or a view says what went ("Deleted Front view, Section A-A and 4 dimensions.") with Undo. Coverage: `sheetEdits.test.ts`, `DrawingSheetTabs.test.tsx`, `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx`, `test/drawing-sheet-review.test.ts` and `drawings.spec.ts` in the browser. Open: exporting all sheets as one PDF. | Design sheet/view document model first; product D-1. No dependency on finishing every M-row. |
| <a id="d02"></a>D02 | Complete | Associative drawing dimensions, centerlines, notes, title blocks and hole callouts; model edits update or visibly invalidate annotations. Comparison decisions: refresh on an explicit Update command with visible staleness is the accepted minimum; the dimension set is line length, point-to-point, point-to-line, line-to-line, arc angle, 3-point angle, line-to-line angle, radius, diameter and min/max distance, each with prefix/suffix and a tolerance (symmetrical, deviation, limits, basic); geometry annotations are 2-point, 2-line, 3-point and circular centerlines, center marks and intersection marks that also serve as dimension references; notes and sheet images; units, angle format, precision, decimal separator and per-class line widths are drawing properties. First handoff slice: place a dimension on a D01 view, preserve its placement/formatting through offline reopen, and update or visibly invalidate it after an explicit source rebind before PDF export. Invalid annotation references retain presentation state and offer explicit qualified repair/re-pick, never nearest-edge rebinding. Title-block fields (title, units, scale, projection angle, sheet size, date) fill automatically until edited. Layouts, fields and repair remain implementation scope, not delivered design evidence ([FreeCAD E06](docs/reviews/freecad-comparison-2026-09-30.md#hands-on-evidence)). First slice delivered (research-audit Stage 7, ZC-146/163; schema v31; [design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md#implementation-decisions-d02-annotations-schema-v31)): `DrawingSheet.annotations` holds horizontal, vertical and aligned two-point dimensions, radius, diameter and angle dimensions, and text notes on the paper or in a view with an optional leader. Each stores only the edges it measures (body id, ADR-011 hash and ADR-013 persistent reference, resolved lineage-first through the fail-closed `resolveEdge` ladder) and its placement relative to its view, never a value: values are read from exact edge vertices and published circle data each time a ready sheet is drawn, so a stale sheet shows none until its explicit Update, and a parameter-widened box reads its new width after Update. A reference that resolves to no edge or to several, a body no longer in the view, a circle now edge-on or edges now parallel keep the annotation in place, marked broken with a reason; export is refused until it is re-picked (placement and format kept) or deleted, and nothing rebinds to a nearby edge. Picking offers only edges the view draws, prefers the nearer of coincident edges and refuses an edge whose identity another shares; aligned spans and angles that slope into a principal view, and every length or angle on the isometric view, are refused rather than read foreshortened (horizontal and vertical spans along a model axis stay exact). Dimension strokes, filled arrowheads and rotated text go through the one page model into the preview, PDF and SVG; a sheet without annotations exports byte-identical to D01. Evidence: `test/drawing-annotations.test.ts` (schema pin and additivity, refusals on load and at the account edge, commands with undo/redo/replay, view delete, horizontal/vertical/aligned/angle/radius/diameter values on real kernel bodies, lineage survival across a parameter edit, broken reasons, picking, iso refusal, layout, PDF/SVG text and reload byte-stability), `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (drawn value, broken state with export refusal and Re-pick, stale sheet, keyboard move/delete, Escape) and `test/e2e/drawing-dimensions.spec.ts` (pick and place horizontal, vertical and leader note on a box; delete/undo; SVG/PDF contents; widen → stale with no values → Update → new value; reload). Open: tolerances, prefix/suffix, centrelines/centre marks as references, point-to-line and min/max distances, title-block layouts, hole callouts and drawing-level unit/precision properties. Finishing-body repair (PR #890): an explicit, undoable sheet Update follows unique one-to-one hole/fillet/chamfer/shell/draft/offset results, retaining subshape witnesses, annotation placement and export guards. Additive Thicken results are excluded. Missing, ambiguous and removed edges still require repair. Draw-mode revision saves use the drawing permission guard; new-sheet controls wait for ready geometry. Documents use schema-v39 (built on v38) to fence replay from older clients that would ignore body replacements. Tests: `test/drawing-body-succession.test.ts`, `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx`, `design-review-fixes.spec.ts`. Production verification remains pending. The broken-annotation warning offers **Delete all broken**, removing only unresolved dimensions and note leaders on the current sheet in one undoable transaction. Healthy annotations and other sheets are preserved. Cleanup waits for every view to finish drawing and is unavailable during rebuilds, read-only access or a proposal preview, so temporary section/leader resolution failures cannot remove saved annotations. Stable lineage and unique geometry hashes continue to update dimensions; removed or ambiguous geometry still requires an explicit re-pick rather than a guessed substitute. Evidence: `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` and `test/e2e/drawing-dimensions.spec.ts`. Tolerances, prefixes and suffixes (schema v68, built on v67; [design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md#dimension-tolerances-prefixes-and-suffixes-d02-schema-v68)): a dimension takes a symmetric (`20 ±0.05`), deviation (`+0.10` over `-0.05`, stacked), limits (`20.10` over `19.95`) or basic (framed) tolerance, and a prefix and suffix (`2× … TYP`). Tolerances are stored as deviations from the measured value, so limits follow the model through the explicit Update (a box widened from 30 to 45 reads `45.10/44.95`). Amounts are stated to at most six decimals, so a stored tolerance never prints as zero; a reference dimension, a chamfer and a counterbore or countersink callout take none; a re-pick keeps the tolerance, prefix and suffix; a toleranced diameter on an M01 hole's rim states the diameter instead of the hole's callout. The stacked pair and the basic frame go through the one page model to preview, PDF and SVG; a sheet without them lays out byte-identical. A content gate refuses the fields below v68. Evidence: `test/drawing-dimension-tolerances.test.ts` (validation by name including sub-print-precision amounts, re-pick keeping the format, each format's text, limits read from the kernel body before and after a widen, stacked/framed layout, PDF/SVG contents and byte-stable reload, the v67 gate), `test/hole-callouts.test.ts` (toleranced bore diameter), `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (stacked drawing, tolerance kind switch, refused upper below lower, prefix/suffix, Reference drops the tolerance) and `test/e2e/drawing-tolerances.spec.ts` (limits, prefix and suffix on a picked dimension, SVG/PDF, widen → Update → new limits, reload). Arc and three-point angles (schema v82, built on v81; [design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md#arc-and-three-point-angles-d02-schema-v82)): the Arc angle tool (E) dimensions a face-on arc's included angle at its centre, the way the arc runs (past 180° when it does, its dimension arc sweeping the long way), and the 3-point angle tool (J) the angle at a vertex point between two others (edge ends, circle centres or marks), completing the row's dimension set with the two-edge angle. Both are read from the model on every draw, take tolerances, affixes and the sheet's angle format, lay out and export through the angle's page model, and break by name (`closed-edge`, `edge-on`, `foreshortened`, `no-angle`, `mark-missing`) rather than rebinding. A content gate refuses them below v82. Evidence: `test/drawing-angle-dimensions.test.ts` (validation by name and a mark on another view refused; on kernel bodies an R3 fillet's quarter arc reading 90° with rays of R3, a toleranced `90° ±0.5°`, a whole circle refused; the long-way sweep of an arc past 180°; the angle at a pin's centre subtended by a box edge in the same plane, the same from the pin's top refused as foreshortened and a vertex on its own arm as `no-angle`; drafts from both tools, a mark as the vertex, a re-pick keeping its format and the v82 gate) and `test/e2e/drawing-angles.spec.ts` (an imported block rounded R5: its quarter arc and three corners each read 90°, export to SVG and reopen). Centre marks, intersection marks and centrelines (schema v73, built on v72; [design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md#centre-marks-intersection-marks-and-centrelines-d02-schema-v73)): a centre mark on a face-on circle, an intersection mark where two straight edges' lines cross, and 2-point, 2-line (midway between parallel edges) and 3-point circular centrelines, each read from the model whenever the sheet is drawn and drawn as ISO 128 chain lines run 2.5 mm past what they mark. A centre or intersection mark serves as a dimension and centreline point (`anchor: 'mark'`); deleting it leaves what measured to it broken (`mark-missing`) until re-picked, never rebound. Tools: Centre mark (M), Intersection mark (X), Centreline (L) and Circular centreline (B), made as soon as their picks are in. A content gate refuses them below v73. Evidence: `test/drawing-centerlines.test.ts` (validation by name; on kernel bodies a pin's centre mark, a box corner's intersection, the midline between its top and bottom edges, the circle through three corners, a centreline from a mark and a horizontal dimension from a mark; `mark-missing`, `parallel`, `not-parallel` refusals; chain lengths past the rim and box; SVG; one undoable command and the v70 gate), `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (an intersection mark made from two picks with no placement click) and `test/e2e/drawing-centerlines.spec.ts` (a centreline between two edges, an intersection mark, a horizontal dimension from the mark reading 30, SVG chain lines, reload). Point-to-line, line-to-line and min/max distances (schema v75, built on v74; [design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md#distance-dimensions-d02-schema-v75)): the Distance dimension measures between a point (edge end, circle centre or mark), a straight edge, a straight centreline and a face-on circle — square to a line where there is one, from a line's midpoint between parallel lines, and between centres otherwise — and `extent` measures to circles' near (min) or far (max) sides. It is read from the model on every draw, takes tolerances and affixes, and breaks by name (`not-parallel`, `degenerate`, `foreshortened`, `mark-missing`) rather than rebinding. A content gate refuses it below v75. Evidence: `test/drawing-distance-dimensions.test.ts` (validation by name; on kernel bodies a point to a line, two parallel lines, a centreline to an edge, an edge to a pin's centre, near and far sides with a tolerance, a centre mark to an edge, crossed lines and a point on its line refused, a deleted centreline broken, the aligned layout, the v73 gate) and `test/e2e/drawing-distances.spec.ts` (picked between two edges reading 30, from a centreline to the top edge reading 5, widen → Update → 45, reload). Drawing properties and title-block fields (schema v70, built on v69 (M17 standard parts); [design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md#drawing-properties-and-title-block-fields-d02-schema-v70)): a sheet's `format` sets the units its dimensions print in (a millimetre model dimensioned in inches), the length and angle precision, decimal degrees or D°M'S", a decimal comma, and a print weight per line class; every dimension, callout and tolerance goes through one sheet number format, and the SVG and PDF writers read the sheet's weights before the ISO 128 defaults, so a sheet without a format exports byte-identical. The title block fills SIZE (`1:1 · A3`), UNITS (the printed units) and DATE (the latest saved revision's, until one is typed) automatically; title, scale and projection were already automatic. A content gate refuses `format` below v70. Evidence: `test/drawing-sheet-format.test.ts` (validation by name; inch, comma and D°M'S" text with carried seconds and dms tolerances; a 25.4 mm kernel box dimensioned `1 ±0.005` in inches; SVG/PDF weights, SIZE, UNITS and automatic/typed DATE; one undoable set/clear, replay, reload and the v69 gate), `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (the panel writes one format, clears it when every field is default, refuses an out-of-bounds weight) and `test/e2e/drawing-properties.spec.ts` (a 38.1 mm box reads `1.5` then `1,5` in inches, SVG with the weight, units and size, reload). Parts lists and balloons (schema v80, built on v78; [design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md#parts-lists-and-balloons-d02-schema-v80)), built from the AS03 BOM table model (`assemblyBomTable`): the Parts list tool (P) places the assembly's BOM table on the paper — a ruled table with a bold header, the chosen BOM columns (default item, quantity, part) and numeric columns aligned to their end — and the Balloon tool (O) names a picked part's item number in a circle with a leader to its edge. Rows, cells and numbers are read from the table each time the sheet is drawn, so the drawing, the Assemble panel and the CSV agree; a list with no assembly (`no-bom`), a balloon whose part left the assembly (`not-in-bom`) and one on an edge of a part in another row (`not-on-part`) break by name; a re-picked balloon names its new edge's part, and its leader points only at what its view draws of the edge (inside a detail's circle, broken as `leader-hidden` when none shows). Both export through the one page model to PDF, SVG and DXF. A content gate refuses them below v80. Evidence: `test/drawing-bom-balloons.test.ts` (validation by name; on kernel bodies an assembled plate and two copies of a pin listed as two rows, balloons reading 1 and 2 from either pin's component, the ruled layout and SVG text; `no-bom`, `not-in-bom` and `not-on-part`; a leader kept to the drawn pieces of its edge and broken when the view draws none; chosen columns in order; drafts from both tools, a balloon re-picked onto a pin naming the pin's item, reload and the v78 gate) and `test/e2e/drawing-bom-balloons.spec.ts` (an imported assembly's drawing gets a parts list and a balloon reading 1, exported and reopened). Sheet images (schema v92, built on v91; [design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md#sheet-images-d02-schema-v92)), completing the row's notes and sheet images: the Image tool (I) places one of the project's reference images (I05) on the paper by its id, so its pixels stay in the content-addressed store and travel as reference images do; Properties chooses the picture and its printed width, and its height keeps the picture's shape. A picture deleted from the project breaks the image by name (`image-missing`) rather than rebinding. Pictures are drawn beneath the lines into the preview, embedded in SVG as data URLs and in PDF as DCT image objects (a JPEG's bytes as they are, a PNG re-encoded on white), and left out of DXF R12 with a status note; a sheet without one exports byte-identical. A content gate refuses them below v92. Evidence: `test/drawing-sheet-images.test.ts` (validation by name, the printed size from the picture's aspect, centred placement and box, a page without pictures unchanged and one off the paper dropped, the `image-missing` break, the tool's draft, the JPEG frame reader, SVG embedding and its refusal without the bytes, the PDF image object once per picture with its placement, DXF leaving it out, the v92 gate) and `test/e2e/drawing-sheet-images.spec.ts` (a PNG inserted in Build, placed on a sheet, resized, exported to SVG and PDF with its pixels, and reopened). Title-block layouts (schema v94, built on v93; [design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md#title-block-layouts-d02-schema-v94)): a sheet's title block is the compact one (unchanged) or the shop one, which adds material, finish, checked and approved by, the company and a general-tolerance note in a taller block the view area makes room for, through the one page model to preview, PDF, SVG and DXF; Sheet Properties choose the layout and edit its fields. A content gate refuses them below v94. With this, sheet images (v92) and the arc and three-point angles (v82), every item of the row's contract is delivered: the full dimension set with prefixes, suffixes and tolerances, centrelines, centre and intersection marks, notes and sheet images, drawing-level units, angle format, precision, decimal separator and line weights, automatic title-block fields and layouts, hole callouts from M01 metadata, and explicit repair. Evidence: `test/drawing-title-block-layouts.test.ts` (validation of the layout and fields by name, the compact page unchanged with shop fields stored, the shop block's height, view area, labels, values and four rules, SVG and PDF text, the layout and field commands, the v94 gate) and `test/e2e/drawing-title-block-layouts.spec.ts` (a sheet switched to the shop layout, its material and tolerance note typed, in the preview and the PDF, and reopened). | D01, dimension semantics from S01/S02; M01 for hole metadata. Product D-2. |
| <a id="d03"></a>D03 | Complete | Section/detail views with reliable exact curves, clipping/cropping and correct scale: a section from a drawn line with the result dragged into place along its arrow; a detail from a drawn circle with its own scale and hidden-line toggle. Section view slice (schema v47, [design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md#implementation-decisions-d03-section-views-schema-v47)): a section line dragged across a front, top or side view (snapped to the view's middle or a circle's centre) makes a section view, moved into place along its arrows and kept aligned with its source on the projection angle's side. `DrawingView.section` stores only the source, a label and the plane's depth. Each body is cut by an exact half-space boolean in the geometry worker and projected like any view, so the cut outline is kernel-sampled exact edges; cut faces are hatched, hidden lines follow the view's toggle, and the cutting-plane line (chain, thick ends, arrows, letters) and "SECTION A-A" caption go through the one page model to preview, PDF and SVG. Where the pinned kernel refuses the exact cut (most planes through a cylindrical hole in a box), the body is cut from its tessellation and labelled approximate, without dimensionable cut edges; a plane that cuts nothing is refused on the view and blocks export. Sections follow the sheet's explicit Update and reopen from the saved document. D02 dimensions resolve on the cut body's own edges: a blind Ø8 bore 18 deep, cut down its axis, dimensions its floor at 8 and its depth at 18, and a moved floor breaks the dimension rather than rebinding it. Evidence: `test/drawing-sections.test.ts` (schema refusals, commands, exact cut of the bored cylinder with hatch area 456 mm², floor and depth dimensions, broken reference, refused plane, tessellation fallback on the bored plate), `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (hatched section with line and caption, refusal without retry, drag-and-click creation) and `test/e2e/drawing-sections.spec.ts` (bored plate: section across the bore, Ø12 dimension on the section's circle, SVG/PDF contents, reload). Open: detail views, free and stepped section planes, lineage for cut edges, exact cuts the kernel refuses. Auxiliary views (schema v81, built on v80; [design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md#auxiliary-views-d03-schema-v81)): the Auxiliary view tool (U) picks a straight edge a front, top or side view draws true length and inclined, and a click on one side places a view seen square to it, so an inclined face is drawn true size. The view stores its frame (direction of sight and page-right, square to each other and to the source's sight), keeps its source's orientation and bodies, is placed freely with an arrow and letter beside its source and a `VIEW A` caption, and reads true lengths in its plane; one `drawingViewFrame` now serves every projection, measurement and pick. Horizontal or vertical edges are refused (a principal view already sees them). A content gate refuses it below v81. Evidence: `test/drawing-auxiliary-views.test.ts` (frame and source validation by name; on a box turned 30° about Y the sloping edge picked, the frame square to it with page-up the front's sight, the face projected true size 20 × 10, a 20 mm dimension on the auxiliary view, the VIEW A caption and arrow; bodies following the source, delete and the v80 gate) and `test/e2e/drawing-auxiliary-views.spec.ts` (an imported tilted block, its sloping edge picked in the front view, VIEW A placed, exported and reopened). Detail views (schema v78, built on v77; [design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md#detail-views-d03-schema-v78)): the Detail view tool (K) circles a region of a view by a drag from its centre and places it, enlarged at its own scale (by default the smallest preset at least twice the sheet's), anywhere on the sheet. The detail looks as its source does (orientation and bodies follow it) with its own hidden-line toggle; what it draws, offers to pick and points leaders at is cut to its circle; the source shows the circle and letter and the detail its boundary and `DETAIL A (4:1)` caption, through the one page model to preview, PDF, SVG and DXF. Dimensions on a detail read true model values. The panel edits its scale and radius; deleting the source deletes its details. A content gate refuses details below v78. Evidence: `test/drawing-detail-views.test.ts` (validation by name and the shared label sequence; the drag → region → draft at twice the sheet's scale; the transform and footprint; on a kernel box the detail's strokes inside its circle with the corner's edge magnified 4×, the caption and letter in SVG, picking lines cut to the circle and a 40 mm dimension on the detail; circle clipping; scale/radius update, the source's bodies, delete with undo and the v75 gate) and `test/e2e/drawing-detail-views.spec.ts` (a circle dragged on the front view's corner, placed by a click, captioned, exported and reloaded). Free section planes (schema v91, built on v90; [design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md#free-section-planes-d03-schema-v91)): a section line drawn at an angle across a front, top or side view (more than 10° off horizontal or vertical; through the press point, snapped to the view's middle or a circle's centre) cuts the part by an inclined plane, and a click beside it places the section seen square to that plane from that side, placed freely with its `SECTION A-A` caption. `DrawingSectionCut.frame` stores the frame like an auxiliary view's; the geometry worker's exact half-space boolean turns its box into the plane's frame, so the cut outline is kernel-sampled exact edges and the cut face is hatched, with the tessellation fallback unchanged; principal sections cut exactly as before. A content gate refuses a frame below v91. Evidence: `test/drawing-free-sections.test.ts` (frame validation by name, the projection frame and plane, the v83 gate, a free section staying put when its source moves and its plane moving along its normal, the tool's free and square lines and its placement and frame, and on a kernel block a 45° cut through its middle: one exact cut face, Z√2 edges, a hatched area of Z√2 × Y, the line at 45° through the source's middle with arrows along the sight, letters and caption) and `test/e2e/drawing-free-sections.spec.ts` (a 45° drag across a block's front view, the section placed beside it, hatched, exact, exported to SVG and reopened). Still open: stepped section lines and lineage for cut edges. Cut-edge lineage ([design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md#cut-edge-lineage-d03)): a section's edges carry ADR-013 references where they verify — an edge the cut left unchanged keeps its model edge's reference, and an edge the cut made, lying in a section plane beside exactly one named model face, is named `section.edge.<face>@<plane>` — so a dimension on a section's own edge follows a model edit through the sheet's Update instead of breaking, while ambiguous traces (a plane crossing one cylinder twice) and bodies without lineage stay hash-only and break as before. No schema change. With auxiliary views (v81), free and stepped section planes (v91, v93) and detail views (v78), the row's contract is delivered; cuts the pinned kernel refuses still fall back to the labelled approximate tessellation cut, a kernel limit the drawing names on the view. Evidence: `test/drawing-sections.test.ts` (the bored cylinder's floor named by its section lineage, a dimension picked on it following the bore deepened from 18 to 22 to read 8 at the new floor, the two traces on the one cylindrical face left unnamed, hash-only references still breaking) and `test/e2e/drawing-section-lineage.spec.ts` (a horizontal dimension on a box section's own top edge reads 30, the box is widened to 45, and after the Update it reads 45). Stepped sections (schema v93, built on v92; [design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md#stepped-sections-d03-schema-v93)): an aligned section's cutting line steps to other depths along its run (`DrawingSectionCut.steps`, edited in the section's Properties). The geometry worker removes one strip per segment with exact axis-aligned box booleans, the shallower strip's box running past each step so only the deeper one makes the jog face; every segment's cut face is hatched, the section view draws no line where it steps (ISO 128-44) while the jog's edges stay dimensionable, and the line on the source jogs, thick at its ends and every change of direction. A cut the kernel refuses refuses the view by name. A content gate refuses steps below v93. Evidence: `test/drawing-stepped-sections.test.ts` (validation by name, the step axis and worker cut, the plane key, edit and removal commands, the v93 gate; on a kernel block cut at z = 3 left of x = 20 and z = 7 right of it, two cut faces hatched 800 mm², no drawn edge along the step, a 4 mm jog edge kept for dimensions, the jogged line's corners on the page and six thick strokes) and `test/e2e/drawing-stepped-sections.spec.ts` (a square section across a block's front view given a step to z = 8 in Properties, its line jogging with thick corners, hatched, exported to SVG and reopened). Still open: lineage for cut edges, and exact cuts the pinned kernel refuses. | D01, M08 for exact sections; product D-3. |
| <a id="d04"></a>D04 | Complete | Whole-sketch/flattened-view DXF export and DXF sketch import with units/entity fidelity and malformed-input tests. Delivered slices: exact section curves export as DXF (#342) and the writer refuses non-finite/oversized coordinates and zero-wire input by name (#349; `test/dxf-writer.test.ts`). The first whole-sketch export contract is now source-grounded in [`docs/plans/sketch-dxf-export-plan.md`](docs/plans/sketch-dxf-export-plan.md): exact local-plane LINE/CIRCLE/ARC output, exact rectangle/polygon line expansion, millimetre conversion, construction exclusion, and fail-closed unsupported/text/plane cases. SVG import/export is owned by I07. Runtime whole-sketch export is delivered in the adapter and worker: `exportSketchDxf` lowers one saved sketch to exact local-plane LINE/CIRCLE/ARC in millimetres through the existing `writeDxf`, expands rectangles/polygons to their edge lines, excludes construction geometry, and refuses by name (`sketch-not-found`, `parameters-invalid`, `stale-plane-attachment`, `invalid-plane-frame`, `unsupported-object`, `invalid-geometry`, `construction-only`, `writer-refused`) (this PR; `test/sketch-dxf-export.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/sketchDxf.test.ts`). DXF sketch import (this PR): an ASCII DXF imports through Import CAD files or a drop as one undoable sketch on Top (XY) named after the file. LINE, CIRCLE and ARC map exactly; LWPOLYLINE/POLYLINE segments become lines, and their bulges the arcs they describe; `$INSUNITS` (inch, foot, mm, cm, m and smaller units) converts into the document units, and a file declaring none is read in the document units and says so. Annotations and blocks (TEXT, MTEXT, DIMENSION, HATCH, INSERT…) are left out and counted, curves a sketch has no exact object for (ELLIPSE, SPLINE, 3D polylines, entities off the XY plane) are refused and counted, and binary DXF, a missing ENTITIES section, malformed group codes, unknown units, more than 2,000 curves or over 20 MB refuse the file by name. Review hardening (#723): reject every nonzero elevation (including legacy vertices), refuse curve/spline-fit parent flags and generated fitted/control vertices before expansion, and require positive CIRCLE/ARC radii. Regression evidence: `test/dxf-import.test.ts` (round trip through the sketch DXF writer, bulge direction, a slot closing into one region, units, counts, refusals, signed/tiny elevations, fitted flags/vertices and zero/negative radii) and `dxf-sketch-import.spec.ts` (inch slot imported and extruded). The sketch menu export entry, flattened views, Bézier/ellipse/spline import and a user choice of units for unitless files remain open. Drawing and flattened-view DXF export ([design](docs/plans/drawing-mvp-design.md#drawing-dxf-export-d04)): Export DXF writes a drawing sheet — its views at the sheet's scale, hidden lines, sections, dimensions with SOLID arrowheads, notes and the title block — as a DXF R12 drawing in paper millimetres, one layer per line kind (`VISIBLE`, `HIDDEN` with an ISO 128 hidden line type, `DIMENSION`, `CENTER` with the long-dash-dot type, `HATCH`, `CUTTING`, `BORDER`, `THIN`, `TEXT`), deterministic and refused under the same conditions as PDF and SVG. Curves are the page's polylines (exact edges sampled to the display tolerance), labelled as such. The export dialog gains the sketch DXF entry, writing one saved sketch through the existing exact sketch exporter. The R12 writer gains SOLID and the HIDDEN and CENTER line types, leaving flat-pattern files byte-identical. Evidence: `test/drawing-dxf-export.test.ts` (every page stroke and text on its layer, two arrowheads, the dimension's text at its page position, Ø escaped, deterministic bytes, read back through the app's DXF importer with its text counted, the file name, the unchanged DASHED table and SOLID corner order), `ImageExportDialog.test.tsx` (the sketch DXF option) and `test/e2e/drawing-dxf-export.spec.ts` (a dimensioned sheet downloaded as `Plate-Sheet-1.dxf` with its layers, arrowheads and text). DXF ellipse and spline import and the sketch menu export ([design](docs/plans/sketch-dxf-export-plan.md#dxf-ellipse-and-spline-import-d04)): ELLIPSE imports as the sketch's exact ellipse or elliptical arc (a -Z ellipse reversed to run counter-clockwise from +Z), and SPLINE as its exact control spline wherever the sketch object is the same curve: clamped uniform degree 2 or 3 (knots matched up to an affine change of parameter, within 1e-9 of their range), periodic uniform ones as closed splines, equal weights as non-rational, degree 1 as lines, and a clamped loop split by knot insertion into two halves that close a region. Unequal weights, other knot vectors, higher degrees, over 64 control points and fit-point-only splines are counted by name; malformed lists refuse the file. A sketch's history menu gains Export DXF…, opening the export on that sketch as DXF. Evidence: `test/dxf-import-curves.test.ts` (every imported ellipse and spline compared point for point with the file's own curve evaluated from its definition, -Z ellipses, full-turn ranges, units, regions closed by a diameter, a periodic ring and three split loops, each refusal and malformed list by name, the undoable sketch), `test/dxf-import.test.ts` (refusal counts and status) and `test/e2e/dxf-curve-import.spec.ts` (an inch cam of a periodic SPLINE around an ELLIPSE bore imported and extruded; a sketch exported from its menu as DXF). With this the row's open list is closed: the sketch menu entry and ellipse/spline import here (a single-span SPLINE is a Bézier, imported exactly), flattened views with the drawing DXF export (#1640), and the unit choice for unitless files with I04 (#752, `ImportUnitsDialog`; `test/dxf-import.test.ts` "says how a unitless file was read").
