| <a id="d06"></a>D06 | Complete | Auto-dimensioning: one request (a Draw button, or the assistant) places a complete, non-redundant dimension set — every feature size and location stated once from a datum, no closed chains or derived values, each in a view that sees it true size. A deterministic generator and checker own the guarantee; the assistant chooses style and scope and its proposal is shown only once the checker passes. Design intent (history, else recognized features with virtual sharps) before raw geometry. [Design](docs/plans/drawing-auto-dimensioning-plan.md): slices A checker → B schema (counts, Ø on linear, silhouette/virtual-sharp anchors, chamfer, reference, provenance) → C button → D imports → E assistant → F sections. Slice E, first half: asked from Draw, the assistant proposes dimensions and notes on the open sheet. `add_drawing_annotations` and `remove_drawing_annotations` (strict schema, nullable fields) name edges only by the refs the sheet digest lists per drawn view (`v1:e4`, with page-mm ends, centres and face-on circles); digest validation grounds each ref to its model edge, and the app adds the lineage a pick would store. A proposal is previewed on the sheet only once every annotation resolves and the dimension check finds nothing repeated; missing sizes are reported in the digest's audit, not refused. It applies as one undoable step; model edits stay out of Draw. Consent copy v2 lists `digest.drawing`. The deterministic generator (slice C) plugs in at `preflightDrawingPatch`. Evidence: `packages/ai-contracts/src/drawing-annotations.test.ts`, `test/drawing-assistant-operations.test.ts` (real kernel plate: digest, two dimensions applied and undone as one step, replay, refusals) and `AssistantPanel.drawing.test.tsx` (fake provider proposes two dimensions). Slice C, the Auto-dimension command: Draw's Dimension check has an Auto-dimension button that, with no assistant, previews on the sheet exactly the dimensions the check finds missing and adds them as one undoable command on Apply. It reads the checker's own requirement model: one radius or diameter per unstated size group, and per model axis one baseline dimension from the lowest datum position to each group of positions not yet linked, so it closes no chain and states nothing derived. Each goes in a principal view that sees it true size and visible, preferring the view holding the body's other dimensions (never the isometric view), on edges whose identity no other edge shares. Linear dimensions stack outside their view, shortest nearest the part; nothing overlaps text, other views or the title block, and all stays on the paper. Existing dimensions are kept and counted; what the check cannot place yet is skipped and reported. Evidence: `test/drawing-auto-dimension.test.ts` (on real kernel bodies — box, stepped part, 2 × 2 hole plate, bolt-circle flange, rounded corner, turned foot: after Apply the check reports zero missing and zero repeated, every layout is clash-free, a rerun adds nothing, a user's dimension is kept) and `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (preview, Apply as one transaction, Escape cancels, disabled for viewers). Slice A, the dimension check: Draw's panel reports, for a sheet that matches the model, which sizes and positions its dimensions state, which are missing, which are repeated (a second dimension on one size group, a closed chain such as 50 = 41 + 9, or a span to a blend's tangent the radius already fixes) and which no D02 dimension can place yet (revolved blend radii, chamfers and tapers, bolt-circle pitch diameters, positions only closed circles mark). Positions per model axis are planar faces square to it and hole/boss axes; blends tangent to a face are positioned by their radius; three or more equal holes equidistant from a turned axis are one bolt circle, not per-hole X/Y positions. Read from the current model like every dimension; nothing stored, no schema change. Evidence: `test/drawing-dimension-audit.test.ts` (on real kernel bodies: the turned foot of the design, a stepped part's closed chain, a plate's hole located by its centre, a bolt circle, a rounded corner's derived tangent) and `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (missing list on a ready sheet; a repeated dimension selects from the check). Slice B, schema v48 (builds on v47, D03 section views): Draw annotations can now dimension a turned or chamfered part. A rim seen side-on is picked as its silhouette end (`silhouette` point), two face outlines as their virtual sharp (`virtual-sharp` point, from the kernel's exact planes, cylinders and cones), and a cone or edge-on plane as a Chamfer dimension (`size × angle`, through blends). Radius, diameter and chamfer dimensions take a count (`4×`), a linear span a Ø prefix, any dimension a reference flag (brackets), and any annotation generated provenance. Older clients refuse the document; load, save and the account edge refuse malformed additions by name; nothing stores a value. The checker now counts rim silhouettes as position marks, a symmetric Ø span as a diameter or bolt-circle pitch, and chamfer dimensions as chamfer groups: the turned foot's Z positions, both chamfers and a bolt circle's pitch move from "can't dimension yet" to satisfiable, and a seven-dimension foot sheet passes with nothing missing or redundant. Auto-dimension (slice C) ends position spans on rim silhouettes, so it now places the foot's heights, and reports chamfers and pitch diameters it leaves to the person. Evidence: `test/drawing-dimension-schema.test.ts` (real kernel bodies: the foot as a revolved profile, a planar chamfer, a two-hole plate; refusals; SVG/PDF byte-stable across a reload; picking), `test/drawing-dimension-audit.test.ts`, and the e2e in `test/e2e/drawing-dimensions.spec.ts` picking a cylinder's rims for its height and a reference Ø. Slice D, schema v49 (builds on v48, slice B): Auto-dimension dimensions a history-less STEP import from the features its faces show. The plan's turned foot, exported through the kernel and re-imported (11 analytic faces, no history), gets exactly the plan's eight dimensions: Ø40, Ø33, Ø25, 2× 4 × 45°, 3× R4, 9, 29 and 50, with no blend tangent height, no Ø32 and no 3.5. The checker groups a step chamfer that runs into a smaller diameter with an end chamfer of equal angle on the same diameter, and locates it at the corner it cut (a new `chamfer-corner` point, the 29). It also states revolved blends from their torus face seen side-on (a radius dimension on a face, `3× R4`), and calls blend tangent heights, a chamfer's own sharps and the contact Ø32 derived. Auto-dimension now places counted chamfer and blend dimensions and counted diameters, and gives every dimension it places provenance naming its requirement. On an import, provenance marks a span to a face no feature explains as raw geometry (`basis: 'geometry'`), and the preview and inspector say so. Evidence: `test/drawing-import-dimensioning.test.ts` (the foot import, a counterbored-plate import and an L-step import whose inner faces are marked, each placed clear of the rest of the sheet; v49 validation), `test/drawing-dimension-audit.test.ts` and `test/drawing-auto-dimension.test.ts`, where the history foot now passes with eight dimensions as well. Slice F: section-aware dimensioning. The checker and Auto-dimension measure a D03 section view's exact cut edges, so a position inside the part (a bore's floor, a pocket's depth) is stated and placed on the visible cut instead of on hidden lines or a rim's hidden silhouette; a requirement only a section marks is reported as "which only section A-A shows". What Auto-dimension can place only on hidden lines is named in its preview ("Add a section through the part to dimension … on visible lines"); it never adds a section itself. A section the kernel cut from the tessellation carries no dimensions and the advice says so. Evidence: `test/drawing-section-dimensioning.test.ts` (real kernel: a Ø20 × 30 bushing with a blind Ø8 bore gets 0–12 and 0–30 on section A-A with zero missing, redundant and unmeasurable, and a 30 − 12 depth is flagged as a closed chain; a pocketed block's floor moves from hidden lines onto the section; the turned foot's advice, and its exact cut refused on the pin) and `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (Auto-dimension places Y on a section). Angles and counterbore callouts. A slope square to one model axis is a requirement, not unmeasurable: one angle per plane (mirrored copies share it) and one corner locating it where it meets a face square to an axis (a real edge before a virtual sharp through a blend); its other corners are derived, and a compound slope stays unmeasurable. The check reads an existing D02 angle dimension between an edge of the slope and an axis-aligned edge in its plane, and Auto-dimension places one in the view along the slope. Schema v50 adds a counterbored hole's callout on a diameter (`counterbore`, the bore's rim): it reads "2× Ø6 THRU, CBORE Ø11 × 3 DEEP" from the model and states the hole and bore diameters and the bore's (and a blind hole's) depth, so Auto-dimension places one callout instead of two diameters and a floor span. A bore's floor must face its opening, so a stepped shaft is never read as one. Evidence: `test/drawing-auto-dimension.test.ts` (sloped part: 135° angle, its corner, R5), `test/drawing-dimension-audit.test.ts`, `test/drawing-import-dimensioning.test.ts` (imported counterbored plate: callout, three requirements stated, a second height span repeats it; v50 validation). Check and placement (PR #1134). Missing lists only what Auto-dimension can place: a position counts as marked only by an edge a dimension can name and only where one view marks it with the datum; otherwise it is reported as not dimensionable yet, with why. A blend tangent to two non-parallel planes is derived (centre and tangent lines). Finding rows are keyed by body and index. Spans ending on an interior feature are placed inside the view through open space; leaders try short placements beside the feature first; text keeps off the editor's view names. Evidence: `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (duplicate findings, shared-hash positions), `test/drawing-dimension-audit.test.ts` (sloped blend), `test/drawing-auto-dimension.test.ts` (hole located inside its view, overall sizes outside); the drawing suites joined the CI smoke selection. Countersink callouts and outside angles (schema v51). A hole size group whose every copy carries a coaxial cone of one chamfer group opening onto it is countersunk; one diameter callout naming the cone's outer rim reads "2× Ø6 THRU, CSK Ø12 × 90°" from the model and states the hole and the cone (instead of a chamfer dimension), and a blind hole's depth. An angle dimension can turn one ray onto its edge's extension (`outside`), so Auto-dimension states a slope as 45° off the part rather than 135° inside. Evidence: `test/drawing-auto-dimension.test.ts` (Hole-feature countersunk plate: one callout, no chamfer, nothing missing; sloped part: 45° outside), `test/drawing-import-dimensioning.test.ts` (v51 validation). Bolt-circle pitch diameters are placed: Auto-dimension states each unstated bolt circle with the v48 Ø span between two opposite holes' centres, in a view along its axis where the pair lines up across the page ("Ø20" on the 4× Ø3 flange). An odd count has no opposite pair and is reported for a note. Evidence: `test/drawing-auto-dimension.test.ts` (flange: one Ø span, centre anchors, nothing missing or skipped, stated = required). Close-out (audit of the row contract against main, 2026-11-20). Slice E's second half: the assistant now dimensions a sheet through the deterministic generator. `auto_dimension_drawing` (`viewIds` or null, `dimensionKinds` or null among linear, diameter, radius, chamfer, angle) states only scope and style; the Draw workspace runs Auto-dimension on the sheet's own annotations, `preflightDrawingPatch` keeps what the scope covers and returns what it leaves out (excluded kinds, dimensions that belong in other views, generator skips, section advice) for the preview status, refuses a scope that leaves nothing to add, and still gates on the dimension check. A Ø span counts as a diameter; Apply applies exactly the previewed plan, and only for that proposal on that document; the request is proposed alone and never names the isometric view (docs/plans/drawing-auto-dimensioning-plan.md#assistant-integration). The plan's corpus gains the bracket with fillets and a slot: zero missing, zero repeated, nothing skipped. Evidence: `test/drawing-assistant-operations.test.ts` (real kernel: all-views plan applied as one undo step, scope reports, refusals, Ø-span scoping, Apply of the previewed plan), `test/drawing-auto-dimension.test.ts` (bracket), `apps/web/src/lib/assistant/describe.test.ts`, `test/e2e/drawing-assistant.spec.ts` (fake provider: a radii-only scope reports the three spans it leaves out; the whole sheet's three spans previewed, applied and undone as one step). The fake-provider e2e the plan asks of slice E already shipped with #1081. Left open, by design: ordinate style (needs a new annotation kind and schema, outside the row's contract) and choosing an individual datum per request (the generator's one-datum rule decides; a specific dimension is still proposed with `add_drawing_annotations`). | D02; D03 for internal features; M05 recognizers for imports. |
| <a id="d07"></a>D07 | Complete | Geometric tolerancing: datum features and feature control frames on drawing views (ASME Y14.5 and ISO GPS symbols), general-tolerance blocks and hole and thread tolerance classes, then the same annotations stored on the 3D model (model-based definition) and written as STEP AP242 semantic PMI. Annotations stay attached through model edits or show as stale, as D02 requires. Slice D, STEP AP242 semantic PMI ([design](docs/plans/drawing-gdt-design.md#slice-d-step-ap242-semantic-pmi)): an AP242 File › STEP carries the model's PMI as semantic PMI, each entry on the exact `ADVANCED_FACE` it applies to. Faces are resolved on the built solid by lineage, located by the kernel's face order, checked against surface type, and the export is refused on any mismatch. A datum feature is a `DATUM_FEATURE` related to its `DATUM`. A frame is its characteristic's tolerance entity (complex where datum references or modifiers need it), with its magnitude in the file's length unit, a `DATUM_SYSTEM` of compartments with Ⓜ/Ⓛ modifiers, and a cylindrical `TOLERANCE_ZONE` for a Ø zone. With Objects folders, each entry goes on its body's own part product, and a frame names only datums on that part. Unresolvable entries are left out, and AP214 files carry none. With this the row's contract is met: - datum features and feature control frames on drawing views (slice A); - general-tolerance blocks and hole and thread tolerance classes (B); - the same annotations stored on the model (C) and written as AP242 PMI (D), all attached through model edits or shown broken. Evidence: `test/step-pmi.test.ts`: - on a real export of a box and a pin, datum A on the plane z = 0, perpendicularity to A on x = 20, position Ø0.1 Ⓜ to A Ⓜ on the radius-4 cylinder about x = 40, and flatness on datum A's face, each face read back from its own surface geometry; - every entity reference resolving; - the inch file's magnitudes in inches, and the AP214 file without PMI; - a stale face left out; - with Objects folders, the box's datum and frames on the box's own part shape, and the pin's frame naming the box's datum left out; - a mismatched shell refused, and a `#` inside a solid name ignored. `test/e2e/step-pmi.spec.ts` makes a datum and a parallelism frame in Objects and exports them through File › STEP as AP242. Slice A, datum feature symbols and feature control frames on drawing views (schema v98, built on v97; [design](docs/plans/drawing-gdt-design.md#slice-a-datum-symbols-and-feature-control-frames-schema-v98)): the Datum feature tool (Q) declares a datum letter (A–Z without I, O and Q, then doubled) on an edge or on a size dimension or frame, and the Feature control frame tool (F) states any of the fourteen ASME Y14.5-2018 / ISO 1101 characteristics with a tolerance in the sheet's units, an optional Ø zone, Ⓜ/Ⓛ modifiers and up to three datum references, pointing at an edge with a leader or attached under a size dimension. The validator refuses what the standards forbid by name (a datum on a form tolerance, orientation or runout without one, Ø or a modifier where the characteristic takes none, a datum named twice). They resolve on every draw like any annotation and break by name rather than rebinding: `datum-duplicate`, `datum-undefined` (datums count across every sheet), `host-missing` and `host-broken` (cascading from a dimension to its frame to a datum symbol on it). Symbols and circled modifiers are drawn as strokes, since sheet text is WinAnsi Helvetica, and go through the one page model to preview, PDF, SVG and DXF; a sheet without them lays out unchanged. Evidence: `test/drawing-gdt.test.ts` (validation by name; on kernel bodies a datum A on a box's bottom, a perpendicularity frame on its side and a Ø0.1 Ⓜ position frame under a pin's diameter with datum B hanging from it, laid out per Y14.5; each break; a parameter-widened box followed by lineage; every characteristic's symbol inside its cell; SVG, PDF and DXF text and byte-stable reload; tool drafts, re-pick onto a host, next letter; undoable commands and the v97 gate), `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (frame properties: form tolerances take no Ø, modifier or datum, perpendicularity names declared datum A, a non-positive tolerance reverts) and `test/e2e/drawing-gdt.spec.ts` (datum A picked on a box, a frame attached under its width dimension made parallel to A, exported to SVG and reopened). Open: general-tolerance blocks and hole/thread tolerance classes, model-based definition and AP242 semantic PMI. Slice B, fits, thread classes and general tolerances (schema v105, built on v101 (#1739); [design](docs/plans/drawing-gdt-design.md#slice-b-fits-thread-classes-and-general-tolerances-schema-v105)): a diameter may state an ISO 286 fit class, printed with the limit deviations ISO 286-1 tables 1–3 state for the size the model has now ("Ø8 H7 +0.015/0"; holes F, G, H, JS and shafts f, g, h, js, k, m, n, p, grades 5–11, up to 500 mm, others refused by name rather than guessed), and a tapped hole's callout an ISO 965 or UN internal thread class ("M6×1-6H"), broken by name on a plain bore (`not-threaded`) or the other system's class (`thread-class-mismatch`). Add general tolerances in Sheet Properties (or W) places a ruled block printing the ISO 2768-1 linear and angular tables for class f, m, c or v (with an ISO 2768-2 class), or ± per number of decimals written and on angles in the sheet's units. Evidence: `test/drawing-fits.test.ts` (ISO 286 limits against eighteen table values including range edges, exact ±IT/2 for js and JS, and k above IT7; validation by name; on kernel bodies Ø8 H7 and g6, Ø25 g6, inch and decimal-comma sheets, a 520 mm pin out of range; an M6 tapped hole classed 6H, refused 2B, a plain tap drill not threaded and reamed H7; the ISO 2768 v/m tables and an inch stated block; layout, SVG, PDF and DXF; the tool draft, undoable commands and the v104 gate), `DrawingWorkspace.test.tsx` (the block drawn, its class and scheme changed) and `test/e2e/drawing-fits.spec.ts` (a pin's diameter fitted H7 and an ISO 2768-mK block placed, exported to SVG and reopened). Slice C, model-based definition (schema v106, built on v105; [design](docs/plans/drawing-gdt-design.md#slice-c-model-based-definition-schema-v106)): datum features and feature control frames are stored on the model itself (`pmi`), each on one face held by lineage then hash, with its label's anchor on that face and the drawing's datum and frame fields (one shared validator; a frame's tolerance in document units; one declaration per datum letter). `pmi.add`/`update`/`delete` are undoable, replay, and never rebuild. A face that no longer resolves leaves the entry broken by name (`body-missing`, `not-found`, `ambiguous`), as does a frame naming an undeclared datum (`datum-undefined`); nothing rebinds, and "Move to selected face" re-points it. With a face selected, Objects adds either, lists them with their state and edits the open one; the viewport labels each at its face with the drawing's own frame and symbol geometry, amber when broken. Evidence: `test/model-pmi.test.ts` (validation by name; commands with undo, replay, storage, no model change and the v105 gate; a parameter-widened box followed by lineage; `datum-undefined` and `body-missing`; drafts with the next letter and unit amounts; the SVG frame) and `test/e2e/model-pmi.spec.ts` (datum A on a box's bottom and a frame on its top made parallel to A in Objects, both labelled in the viewport, reopened). | D02 dimensions and tolerances; D06 output; I02/I04 STEP fidelity and export options. |

## 4. Assemblies

| ID | Status | Outcome and completion evidence | Depends on / legacy item |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="as01"></a>AS01 | Partial | Multi-part documents, explicit part switching and transformed instances with offline save/reopen and a parts browser. Reuse existing schema seeds after design review. First bounded slice, independent of joints: insert another project's bodies into the open document as one import step (the reference CAD ships this and nothing else of assemblies). Before AS02, define source identity, per-instance placement, grounded state and copy-versus-link behavior. Two instances of a supported shared source retain independent placements; source changes update them or mark them stale by contract. Grounded state and source/instance roles stay visible and persist offline; a cross-project import does not imply a live external link ([FreeCAD E07](docs/reviews/freecad-comparison-2026-09-30.md#hands-on-evidence)). Assemble keeps everything in one left sidebar: parts, joints and motion, each opening its controls under its row; the joint tool and the first run use the same sidebar. Bodies not yet in the assembly are listed with Add, fixing a gap where a body made after the first run could never join. Evidence: `AssemblyPanel.test.tsx` and `test/e2e/assembly.spec.ts` (add-body regression). Schema v54 `assembly` section: components reference bodies in the same document (following the bodies' lineage), start at the modelled pose, and are grounded or free; the Assemble workspace adds them in one undoable step from a first-run card. Validation, storage, history and diagnostics redaction cover the section ([plan](docs/plans/assemblies-plan.md)). Evidence: `test/assembly-commands.test.ts`, `test/assembly-kinematics.test.ts`. Cross-project insert, first bounded slice: File › Insert project… copies another project's shown bodies into the open one as one undoable step. The backup is rebuilt in a disposable worker with its own kernel (its imported sources served from the backup, nothing written to the device) and written as one millimetre STEP through the new `exportStepBodies`, which reports each body's run of solids. That STEP imports like any other (one stored source, the same exact check and archive), one body per source body under its name, its chosen colour and opacity, filed in an Objects folder named after the project. A copy with no link back; history, parameters, assembly and drawings stay behind; mesh references and failed bodies are named as left out. Import project… still opens a separate copy. Evidence: `packages/kernel-adapter/src/exact-step-bodies.test.ts`, `test/project-insert.test.ts` (inch target, source references, name collisions), `apps/web/src/lib/stepImportRun.test.ts` (layout, one undo) and `test/e2e/insert-project.spec.ts`. Offline save and reopen of the assembly: components, their grounding and display order reopen from the device store unchanged, and the BOM derived from the reopened copy matches the saved one; concurrent part additions of one body on two devices are refused by the room merge rather than doubled (`apps/web/src/lib/assembly/assemblyPersistence.test.ts`; offline drag and reopen in `test/e2e/assembly.spec.ts`; AS02 records the conflict cases). | Product AS-1. Do not require a 3D constraint solver for rigid placement. |
| <a id="as02"></a>AS02 | Partial | Typed rigid/revolute/slider/cylindrical/ball joints and DOF-aware drag previews; consistent saved transforms and conflict behavior. Shared sample slice ([#1197](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/pull/1197)): the supplied Scissors assembly is available in the launcher with editable geometry and its limited revolute pivot; `test/scissors-demo.test.ts` and `StartScreen.test.tsx` cover exact rebuild, stored state, independent ownership and signed-out access. Set up motion: Assemble's Joints section (and the header's Motion button, once a part moves) opens one card that makes a part slide or turn without picking joint geometry. You choose what stays put, what moves, a world axis with Reverse, a range in mm or degrees, and a cycle and speed. The viewport previews that exact sweep, and Create and play saves an as-built slider or revolute joint with those limits, fixes a floating base first, and plays it. Playback stays transient. A supplied tool-in-holder project defaults to lifting the tool 0–55 mm up Z out of its fixed holder and back. The reference recording shows the same motion made by hand as 0–50 mm Move steps. A fixed moving part, an empty range, and a second motion on a part that already moves (which would close a loop) are refused with the reason; "Pick on the model" hands over to the Joint tool. Evidence: `motionSetup.test.ts` (exact poses at 0/50 mm, turn pivot, reverse, defaults, refusals, preview sweep), `MotionSetupCard.test.tsx`, and the `assembly.spec.ts` CI smoke case (preview moves the part, Create plays back and forth, Pause returns to 0, one undo removes it). Floating-base preview parity: `assemblyView.test.ts` verifies that a base listed after its moving part stays stationary during preview, matches the committed motion, and restores the saved floating poses on Cancel. Preview grounding is transient and preserves the saved document. Included in the web CI smoke selection. The Scissors sample opens in Assemble with its pivot controls expanded, on first seed, catalogue reopen and reset. Reload restores the open pivot while retaining the edited angle. The default is applied at project opening, so later edits, selections and workspace switches remain under user control; other projects keep their existing launch behavior. No geometry, saved model format or dependency changes. Acceptance covers desktop/mobile signed-out opening, immediate angle/Play access, edited-copy reopening and reload, and the ordinary assembly path. Regression coverage lives in `assembleWorkspace.test.ts` and the CI smoke cases in `assembly.spec.ts`. The Scissors sample's pivot now opens the way scissors do: its limits are 0–55° (opening at 30°), so spreading the loops opens the blades instead of driving the thumb loop through the finger loop (`test/scissors-demo.test.ts` checks the loop gap grows across the whole range). The shared sample catalogue includes the supplied `Scissors-Revolute-Pivot-Assembly.openzcad` as an editable Scissors project. The bundled template retains its sketches, features, body colours, three components, grounding and revolute joint: −30° initially, limited to −55°–0°. It needs no account or remote source assets and loads only when opened. Each device seeds its own project with the current user's ownership; reopening uses that edited copy, and the existing reset action restores the template. The supplied backup has no stored design revisions. The sample promises no A/B/C walkthrough and stores a checkpoint of the complete assembly instead. Exported owner/project identity, undo snapshots and derived meshes are omitted from the template; geometry is rebuilt through the ordinary exact-kernel path. Acceptance: rebuild three exact solids without warnings; preserve editable features and the joint's values, limits and grounding; isolate separate seeds; open the catalogue entry, change the angle and retain it after reload. `test/scissors-demo.test.ts` checks the exact rebuild, component grounding, joint state, stored checkpoint and independent seeds. It runs in the CI unit smoke selection. `StartScreen.test.tsx` checks the signed-out catalogue entry; the existing demo parity harness also checks all three solids for watertight meshes, replayed volumes and recorded exact face counts. Local production-preview Chromium checks at 1440 × 1000 and 390 × 844 confirm the catalogue opens while signed out. In Assemble, changing −30° to −45° turns the moving blade, and reload retains −45°, three parts and zero warnings. The browser console reports no errors or warnings. Source comparison confirms the geometry nodes, commands and assembly match the supplied backup. This is local verification; merge and production availability remain separate. No schema, dependency or deployment changes are required. Typed rigid, revolute, slider, cylindrical, ball and planar joints between picked frames, as-built when the frames coincide; exact tree kinematics with loop-closing joints reported as not solved; a drag moves the nearest movable joint and soft-stops at its limits, one undo step per drag. Evidence: `test/assembly-kinematics.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/assembly/*.test.ts`. Gear coupling (schema v56, `assembly.couplings`): meshed gears turn together. A coupling is one linear relation between two joint values, `driven = ratio × driver + offset`: −z₁/z₂ for an external pair, +z₁/z₂ for a pinion in a ring, pitch radius × π/180 per degree for a rack and its pinion, the offset holding the tooth phase the pair was placed at. Joint values are settled first as plain numbers and the tree is then placed as before. Couplings never form a loop, so each group has one free value and the solve is exact. One member leads and the rest follow, and the leader is named rather than guessed from values: each caller passes the motions being driven (most recent first) beside the values, so a dragged or typed motion leads even over a value an earlier scrub left on screen, a study's tracks lead in track order wherever their values stand, and `assembly.pose.set` stores the drivers so the saved pose is the one shown. The paths that move joints each carry them: viewport drag and its cancel, the joint's slider and number, Animate, study playback and scrubbing, View playback, and keying a pose in an open study (which keys the motion the study already tracks in that group, so coupled joints share one track, and only a group the pose really moved: moving an unrelated joint never rewrites a key that a limit holds short of, and a part put back where the study shows it is no edit, since movement is judged on the driving motion in its own units rather than on values the ratio chain has scaled; a real move is keyed however small it is on a geared-down tracked shaft). The moved motion is required: the runtime keeps what the user moved apart from what a scrub or playback drives, and a pose that writes into a coupled group without naming a motion of it is refused rather than judged from the track. The lead is clamped to the range every member's limit allows (a driven joint's limit stops its driver); a drag, an Animate sweep and View's joint playback all read that narrowed range. Refused by code (`coupling:*`): an unknown joint, a joint coupled to itself, a rigid joint or a motion the joint lacks, a joint that never moves, a zero or non-finite ratio, a second link between motions already coupled (`redundant` at the same ratio, `conflict` at another) and limits that leave no shared position. A coupled joint must stay movable: an edit that would stop one (fixing a coupled gear in place) is refused until the coupling is removed, and storage validation rejects a coupling on a joint the solve cannot move. A coupling is removed with either of its joints, and a joint's details name it and offer Remove. Place gears gains a Motion step: Turn together (where the card starts whenever it can be made) gives each gear a revolute joint about its own axis (a rack a slider along its teeth, limited to its length) on the part the pair is mounted on, plus the coupling, as one undo step; Fixed together saves the rigid joint as before and is all a bare pair can have. A floating mount is fixed, a fixed first gear is released, and a first gear that already turns on a joint about its axis keeps it, so a train is built gear by gear. Refused by code (`gear-coupling:*`): no part to mount the pair on, a first gear held by a rigid joint or moving on a joint off its axis, joints that would close a loop, and a mount nothing holds still. The ratio and its sign come from the M14 helper (`meshGearPair`) and the placed axes ([plan](docs/plans/assemblies-plan.md)). A coupling's ratio is fixed when the pair is placed; when a gear's teeth no longer give it, the joint's details and the reopened card warn and ask for the pair to be placed again. The coupling records its gear pair (`gears`, component ids), so the check reads the real gears also when one is keyed to a shaft or two share a shaft, and the coupling goes when either gear leaves the assembly; removing a part or a joint in the sidebar names the couplings that went with it. v56 sits on top of v55 (M14 partial-span teeth, which merged first): a v55 document opens with its span, and only couplings need v56. The three coupling commands are in the analytics operation vocabulary. Not in this slice: a coupling editor outside the card, couplings for non-gear joints, screw couplings and bevel or worm pairs (AS08, M14). Evidence: `test/assembly-coupling.test.ts` (CI smoke selection: external, internal and rack ratios with poses, phase-preserving offset, either side driving, an idler train, limit clamping and the narrowed drag range; the edited motion leading over a value a scrub left, the lead holding where a keyed value crosses its saved one and in either track order; every refusal code, stored loops rejected; add/update/delete with undo and redo, a pose or typed value on either joint, a pose saved with its drivers, limit conflicts, fixing a coupled gear refused and rejected in storage, pruning with a joint, component or kind change, the recorded gear pair validated and the coupling dropped with a keyed gear, replay, save and reopen; v54 documents opening unchanged, a v55 partial-span document opening with its span, and couplings refused below v56), `test/gear-coupling.test.ts` (CI smoke selection: the saved joints and coupling land the second gear exactly where the fixed placement does; The CSV is written from the table, byte for byte as before. The Assemble sidebar shows each row's item number, lights up the row of a part picked on the model or in Parts, and picks every instance of a row when the row is clicked (a second click clears it). Rows and CSV are unchanged by posing or dragging parts and after save and reopen; a missing part keeps its number so later numbers never shift. No schema change. Evidence: `test/assembly-bom.test.ts` (table cells and columns, item numbers, CSV equality, pose and reopen invariance, missing-row numbering), `AssemblyPanel.test.tsx` (row pick and highlight) and the CI smoke case in `test/e2e/assembly-bom.spec.ts` (pick on the model lights the row, a row picks its part, a drag to the joint limit leaves the rows and the downloaded CSV identical). Offsets are stored in display order without zeros, go with a deleted part, reopen exactly after save and reload, and are refused for unknown parts, reserved keys or bad vectors; an older client refuses the document. Room merges keep offsets on different parts once the view has one, and an offset with a pose made elsewhere; two offsets for one part, an offset on a part removed elsewhere, and the first offsets made on two devices at once are conflicts. | <a id="as04"></a>AS04 | Complete | Pairwise assembly interference and STEP hierarchy round-trip. Pairwise interference slice (no schema change; a read-only query on schema v61): Assemble's sidebar gains an Interference section whose Check runs an exact pairwise check of every pair of components at their solved pose (never the exploded view) in the geometry worker. It is M09's body clash query (`packages/kernel-adapter/src/exact-body-clash.ts`) run on posed copies, so AS04 and M09 share one implementation and there is no second engine or mesh test: disjoint boxes prove clearance; the kernel's exact intersection measures shared volume, and where Remus 1.141 declines an intersection it cannot build (a pin in a clearance hole) its exact difference measures it as `volume(b) − volume(b − a)`. Each overlap carries witnesses (exact volume, the shared region's box, a point both exact classifiers place strictly inside both parts); clearance carries the certified distance and closest points where the kernel certifies them, zero being contact; a pair no boolean answers is reported unknown with the kernel's refusal, never clear, and a component whose body is gone is listed as unchecked. Picking a pair picks both parts and shows its witness; a result says "Out of date" once the model or poses change. Contract: docs/plans/assemblies-plan.md (Interference). Evidence: `packages/kernel-adapter/src/exact-body-clash.test.ts` (overlapping boxes, face contact, certified gap, pin in a clearance hole, line-to-line fit, off-axis pin, sphere in a box face, multi-solid bodies, pair order, verdict combination), `test/assembly-interference.test.ts` (real-kernel document check: volumes, witnesses, distances, exploded view ignored, a rotated component, a missing body, ordering), `apps/web/src/lib/assembly/interferenceUi.test.ts`, `AssemblyInterferenceSection.test.tsx` and the CI smoke case `test/e2e/assembly-interference.spec.ts` (2880 mm³ overlap with its witness, pin clear, out of date after turning a joint, re-check). The STEP hierarchy round-trip and interference swept through motion followed (below). Interference through motion (no schema change; a read-only query): under the static check, Assemble's Interference section gains "Through motion", which runs the same M09 clash query (`exact-body-clash.ts`) at positions along one joint's Animate sweep or one motion study, for the pairs that move against each other (`planAssemblySweep` and `runAssemblySweep` in `packages/document-core/src/assembly-interference-sweep.ts`; the adapter's `assemblyInterferenceSweep` poses copies per position on one build in the geometry worker). 37 even positions along a joint (8 per second of a study), then four rounds halving each gap where a pair turns between overlap and no overlap, so each overlap's start and end is found to within 1/16 of a step. Each moving pair reports its overlapping stretches in the motion's own unit, its largest measured overlap with its position and witnesses, undecided positions (never read as clear) and its closest certified gap; the result states the positions, step and resolution and that an overlap shorter than a step can fall between positions. "Show at" poses the worst position as a transient scrub, never saved. Contract: docs/plans/assemblies-plan.md (Interference through motion). Evidence: `test/assembly-interference-sweep.test.ts` (real kernel: a pin passing through a block on a slider found overlapping strictly between 30 and 50 mm to within the resolution, 1000 mm³ at 40, a still pair excluded, a motion study swept in seconds, refusals, refinement rules), `AssemblyInterferenceSection.test.tsx` (sweep, labels, worst position shown) and the CI smoke case `test/e2e/assembly-interference-motion.spec.ts`. Each body an assembly part shows is a STEP part whose geometry stays in its own frame, and its occurrence under the project carries the part's solved pose (stored joint values, never an exploded offset) as an `ITEM_DEFINED_TRANSFORMATION`, its translation in the file's length unit (`exportAssemblyPlacements` in `packages/kernel-adapter/src/exact.ts`, `inversePlacementFrame` in `step-export-metadata.ts`). Objects folders stay sub-assemblies, bodies in no part keep the identity, a document with neither is still one flat product, and the written solids are unchanged for a reader that ignores structure. I02's structure import reads the file back with every part as its own body at its assembled place, named after its part; Assemble's first run makes them parts again. Joints, couplings and studies are not STEP content. Contract: docs/plans/assemblies-plan.md (STEP hierarchy round-trip). Evidence: `test/step-structure-import.test.ts` (real kernel: a cylindrical joint turned a quarter turn and lifted 10 mm, with an exploded offset that is never written, exported in millimetres and inches and imported through the real import run, the arm read back at its posed box and the base where modelled, both named after their parts) and the CI smoke case `test/e2e/assembly-step-roundtrip.spec.ts` (File › STEP writes both parts with the arm's turned occurrence frame, and a fresh project imports both back). | AS01, M09, I02; product AS-4. |

## Parallel kernel and web reliability lane

These are consumer requirements. Before a Remus implementation task, refresh its
own ledger and the exact OpenZCAD pin: inherited P-Class numbers and old defect
reports are not proof that the current upstream kernel is missing an operation.
A kernel merge closes a consumer row only after pin adoption and product tests.
Since 2026-11-15, K10 with K01, K07, K08 and M03, and the W01 budgets, are
[Active 1](#delivery-order). K11 is paused and K09 and W03 are Deferred; the
other rows here run alongside.

| ID | Status | Outcome and completion evidence | Depends on / legacy item |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| <a id="k01"></a>K01 | Partial | Reproduce remaining tangency/operand-loss, cap-offset, thin-bore tessellation and silent-success examples on the pin; fix or type-refuse upstream, then retire only the workarounds whose replacement passes the original oracles. The exact-only boolean policy now refuses by category instead of shipping a faceted result, and the boolean arm of the face-count census is retired; approximation is measured as no rescue (#330). NURBS-trimmed cylinder display regression: `test/step-nurbs-trimmed-cylinder.test.ts` (synthetic exact STEP, wall chord bound and exact roundtrip); [local private-source evidence](docs/qa/2026-09-19/trimmed-cylinder-render.md) is recorded separately. Concave planar display faces now validate triangle winding and area, then use bounded ear clipping instead of the overlapping vertex fan for valid concave boundaries ([remus#601](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/pull/601)); its synthetic repeated-fin native and installed-WASM regressions ship on the `cb13b53` pin (merged upstream as [remus#606](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/pull/606)). Extrude side walls: the `74422f0` pin reads a nearly straight polynomial bezier as a circle (a 1e-5 fit) and builds a cylinder wall that misses its own bezier cap edges, so every exact boolean that trims the wall refuses (Open Sans `b` at em 10; about 2 % of bundled glyph segments at some size). The adapter splits such curves exactly into pieces the kernel reads as lines (`kernelSafeBezierPieces`; kernel canary and on-face regressions in `test/text-kernel-build.test.ts`) until upstream checks the circle; the same PR certifies away a trimmed B-spline face's untrimmed box (`refineBoundsAtSplineFaces`), which read an engraved 10 mm slab as 10.01. Production QA CAD-001 (11 October): a mounting ear would not union with a shelled housing. Reduced on the pin, and still present on `c2cd99d0`, to any open cup whose bounding-box centre line passes through a round through-hole. The kernel's internal sub-face classifier calls the material inside such a cup outside, so every boolean with a tool touching it refuses exact-only, and a fuse of a tool wholly inside the wall answers `ok` with the tool's volume added. The adapter stays fail-closed (typed refusals; the union connectivity gate catches the silent `ok`, though its "disconnected groups" message misdescribes overlapping bodies). Raw-kernel canary and adapter guard: `test/cup-bore-boolean-refusal.test.ts`; minimal upstream reproduction, trace evidence and the exact union-before-bore workaround in the [QA record](docs/qa/2026-10-11/cad-001-cup-bore-boolean.md). Open: upstream fix ([remus#984](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/issues/984)), then flip the canary to the exact answers. 2026-10-11 queue integration: Review correction: the cup-bore adapter canary requires the typed exact_only_unattainable / quality_refused kernel refusal for the crossing-wall case, and an explicit connectivity refusal for the contained tool; unrelated exceptions no longer count as expected refusals. 2026-10-13 Remus 2026.1.53 adoption (`957201b8`, updater #651): the reduced cup-bore regressions now require exact crossing-wall and contained-wall unions through the adapter and installed WASM. Independent volumes, 17 crossing-wall faces, zero validation errors, watertight meshes and serialized operand preservation pass; [QA follow-up](docs/qa/2026-10-11/cad-001-cup-bore-boolean.md#installed-wasm-follow-up--13-october-2026). The original housing production-UI rerun and broader K01 families remain open. Coplanar boolean walls use orientation-aware Remus face unification, including retained and absorbed hole boundaries. See the [source fix](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/pull/1500) and [paired packages](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/pull/1503). The consumer regression in `packages/kernel-adapter/src/union-unify-gate.test.ts` builds a cut plate with two added uprights and requires one face on each flat inner wall, analytic volume, strict solid validity, and a closed display mesh; it runs in the required CI smoke selection. Local private saved history replay preserves volume and downstream fillets/direct edits while removing the redundant planar boundaries; the fixture stays outside the repository. Local Edge verification covers import, standard-view changes, and reload with the full renderer. This is bounded face-unification evidence, not closure of the remaining K01 families. Reversed-plane STEP shell (#1247): an independent AP214 producer's 20 x 16 x 12 box, three faces written `same_sense = .F.`, shells to the same valid 2 mm top-open 1920 mm³ solid as the native box on Remus 2026.1.135 (fixed upstream in the shell fold detector; no adapter workaround). SDK, adapter and STEP round-trip regressions in `test/step-imported-box-shell.test.ts`; production Shell command on imported and native boxes in `test/e2e/imported-box-shell.spec.ts`. Offset-circle loft (#1253) on the Remus 2026.1.135 pin: two parallel circles with laterally offset centres loft as two planes and one exact ruled B-spline wall between two exact circle rims in Ruled and Smooth, matching the closed-form volume (61,261.0567 mm³) and centroid to 1e-9 through preview, commit, reload and a STEP round trip; the coaxial control stays one cone (`test/loft-offset-circles.test.ts`). Lofts the pin still rebuilds from 32 chords — Smooth through three or more sections, coaxial circles included, and curved-to-straight or elliptical sections — are measured from body topology and disclosed in Diagnostics on the preview and the committed body, as faceted sweeps are (`apps/web/src/lib/loftFaceting.ts`, `test/e2e/loft-faceting.spec.ts`). Face-touching cylinder union (production QA 2026-11-14 finding #1): on the `42389c8` pin, `fuseAll` joins two cylinders that meet cap to cap (a hub standing exactly on a flange, coaxial or not, either order) without a boolean. The result is one solid that validates and still holds both caps, so the flange top reads as a full disc, a foot fillet rounds convexly and a later bore cut drops the hub ([remus#1738](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/issues/1738)). Pairwise `fuse` merges that pair but refuses two parallel r20 cylinders that overlap by 1 mm or less, whatever their cap planes ([remus#1742](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/issues/1742)); `fuseAll` fuses them when their caps are shared. The union gate (`fuseUniformSolidChecked`, used by Union, extrude Add and the move suggestion) is narrow and certifies per contact interface. A result in one face-connected component, or one whose operands touch nowhere, is returned exactly as before. An interface is a pair that shares material (a shared interior point or a positive intersection) or whose exact pairwise fuse joins it (fewer face components, strictly valid, exact combined box). Vertex- or edge-only contact is no interface. Each operand gets one interior witness per material lump (a cavity winds −1 and gets none). An interface requires the lump witnesses its pairwise fuse encloses together to be enclosed together in the result, by winding number against the result's boundary components. A witness near a curved chord re-tessellates that component alone, within a 400k-triangle budget. Lumps no contact joins are never compared. An interface that does not certify gets its group repaired on its own operands, `fuseAll` first and then the pairwise fuse, with per-operand preservation checks. Whatever still cannot be certified refuses with `union_unmerged_contact` (fail closed). Witness agreement is evidence at finitely many points, not a proof that no doubled sheet exists elsewhere. Regressions with closed-form oracles in `test/touching-cylinder-union.test.ts`: annulus area, concave R2/R5 foot fillet volume, bore cut, circular boss extruded onto a cylinder face, two overlapping bosses on a flange in all six operand orders, hub-on-flange at 0, 1e5 and 1e7, cavity bodies, injected material-dropping repairs, twelve pins past the repair cap, a hub beside one or eleven separate thin tubes, thin-wall tubes on a flange, a no-contact union that tessellates nothing, compound operands with separate contact regions, cubes sharing only a vertex, a block on a 0.001 or 0.0001 mm plate in a compound body, and a pattern ×4/×8 bolt-circle subtract about the world and bore axes. The round-4 report records the regression and the updated UI route without replacing earlier timing evidence. 2026-11-05 imported-twin R-slice (WS1, reference scenarios R1–R9). `test/support/imported-twin.ts` builds a synthetic twin of the recorded body: block, r 6 post, seam, rim and corner blends. It exports the twin to STEP and edits the `importStepBody` import, which has no construction history. Oracles are closed forms, Pappus sections and the integrated overlap; one tangent-ended edge uses a labelled interval. On Remus `2026.1.123` the 74 cells are 28 qualified, 1 typed refusal, 43 untyped refusals and 2 wrong; with #1265 and #1266 merged, the four R2 cells refuse typed and no cell is wrong. The CI slice holds 5 cells (`combination-matrix.imported-twin.ledger.json`); the full campaign holds 69 (`combination-matrix.imported-twin.full.ledger.json`). The tilted post's seam takes neither a fillet nor a chamfer, so the edits run on upright and Y-tilted unblended fallback twins. R1, R3, R4 and R9 qualify. R2 is wrong on the upright twin: the wall resizes and both blend bands stay on their r 6 carriers. On the tilted twin R2 refuses untyped. R5 and R6 qualify at r 1.5 and with an equal-radius r 4 chain, but refuse at r 3. R7's refusals keep the body and use plain sentences, but none is typed and negative sizes are refused only during the rebuild. Local reference-workflow baseline on the supplied boss/block native and STEP fixtures with a process-local paired Remus 2026.1.124 overlay, without feature or kernel changes. [Scope, gaps and reproduction](docs/qa/video-parity-baseline.md) and [per-attempt measurements](docs/qa/video-parity-measurements.md) distinguish isolated success, approximation, UI/lineage gates and exact kernel refusal. The actual browser wasm response hashes match the committed package bytes. This is local evidence, not production or complete cumulative-video qualification. Exact-only approximate-reblend refusals retain their typed cause, show clear product copy, and stop pointless waypoint retries. Exported reproductions retain that same classification and sentence. The newly supported coaxial torus-band removal is asserted closed and valid; the remaining off-axis freeform WS6 limitations stay explicit. Axial equivalence cases retain every geometry, reference and cache assertion with cold-replay timeouts and opt-in cold-versus-cached timing evidence. Kernel size limits and load-time review requirements are unchanged. This does not implement WS6 reblend or claim production qualification. Union connectivity no longer calls overlapping box×sphere bodies disconnected. The pin has no certified box/sphere distance and refuses their exact intersection, so an overlap that was never proven used to be reported as "2 disconnected groups". `sharedInteriorPoint` (`exact-boolean-helpers.ts`) now proves shared volume from a point that both `classifyPoint` and `classifyPointRobust` place strictly inside both solids, probed only in the operands' common box. Tangent, stacked, gapped and near-corner pairs yield no witness and still refuse as disconnected. The 27 box×sphere raised and sliver union cells keep `untyped-refusal`, but now with the correct reason: the kernel's fuse fails strict validation ("1 shared edges have inconsistent face orientations"), which is an upstream defect, not a connectivity fault. Analytic witness checks, closed-form cap volumes, and adapter regressions on both sides are in `test/union-connectivity.test.ts`. This also answers the misdescription noted under K01 (QA CAD-001). 2026-11-05 acceptance gates for the K10 `wrong` cells (`packages/kernel-adapter/src/result-acceptance.ts`, `result-acceptance.test.ts`). A subtract whose overlap could not be measured refuses as `cut_kept_tool_material` when a point the kernel classifies inside the target, the tool and the result proves the cut kept material. A direct wall resize refuses as `resize_left_blend_behind` when a curved face meeting the wall no longer meets the resized wall, and as `resize_volume_mismatch` when its volume change is beyond 3× of the swept sleeve. Cuts and intersects are held to strict validation. On the `669d1e2` pin, 81 wrong cells become typed refusals (box − torus 70, seam-blend resize 8, tangent drill 3), plus one grazing drill whose B-rep fails strict validation. The ledgers stand at 724 qualified, 521 typed, 176 untyped and 13 wrong (including #1265's typed resize refusals). The 13 Steinmetz cells stay wrong: their body is exact (exact ellipse edges, clean validation), and the kernel's volume integration is 1.25e-5 low, which is reported upstream. Full ledgers re-recorded on the 2026.1.135 pin (2026-11-13): 52 cells move typed-refusal → qualified and no cell moves the other way. Full matrix 903 / 476 / 9 / 4 wrong → 950 / 429 / 9 / 4; imported twin 26 / 42 / 1 / 0 → 31 / 37 / 1 / 0. Every one of them passes its kernel-free oracle, closed mesh, replay, reopen and (where applicable) STEP round trip. All 52 flip at Remus 2026.1.131 (`7a58c8e..3483e2d`, pinned by #1338), measured with the kernel overlay on current product code; on 2026.1.130 they refuse or, for the five imported-twin cells, build wrong. 46 are `box×cylinder/tilted/*` booleans that 2026.1.130 refused with `exact_only_unattainable`, and one is `cylinder×cylinder/tangent/subtract/no-modifier/source-grow`, refused as an invalid solid (most likely [Remus #1641](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/pull/1641), periodic exact section partitions). The five imported-twin fillets (upright R5 single-edge and sequential-chains, R6 tangent-chain, tilted-unblended R5 single-edge and R6 tangent-chain) were already `wrong` on main against a stale `typed-refusal` entry, as #1330's PR body recorded. They now meet the closed-form oracle (49649.350 mm³ for the upright R5/R6 tangent chain, against 49647.728 on 2026.1.130), most likely from Remus #1631 / #1638 (exact spindle tori, line–torus contacts). The full campaign runs only in `pnpm test:unit:full`, so the drift went unseen while the CI slice stayed green. Issue #1244 (torus ∩ holed block kept the hole on Remus 2026.1.122): on the `24040dd` pin the issue's STEP operands intersect exactly in both orders (570.759 mm³ against the closed-form 570.789, hole witness excluded), and app-modelled operands refuse by name (`exact_only_unattainable`), both fresh, after a cold reload and after an upstream edit (remus d9d51f52). Intersect results, from the Boolean feature and extrude Intersect, are now also checked against their operands: a point the kernel classifies inside the result and outside either operand, confirmed by its dual-method classifier, refuses as `intersect_kept_outside_material` (`result-acceptance.ts`, `test/intersect-material-acceptance.test.ts`). 2026-11-13 issue #1242 (Subtract of overlapping curved STEP bodies) re-qualified on the `2026.1.135` pin: `test/subtract-curved-operands.test.ts` imports the four named recipes (revolve/sweep, revolve/trimmed NURBS, sweep/sweep, sweep/loft) as OCCT-written STEP from `test/fixtures/step/subtract-curved/` and checks commit, reload, Undo and STEP export against OCCT reference volumes and an overlap witness. The primary recipe now cuts correctly (175.430 vs OCCT 175.430 mm³); the other three refuse by type before consuming either input. `cut_kept_tool_material` no longer relies on the kernel's point classifier alone: a tessellation-based witness (winding numbers and point-to-mesh distance, four chord deflections clear) also refuses, and both now run when the measured overlap is zero as well as unmeasured (`test/subtract-classifier-blind.test.ts`). Open upstream: the primary cut result and the swept target fail STEP export (arena edge residual; zero-length closed NURBS pcurve), and closed-section OCCT lofts fail STEP import. CAD-006 (#1246, 2026-11-13): three overlapping boxes now unite to the same exact material in every grouping and operand order. Re-reproduced at the SDK level: Remus 2026.1.121–129 refuse `A ∪ (B ∪ C)` in both orders with `exact_only_unattainable` straight from `fuseDetailed`, not from an adapter gate, while `(A ∪ B) ∪ C` builds the closed 26748 mm³ solid. The B ∪ C intermediate's L-shaped planar wall left the next fuse's section ending at its reflex corner, which the kernel's greedy face walk traced as an out-and-back spur, leaving the wall unsplit. Part B's edge-round switch to one corner is the kernel group route's own limit: the direct resize keeps all twelve rounds uniform through radius 5.88 and re-blends only the picked corner from 5.89, the first radius at which an independent twelve-edge Fillet history refuses. A drag release did not follow that route. It committed the prepared journaled edit, which is the single-band `resizeBlendJournaled`, so at every radius probed from 1.6 to 5.88 mm one corner changed while the preview and a reopened document resized all twelve rounds (3.75 mm: 17,085.07 mm³ released against 16,288.37 mm³ replayed, on native and imported-STEP B). A blend release now rebuilds the group route that its frames and cold replay use. `test/prepared-resize-release.test.ts` drags B to 3.75, 5.88, 5.89 and 5.9 mm. The last frame's closed display mesh must enclose the release's and the cold rebuild's mesh volume within WS1's two-resolution estimate at drag quality; unfixed, it missed by 813.7 mm³ against a 69.4 mm³ tolerance. Each release also matches the closed form or the independent Fillet history, its band count and a cold rebuild. CAD part QA P2 adds a seeded direct-edit chain generator and its fixed-seed PR slice to root Vitest (`test/direct-edit-corpus/chains.test.ts`, ledger `chains.ledger.json`): three seeds × 20 steps on each of parts A–D, 12 chains and 240 steps. Steps offset planar faces, resize blends and cylinders, edit an earlier step's value, undo (with or without redo) and reopen from the saved form at step 10, so later undos cross the reopen. Each build passes strict STEP solid validation and a closed, consistently oriented mesh check. Each refusal must be typed, specific and pinned by its literal sentence and owner in `CHAIN_REFUSAL_PINS`, and must leave history and geometry unchanged. Each resize must apply its radius, and each earlier-value edit must change the body unless a later resize supersedes it. Undo, redo, reopen and the end-of-chain cold replay match the incremental body exactly (volume, bounding box, census, and every face's centre and area), and the end-of-chain STEP round trip keeps volume and face census. All 12 chains qualify: 168 builds, 42 pinned refusals, 18 undos (8 with redo) and 15 earlier-value edits. The pins cover nine refusal classes. Eight are owned by K10, including the design limit probes, part A's chamfer-plane offsets and the D/wall chamfer carry. The `reference_missing` re-pick, raised when an earlier edit removes a later edit's face (O4), is owned by K05. Two consecutive runs produced identical step lists and volumes. The file took 53 s on a shared 32-core workstation at load average 9: 43 s for part A's chains and 9 s for B–D together. At load average 60–90 it took 128 s. It is not yet measured on a CI runner. `K10_SHRINK=1` enables a chain shrinker through `LedgerRunner.shrink`. It drops steps and rounds values only while the failure signature (oracle, refusal category and code, or wrong-result check) stays the same. Each refusal is pinned at its own step, so one occurrence that starts building fails the pin check. CAD part QA P1 on Remus 2026.1.159 runs 195 signed edits in the root Vitest corpus. Part A, a synthetic truncated Cone primitive united with a box, has an exact junction fillet and a second fillet on the 12 box edges and cone rim. Its census is 30 faces (7 planes, 12 cylinders, 1 cone, 2 tori and 8 spheres). Comparison with the recorded part's census is pending. Readout (exact local builds / typed refusals): A 11 / 50, B 25 / 6, C 50 / 25, D 11 / 17. There are no unexplained monotonic violations. The conical fillets on A limit direct edits even at 0.1 mm. On B, zero removes the connected rounds, and the larger positive steps re-blend only the selected corner. A separate feature history with the enlarged corner and the remaining rounds at their original radius confirms its volume and blend census. On C, zero removes the boss root fillet. The local resize refuses at an outward root radius of 9.9 mm and an inward wall radius of 0.1 mm. Both −11.9 mm shoulder moves build locally, while the source fillet rebuild refuses with a 0.1 mm plate. On D, local radius edits cannot carry both chamfers, and the independent source rebuild succeeds wherever the radius leaves both chamfers intact. The cap translates with its chamfer, with the volume delta measured against the full cylindrical section. These findings and each literal refusal are pinned in `test/direct-edit-corpus/`. The suite checks strict solid and mesh validity, shape oracles, cold replay, refusal preservation and undo/redo. P0 awaits captures. Multi-face offsets now report the specific blend refusal when the first group cannot move. The direct-edit corpus pins the same message as the corresponding single-face move for a cap and shoulder meeting conical fillets at ±1 mm and for a shoulder and side reaching a fixed rim at +20 mm. The authored Part A reproduces the recorded pre-edit census of 34 faces: 11 planar, 10 cylindrical, 6 conical, 4 toroidal, 1 spherical and 2 B-spline. It uses the corpus plate and cylinder dimensions. All six cones come from chamfering the base perimeter. The five-edge junction fillet produces both fitted faces and an approximation advisory. The earlier 30-face truncated-cone scenario retains its ladders and refusal pins as the frustum control. Part A's 59 signed edits comprise 38 builds with no new approximation, 19 typed refusals and 2 disclosed approximate cap joins. Pushing the post cap down or the shoulder up refuses at 8.5 mm of travel. At 8.6 mm the rim crosses the junction and the edit builds with five tori and four B-spline faces, 37 faces in total. Each result exceeds its independent source rebuild by 8.11 mm³. The ladder pins both findings, including the advisory, and checks the joined body's closed mesh, STEP solid, volume, cold replay and undo/redo. Resizing the root alone changes the selected torus while the four other junction edges retain their radii. The project used to read the census stays local. P0 still awaits a Save reproduction fixture. Re-verified on Remus 2026.1.159 (pin `d5c7248`, OpenZCAD #1942): all four K10 ledgers stay byte-identical to 2026.1.158's. A local record-mode run of `test/combination-matrix.test.ts` and `test/combination-matrix.full.test.ts`, against packages whose SHA-256 digests match the pin's `remus-checksums.json`, found 1,289 qualified, 219 typed refusals, 0 untyped refusals and 0 wrong of 1,508 cells. That pin carries Remus #1942 (variable-fillet tangent-ball corners, exact-only disclosure and cliff details), #1945 (variable support cliffs checked on the tangent-ball setback span) and #1905 (threaded bores kept through planar face moves). The byte-identical ledgers show that no matrix cell moved. Re-verified on Remus 2026.1.160 (pin `f3dcc43`, OpenZCAD #1980): all four K10 ledgers stay byte-identical to 2026.1.159's. | <a id="k11"></a>K11 | Open | Exact face-drag preview within 100 ms on filleted parts: an exact offset-face preview (kernel build plus install) fast enough to stream while the pointer moves. On the RX 6600 the exact preview of the public filleted union takes 385–404 ms of kernel time, its history 171–185 ms and the native holder 61–64 ms ([drag baselines](docs/performance/2026-11-20-drag-orbit-sustained-baselines.md)). Today proxy frames follow the pointer (15–16 ms p95) and exact geometry is built once the hand rests; H02 accepts that (owner decision 2026-11-21), so this row is the exact-geometry follow-up, not an H02 blocker. Commit validation stays blocking. 2026-11-21 findings (owner keeps commit checks blocking and exact). A flagged prototype (`h02/exact-drag-streaming`, unmerged) streams exact face-offset previews under the drag proxy and builds blends display-only from the first changed feature. On the RX 6600 it reaches first exact geometry at 95–103 ms on the native holder and 174–215 ms on the filleted-union cases (from 2.3–2.7 s), but regresses the filleted-union width release from 98 to 330 ms, because the release then pays the fillet's blocking checks. Profiling those checks on a native Remus reproduction (Remus branch `perf/k11-blend-guards`, `crates/operations/tests/blend_guard_filleted_union.rs`) found no exact 2× speedup. The material guard (~91 ms) is spread over NURBS-band ray crossings (~36 ms, mostly certified subdivision), ray–torus tests (~23 ms) and BVH bookkeeping (~17 ms). The volume guard (~86 ms) is cylinder Green's-theorem integrals that are genuine cache misses. Backlog of exact savings (about 20–30 ms in all): reuse per-surface extraction operators (2–3 ms); a proven torus early-out (≤ 20 ms; needs the solver's tangent-ray behaviour worked out); a first-derivative-only NURBS path for the cylinder integral (5–8% of the volume guard). Next app steps if resumed: prepare the drag before the first move (cold first preview ~200 ms), and keep a checked fillet checkpoint for the release. | Split from H02; Remus offset-face and blend tessellation; measured by the `face-drag` probe (`Face drag exact preview round trip`, `Face drag first exact geometry`). |
| <a id="w01"></a>W01 | Partial | Measure actual worker cold load/compile/instantiate, warm rebuild/cache hit, eviction, retained memory, cloning and STEP first-load on desktop and constrained hardware; set median/p95 budgets from those results. The opt-in [CAD operation harness](docs/cad-operation-performance.md) records seven operation families at 30/33/100 features with source/package identity, phase timings, replay counts, memory observations and fresh-rebuild oracles. Health, authentication config, settings, collaboration config and speculative chunk prefetch no longer block the initial library. Selecting a project immediately shows its named loading screen with cancellation; document reconciliation still precedes hydration, and the file count loads afterward. Regression coverage includes 150 encrypted cards, delayed optional services, cached rows, rejected/stale metadata, local settings changed during discovery, shared viewers awaiting capabilities, cancellation during device writes, superseded navigation and late configuration responses after retries or cloud-mode changes (`progressive-loading.spec.ts`, `apiProjectListing.test.ts`, `progressiveShelf.test.ts`, `projectShelf.test.ts`, `cloudConfigurationReads.test.ts`). Full-render Chromium on an RTX 3090 measured six fresh library opens per build and plate size against parent `c0c784625`. Median click to first frame showing the model fell from 546 to 515 ms for 12 holes, 822 to 639 ms for 32 holes and 1,419 to 1,210 ms for 100 holes. Both builds used the released kernel. All 36 opens sent one live rebuild, and opening and edited geometry matched across builds. The first viewport frame, which can still be empty, took 42 ms longer on the 32-hole candidate and 28 ms longer on the 100-hole candidate. The CI smoke case in [new-project-startup.spec.ts](test/e2e/new-project-startup.spec.ts) checks that opening an owned project submits one rebuild with its persistence policy before viewport initialization. On a 500-body isolated width edit, 499 bodies retained the same picking topology across warm rebuilds. The worker now reuses the overlap checks between topology trees that occurred together in its last successfully packed large UTF-8 reply. It still checks every reused tree against the current metadata and changed trees. Seven recorded edits decoded identically across all 3,500 bodies. In 18 fresh desktop Chromium sessions with the same modeling and I/O binaries, each build ran 42 edits. Median Apply to matching rendered frame measured 259.2 ms on the original baseline, 214.1 ms on the parent and 209.3 ms with this change. Worker request to response measured 140.3, 97.0 and 93.7 ms respectively. Five of six paired sessions improved on the parent. Warm reply preparation alone fell from 11.08 to 4.57 ms in a separate Node replay. 100-feature isolated edit, main-thread presentation: stage lane metrics no longer restyle the model panels. Chromium traces of the `w01-history` probe (`test/e2e/perf-cad-edit.spec.ts`, 100 features, isolated part) showed the time after the worker reply was mostly native style and layout. The cost was not the viewer's scene update: the install effect's JavaScript was about 2 ms. It was a forced layout in a commit effect (`TopBar` `fitProjectName` in the installed → frame window, `useRovingToolbar` `checkVisibility` in the install window), paying for one cheap-looking write. When the edit card closes, `WorkspaceReadout` writes `--workspace-guidance-h` on `.viewer-area`. Custom properties inherit, so any custom-property write there restyled and re-laid out all of the roughly 3,400 nodes below it, including every History and Objects row. On the idle 100-feature page, one write cost 38–79 ms of style + layout on the measuring host, even for an unused property. A small DOM change cost 0.2 ms. `.model-panel` now redeclares the stage's lane metrics `initial`. Nothing in a panel reads them, so a write stops at the panel frame. In the same run, a `--workspace-guidance-h` write took 37.6 ms without the redeclaration and 5.5 ms with it. Only the listed metrics are isolated: a property outside the list still reaches every panel row. `test/stage-metric-isolation.test.ts` derives the properties written on the viewer area from source, plus every property the stylesheets derive from them, and fails when the panel frame misses one or a panel rule reads one (mutation-checked). `test/e2e/stage-metric-isolation.spec.ts` checks in Chromium that the lanes still read the published values and panel rows read none. Worker reply and install stages matched within noise per round. The RX 6600 budget (100 ms median) has not been re-measured: that needs `node scripts/measure-active1-baselines.mjs --only w01-history` on that machine.
