This qualifies the reconstructed fixture only; broader blend families and live product acceptance remain separate work. CI qualification staging ([PR #1226](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/pull/1226)): critical plus affected unit selection, changed browser regression inclusion, two root partitions, independent lint/typecheck, combined-tree queue journeys, and a separate label-sensitive kernel declaration guard. The active v6 caller remains pinned until the staged v7 callee lands on main. Complete daily root/web coverage and six full browser partitions retain source-specific reports and a fail-closed receipt. The first full web run exposed a stale stylesheet contract; its media-block scoping is repaired with all height/ordering assertions retained and seven focused tests passing. Full web qualification passes on `7a700834`; the same [full run](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/actions/runs/41965072370) found a root dense-oracle timeout and 58 browser failures plus one flaky case. The root oracle now uses a closed-interval candidate sweep, retaining all 120 curves, 4,000 chords, crossing arithmetic and the five-second deadline, with differential checks against its original scan and repeated coverage probes. The repair integrates current main without dropping either side's unit manifest entries. Static smoke assertions and weight refreshes now exclude the changed-file overlay, with real Git-fixture coverage for untagged additions, edits and deletions. Settings search includes the touch-history and Pencil controls. The workspace-switch Revolve regression waits for a ready non-text keyboard target; its original failure reproduced twice under concurrency, then all 12 repeated spec executions passed with two workers and no retries. Full root validation also exposed an intersection symbol introduced on main without font coverage. The pinned math-font subset and its CSS range now include U+2229, with an explicit glyph regression assertion and a passing interface-wide coverage audit. Further complete browser qualification exposed stale harness assumptions: the rail test now resolves its CSS length through layout, the contrast test opens the keypad before measuring its unit buttons, and camera regressions start gestures on measured bare canvas rather than covered fixed coordinates. The checkpoint-module fault test uses Save-Data to isolate the explicit import from idle prefetch, retaining its exact request-count and persistence checks. The imperial-unit refusal reads the validation warning, and the unresolved extrude waits for History's failed-feature state before opening its editor. The datum repair journey re-projects the known face after workspace changes; its old pixel was proven to hit empty canvas. The gear preview journey waits for committed undo/redo heights before reload, and encrypted STEP coverage requires exactly one import source apart from separately encrypted thumbnails, retaining the all-object ciphertext and fresh-browser checks. Extrude repair also waits for the canceled editor to unmount and projects the roof-face center directly from History's action menu. Constraint-control hit tests re-scroll after flyout reflow. Gear previews retain all six asserted reversal targets and committed drag paths while omitting unobserved pointer interpolations, preserving every assertion and the 120-second deadline. Evidence validation reuses parsed row IDs within a fold instead of parsing the full wide ledger per fragment. A 250-row regression retains escaped/code pipes and validates every resulting row; all refusal rules and the five-second deadline remain unchanged. The ASCII STL tokenizer now scans native token matches with the same six ASCII separators, preserving lookahead, grammar and refusal behavior. Eight separator regressions and the existing 500,000/500,001-triangle boundary tests pass under coverage without extending their five-second deadlines. The four formerly failing journeys also pass in isolation; complete hosted qualification remains the queue-admission requirement. Full browser qualification uses one attempt and retains failure contexts; routine PR browsers run critical and changed journeys, with complete units on broad inputs. Immutable-pin activation remains pending; this staging is not evidence of a completed rollout. See `docs/ci-qualification.md` and `docs/runner-routing.md`. Companion kernel qualification reuse: [Remus PR #1541](https://github.com/esaueng/remus/pull/1541). CI runtime follow-up stages an immutable v8 callee with independent lint/typecheck, two root partitions, critical plus affected units and a kernel preflight inside required validation. Browser timing metadata uses conservative defaults for unmeasured selected specs while preserving inventory checks; six shards use refreshed successful current-kernel measurements. Dependency audits select JS/Rust inputs independently and verify a checksum-pinned RustSec tool instead of compiling it on every run. Failure artifacts retain traces/context for fourteen days; full daily tests and coverage remain complete. Regression coverage exercises failed/canceled/missing gate results, additive manifest classification, dynamic assets, audit selection and corrupt tool refusal. Activation requires a separate main-reachable pin PR and exact-head/queue verification. See [implementation and qualification](docs/ci-runtime-optimization.md). Full-qualification fixture follow-ups open feature Measurements explicitly instead of toggling a card that may already be pinned. Constraint recovery checks use a trial click to settle an expanded flyout before retaining full viewport and real-pointer assertions. Oversized request streams cross the unchanged byte ceiling by one byte across two individually legal-sized chunks, avoiding a coverage-instrumented scan of tens of MiB of whitespace. The exact 10,000-object 3MF acceptance and 10,001-object refusal are independent parser cases, each using the existing test deadline. All limit values, rejection responses, numeric geometry, export, undo and persistence assertions remain. These changes follow two browser failures and three root-coverage timeouts in [qualification run 42057401913](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/actions/runs/42057401913). CI staging remains held pending a fresh complete qualification. Fleet v8 activation and daily qualification tracking (this PR): `ci.yml` pins `fleet-ci-v8.yml` at #1295's landed commit `158a250b` and retires v6, so kernel updates run complete unit suites before merge. The queue-routing harness now checks the active callee and pins v8's workflow header, queue probe, isolation guard and runner expression to v6's bytes; policy tests follow v8's plan preflight, split typecheck, root partitions and combined-tree queue canaries. A red scheduled full qualification opens or updates one tracking issue naming the source SHA and failing files, and the next green run closes it; only a schedule-gated job holds `issues: write`. Jane routing still needs the v8 runner-group entry; queue-candidate verification remains delivery evidence. | Kernel O1–O4. Publishing is a maintainer decision; neither a tarball pin nor this roadmap proves release readiness. CI maintenance: [trusted merge-queue overflow](docs/runner-routing.md#staged-merge-queue-overflow) is staged with live candidate/owner checks and hosted fallback, combined with the approved three-slot 5 GB/page-aligned guard and a distinct profile selector that quarantines legacy callers. Exact-delta and runner-profile regressions retain every other reviewed workflow byte; activation and real queue verification remain pending. |

| <a id="l02"></a>L02 | Partial | iPad, touch and Apple Pencil, selected 2026-11-05 ([plan](docs/plans/ipad-touch-plan-2026-11-05.md)): iPadOS Safari scaling and safe areas, touch navigation and selection in the 3D viewport, replacements for hover and keyboard, touch sketching, a Pencil mode (Pencil draws and selects, the finger navigates), an installable Home Screen app with persistent storage, and rendering budgets measured on iPad. Choose behaviour by `pointerType`, so mouse and trackpad input on any device stays unchanged. Eight bounded slices; device-only behaviour needs real-iPad evidence before a slice is Done. Preserve offline behavior and accessible input. Slice 6, Apple Pencil. - After a Pencil is used, a finger in a sketch drawing tool only navigates. This is controlled by Settings › Viewport "Only Apple Pencil draws" (default on), a new app-settings field that the Worker carries. - Palm rejection: fingers are ignored while a pen is down and for 300 ms after it lifts, and a landing pen cancels a finger gesture in progress. - The Pencil keeps the mouse path: precise taps, drags, marquee and hover. Evidence: - `touchNavigation.test.ts` (navigation-only fingers, abandoning a palm) - `app-settings.test.ts` - `api-routes.test.ts` (the setting is carried across an older client's save) - `test/e2e/touch-navigation.spec.ts`, driving a real pen and fingers through Chromium's input domain. Mutation-checked: with Pencil mode forced off, the finger draws. Not yet verified on an iPad: Pencil hover and Safari's own palm rejection. Slice 7, the Home Screen app and files. - **Installable:** a web manifest and Apple Home Screen tags. - **Installed-app only:** persistent storage and an offline shell (`sw.js`). The shell is network-first for pages and cache-first for content-hashed assets. It is never registered by a browser tab, the desktop shell or a test build. - **Exports** open the share sheet on a touch screen that can share files, and otherwise download. - **Import pickers** drop the `accept` filter on iPad and iPhone, so `.step`, `.openzcad` and `.shapr` files can be chosen. - **Download fix:** blob URLs are no longer revoked at click time (WebKit). Evidence: - `saveFile.test.ts`, including the revoke regression - `desktopBridge.test.ts` - `appleTouch.test.ts`, covering the manifest, pickers and storage - `offlineShell.test.ts`, which runs the shipped `sw.js` against an in-memory cache and a scripted network Not yet verified on an iPad: installing to the Home Screen, launching offline, and the share sheet. Slice 1, screen scaling and iOS basics: - The shell and stage size to `100dvh`, so Safari's toolbars no longer cut off the bottom of the workspace. - Touch pointers get 44px rail, command and Done/Cancel targets, and 16px fields so iOS does not zoom the page into a focused value. - Over the model, a press-and-hold no longer selects text or shows a callout, and the page no longer rubber-bands. - The numeric keypad no longer raises the system keyboard over its own keys. A touch on the field itself raises it, and the keypad stays above it. Evidence: - `touchContracts.test.ts` and `NumericKeypad.test.tsx`. - `test/e2e/tablet-layout.spec.ts`: six iPad sizes under Chromium touch emulation. Real-iPad verification is still pending. Follow-up to [#1230](https://github.com/esaueng/OpenZCAD/pull/1230): keep a long press's release click suppressed for the entire hold, then start the one-second delayed-click grace period when its pointer ends. A new deliberate press clears leftover suppression after a cancelled hold or a missing click. The focused `touchLongPress.test.ts` suite passes all eight cases using the repository's Vitest 5.0.5 and happy-dom 21.0.4 versions in an isolated local runner. Regressions cover holding the menu open for five seconds, a new tap after cancellation, and expiry after release. Restoring the original module makes the long-hold and cancellation regressions fail. This is local regression evidence; the updated PR heads still require the full CI gates. Slice 8, rendering limits. Desktop rendering is unchanged. - **Smaller shadow map:** touch devices render the key-light shadow at 2048² instead of 3072². - **Lower navigation resolution on slow devices:** a device under 45 fps navigates at a 1.25 pixel ratio and returns to its full ratio at rest. - **Context-loss recovery:** a lost WebGL context comes back drawing, with its environment and shadow map rebuilt. Evidence: - `renderBudget.test.ts` - `shadowMapSize.test.ts` - `test/e2e/touch-navigation.spec.ts`, where a real `WEBGL_lose_context` loss and restore leaves the viewer drawing with its shadows rebuilt iPad memory and frame timing remain device measurements under W01. Slice 2, touch gesture recognizer: `packages/viewport/src/input/touchGestures.ts` turns touch pointer samples into gestures: - tap and double tap - long press - one-finger drag - two-finger pinch (midpoint pan and spread scale) - two- and three-finger taps The timing rules are covered too: - a second finger takes over from the first; - the finger left behind after a pinch does nothing; - a press the host claims for a handle yields nothing more; - `pointercancel` cancels what was in progress. It is not yet wired to the viewport; that is slice 3. Evidence: `touchGestures.test.ts`, which is also in the unit smoke selection. Slice 4, stand-ins for hover, keys and right-click. - **Viewer-bar toggles**, shown to a coarse pointer only: - Multi-select: taps add or remove, as Shift or Ctrl does. - Free values: no snaps or inference, as Shift does. - **Long press**: a finger held 550 ms anywhere in the chrome opens that element's context menu (History rows, Assemble parts and joints), or else shows its tooltip. The click from the lift is swallowed. - **Settings › Viewport** gains "Undo with two fingers" (default on). It is a new app-settings field: the Worker accepts it as optional and keeps the stored value when an older client saves without it. Evidence: - `touchLongPress.test.ts` - `Tooltip.test.tsx` - `ViewerToolbar.test.tsx` - `app-settings.test.ts` - `api-routes.test.ts`, with a mutation check that the stored value is carried - `test/e2e/touch-navigation.spec.ts`, covering the Multi-select toggle and a held History row Slice 5, touch sketching. - A finger gets twice the screen tolerance for: - sketch, move, offset and measure snaps; - the sketch grab dot and the rotate ring's band; - curve picks. - While a finger drags sketch geometry, a magnifier above it shows the hidden point at 2× with a crosshair and names the snap it took. Evidence: - `pointerSlack.test.ts` - `session.test.ts` (finger grab and ring band) - `touchLoupe.test.ts` - `test/e2e/touch-navigation.spec.ts`: a finger draws a circle, and the loupe shows and then clears. Slice 3, touch on the 3D viewport. On empty space: - one finger orbits (it pans in a sketch); - two fingers pan and pinch-zoom about their midpoint; - a tap selects; - a double tap acts as a double click; - a held finger opens the pick list or context menu; - two- and three-finger taps undo and redo. A first finger that lands on a handle, gizmo, footprint, armed face or sketch-tool stroke keeps the mouse behaviour, and other fingers cannot disturb it. Touch picks edges in a band about 15 px wide. Safari's page pinch is suppressed over the viewport. Evidence: - `touchNavigation.test.ts` - `CameraController.test.ts` (touch pinch) - `PickService.test.ts` (touch edge band) - `test/e2e/touch-navigation.spec.ts`, real multi-touch through Chromium's input domain The existing touch and mouse viewport specs are unchanged. Device timing is not yet verified on an iPad. Owner-requested native iPad prototype: a Swift/WKWebView host packages the verified shared CAD/WASM bundle with private-origin resources, persistent local projects, native Files exports, an app icon, and an edge-to-edge viewport. Eight Xcode tests pass on the iPad Pro 11-inch (M4) simulator, including touch sketch/extrude, process-restart persistence, storage copying, and portrait and landscape canvas bounds. See [simulator evidence](docs/qa/2026-11-17/ipad-prototype.md) and [build instructions](apps/ipad/README.md). Physical iPad/Pencil acceptance and measured W01 memory/rendering budgets remain outstanding; this does not close the existing real-device requirements or add a ninth touch-plan slice. | Product P-1/P-2. |
| <a id="l03"></a>L03 | Complete | Save-state naming, read-only preview, per-checkpoint thumbnails/diffs, a versions list with editor/device/sync columns, and optional content dedup. Existing restore/branch browser tests are complete baseline, not a missing feature. Slice 3, per-checkpoint thumbnails, kept on the device only (plan: docs/plans/l08-l03-review-and-versions-plan.md). When the model stands complete on its newest save point, the browser draws that save's card from the meshes already on screen (the project-card renderer) and keeps it in a new IndexedDB store, `checkpointThumbnails` (local database version 14), keyed by the save point's random id so an account adoption that changes the project id needs no move. A preview draws the card of a save this device never drew. The versions list shows the card in an opened row. Cards are capped at 600 across all projects (oldest first, never the one just drawn) and are deleted with their project. Nothing is uploaded, so an encrypted project's save points gain no server-side record (ADR-024); another device shows no card until it previews that save. Evidence: `apps/web/src/lib/checkpointThumbnails.test.ts` (replace, notify, cap, deleted with the project), `RevisionsPanel.test.tsx` (card in the details), and the CI smoke case `test/e2e/save-state-versions.spec.ts` (a saved box's card appears as a data image in its row). Slice 1, save-point names and the versions list (plan: docs/plans/l08-l03-review-and-versions-plan.md). A save point can be renamed after it is made (Revisions › open a row › Rename; Escape keeps the name). The new `name` shows in place of the reason it was saved with, which stays in the row's details ("Saved as …"); an empty name, or the reason itself, clears it. A rename is a forward document write (a "Rename save point" revision, version advanced, no undo entry, since undo never rewinds the save-point list), so it syncs like any edit and needs edit rights. Each save point now records its author (`userId`, display name, and a device name such as "Chrome on macOS"), and every row shows three columns: editor ("You" for the signed-in account's own saves), device, and where the save's model is stored (this device, the account, both, or not stored). Both fields are optional and additive: older save points show only the storage column, older clients keep the fields through normalization (no schema version), and a malformed name or author is dropped without losing the save point. E2EE: the fields live inside the project document, which an encrypted project keeps encrypted and signed (ADR-024), and share-link snapshots carry no save points, so the server and link visitors learn nothing new. Evidence: `test/checkpoint-versions.test.ts` (author on all four save-point constructors, bounds, rename/clear/no-op, rename kept through undo, normalization and the account's save-point check), `RevisionsPanel.test.tsx`, `checkpointAuthor.test.ts`, and the CI smoke case `test/e2e/save-state-versions.spec.ts` (save, the three columns, rename, details, reload). Slice 2, read-only preview. Revisions › Preview on any stored save point that is not the current one builds that save state exactly (`geometry.syncOnce`, the same path as the assistant's exact preview) and shows it in the viewport in place of the project, behind a notice in the workspace's notice row: "Previewing “name” · Saved … · N bodies · read only", with Restore, Branch and Close preview. The row being previewed is marked in the list. Nothing is written: the project, its undo history and its save points are untouched, and every edit is refused ("A saved version is open for preview — close it to edit") until the preview closes; anything that replaces the preview geometry ends it. Restore and Branch from the notice close the preview first and then run the ordinary restore and branch, so they meet the project's own guards; a seat that cannot edit sees no Restore. The save state is read from this device first and then the account; on an encrypted project the account read is ADR-024's historical read (`encryptedProjectStore.loadRevision`: accepted only as the pinned head or an ancestor in the trusted chain, never moving the pin), so a preview never moves this browser's view of the project. Evidence: `SaveStatePreviewNotice.test.tsx`, `RevisionsPanel.test.tsx` (Preview offered only for a stored, non-current save; the previewed row marked) and the CI smoke case `test/e2e/save-state-versions.spec.ts` (a box saved, a cylinder added; preview draws one body while the project keeps two, Undo does nothing while previewing, Close shows two again, Restore from the notice leaves one). Slice 4, diffs between save states. Revisions › open a stored row › Compare opens "Compare “name”" with a choice of the project now or any other stored save point, and lists what changed in modelling terms: project name and units, features added, removed, renamed, suppressed or unsuppressed, edited, and a changed history order; parameters added, removed or re-expressed (`width: 20 → 35`); sketches (with their objects), bodies and drawing sheets. `diffSaveStates` (`packages/document-core/src/save-state-diff.ts`) is a pure function over two documents' canonical content: save points, revisions, the command log, undo history and meshes are left out, so a save compared with itself reads "No changes to the model". Both documents are read the way restore and preview read them (this device, then the account's trusted history, an ADR-024 historical read on an encrypted project) and compared in the browser; nothing is sent. Evidence: `test/save-state-diff.test.ts` (identical across a save point; added/renamed/suppressed/re-expressed and the reverse direction; an edited feature, a deletion with its body, a reorder and a project rename), `SaveStateCompareDialog.test.tsx` (wording, switching the comparison, an unreadable save), `RevisionsPanel.test.tsx` (Compare only for stored saves), and the CI smoke case `test/e2e/save-state-versions.spec.ts` (a saved box compared with the project after a cylinder: a feature and a body added, nothing changed by comparing). All L03 slices the plan takes up are on main: - save-point names and a versions list with editor, device and sync columns (#1668); - a read-only preview, a per-checkpoint thumbnail and diffs between save states (#1674). Each is safe under end-to-end encryption, since names and authors live in the encrypted, signed document, thumbnails stay on the device, and previews and diffs read the trusted history without sending anything. Optional content dedup was not taken up, as docs/plans/l08-l03-review-and-versions-plan.md (#1667) records. | Save-state plan “Not built”; no branch merging commitment. |

| <a id="l08"></a>L08 | Complete | Share-link review: comments anchored to a point on the model with viewer/commenter roles, and drawings on share links with a PDF download. Slice 2, comments anchored to a point on the model, for members of a plaintext project in the account (plan: docs/plans/l08-l03-review-and-versions-plan.md). File › Review › Comments opens the panel; Add comment, then a press on the model pins a numbered thread at the picked point (model coordinates, surface normal, body and document version), and the press selects nothing. Pins are drawn on the viewport's HUD at their points every frame, hidden behind the camera, and a press on one opens its thread. Threads take replies; the author, an editor or the owner resolves or reopens a thread (resolved ones fold under a count and their pins dim); the author or the owner deletes a comment, a thread with its replies. Viewers comment like any member. Storage is D1 table `project_comments` (migration 0058): 2000 characters per comment, 500 comments per project enforced inside the insert, deleted with the project, writes fenced during account erasure, and an erased account's comments in other people's projects deleted with it. Routes refuse an encrypted project (`assertPlaintextProject`) and answer 503 `COMMENTS_UNAVAILABLE` until the migration is applied; the browser refuses an encrypted project before any request ("Comments are not available on encrypted projects yet"), so nothing about an encrypted project's comments is sent in plaintext; encrypted comments are slice 4. Evidence: `test/project-comments.test.ts` (on the migrated SQLite schema: threads, replies from every role, reply-to-reply and cross-project refusals, resolve and delete permissions, cascade, the 500 bound, the erasure fence, request and anchor parsing), `CommentsPanel.test.tsx`, `projectCommentsApi.test.ts` (no request for an encrypted project), and the CI smoke case `test/e2e/project-comments.spec.ts` (a comment pinned on a saved box with its body and point, a reply, resolve, and the pins gone with the panel). Slice 3, viewer and commenter roles. Members: owner, editors and viewers all comment (slice 2); the author, an editor or the owner resolves; the author or the owner deletes. Share links: a plaintext link is a viewer link, view or tweak only, as before, unless the owner ticks "Visitors can comment" when creating it, which makes it a commenter link (migration 0059 adds `project_share_links.allow_comments`, off for every existing link; the owner's list names it "Commenter link"). A commenter link's visitor, with no account, opens Comments from File, signs with a typed name (1 to 60 characters) and pins threads or replies; their comments show as "Name (via link)", carry no account and record the link they came through (`project_comments.share_link_id`, set to null if the link row goes). A visitor never resolves or deletes, sees every comment with no member's account id, and posts through `GET/POST /api/share/comments` with the token in `x-openzcad-share-token`, never in the URL. That route applies the link's own read rules (not revoked, within 30 days, project not trashed, owner's sharing on, plaintext only) plus the link's comment flag, answers one opaque 404 otherwise, and is rate limited per address like every public share route. Encrypted links keep view and tweak only until encrypted comments (slice 4). Evidence: `test/share-link-comments.test.ts` (D1: the flag through create, list and the visitor's read; the route signed without an account and recorded against its link, members seeing it, and 404 for a viewer link, an unknown token, a revoked link, a trashed and an encrypted project; the name rule), `ProjectSharingDialog.test.tsx` (a commenter link is minted only when ticked), `CommentsPanel` visitor mode, and the CI smoke case in `test/e2e/project-comments.spec.ts` (a commenter link's visitor signs a comment pinned to the shared model, with no Resolve or Delete). All L08 slices are on main, each safe under end-to-end encryption (ADR-024, L11) by the owner's decisions in docs/plans/l08-l03-review-and-versions-plan.md (#1667): - comments pinned to the model for plaintext projects, for every member role (#1671); - commenter share links whose visitors sign comments (#1689); - encrypted comments as one signed, encrypted log per member (#1690); - drawing sheets chosen per sheet on share links, read only with a PDF download (#1670); - a confirmation that names the count before encrypting deletes a project's plaintext comments (#1708); - commenter links on encrypted projects: an owner-signed comment grant beside the link, and visitors' comments encrypted under the link's share key (#1716). Each slice's CI smoke spec is named in its evidence above. Slice 1, drawings on share links with a PDF download (plan: docs/plans/l08-l03-review-and-versions-plan.md). A share link still serves the current model only, and drawing sheets stay with the project unless the owner ticks them under Share › Drawings on links, which says that title blocks show as drawn (author, date, drawing number). The choice is a document field, `shareLinkSheetIds`, kept outside `drawings` so no sheet record or reader changes, and written as a forward write ("Choose drawings for share links": one revision, no undo entry) that syncs like any edit; no schema version (an older client keeps the list and shares no sheets). `sharedProjectSnapshotDocument` keeps exactly the chosen sheets, whole, in sheet order, and drops the list itself; a deleted sheet drops out and an empty choice removes the field. A visitor opens Draw on the link read only ("Drawings on a shared link are read only — Make a copy to edit them": no new sheet, no annotation tool) and exports each sheet as PDF, SVG or DXF in the browser. A link that carries no sheet keeps Draw locked ("This link shares no drawings"). E2EE: the choice and the sheets travel inside the document and the encrypted share snapshot every editor's browser republishes (ADR-024); no envelope, grant or route changes. Evidence: `test/share-link-sheets.test.ts` (only chosen sheets, sheet order, duplicates and unknown ids dropped, forward write, cleared field, deletion, undo of a model edit keeps the choice, malformed list dropped on load, accepted by the account's document check), `packages/document-core/src/shared-snapshot.test.ts`, `ShareLinkDrawings.test.tsx`, and the CI smoke case `test/e2e/share-link-drawings.spec.ts` (an owner's sheet of a 40 × 20 × 10 box, unchosen in the snapshot; chosen, a visitor with no account opens it in Draw read only and downloads a `%PDF-` file). Comments when a plaintext project is encrypted (plan decision 5, confirmed by the owner on 2026-11-17). Encrypted comment logs cannot carry a plaintext project's comments yet, so the migration's cutover deletes them with the rest of the plaintext. The deletion is never silent. Before anything is staged, the migration engine counts the project's comments through `GET /api/projects/:id/comments`; a server without comment storage counts none. While the project holds more comments than the owner has confirmed, the run stops on it. That covers a manual Encrypt projects run and the automatic default-on migration alike. Settings › Encryption › Encrypt existing projects then shows the row as "Waiting for your confirmation", says "Encrypting deletes N comments", and offers "Delete N comments and encrypt". A project that gains comments after the confirmation asks again. The "What changes" notes say comments are deleted on encryption. Evidence: `test/e2ee-project-migration-engine.test.ts` (nothing staged and both comments kept until confirmed, a confirmation for fewer asks again, the confirmed run cuts over and deletes them); `ExistingProjectEncryption.test.tsx` (the row names the count, offers no cancel while nothing is staged, and passes exactly the confirmed count to the engine); and the CI smoke case in `test/e2e/encrypted-migration.spec.ts` (two comments, the confirmation, then encrypted with no comments left). Commenter links on end-to-end encrypted projects (plan decisions 2 and 3a, approved by the owner on 2026-11-17). Visitors of an encrypted link now comment like plaintext commenter-link visitors. Their comments are sealed in their browser, so the server stores ciphertext only. When creating an encrypted link, the owner ticks "Visitors can comment". This signs a comment grant ("OZM", an owner-signed statement naming the project, link id and share-key hash). The Worker keeps the grant beside the link (migration 0061, `e2ee_share_comment_grants`) and stores it only from a device the owner's current device list certifies, and only for a live link. Per decision 3a it is not a third link-grant mode: the link stays `view`, and the encrypted document's link set is unchanged, so older builds still open the project. The visitor's snapshot frame carries the grant. The visitor checks it against the owner devices its verified share delegation names; the server's word alone never opens the comment box. A comment is an unsigned `share-comment` project envelope (wire code 8) under a fresh key, wrapped with the link's share key (`share-comment-key`, bound to the comment id). The visitor reads and writes them through the token header at `/api/e2ee/share/comments`, at most 500 per link. The server checks only the envelope's kind and project. Members open every commenter link's comments with the share keys they hold, after checking each grant against the owner's devices on their pinned list. The comments merge into the encrypted comment logs as "(via link)" threads and replies. Members reply to them; only the owner resolves or hides them, and hiding also deletes the comment from the link. Forgetting a link drops its grant and comments, and turning the project back into plaintext purges both tables. Evidence: - `packages/crypto` tests: comment grants (round trip, wrong link, unknown signer, forged signature, kept apart from link grants, canonical bytes) and wire codes 1–8. - `test/e2ee-share-comments.test.ts`: - against the Worker, a link without a grant takes no comments; - a granted link's visitor posts a thread and a reply, which members read "(via link)"; - the stored rows contain neither text nor name, and the link stays `view`; - an editor's grant and a grant for another share key are refused; - the owner hides a comment; - a malformed envelope gets 400; - a revoked link serves and takes nothing. - The route inventory, the share-token mapping and the recovery tool's review of 0061. - The CI smoke case `test/e2e/encrypted-link-comments.spec.ts`: the owner ticks the option, and a signed-out visitor signs and pins a comment. Only ciphertext and an `lcm_` id reach the account, and the owner's panel shows it "(via link)". Slice 4, comments end-to-end encrypted (L11; held for the owner's decisions in #1667). On an encrypted project every member, viewers included, keeps one comment log: a `comments` project envelope (wire code 7, appended to the kinds; 2 MiB plaintext ceiling) holding the comments they wrote and the resolutions they made, encrypted under the project key with a fresh version key, signed by their certified device and chained on the hash of the log it replaces (`/api/e2ee/projects/:id/comments`, D1 `e2ee_project_comment_logs`, migration 0060; sequence only moves forward; erased with its writer, purged with the project's encrypted data on a reverse migration). The account stores ciphertext only: who wrote a log and when stay readable, as for every envelope. The browser opens every member's log (signed by a device of a member of the project in that key epoch, any role; the log's sequence pinned per writer, so the account cannot roll one back; a log signed by someone other than the member it is served as is left out) and merges them (`mergeCommentLogs`): a comment belongs to the log that holds it, a thread is resolved by its author or the owner (latest event wins), and the owner removes others' comments by hiding them. Encrypted share links stay view or tweak only. Evidence: `test/e2ee-comments.test.ts` (real client, Worker and SQLite: a viewer's and the owner's comments, ciphertext only, a viewer's resolution of the owner's thread ignored, owner removal, a log moved to another member left out, a rolled-back log refused), `test/encrypted-comment-logs.test.ts` (parsing and merge rules), `packages/crypto` envelope and body tests (wire code 7, round trip), and the CI smoke case `test/e2e/encrypted-comments.spec.ts` (a comment pinned on an encrypted project, one encrypted log and no plaintext anywhere in the account, read back after a reload). Share links without history. When creating a link the owner may untick "Include history" (ticked by default; existing links are unchanged). Such a link serves the finished model only: the owner's browser writes each shown body on its own as STEP, the kernel's exact exchange form, and builds a new document holding one imported solid per body (`frozenShareDocument`), with the project's name and units and each body's name, chosen colour and opacity. No feature, sketch, parameter, save state, command log or import source of the project is in it, so none is sent. The link is view only (Tweak is disabled, comments stay available), is fixed when made, and the owner's list marks it "No history" with an Update action that freezes the current model into the same link and URL. Make a copy gives the visitor the imported solids as an ordinary project. A visitor is told "The owner shared it without its history" in the status line, the Shared chip and the Tweak tab's reason. Plaintext links (migration 0065, additive: `project_share_links.history`, `frozen_document`, `frozen_at`): the Worker stores the frozen document on the link row (gzip, base64, at most 1.9 MB stored), serves only it and never reads the project's document for such a link, serves no asset or import source through it, and accepts a document only if `frozenShareDocumentProblem` passes (an allowlist: imported-solid features with embedded STEP and nothing else), on create, on Update and again on every read. Encrypted links (ADR-024): a third owner-signed grant mode, `frozen` (wire code 3), so every member's browser learns it with the link and a build from before it refuses the link set instead of republishing the project into the link; publishers seal the frozen model given at create or Update, and on later republications (a new statement or key epoch) read the link's own stored snapshot back with the share key, check one of the owner's devices signed it and that it passes the same allowlist, and seal that again; with neither the link stays unpublished ("Needs an update"). The visitor's browser refuses a `frozen` link whose snapshot holds more than the frozen solids. The server keeps the mode column's values and marks the row `history = 0` (migration 0066, additive: `e2ee_share_links.history`). A plaintext link without history is not offered again as an encrypted link when its project is encrypted. Evidence: `packages/document-core/src/frozen-share.test.ts` (the builder, the allowlist, a copy), `test/share-link-without-history.test.ts` (D1 and in memory, and the routes: only the frozen solids served, the project refused as a model, Update, revoke clears the model, no asset or source, existing links unchanged), `test/e2ee-share-links-without-history.test.ts` (real client, Worker and SQLite: the visitor's document, the owner's and an editor's saves leave the model, forced republication, Update, reissue, a visitor refusing a snapshot that holds the project), `apps/web/src/lib/frozenShareModel.test.ts`, `ProjectSharingDialog.test.tsx`, `EncryptedShareLinks.test.tsx`, `projectShareClient.test.ts`, and the CI smoke case `test/e2e/share-link-without-history.spec.ts` (a visitor opens the frozen model, asks for no source, and copies only the solid). Review hardening (#1872): a plaintext link without history stores its token hash in a domain of its own (`frozen-v1:` and a labelled digest, held by triggers in 0065), so the bare-hash lookup of an earlier Worker, or of one whose schema probe still says 0065 is missing, cannot resolve it and serve the live project; the link insert and Update are fenced on the project still being plaintext in the same statement, so neither lands after the cutover that encrypts it; and a commenter link without history shows only the threads started through it. An encrypted link without history takes a snapshot from the owner alone (the server refuses an editor's, and visitors refuse one not signed by a device the owner's delegation names, `author-not-owner`), editors' pages leave such links alone, and a republication replaces exactly the snapshot it read (`replaces`, 409 `E2EE_SHARE_SNAPSHOT_REPLACED`), so it cannot undo an Update. Evidence: the same two test files and `packages/crypto/src/share-links.test.ts`. Encrypted comment and related E2EE request readers enforce their existing decoded-text or frame-byte ceilings while consuming the stream, before retaining an oversized body. Cancellation and split UTF-8 controls are covered in `test/request-body.test.ts`; full Worker visitor-path, visitor-header and owner grant regressions, plus legitimate encrypted-comment controls, are in `test/e2ee-share-comments.test.ts`. Existing limits, errors, encryption formats and access checks are preserved. | L01 live evidence, D01; [comparison §2.11](docs/reviews/reference-cad-comparison-2026-09-26.md). Tweak links stay the differentiator. |
| <a id="l09"></a>L09 | Complete | Manufacturing hand-off: direct send of a 3MF/STL export to a slicer, and a CNC-cut layout (copy, lay flat, project top faces, export DXF). Maker slices (2026-11-15): per-body names and colours in 3MF that common slicers read as separate parts or filaments, an "Open in slicer" action where a slicer accepts a local file (confirm that before relying on it for encrypted projects), and a document print-clearance parameter that threads, gears and joints read. Select from a measured use case. Maker slice 1, per-body names and colours in 3MF (PR #1558): each 3MF object is named after its body and coloured in its display colour through one core `basematerials` group (`object pid/pindex`, no extension required). Read from the slicers' public 3MF importers: Bambu Studio (colour import shipped in 02.09.01.70) names the parts and makes one filament per distinct colour; OrcaSlicer (main 2026-11-14) and PrusaSlicer (main 2026-11-06) name the parts but do not read core `basematerials` (Orca reads only `m:colorgroup`). Not confirmed in a running slicer. The exchange-loss contract now counts 3MF names and colours as kept; the reimport still merges the objects into one body (`three-mf-labels.test.ts`, `exchange-round-trip.test.ts`, `test/e2e/three-mf-names-colors.spec.ts`; docs/exchange-loss-contract.md). Maker slice 3, "Open in slicer" (PR #1564): a spike found that no slicer URL scheme accepts a local file the browser made (`bambustudio://` and `orcaslicer://` take only an http(s) URL; Bambu Connect's `import-file` takes only a sliced `.gcode.3mf`), so none is used and no file is hosted, which keeps encrypted projects off the server (L11). The desktop app opens the 3MF in Bambu Studio, OrcaSlicer or PrusaSlicer through Launch Services from a private temporary folder (not yet run on a Mac: the shell's tests ran on Linux, and the Apple Silicon workflow is manual); the browser downloads it to open from there. Neither uploads or archives it (docs/open-in-slicer.md; `slicer_handoff.rs` tests, `desktopBridge.test.ts`, `ExportDialog.test.tsx`, `test/e2e/open-in-slicer.spec.ts`). With slices 1 and 2 the maker slices are delivered; the CNC-cut layout remains. Maker slice 2, document print clearance (PR #1563, schema v60): `printClearance` on the document, a length in document units (a number, or an expression with units and parameters), set under Parameters → Print clearance as one undoable command. ISO, printable and custom threads without their own clearance (the Thread panel's new _Document_ fit) take it as their radial clearance; pipe threads never do. Gears without their own backlash take it as each flank offset by it (`2c / cos α` of thinning at the pitch circle), and the gear-mesh helper agrees. No joint has fit geometry yet: `documentPrintClearance` is what a joint fit reads. Unset, every build is unchanged and no stored document is rewritten; the rebuild cache digests the resolved clearance for the features that read it (`test/print-clearance.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/inPlace/thread.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/modelPanels/ParametersPanel.test.tsx`, `test/e2e/print-clearance.spec.ts`; docs/print-clearance.md). 3MF colours for OrcaSlicer: alongside the core `basematerials` group, each distinct body colour is written as its own Materials-extension `m:colorgroup` (declared under `m`, not required), and each object's `pid`/`pindex="0"` selects it. Read from the importers' source: OrcaSlicer (main 2026-11-20) keeps one colour per `m:colorgroup` by `pid` and reads no `basematerials`, so bodies now start on their own filaments; Bambu Studio (main 2026-11-17) reads both groups and resolves the same colours. PrusaSlicer (main 2026-11-06) reads no 3MF colour in any encoding (`pid` and `pindex` are parsed and unused, `basematerials` handling is commented out, no `m:colorgroup` reader), so no file change can colour bodies there; recorded in docs/exchange-loss-contract.md. Trade-off: an object names one property group, so a core-only 3MF reader now binds no colour to it (the colours stay listed in `basematerials`). Not confirmed in a running slicer. Evidence: `three-mf-labels.test.ts`, `test/exchange-round-trip.test.ts` and `test/e2e/three-mf-names-colors.spec.ts` (filaments 1 and 2 by the Orca and Bambu import rules in `test/support/three-mf-export.ts`). With the CNC-cut layout the row's scope is delivered. CNC-cut layout (docs/cnc-layout.md): File › CNC DXF (palette: Export CNC cut layout (DXF)) writes the export's bodies, unchanged in the document, laid flat on their largest planar face (ties to the face facing furthest down), every planar face then looking straight up projected exactly onto the bed as LINE/CIRCLE/ARC (polylines only for ellipses and splines), one `Z<height>` layer per face height in the export unit to 1 µm (a top face must be parallel to the bed and flat across its vertices), seams between coplanar top faces left out, parts in rows 10 mm apart with their body names on `LABELS`. Parts with no flat face or no upward face, and mesh references, are named in the status; with none left the export refuses by name. A row layout, not nesting; side walls, slopes and undercuts are not represented. Evidence: `test/cnc-layout.test.ts` (real kernel: a counterbored plate on `Z10`/`Z7`, a puck on its cap, a sphere refused, a 0.02° slope refused, a big plate resting on its intact side; inch output, byte-stable, read back through the DXF importer), `apps/web/src/lib/cncLayoutDxf.test.ts`, `test/e2e/cnc-layout.spec.ts`. | I04; D04; [comparison §2.9](docs/reviews/reference-cad-comparison-2026-09-26.md). |

| <a id="l11"></a>L11 | Partial | The browser encrypts project data (documents, revisions, sources, names, thumbnails, measurements, view state, exports), so the Worker, D1, R2 and the collaboration room hold only ciphertext; design in [ADR-024](docs/adrs/ADR-024-end-to-end-encrypted-projects.md), group keys on MLS (`mls-rs` 0.56). **Delivered:** Phase 0, the ADR (#743) and the public privacy page; Phase 1, document logic moved into the browser (#745, #759, #827); Phase 3, keys and devices (#785): signed device lists, recovery key, passkey unlock, device approval, macOS Keychain keys, the MLS engine (#784); Phase 4, format-2 encrypted projects and a ciphertext room relay; Phase 5, sharing: contacts, invitations, roles, member removal, encrypted share links; Phase 6, per-project assistant consent; Phase 7, encrypting existing solo and shared projects, plus a closed beta reverse path; Phase 8 groundwork: review packet, default-on and plaintext-freeze gates (both off), browser legacy plaintext reader, self-hosting docs, historical MLS authorization (#959), D1/R2 recovery tooling. The official beta offers opt-in encryption to every signed-in account. **Remaining:** written outside-review clearance (review tag not yet set; any open P0 or P1 blocks default-on and the deletion PR, 8-5); then default-on, the plaintext freeze, and deleting the server document pipeline and off switch; remaining L11 acceptance and migration/legacy-reader qualification; deferred macOS Keychain and native verification. Confirm whether the owner-device statement refresh and fresh-browser share-set ancestry follow-ups are closed. [Full history](docs/roadmap-history/l11.md). Assistant provider retention: an encrypted project's assistant request through OpenRouter carries `provider.data_collection: 'deny'`, so only providers that do not store or train on prompts receive it; the consent dialog states what each route keeps, and ADR-024's assistant section now lists the drawing sheet. Regression evidence: `test/assistant.test.ts`, `test/e2ee-assistant-consent.test.ts`, `AssistantPanel.consent.test.tsx`. The app's Content-Security-Policy no longer allows Cloudflare Web Analytics (script or beacon), so no edge-injected analytics script can run in the origin that holds decrypted models, unlocked keys and share-link keys; only Turnstile on the sign-in form remains. The privacy page says so (effective November 14, 2026). Regression evidence: `test/deploy-safety.test.ts` pins `script-src` and `connect-src`; `privacyPolicyPage.test.ts`. 13 November design review I83: the new device's and Add a device's hints name the controls an unlocked device actually has (Devices asking to join › Scan code or Compare codes), and a locked device shows no Remove beside the certified ones (`encryption-device-approval.spec.ts`). 13 November design review I79: the encrypted Share dialog makes no plaintext sharing call (only the metadata `GET /sharing` the format gate lets through; the red banner came from a review fixture that refused that route), and Create link now asks for the sharing confirmation itself and then makes the link, with no separate "Confirm sharing…" step unless existing links need it (`EncryptedShareLinks.test.tsx`, request log in `encrypted-sharing.spec.ts`, `encrypted-share-links-fixtures.ts`). Library rule: an account that owns or is a member of an encrypted project seals its library folders and adopts device projects encrypted even without the "Encrypt new projects" opt-in, so a "Keep both" or recovery copy of an encrypted project never reaches the account as plaintext. The Worker refuses plaintext folder records and plaintext adoptions for such accounts and reports `libraryEncrypted` on the preference route; the browser decides before sending (`encryptsAccountLibrary`). Regression evidence: `test/e2ee-project-format.test.ts`, `test/library-folder-sets.test.ts`, `projectFormatLoads.test.ts`, `libraryFolderAccountSync.test.ts` ([ADR-024 amendment 2026-11-14](docs/adrs/ADR-024-end-to-end-encrypted-projects.md)). 13 November design review I80, I76 and I84 (keys), held for owner review of the copy: setup step 1 says encrypted cloud projects cannot be recovered without the devices and the recovery key, and the acknowledgement is a checkbox; the fingerprint and the contact code each say what they are for; the profile deletion dialog says the encryption keys stored with the account go too (`CloudDataDeletionDialog.test.tsx`, `encryption-settings.spec.ts`). Sealed account settings: an account whose library is encrypted stores only the sharing switch and the assistant's route readable; every other setting, custom assistant instructions included, is sealed under the account key (`user_settings.sealed_settings`, migration 0055) and opened in the browser. Custom instructions travel with each assistant request, and the consent copy moves to version 3 to list them. The sealed-record layout is shared with library folders, whose fixed vector is unchanged. Regression evidence: `packages/crypto/src/sealed-records.test.ts` (independent fixed vector), `test/sealed-account-settings.test.ts`, `accountSettingsSealing.test.ts`, `consentCopy.test.ts` ([ADR-024 amendment 2026-11-14](docs/adrs/ADR-024-end-to-end-encrypted-projects.md)). Contacts lists are sealed under the account key (ADR-024 amendment 2026-11-14): each identity-signed entry travels in an AES-256-GCM record of fixed size that the Worker can order, link and signature-check but not read; account-key rotation reads older records through the account-key history; a plaintext list is resealed with the same entries, keeping every pin and approval head (`packages/crypto/src/sealed-contacts.test.ts` with a fixed vector checked against Python `cryptography`, `test/e2ee-contacts.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/EncryptionContacts.test.tsx`). Sealed shelf state: an account whose library is encrypted keeps pins, manual order and the archive in its sealed library record (folder set version 3) instead of readable project rows; the trash stays readable for sharing, purging and quotas. The Worker refuses readable shelf state for such accounts (`PROJECT_SHELF_STATE_SEALED`) on the organization and reorder routes and starts their copies at order 0; the browser sends only trash moves, sends pins, order and archive readable only after the library rule answered plaintext, overlays the account's own sealed state on listings (this device's rows, which every account signed in shares, add only an unmirrored trash move, also when the account's listing fails), journals an edit under the account that made it as the fields it changed over the account's own state, and migrates readable rows into the sealed record before a Worker-side conditional reset that never touches the trash. Regression evidence: `test/e2ee-sealed-shelf-state.test.ts`, `test/e2ee-encrypted-copies.test.ts`, `test/persistence.test.ts`, `sealedShelfState.test.ts`, `sealedShelfAccounts.test.ts` ([ADR-024 amendment 2026-11-14](docs/adrs/ADR-024-end-to-end-encrypted-projects.md)). 13 November design review I75 (held for an owner decision on the default): encryption setup ends with "Encrypt new projects?", ticked by default, and says existing projects stay as they are until encrypted below; Done saves the choice (`ProjectEncryptionPreference.test.tsx`, `encryption-settings.spec.ts`). Automatic migration again checks all four rollout gates before it loads any optional chunk, so closed gates and older servers leave Settings closed after the controller moved off the entry chunk (`automaticMigrationLoader.test.ts`; `encryption-settings.spec.ts` closed gates). Confirmed profile/all-data erasure suspends cloud-settings autosave and drains in-flight writes without requiring a locked device to upload preferences that the erasure removes. Device settings remain pending locally; failed erasure restores only the unchanged account session. Existing project retention, key cleanup, logout flushing and projects-only deletion behavior are preserved. Regressions cover failed settings saves, in-flight responses, failed erasure and account switching (`cloudSettingsAutosave.test.ts`). Simpler setup, unlock and device approval (plan `docs/plans/l11-encryption-ux-simplification.md`, ADR-024 amendment 2026-11-16): setup is one guided dialog (loss warning, recovery key saved and typed back in full, an optional passkey made with the key already in hand, then "Encrypt new projects" ticked by default); every key-authorized action confirms in one dialog with a passkey first and errors at the field; Settings › Encryption shows one status line and folds identity, recovery and beta tools under Advanced. Approving a device always certifies it (passkey or recovery key), so approved devices can save; existing read-only devices show as not finished. The new device's QR code is an `#approve-device=` link a phone's camera app opens straight into the approval, checked against the request's full key hash. Regression evidence: `test/e2ee-account-keys.test.ts` (approval always certifies), `deviceApprovalLink.test.ts`, `AuthorizeDialog.test.tsx`, `SetupDialog.test.tsx`, `ApprovalRequests.test.tsx`, `encryption-device-approval.spec.ts` (QR link approval, legacy read-only), `encryption-settings.spec.ts`, `encryption-passkeys.spec.ts`. Issue #1704, encrypted projects stranded by an identity discontinuity: a profile deletion keeps owned encrypted projects but erases the account's identity, wraps, leaves and authority records, and stable per-email user ids bring a later sign-in back to the same account, where a fresh setup is an unrelated generation 1. The deletion preview now counts owned encrypted projects, the dialog says their cloud copies stay unreadable to the account, and the Worker refuses a profile deletion (409 `ACCOUNT_DELETION_ENCRYPTED_PROJECTS`, before any fence) that owns more than the request acknowledges. A browser that still pins the erased owner identity keeps refusing, with every pin kept, but names the cause and the way forward (export, import, save the copy) instead of a retry; a rotated identity or an unchanged one keeps the generic withdrawal refusal. Regression evidence: `test/e2ee-identity-reset.test.ts`, `CloudDataDeletionDialog.test.tsx`, `TopBar.test.tsx`. Assistant settings distinguish deployment configuration from encrypted-project availability. The built-in Nemotron free endpoint is marked unavailable for an open encrypted project. OpenRouter no-route errors explain the privacy requirement, and the Responses-to-Chat fallback keeps data collection denied and the zero-price cap in place (`test/api-routes.test.ts`, `test/assistant.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/SettingsPage.test.tsx`). Hosted deployments let encrypted projects use built-in free OpenRouter inference after fresh consent explicitly discloses plaintext retention and possible training. The consent binds this policy and both provider requests retain zero price caps. Personal OpenRouter tokens keep the existing encrypted routing policy (`test/e2ee-assistant-consent.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/assistant/AssistantPanel.consent.test.tsx`). |  |
| <a id="l12"></a>L12 | Deferred | Curated materials/environment presets (drag a preset onto a body or face, one lighting environment with rotation/elevation/intensity, a capture with resolution presets); decals, custom materials, AR and generated imagery are out. Split from L02 on 2026-11-05. Select independently from demonstrated user need. | Product V-1. |
| <a id="l13"></a>L13 | Partial | 3Dconnexion SpaceMouse navigation, selected 2026-11-13. The browser reads the device directly over WebHID (Chrome and Edge; Safari and Firefox have no WebHID): connect from Settings › Controls, then the cap pans, zooms, tilts, spins and rolls the view about the selection's or model's centre, every SpaceMouse Pro key acts (views, roll, projection, display, rotation lock, Esc and modifiers), and synced settings tune speeds, dead zone, one motion at a time, rotation centre, roll and reversed directions. Remaining: recorded real-device evidence for axis directions and button bits on a SpaceMouse Pro (3DX-700040); button remapping; the desktop shell, whose WKWebView has no WebHID, through a native HID reader; Safari and Firefox only through 3Dconnexion's driver SDK, if users ask. Browser slice: a pure HID report decoder with preference-aware axis shaping (`packages/viewport/src/input/spaceMouse.ts`); a continuous 6-axis camera step in `CameraController` that turns rigidly about the selection's or visible model's centre (free orbit with roll held, or an upright turntable that stops at the poles), zooms toward that centre, and rolls a quarter turn on the roll key; a WebHID connection hub (`apps/web/src/lib/spaceMouse.ts`) with a rotation lock; every SpaceMouse Pro key mapped (`apps/web/src/lib/spaceMouseActions.ts`); synced `viewport.spaceMouse` settings (speeds, dead zone, one motion at a time, rotation centre, roll, reversed directions) in Settings › Controls under Navigation, with a key reference; and `hid=(self)` in the Permissions-Policy. Coverage: `spaceMouse.test.ts` (viewport and web), `spaceMouseActions.test.ts`, `CameraController.test.ts` (3D mouse motion), `SpaceMouseSettings.test.tsx`, `app-settings.test.ts`, `api-routes.test.ts` and `test/e2e/space-mouse.spec.ts`, driving a simulated device; the owner's SpaceMouse Pro connected and moved the view on a local build, with no recorded run yet. | New 2026-11-13. |
| <a id="l14"></a>L14 | Complete | Agent API: expose the canonical document, the schema-constrained patch operations and exact preflight to user-chosen AI agents through a Model Context Protocol (MCP) server, so users can bring their own model. Proposals keep the assistant contract (validated patch, exact preflight, one undoable transaction). The bridge runs on the user's machine (the desktop shell or a localhost relay), so end-to-end encrypted projects never reach the server in plaintext. Start with a design note covering authentication, scope and rate limits. Grant hardening follow-up: `openzcad-mcp` now applies the strict-path rule to every folder from a granted file up to the filesystem root, at grant time and on every read and write. Each folder must be a plain folder (read with `lstat`, so a link is refused), owned by the user or root, and writable by no other account unless sticky. No other account can then rename a path component between the check and the no-follow open (the second independent review's ancestor-swap finding on #1622). Regression evidence: `test/agent-bridge.test.ts` (an ancestor folder opened to others refuses a new grant and an existing grant's next read; the sticky bit restores it; a link in the path is refused by the walk). Delivered as Phase 1 of the [design note](docs/plans/l14-agent-api-design.md): `openzcad-mcp` (`apps/agent-bridge`), a local stdio MCP server on user-granted `.openzcad` files, built on the transport-free tool core `@openzcad/agent-api`. It exposes the canonical document (`get_document`), the assistant's exact digest (`get_digest`), the schema-constrained patch vocabulary (`get_patch_schema`; `CAD_PATCH_JSON_SCHEMA` is also the patch tools' input schema) and exact preflight (`preflight_patch`), and `apply_patch` re-runs `parseCadPatchProposal` against the digest and `preflightCadPatch` on the exact kernel before committing one `CommandManager` transaction (one ADR-023 undo entry, "Apply agent patch"). Authority is the launching OS user: no listener, no network (a guard replaces the web network globals), project files must be owned by and writable only by that user and sit in a folder others cannot write (sticky folders excepted), rechecked on every read and write with the path required to resolve to itself and the file opened without following links, ciphertext is refused by shape, and tools address projects by alias or id only. Scope (`read`/`preflight`/`edit`, default `preflight`) is fixed on the command line; rate limits are 120 reads and 12 exact-kernel calls per minute (lower-only flags), one kernel job running and one waiting, a 120 s cooperative derivation deadline read from the clock (so a kernel call that holds the event loop cannot slip past it) and a commit claim that refuses to write once the deadline has passed and is never reported as timed out after writing, and a refusal of any patch whose undo entry ADR-023's history budget would drop, 2 MiB request lines and a 4 MiB document answer; writes are fenced by stored-revision hash and document version and replace the file atomically. E2EE: plaintext stays on the user's disk and goes only to the agent host the user configured; nothing reaches the server. Regression evidence: `packages/agent-api/src/agent-api.test.ts` (protocol, scope, limits, gate, transaction and undo), `test/agent-bridge.test.ts` (grants, ciphertext refusal, atomic write and conflict fence, network guard) and `test/agent-bridge-e2e.test.ts`, which spawns the real server over stdio under a network tripwire and drives a real edit: digest, exact preflight of a 40 → 60 mm width change (24 000 → 36 000 mm³), refused and stale applies leaving the file byte-identical, the apply, an importer-accepted file whose exact rebuild measures 36 000 mm³, and one undo back to 24 000 mm³. The live bridge to an open workspace (loopback relay or desktop shell) remains a separate owner decision (design note, Phase 2). | F03 operation vocabulary; L11 plaintext boundary; L06 for the desktop bridge. |
| <a id="l15"></a>L15 | Complete | Scripting and custom features: a typed text form of the feature history that round-trips with the GUI (an edit in either updates the other), scripted custom features whose inputs are parameters and query-based picks rather than face indices, and a versioned stability contract for published scripts. Scripts run in the kernel worker under the same exact-or-refuse rules. Design note delivered ([docs/plans/l15-scripting-design.md](docs/plans/l15-scripting-design.md)): the history text is a projection of the canonical document compiled back into the L14 patch vocabulary (one validator, exact preflight, one undo step); custom features are declarative, bounded scripts with typed inputs and exact-count topology queries expanded in the kernel worker's build loop; a v1 language and script-version contract with a golden corpus. Delivery slices A–D follow. Slice A, history text read path ([design](docs/plans/l15-scripting-design.md)): a new lazily loaded `@openzcad/scripting` package prints the document's history (`printHistory`: `openzcad history 1`, typed parameters, every feature kind in history order, sketches with their objects, node metadata) and reads it back against the document (`readHistoryText`) with line-and-column diagnostics. Expressions sit in backticks only where document-core's own expression walker says a field is one, and are evaluated with the field's F05 quantity (an expression the document already cannot evaluate is a warning, a new one an error); ids print as `#id` and must resolve; stored V5 picks print as `face(#feature, "lineage")` / `edge(…)` with their implied hashes left out and are restored from the stored witness, never invented; another version, other units, an unknown kind, field or reference and a parameter cycle are refused by name. The History panel's header gains History as text, a read-only dialog loaded on demand that follows every edit. No schema change. Regression evidence: `packages/scripting/src/history-text.test.ts` (print shape, determinism, round trip of awkward values, comments and layout, detached sketches, typed refusals), `test/history-text-corpus.test.ts` (the identity law over 19 fixture documents (kernel-built with real V5 references, parameter-driven and legacy): the text reads back to exactly the history the document holds), `HistoryTextDialog.test.tsx` and `ModelPanel.test.tsx`. Slice B, history text edits: `compileHistoryEdit` diffs an edited history text against the document and compiles it into a `CadPatchProposal` (parameter set/rename/delete, feature rename/delete, new primitives, and three app- and agent-compiled operations kept out of the model's schema: `update_feature`, `reorder_feature`, `update_sketch_object`), which passes the assistant's parser, digest check and exact preflight and commits as one undo step; unchanged text compiles to nothing, and what the text does not edit (a kind, @metadata, a sketch's plane, drawing new sketch objects) is refused at its statement. The History as text dialog edits, checks (problems by line and column, changes listed) and applies through the app's own patch path with a version fence and a stale-edit banner; the agent API gains `get_history_text`, `preflight_history_text` and `apply_history_text`. Regression evidence: `packages/scripting/src/compile.test.ts`, `test/history-text-edit.test.ts` (an edited text applied on the exact kernel prints back as exactly that text, volumes follow, one undo restores the original text; a kernel refusal stops preflight), `packages/agent-api/src/agent-api.test.ts`, `HistoryTextDialog.test.tsx`, `describe.test.ts`. Slice C, scripted custom features (schema v110, builds on v108; v109 is claimed by #1741): a `custom-feature` step embeds a versioned script (`id`, `version`, `api 1`, `source` and its SHA-256 `digest`) and replaces its target body. Scripts are data, not code: `@openzcad/scripting` parses and checks them (typed `input`s, `pick`s that are exact queries on witness data — `type`, `normal`, `axis`, `radius`, `max`, `min` — with a declared count, and `fillet`/`chamfer`/`shell` steps; at most 60 steps, no loops, functions, imports or I/O) and interprets them in the kernel worker's build loop, where each step runs as the ordinary builder under the instance's id on the body the previous step left, its picks re-evaluated on that body on every build. A pick that does not find exactly its count, a digest mismatch, an unknown input or any step's refusal refuses the whole instance and publishes nothing from it; a published version is immutable within a project. Instances are created, edited and moved to a new version in the history text (the app's scripting surface) and through the `add_custom_feature` patch operation. Regression evidence: `packages/scripting/src/script.test.ts` (grammar and diagnostics, SHA-256 vectors, exact query semantics, interpreter), `test/custom-feature.test.ts` (a rounded, shelled tray matching its closed-form volume, picks re-run after a parameter edit, count, digest, input and republish refusals, creation and version bumps through the text, script errors at their line), `test/e2e/history-text-custom-feature.spec.ts` (`@ci-smoke`: add, edit, refuse, undo, redo and reload in the browser). Slice D, the stability contract: [docs/scripting.md](docs/scripting.md) is the reference for history text version 1 and scripts api 1 and states the contract (within a major nothing changes meaning; additions are optional; a published script version is immutable and pinned by digest; moving to a new version is an explicit, previewed, undoable edit). `test/scripting/contract/v1` freezes four scripts with the volume, face count and warnings each builds to on the exact kernel (a rounded tray at two input sets, a chamfered lid, a shelled cup with a filleted foot, and a recorded count refusal) and a document with the exact text it prints; `test/scripting-contract.test.ts` pins every file by SHA-256 and checks them on every change. With slices A–C (history text that round-trips with the GUI through the shared L14 patch surface, and scripted custom features with exact query picks run in the kernel worker), L15 is complete. | F05 expressions; R01/I01 query-based references; K05 lineage; shares its operation surface with L14. |

## Completion and maintenance rules

1. Refresh main, the pinned kernel and open PR ownership before selecting a
   master ID. Use a new isolated branch; one bounded slice and one ready PR.
   A stacked PR names its dependency. Do not silently switch items when blocked:
   record the missing evidence/input against the selected row.
2. Before coding, record the slice, observable acceptance cases, dependencies
   and any schema/API/rollout decision. Preserve exactness/provenance, units,
   verified topology references, offline persistence and one-transaction undo.
   AI and manual edits use the same validated command path. Never weaken a
   refusal, oracle or tolerance to mark progress.
3. Run the root [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md) verification gates and the row's browser,
   geometry or performance checks. Exact-head hosted checks are required before
   a merge decision; queued/skipped jobs are not passing. Deployment and manual
   Apple Silicon execution retain their separate authorization requirements.
4. Do not edit a row in the implementation PR. Add one
   [evidence fragment](docs/roadmap-evidence/README.md)
   (`docs/roadmap-evidence/<row>--<slug>.md`) with the shipped subset,
   evidence (tests plus PR), remaining acceptance and changed dependencies,
   and a `state` only when the status changes. A fragment merges with its
   code, so its state is never "In review": use `Partial` or `Complete`.
   A standalone `docs(roadmap): fold evidence` PR folds fragments into rows.
   Edit this file directly only to add, split or restructure rows. Add a
   linked child ID for a split, never a second untracked plan. Keep detailed
   acceptance specifications linked; do not copy their status tables back here
   as multiple competing versions.
5. Close only the scope proved. Move completed rows into the delivered baseline
   with their stable IDs, or retain a short `Complete` row so links survive.
   A row states the outcome and the remaining work; when its evidence outgrows
   a few sentences, move the history verbatim into
   `docs/roadmap-history/<id>.md` (or its plan or ADR) in a standalone docs PR
   and keep the outcome, a delivered summary, the remaining work and a link in
   the row.
   Record live acceptance separately from merged source. Revalidate old kernel
   claims upstream and old UI findings on the rendered current app before work.
